# Mockup catalog

**Status:** ACCEPTED (living index — update it in the same change whenever a mockup is added, or a listed one is superseded/ships)

Interactive HTML mockups live in `mockups/` at the repo root. This is the map of what each one is for, what it promotes/supersedes, and its status relative to the shipped app — read the entry for the surface you're touching, not the whole file. Moved out of the root `CLAUDE.md` (ADR-0097) so a task that never touches design/mockups doesn't load this list.

On conflict between an older mockup and `design-language.md` or an accepted ADR, the newer accepted doc wins — each entry below says so where it applies.

## Writing one

**The procedure is a skill: `.claude/skills/design-mockups/`** ([ADR-0175](../decisions/0175-the-mockup-procedure-is-a-skill.md)) — a working template, the traps that only appear once a file is rendered, and a renderer that shoots every theme × width and reads the measurement table back. It carries the _how_; this section and the ADRs it cites stay the home of the _rules_, so a rule changes in one place only.

**A mockup renders in BOTH themes and carries a toggle** (owner, 2026-08-02; [ADR-0158](../decisions/0158-dark-mode-ships-and-the-ink-a-surface-carries-is-a-token.md) §16). Dark mode ships, so a surface drawn only in light is drawn half. The mechanism is three lines, because a mockup that inlines the app's stylesheets already contains the dark remap, inert: two buttons that set `data-theme` on `<html>` — the same attribute `lib/theme.ts` writes. The mockup's own `mk-*` chrome flips with them (its own variables plus a `:root[data-theme='dark']` block after the light one), or a dark frame is judged on a white page the app never has. Binds on **create or revise**; the older files below are dated records and are not retrofitted.

Two conventions the newer files already follow: inline the app's real CSS with `mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs` (an `APP-CSS:` manifest plus a generated `<style data-app-css>` block) rather than hand-copying token values, and **read measurements off the live DOM** in the file rather than typing them into the prose.

- [`mockups/map-basemap-ours-v2.html`](../../mockups/map-basemap-ours-v2.html) — the approved **two-theme MapLibre terrain rebalance**, superseding `map-basemap-ours-v1.html` for colour while keeping its renderer and pin findings. Drawn after the first working device map was reported too dark and too compressed between terrains. It lifts the night field, widens water/built/natural/road steps, and moves light with dark so both themes keep one hierarchy. The owner’s Iceland question exposed a separate Protomaps `landcover` palette inherited unchanged by v1; v2 therefore stress-tests and measures grassland, barren/volcanic ground, urban area, farmland, glacier, scrub and forest at regional zoom as well as the close-up terrain keys. Mountains still have no relief or contours in this archive: peaks can be labelled, but palette cannot invent elevation. Interactive: theme, 360/390 width and current/proposed palette. Renders all four theme×width combinations with webfonts and no console errors; every proposed terrain stays below chroma 14, the day floor stays above L* 78, water stays at least ΔE 14 from land, and the smallest regional land-cover separation is ΔE 4.7 dark / 4.8 light. Approved by the owner on 2026-08-14 and built into `lib/map-style.ts` in the same session.

- [`mockups/trip-dashboard-v2.html`](../../mockups/trip-dashboard-v2.html) — the interactive **Trip-mode** design reference _(predates ADR-0028; colors retrofitted — on conflict the design docs win)_. Its HOME section is superseded by `trip-home-v3.html`, its INDEX section by `trip-index-v1.html`, its DAY section by `trip-mode-day-view-v1.html`, and its MAP section by `map-tab-v1.html` below (ADR-0109) — so all four tabs now have a dedicated mockup and this file is kept only for its cross-tab reference value.
- [`mockups/trip-mode-day-view-v1.html`](../../mockups/trip-mode-day-view-v1.html) — the **Day-view tab** design exploration for ADR-0043 (now-line + derived phases, phase-scoped verbs, archive chrome): two review rounds recorded in this file; the shipped now-line is a quiet hairline, superseding the mockup's louder first-pass (dark chip + glow + pulse).
- [`mockups/logo-v1.html`](../../mockups/logo-v1.html) — historical design record for the **app logo** (ADR-0087, Accepted): four explored directions (waypoint marker with an amber core / marker holding a mini departure board / brighter board-tile evolution / RTL route to an amber destination), the chosen one fitted into the landing + invite screens. Kept for the design rationale, not a build spec — and note it draws the mark with the rounded teal edge ADR-0087's 2026-09-03 amendment removed, so the file no longer matches the shipped icon.
- [`mockups/trip-index-v1.html`](../../mockups/trip-index-v1.html) — the **Index tab** (bookings + documents), promoting ADR-0047 (linkage, delete/unlink, per-file docs, notes + hotel WiFi), ADR-0048 (merged edit sheet with the shared TimePicker/IconPicker; transport origin/destination place fields), and ADR-0049 (mode = chrome only; a during-trip past/upcoming split; a read-only washed archive). Interactive: the demo toggle switches during / empty / archive; tapping a booking opens the merged edit sheet, its delete opens the delete/unlink prompt. The booking-entry _form_ lives in `plan-mode-v1.html`. Post-build fixes to this screen are mocked in `index-fixes-v1.html` below.
- [`mockups/index-fixes-v1.html`](../../mockups/index-fixes-v1.html) — **post-build fixes** to the shipped Index (2026-07-17 triage), promoting three _Proposed_ ADRs: ADR-0052 (documents: mobile-first PDF open/download, per-row ⋯ → rename/change-type/replace/delete, upload+load spinner, cause-aware errors, four distinct type badges), ADR-0053 (bookings: a read-only detail view + ⋯ menu → edit/delete like the event card, and the merged edit surface reachable from the linked event), ADR-0054 (a hotel / multi-day booking is an ambient span shown as backdrop, excluded from the day-at-a-glance rail + count). Demo toggle: מסמכים / הזמנה / לוז. Supersedes `trip-index-v1.html` only on booking tap-to-detail (the rest of that file stands).
- [`mockups/index-findability-v1.html`](../../mockups/index-findability-v1.html) — historical design record for the **single-scrolling-page findability direction**, superseded by `index-findability-split-v1.html` below (ADR-0098, Accepted — not yet built): a category filter chip row over the bookings list (neutral, not category-tinted — the color budget stays reserved), a search box (icon → text input) over title/confirmation code, past bookings collapsed by default mirroring ADR-0061's Plan Home "completed checks" toggle exactly, and the bookings/documents sections each independently shrinkable via one big tappable title. Kept for the design rationale (why the split was chosen over this), not a build spec. Directional glyphs are the real `ui/NavArrow.tsx`/`ui/Icon.tsx` SVGs, not Unicode arrow characters — a `‹`/`›`/`→` glyph is `Bidi_Mirrored` and silently flips direction inside `dir="rtl"` (confirmed by rendering test). Carries the motion pass described below.
- [`mockups/index-findability-split-v1.html`](../../mockups/index-findability-split-v1.html) — the **accepted Index redesign** (ADR-0098, Accepted, shipped session 60): the Index becomes a landing with two peer tiles (🎫 הזמנות / 📎 מסמכים, each showing its count + a one-line preview), tapping one pushes a dedicated full screen with an icon-only back button + "אינדקס" label (matching the shipped `.back` pattern in TripSettings.tsx/AllTrips.tsx). The bookings screen carries the category filter chips + search + past-bookings collapse (mirroring ADR-0061); documents keep their existing type-grouping, unchanged. Real `NavArrow`/`Icon` SVGs throughout (see ADR-0098's arrow-mirroring note), plus a **motion pass**: the past-list animates open/shut instead of snapping via `display:none`, a chip/search change reveals matching rows with a small per-row stagger, and every tappable control gets a brief scale-down on tap — motion instead of more color/content, since the color budget and Home's board-hero stay off-limits (ADR-0028); all of it respects `prefers-reduced-motion`. Also carries a **scale check** (not a shipped feature): three disabled "בקרוב" placeholder tiles (📝 הערות / 🔎 מחקר / 🖼️ מדיה) testing how a 5-tile landing reads, since Assaf may want more content types later — nothing beyond bookings/documents is decided or in scope; ADR-0098 explicitly flags that "מחקר" already names the shipped Map-tab research feature and would need a distinct meaning before it's ever real. Demo toggle: טיול עמוס (17 bookings) vs טיול קטן (a handful). Post-ship polish feedback on this build is explored in `index-bookings-compact-v2.html` below.
- [`mockups/index-bookings-compact-v2.html`](../../mockups/index-bookings-compact-v2.html) — the **accepted bookings header/search redesign** (ADR-0100, Accepted, shipped session 62), reacting to polish feedback on the shipped ADR-0098 build ("the search bar and the closeby area [don't] read as polished"): the back-row and title/count row MERGE into one compact line (`[← beside "אינדקס"] ... [count]`, arrow pointing right — fixing a wrong-direction/orphaned-arrow arrangement on Assaf's own reference screenshot); the category chips + search become one denser row (each chip carries label+icon+count, e.g. "רכבת 🚄 2"; a minimal real-SVG, non-circular search icon sits fixed at the row's end and, when opened, covers the chip strip in place with a small animation rather than opening a second row); the chip row's edges fade (`scroll-snap` + a `mask-image` gradient) instead of hard-clipping a partially-visible chip; and a **Plan vs. Trip mode color palette** — the Index's own accent (selected chip / search icon, not per-category tinting) follows the active mode: neutral ink in Trip (unchanged), plan violet in Plan, extending ADR-0028's existing per-mode selected-accent rule (the day-strip's own selection already does this) rather than adding new color policy. Colored category badges are explicitly deferred. Demo toggle switches Trip/Plan chrome + accent live. Superseded, for this header/chip/search area only, the interim search-bar-replaces-title-row shape shipped straight to `IndexBookingsView.tsx` in the ADR-0098 follow-up session — ported into `ui/IndexBookingsView.tsx`/`ui/IndexBackRow.tsx`/`ui/IndexDocumentsView.tsx`/`screens.css` in session 62 (`docs/planning/2026-07-21-session-62-index-bookings-header-redesign-build.md`).
- [`mockups/trip-home-v3.html`](../../mockups/trip-home-v3.html) — the redesigned **Trip-mode Home** (ADR-0045): board hero unchanged, quick-access reworked to three real shortcuts (next code / WiFi / documents; the mockup's fourth tile, navigate-to-next, was deferred to the maps work and **shipped in session 104** — the app is now 4-up, so the mockup's 3-up grid is behind the shipped Home on that one point), and the fixture weather/FX/budget glance row replaced by a derived "day at a glance" card. Real-data-only home. The quick-access tiles deep-link into the Index and follow the derived-vs-managed empty-state rule (ADR-0050): derived tiles (next code, WiFi-from-hotel-Booking) vanish when there's no source; the managed documents tile stays with a ＋ invite. Companions: `mockups/trip-home-glance-options-v1.html` (glance counting-model options — lead-with-"נותרו" chosen) and `mockups/trip-home-refinements-v1.html` (quick-access derived-vs-managed states incl. the no-hotel WiFi reflow per ADR-0050, the glance under overlapping/contained events → counts top-level blocks, and the empty-day teach state).
- [`mockups/booking-presentation-v1.html`](../../mockups/booking-presentation-v1.html) — historical design record for **booking presentation across the Trip-mode Home & Index** (ADR-0059 applying ADR-0063's per-category time-behaviour profile — **Accepted, shipped** session 33): the board hero's transition-moment surfacing (hotel check-in/out, transport departure/arrival), the "inside a booking" ambient/teal treatment, the shared row/detail/hero grammar, and the glance's uncounted check-in/out markers (ADR-0054 rebased). Kept for the design rationale, not a build spec.
- [`mockups/glance-transition-labels-v1.html`](../../mockups/glance-transition-labels-v1.html) — first exploration of the **glance transition-label** clutter (the amber-pill-per-edge markers laddering on a busy day): current-vs-three-alternatives (journey brackets / minimal pins / legs line). Superseded by `glance-timeline-labels-v2.html`.
- [`mockups/glance-timeline-labels-v2.html`](../../mockups/glance-timeline-labels-v2.html) — the **unified glance rail-annotation grammar** (ADR-0077): one pill primitive where tint = family (amber time-anchor / neutral structure) and the connector = shape (bar+feet span / stem point / tick block-note); time-anchors above the rail, structure below; a same-day flight/ferry is a span, a lone hotel check-in a point, overlap/containment block-notes; a legs-line collapse past two lanes, plus edge anchor-in and short-span handling. Includes a three-grammar head-to-head (two-bands chosen) and scenario stress-tests (ordinary / arrival edges / crowded departure / short span).
- [`mockups/when-field-v1.html`](../../mockups/when-field-v1.html) — the **WhenField date/time standard** (ADR-0083): the one date/time entry primitive every form uses. Before/after (the cramped native `datetime` box that clipped the date + showed AM/PM → the standard), the `span` variant's two [native full-width date + compact tap-to-open amber time] endpoints with an auto-closing time panel, a `+N` days badge, and a duration read-out. The `day` variant (native date + amber TimePicker) is the event grammar unchanged.
- [`mockups/when-field-drawing-v1.html`](../../mockups/when-field-drawing-v1.html) — **how a "when" is DRAWN** (2026-08-09), promoting [ADR-0177](../decisions/0177-a-when-reads-as-a-sentence.md) (**Proposed**). The sibling of the file above and not its replacement: `when-field-v1` settled _which primitive_ collects a date/time (ADR-0083, which stands); this one settles _how one looks_, which nothing ever did — so four hosts painted the one `DateField` four ways and the time beside it made a fifth. **§1 is the census**, measured off the four rendered boxes rather than asserted. The proposal: a when reads as a **line of prose whose values are tappable**, the token generalised from [ADR-0161](../decisions/0161-a-move-names-a-position-and-an-event-owns-its-length.md) §7's one-off `button.bld-time` (hairline chip, and its `::after` touch-target trick), one subject per row per `RouteField`'s existing rule, amber spent on the clock alone. Panels, bounds and the native `<input type="date">` are untouched. **§6 is the rejected alternative, drawn**: the first draft's tidied boxes, kept so it could be rejected on evidence (207.9px against the sentence's 144.5px for the same event). Interactive: theme, width, affordance loudness (hairline · underline · tint) and date form (named · numeric) — the two calls left to a device pass. Measurements re-run on `document.fonts.ready`. **Two shipped defects it exposed**, both logged separately: `App.css`'s `.field .df` mono rule is dead (beaten by `field.css` from a lazy chunk — the cascade here is chunk load order, not import order), and `.wf-date-cell`'s `flex: 1.3` is a workaround for a 19px date that does not fit half a row at 360px, not a weighting.
- [`mockups/plan-mode-v1.html`](../../mockups/plan-mode-v1.html) — the interactive **Plan-mode** design reference (prep-dashboard Home, itinerary builder, booking entry, research; phone + tablet) _(colors + light "drafting table" chrome + violet prep hero retrofitted to match the shipped app / ADR-0028; dark-mode token remap still absent — on conflict the design docs win)_. Its **booking-entry form** carries the ADR-0047/0048 fields: all 6 booking types, IconPicker chip + derived category, shared TimePicker date fields, per-type notes, hotel WiFi, and transport origin/destination place fields. Its **research panel** is superseded by `map-research-v1.html` below (ADR-0115): the `＋ אולי` action and the shelf beside it stand, but the result card's `4.5★`, `1.2 ק״מ`, and category glyph were drawn before the cost model and are fiction against the shipped pipe — a prediction carries none of the three.
- [`mockups/plan-home-readiness-v1.html`](../../mockups/plan-home-readiness-v1.html) — historical design record for the **Plan-mode Home "what's missing" rework** (ADR-0061 — **Accepted, shipped**): the real-data-only checklist with do-the-thing CTAs (add-booking sheet / seed the day builder / settings invite), the two new checks (🛂 documents/passports per-traveller; 🔑 confirmation-code completeness), and completed-checks collapse. Kept for the design rationale, not a build spec.
- [`mockups/document-upload-v1.html`](../../mockups/document-upload-v1.html) — historical design record for the **document-upload pick control redesign** (ADR-0086 — **Accepted, shipped** as the reusable `FilePicker`/`ChoiceGrid` primitives): the drop-zone stating the accept+size contract up front, the camera-capture path (phone-first, ADR-0017), and the picked-file preview card, replacing the raw native `<input type="file">`. Kept for the design rationale, not a build spec.
- [`mockups/loading-states-v1.html`](../../mockups/loading-states-v1.html) — the **loading states** design, promoting [ADR-0105](../decisions/0105-loading-states-design.md) (**Accepted, boot + snapshot shipped session 69**): the three loading moments as one language, extending the U-10 feedback family (ADR-0078). **Boot** (`shell.booting`) is now `ui/feedback/BootScreen` — the loud "board power-on": the dormant departure board warming up (the glow _ramps_ via `.fb-boot-mark::before`, never the reserved live pulse; mono carries the numerals since JetBrains Mono has no Hebrew glyphs). Theme, unlike mode, is known at boot (a device setting), so it is theme-aware: **light = cool paper (`--screen`) day ground + ink numerals + a small amber halo (shipped)**; dark = the night board, amber lit — **designed, not built** (every color already reads a token via `color-mix`, so the remap is inert-ready, not wired, until `data-theme='dark'` ships per design-language "dark-mode readiness"). **Snapshot** (`snapshot.loading`) is now `ui/feedback/HomeSkeleton` + `ChromeSkeleton`, replacing the old two-bar shimmer: a content-shaped, per-mode skeleton (Trip = board hero + quick-access tiles + glance card, Plan = prep hero + readiness bar + checklist) rendered through the **real** `.wp-board-*`/`.quick`/`.qa`/`.glance-day`/`.prep-*`/`.checklist`/`.chk-*` classes (not a parallel copy — ADR-0096), behind a mode-themed header bar that shows the real trip name/icon immediately. **Upload** (`docs.upload.saving`) is still the pending doc-row trailing status (dim + cloud-up, ADR-0092), unbuilt: a **determinate `NN%` + mini-bar** while uploading (an upload has a known size), replacing the indeterminate `מעלה…` spinner, needs upload-progress wiring — the outbox mechanism (ADR-0056) is wired (`OUTBOX_VERB.UPLOAD_DOCUMENT`) but `lib/api.ts`'s `uploadDocument` reports no progress events yet; offline stays the static `ממתין להעלאה`; mode-independent sync grammar (`--sync-*`). Amber is kept a **time accent** throughout — a full amber ground was tried and rejected as off-budget (design-language / ADR-0028). Grounds in ADR-0028/0052/0078/0092 and ADR-0016/0040 (mode is derived, so boot can't know it). Build session note: `docs/planning/2026-07-23-session-69-loading-states-build.md`.
- [`mockups/map-tab-v1.html`](../../mockups/map-tab-v1.html) — the **Map tab** design (ADR-0109, Accepted; **Phases 3 + 4 shipped**, sessions 80/81/104/105), the list-first Phase-3 surface + Phase-4 near-me. Two points where the shipped app deliberately differs, both recorded as ADR-0109 amendments: the denied banner offers a **retry or a settings instruction**, never the mockup's OS-settings deep-link (impossible for a web page), and offline `מרחק לא זמין` appears only on rows that were already showing a distance, not on every coord row. Its Phase-6 panels (the rendered-map preview + the map↔list integration diagram) are a foreshadow, now superseded as the build spec by `map-embedded-v1.html` below (ADR-0121), which designs that phase in full. Promotes: the pinned-place **list re-emphasized by mode** (Trip pre-selects today, Plan defaults to all) with per-row deep-links; **day scope via the real shared header day strip** (`ui/domain/DayStrip`) — the map is a day-scoped surface like the Day view, so tapping a day focuses it **in place** (no navigation), and "all days" is a **map-local scope chip** since the app tracks one active date; the **filter chip row (type · maybes) reusing the Index grammar** (ADR-0098/0100 — label+count pills, edge-fade mask, covering search, mode-tinted `--idx-accent`), all pure offline-safe derivation; **category-coloured pins** on "quiet base, loud pins" (ADR-0106 C / 0028 / 0105) — the 5-hue palette (ADR-0038 §2), a rounded **badge** in the list and a **teardrop (tip pointing down)** on the map; commitment is the hard/soft grammar (solid + 🔒 / dashed / muted ambient / hollow coordless), not colour; teal stays for location affordances (near-me · distance · נווט), amber for time (route ETA + the next-stop ring). **Audited row** (ADR-0109 §2): one labeled "נווט" action + row-tap-to-open (no eye/compass icons), meaningful context ("18:40 · רכבת לקיוטו", never a bare "יציאה"), cached Google **rating** as a `★` tag (§9). **just-in-time geolocation** (asked only on tapping near-me, behind a pre-prompt; degrades to the default sort if denied, ADR-0006); **near-me as a list re-sort + distance chips** (no spatial dot before Phase 6). Side panels: pin legend, the Phase-6 rendered-map preview, the **Phase-6 map↔list integration vision** (map pane + draggable list sheet + `רשימה/מפה` toggle, §10), the **Places picker flow** (Phase-1 keystone — search → Autocomplete predictions with a "כבר בטיול" `googlePlaceId`-dedup chip → an enriched cached `Place` [coords · address · timezone · rating] → the name-only "Place-lite" fallback; §12), the **zone chip**, and **places+timezones in forms** (§8 — manual event = 1 place + an explicit **category selector in the booking type picker's card idiom** (a compact horizontal-scroll row of 9 categories, no colour swatch) [§11] + a glyph-only icon + zone chip; **event-from-booking** shows a read-only "✨ נגזר מסוג ההזמנה" category (derived from `Booking.type`, ADR-0038); transport = 2 places + 2 zone chips + `+1`; empty Place-lite picker). Demo toggles: mode (Trip/Plan) + state (normal / near-me / denied / offline). _(Note: an earlier draft used a teal Waypoint pin + category-glyph; reverted to category colour per ADR-0038/0028 — see ADR-0109 §3 revision note.)_
- [`mockups/map-research-v1.html`](../../mockups/map-research-v1.html) — **Plan-mode place research** (Phase 5 of the Maps & Places epic), promoting [ADR-0115](../decisions/0115-plan-mode-place-research.md) (**Accepted, shipped session 111**): the design ADR-0109 deliberately deferred to its build session, mocked — as ADR-0109 instructed — **against the real picker** rather than against `plan-mode-v1.html`'s research sketch. Promotes: the Map tab's **one search control with two halves** (it opens on the free half, filtering the trip's own places exactly as shipped; in Plan mode it also offers `חיפוש בגוגל`) where the **paid half is armed by intent** — this is the first surface in the app that spends money per keystroke, so filtering your own list and buying an Autocomplete session must not be the same gesture; once armed it is the picker's behaviour verbatim (pause-gated debounce, one session token, dedup-before-spend, the `usePlaceSearch` core untouched). A **result card says only what the relay returns** (neutral pin badge + primary name + secondary address + one `＋ אולי`, the name a free `place_id` deep-link out to Google Maps so a candidate can be vetted before we pay to resolve it) — which **supersedes `plan-mode-v1.html`'s research card** on its `4.5★` (an Enterprise field ADR-0111 defers), its `1.2 ק״מ` (a prediction has no coordinates), and its category glyph (types aren't in the field mask). `＋ אולי` **is** the pin in a list-first world: one pick enriches the `Place`, an **uncategorised** unconsumed idea references it, and the place appears on the Map list (`על המדף`) + the Plan shelf; a result already referenced is **stated, not re-addable** (`על המדף` / `כבר בטיול`). Offline the Google half is **absent, not disabled** (the near-me rule, ADR-0109 §7); a 429 is a soft banner. Rows reuse the shipped `.place` grammar and `.map-grouphead` headers, not a second row. State toggle: ריק · טוען · תוצאות · נוסף למדף · חסימת קצב · אופליין, plus design-notes panels on the cost model and the boundaries (Plan-mode only).
- [`mockups/map-embedded-v1.html`](../../mockups/map-embedded-v1.html) — **the embedded map** (Phase 6 of the Maps & Places epic, the epic's last phase), promoting [ADR-0121](../decisions/0121-embedded-map-phase-6-design.md) (**Accepted, shipped session 133** — read the ADR's build log for the ten places the build refined it). The human Google Cloud gate it was waiting on was cleared in session 132; two non-blocking items remain outside the code, both on the backlog: the **cloud styles** are authored but not yet imported/associated (so a real map still wears Google's default look), and the build vars are not on Railway **staging**. **The shipped app now supplies the pin/pane CSS this file inlines** — `ui/domain/map-pane.css` and `ui/primitives/snap-sheet.css` joined the `APP-CSS:` manifest in session 133, so the mockup's own **Phase-6 delta** block is now a historical design record sitting _above_ the real rules rather than the spec for them. Two things the app does differently on purpose, neither a divergence in the design: **a coordless row IS tappable** (it still selects — it is still referenced, it just has no camera to move; the mockup's note says untappable, which ADR-0121 §8's revision 4 already corrected), and **offline the app renders no sheet at all** rather than a full-height one, because with the map absent the tab is simply the scrolling list it has always been (the mockup keeps one DOM tree for six states, which the app does not have to). The design ADR-0109 deferred with an explicit condition — reconfirm current Maps API + pricing **first** — which the promoting session did (ADR-0121 §1: ADR-0106 §A–F and ADR-0108 §1 held; a `mapId` is now **mandatory** for advanced markers and Routes Essentials caps at **10** intermediate waypoints). Promotes: **a map pane over a draggable list sheet at three snap heights** (peek/half/full) with a `רשימה · מפה` segmented toggle as the shortcut to the two extremes of the **same** height axis the handle drags, making ADR-0109 §10's vision concrete; the sheet **always peeks**, because the list is the only view that works offline and the only one that can hold a **coordless** place; the sheet + toggle are **view state, not a back layer** (a pane of the tab, not an overlay over it — no `Modal`/`useOverlay`, no back registration, back leaves the tab at any height, the same reading ADR-0109 session-105 applied to the geolocation pre-prompt); **pins as `AdvancedMarker` content built from our own markup** over the **same** `place-usage.ts` derivation the list reads — Google's `PinElement` cannot express the dashed-idea / desaturated-ambient commitment grammar, so the list badge and the map teardrop share the `--cat-*` tokens by construction (ADR-0109 §3), coord-bearing places only, with §6's **single amber next-stop ring finally on a pin** as the session-104 amendment promised — drawn as an **outline on** the teardrop (a `box-shadow` spread, which follows `border-radius`, so it traces the pin's own silhouette) rather than a pill-shaped box **around** it, which read as a circle drawn near a pin instead of one highlighted shape and the quiet treatment of ADR-0117's behind-you outcomes carried onto pins too; **the order carried on the pins, as numbers** — a line between two stops is symmetric and never said which end you reach first, and the number is free (the index in `comparePlacesBySchedule`'s day sequence, the order `DayView` already renders, so map and list cannot disagree); a pin with **no position** in that sequence gets no number (an unconsumed idea, an ambient stay night), so numbered-vs-unnumbered is itself the plan/idea distinction, and the 🔒 comes off the pin to free that corner since solid-vs-dashed already says committed-vs-idea (the row keeps it); a **fourth pin population the list never had to name** — a place that fails the day filter but sits **inside the viewport** (Thursday's café you are standing next to), rendered as a hollow, glyph-less, unnumbered **ghost** in its category hue, day-scope only since that is the only scope which excludes anything, and tappable because its row is not in the sheet; the whole four-tier ladder being **prominence and fill with no new colour**; and a **dashed, neutral day connector in Plan mode only** (a straight segment is not the route you will walk, so drawing it solid would lie; with the order on the pins its one remaining job is revealing the day's _shape_ — the zigzag worth reordering — which is a planning question, not a live one, so Trip mode's canvas stays quieter, and one day means no per-day palette, which makes the tab's existing scope chip ADR-0106 §E's "day toggle") and leaves **solid + amber** unspent for a real paid Routes polyline + ETA later); **offline the map pane, the toggle and the billed map load are all absent, not disabled** (the third application of the near-me / research rule); and **our controls, not Google's** — `disableDefaultUI` with one re-centre button (which is also the escape hatch from "a manual pan wins until the next scope change"), `gestureHandling: 'greedy'` so one finger pans without Google's un-styleable two-finger overlay, and the Google logo/attribution kept per ToS. Notes panels carry the reasoning, including the **cost mechanic that shapes the build** (Dynamic Maps bills **per map instantiation**, so one instance per tab visit is fine on arithmetic but re-creating on the toggle / a filter / a sheet drag / the tab's per-second clock tick is forbidden) and the reconfirmation table. **This mockup renders through the app's real CSS, and is self-contained** — the shipped stylesheets are **inlined** in the app's own import order (`styles/tokens.css` → `App.css` → `screens.css` → `ui/domain/day-strip.css` → `choice-grid.css`/`feedback.css` → `screens/map.css`) by [`mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs`](../../mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs), which reads the file's `APP-CSS:` manifest comment and rewrites its generated block in place — **re-run it after changing any of those stylesheets** (`node mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs mockups/map-embedded-v1.html`; idempotent, and the file is in `.prettierignore` so formatting can't fight it). Relative `<link>`s were the first attempt and failed on a phone, which cannot resolve `../frontend/src/…` through a share sheet or `content://` provider; inlining mechanically keeps the CSS genuinely the app's (an app change shows in `git diff`) while letting the file open anywhere. The tab is built from the real class names and markup (`.header.mode-chrome`, `.wp-daystrip`/`.wp-daypill`, `.map-screen`, `.choice-grid.pills`, `.map-scopechip`/`.map-nearchip`, `.map-grouphead`, `screens/Map.tsx`'s `PlaceRow` verbatim, `.fb-banner`), following `loading-states-v1.html`'s precedent rather than hand-copying a parallel set (ADR-0096 / rule 8). Only three things are its own, each fenced and labelled in the file: `mk-*` mockup chrome (page, phone frame, state toggle, notes panels), the **Phase-6 delta** — the genuinely new CSS, written in the app's `map-*` naming so it reads as the diff for `screens/map.css` — and the faked base canvas. Rendering against the real sheets **caught two divergences** a hand-drawn copy had hidden, both fixed: the shipped `.map-tag.next` is `--amber-deep` bold text rather than a filled amber pill, and `.place.nextstop`'s ring is a specific 34%-over-22% amber, so the pin's tag and ring now reuse those exact values instead of a second amber. **The base canvas is faked in CSS** — a real map needs the browser key the human step still gates — so it is a look reference for the cloud-styled quiet base (desaturated cool paper, POI clutter dropped, no colour flood), **not** a rendering. **The row tap focuses our map and never leaves the app** — the Phase-3 tap opened Google's place view, which was only ever the stopgap for having no map; going to Google is now the `נווט` button (directions), "view on Google" is retired from the row, and tapping a row while the list is at **full** height drops the sheet to **half**, since focusing a map you cannot see is useless (a coordless row and an offline tab have no pin to focus, so neither is tappable). Interactive: tap a row or a pin to see them select as one; tap a **ghost** to see it state which day it belongs to. **Selecting a place reveals the way through to the entity behind it** — a `Place` holds only name/address/coords/zone/rating, while the confirmation code, notes and documents live on the referencing booking or event (ADR-0112), so the selected row lists one labelled entry per in-scope reference (`הזמנה · רכבת לקיוטו · יציאה`, in the meta line's own vocabulary rather than a generic "details"), full-width and ≥40px because an 11.5px meta tag is not a phone target. A **coordless** row selects and shows its reference too — it is still referenced, it just has no camera to move, which is why the verb is _select_ and focusing is what selection does when coordinates exist. Also carries the **two ride-alongs taken into the phase** (ADR-0121 §9): the outcome filter as **one** `מה נשאר` toggle in the `אולי` chip's idiom — hiding everything settled, on the canvas and in the list, ghosts included — and a quiet **`6 באזור`** count top-inline-end, clear of Google's attribution, which finally makes ADR-0106 §4's "pan/zoom **is** the area filter" visible. Drawing them caught a real trap worth reading: the chip's count and the `באזור` readout **answer different questions and legitimately differ** — a chip counts what the _list_ will render (ADR-0119), the readout counts what is on the _canvas_ — and a first pass mislabelled the chip with the scheduled-unsettled count (`4`) where `5` rows actually survive, which is exactly the count-vs-render defect ADR-0119 was written to fix. Transit/traffic layers were considered and **dropped**. State toggle: חצי (default) · מפה מלאה · רשימה מלאה · יום (טיול) · יום (תכנון — the connector's only home, with Plan chrome + violet strip selection) · אופליין; plus the `מה נשאר` chip, which is live in any state.
- [`mockups/map-reach-v1.html`](../../mockups/map-reach-v1.html) — **every place-bearing surface reaches the map** (Phase 5 of the Maps & Places epic, plus its sibling "a booking with NO place"), promoting [ADR-0121](../decisions/0121-embedded-map-phase-6-design.md)'s **2026-07-28 amendment** (Accepted; **built session 148 — so this file is the built design's reference, not a proposal**). **Read this entry before adding any affordance to a dense row: this file's job is MEASUREMENT, and it changed the design twice.** The first placement — a distinct `מפה` control in each row's trailing slot — looked obviously right and was killed by rendering it against the real stylesheets: at 390px it took `Ichiran Ramen` from one line to two on both the day card and the builder row, at 360px it exploded a long Hebrew builder title to **five** lines (`.bld-ttl` has no ellipsis, so it wraps without limit), and it **silently cut** a transition title from 184px to 126px, which is worse than a visible wrap because nothing on screen says it happened. Promotes: the way to the map being the row's **category badge** — zero layout cost, and the right object rather than the free one, since ADR-0121 §6 / ADR-0109 §3 already make the pin and the badge one thing in two form factors sharing the `--cat-*` tokens; a teal ring plus a corner **pin** (a bare dot says "something is here", the pin says what the tap does, in the silhouette of what you land on, ADR-0087) as a real SVG and never 🗺️, since emoji are content and the row's one emoji slot is the category glyph (ADR-0038); a badge with no place staying **exactly the inert badge it always was** — no ring, no marker, no role — which the file draws deliberately beside a live one, because "absent, not broken" is only legible as a contrast; `מפה` and `ניווט` **not** being an atomic pair (directions are a live on-the-ground verb, orientation is mode-neutral, so the builder row carries one and not the other); and, for the sibling half, the `מיקום` fact **always** rendering for a single-place booking — stating `לא הוגדר מיקום` in a muted voice with `＋ הוספת מקום` beside it, where before the row simply did not render and no surface anywhere said the booking was placeless (it cost a false bug report: a two-night hotel "missing from the map" was a hotel with no place) — plus the quiet `Field` `hint` under an empty location field in both authoring forms, naming the five things a placeless entity loses, with the save **never** gated (ADR-0109 §6's anti-nag rule). Its first panel is a **table, not a screen**, because most of this decision is about where the affordance deliberately does NOT go, and a table is the only honest way to draw an absence. **Two rejected alternatives are drawn and labelled rather than deleted:** the trailing-slot control with its measurements, and **the board** — wiring the badge onto Home's hero was tried, read too loud on the app's one dark glowing surface, and was backed out to the hero's own redesign (the backlog's Hero 2.0, briefed in [`docs/planning/2026-07-28-hero-2-0-design-brief.md`](../planning/2026-07-28-hero-2-0-design-brief.md)). **Renders through the app's real CSS, and is self-contained:** the shipped stylesheets are inlined in the app's own import order by [`mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs`](../../mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs) from the file's `APP-CSS:` manifest — **re-run it after changing any of them** (`node mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs mockups/map-reach-v1.html`; idempotent, and the file is in `.prettierignore` so formatting cannot fight the generator). Every fragment is the app's own markup and class names (`.wp-event` + `.wp-event-face`, `.bld` + `.row-actions`, `.transition-row`, `.wp-listrow`, `.bk-fact`/`.bk-loc`, `.field` + `.field-hint`, `.modal-overlay[data-variant=sheet]`), so the three genuinely new rules — `.wp-placebadge`, `.field-hint`, `.pp-addbtn` — are drawn by the real declarations rather than hand-copied values. Only `mk-*` chrome is its own, plus **two labelled app overrides**, both because a phone frame stands in for the viewport: `.modal-overlay` `fixed` → `absolute` (so a sheet lands inside its frame) and `.app` `100dvh` → `100%`. **It also found a bug it does not fix:** a Latin address beginning with a numeral reorders in the RTL flow (`2-14-5 Kabukicho, Shinjuku, Tokyo` → `Kabukicho, Shinjuku, Tokyo 2-14-5`) because `.bk-fact-v` carries no `dir` — the ADR-0118 family, affecting every address in every `bk-fact`, so it went on the backlog rather than into an unrelated diff. Seeing it at all is the argument for this whole convention. Not drawn, because nothing changed there: the Map tab's own row, and `EventCard`'s expanded action row.
- [`mockups/map-basemap-ours-v1.html`](../../mockups/map-basemap-ours-v1.html) — **הקרקע שלנו · what the map looks like when WE draw it**, the look reference for [ADR-0186](../decisions/0186-the-map-is-ours-and-it-works-on-a-plane.md) (**Accepted as a design; not built**). Written because the owner, on being told that ADR would stop Google drawing the map, asked _"Wait what you're saying is that we stop rendering a Google maps map? What's map Libre?"_ and then _"I'd like to try it and see how it looks. I'd like it to kind of look similar to how it is with Google maps and also support dark and light themes."_ **This is the first map mockup in the catalog whose canvas is real** — every predecessor above fakes the base in CSS because a Google map needs a browser key and a billed load, which is exactly the dependency ADR-0186 removes. Here it is MapLibre GL JS rendering a genuine 22.7 MB OpenStreetMap extract of central Tokyo, so the ground, the labels, the roads and the app's real `.map-pin` markup are all on screen together at 360/390. Promotes: **ADR-0125's terrain vocabulary as a Protomaps _flavour_ rather than a Google cloud style** — the palette ports almost verbatim precisely because §8 wrote it as relationships (warm land against cool water, built mass achromatic against chromatic nature, everything below chroma 14) rather than as a list of CBMS feature ids, which is what made it portable at all; **two themes from one download**, restyled on a live map, against the two latched Map IDs ADR-0121 §11 needs today; and §7's pedestrian mall landing on `--paper` exactly, which becomes one flavour key. **Three findings the render produced and reading could not.** (1) **`.map-pin` must sit INSIDE the marker element, never be it** — MapLibre positions its marker with `.maplibregl-marker { position: absolute }` and `map-pane.css` sets `.map-pin { position: relative }` (ADR-0123, since the pin's parts position against it); both are one class deep, so **whichever sheet loads last wins**, ours does, and every marker laid out in normal flow — measured, six pins stacked into a 204px column, painting outside the pane and clipped. vis.gl's `AdvancedMarker` renders our div as a child, which is why the collision has never existed; keeping that shape is what makes ADR-0186 §2's "the pins port untouched" true rather than nearly true. (2) **The white ring does nothing in dark.** ADR-0125 §8 describes the hues running "with a white ring and a shadow", and that ring measures **1.31:1** against park in light and **1.01:1** in dark, where the ring is `--card` (`#1a2740`) on a dark ground — so in dark the **fill** carries the separation alone (6.15–9.5:1). Coherent, but it means anyone dimming the pin fills for dark loses both separations at once. (3) **MapLibre measures its container once, at construction**, so a pane that settles later needs an explicit `resize()` — which the app already has the answer for in `lib/observe-resize.ts`, watching that same box for ADR-0122 §7's band. Also **measures what ADR-0186 could only estimate**: the Tokyo extract (139.55,35.55 → 139.90,35.80, ~32×28km, z0–14) is **22.7 MB**, pulled from the 127.88 GiB planet in 13.4s over 40 HTTP range requests — an order of magnitude under that ADR's own worst case. **Renders through the app's real CSS** (`styles/tokens.css` → `App.css` → `ui/domain/map-pane.css`, inlined by [`mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs`](../../mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs); in `.prettierignore`), and its own proposed block is **almost empty on purpose** — the pane, the pins and ADR-0123's whole ratio system are already ours, so swapping the renderer beneath them needs no new pin CSS at all, which is the cheapest possible statement of the ADR's central claim. **Its one honest limit:** tiles are served locally (`pmtiles serve` on :8081), so the canvas is grey for anyone opening it cold and everything else still measures; the recipe is in the file's own foot panel. What it deliberately does **not** answer: how this reads on a phone in the hand, and whether OSM's coverage in Japan is good enough in practice — both device-pass questions, not desk screenshots. Interactive: theme (בהיר · כהה), screen (360×640 · 390×844), pins (מוצגות · מוסתרות), and **place labels** (מוצגים · מוסתרים) as a feel call rather than a decision the file pretends to have made.
- [`mockups/map-split-v2.html`](../../mockups/map-split-v2.html) — **the split earns its screen** (Phase 2 of the map panel's second pass), promoting [ADR-0122](../decisions/0122-map-split-controls-over-the-canvas.md) (**Accepted; built session 141 — so this file is the built design's reference, not a proposal**), which amends [ADR-0121](../decisions/0121-embedded-map-phase-6-design.md) §5. **Read this entry before authoring any full-bleed mockup: this file reproduces the app's LAYOUT TREE, not only its CSS.** Session 131's `map-embedded-v1.html` read the real stylesheets and still designed something unbuildable — it silently supplied a flex column `.map-screen` did not have (ADR-0121 revision 5: "a mockup that reads the app's CSS still does not inherit its layout tree") — and Phase 2 _is_ a layout-tree change, so the frame here contains the real `.app` flex column → the real `.header.mode-chrome` with the real `.wp-daystrip` → the real `<main class="body is-fullbleed">` → `.map-screen.is-split` → `.map-split` → `.map-pane` + `.wp-snapsheet` → **and the real `.nav`**. The Phase-6 mockup had neither the body element nor the nav, which is exactly why nobody noticed that **370 of 844 phone pixels are spent before either half of the split gets one**. The **only** app rule this file overrides is `.app { height: 100dvh }` → `100%`, because the frame stands in for the viewport (labelled as such in the file); there is deliberately no fake OS status bar inside the frame, since a PWA's `dvh` already excludes it. It also **prints its own measurements**: a ⟨תקציב הגובה⟩ panel reads the live DOM (`getBoundingClientRect`) and tabulates the budget for the shipped two-row layout and this design side by side, at 390×844 / 390×734 / 360×640 — so every number in ADR-0122 is checkable on screen rather than asserted, and a wrong claim shows up as a wrong row. Promotes: **the controls leaving the layout** — the two fixed rows become ONE row floating over the canvas, so the split is the whole body and the map runs _underneath_ the chips instead of starting below them (measured: the pane 208→250 at `half`, 358→420 at `peek`; the list 2.6→3.2 rows; larger relative gains on the smaller phones, which is where the shipped split fails worst), with **pins kept clear of the row by the camera** rather than by layout (§7's fit inset grows by the same constant that writes `--map-controls-h`; the ⟨אזור שהמצלמה שומרת פנוי⟩ state draws the band, and the file states the two limits — a manual pan can still put a pin there, and `fitPaddingFor` drops padding claiming half an axis); **three controls at rest instead of seven** (scope · one `סינון` control · search), the facets opening **in place** on one tap in ADR-0100 §3's cover-the-row shape — which ADR-0101 superseded on the Index because a keyboard and a result list had nowhere to go, neither of which a facet strip has, so the reason it failed for search is the reason it fits here — with the `✕` pinned at the row's fixed end, and the categories as **glyph + count** chips — all six fit one 390px row where the shipped worded pills fit two and a half, because the glyph is already the category's whole vocabulary (ADR-0038) and the label survives as the button's accessible name; the collapsed control **stating which facets are on and carrying no count** (ADR-0119); **`קרוב עכשיו` moving to the sheet's own top row** beside the toggle (scope belongs to the tab, filters to the split, **sort to the list** — session 138's own distinction); **the list-sliver peek retired outright** — 116px shows 0.8 of a row and buying a whole one would have cost the map 97px permanently, so the map extreme is the sheet's own **top row and nothing of the list** (517px of map against 358 shipped) — with **`full` stopping below the controls** as a third `SnapStop` variant and **`half` keeping 0.56** because the measurement exonerates it; the **drag target widening to the whole `.wp-snapsheet-top`** (390×51 from 76×16, ADR-0017's floor) with a **live, draggable** demo — the slop threshold, the window-bound move listeners and pointer capture taken at **drag start** are all implemented here, and the file records that it got the last two **wrong first** (a region-bound listener stops hearing the gesture two frames in, and capturing at `pointerdown` retargets the following `click` to the capturing element, silently killing every tap inside the region); a **flick that commits in its direction of travel**; the handle as a real ARIA splitter with arrow keys; the toggle's fill becoming a **thumb that sits between the labels at `half`**, drawing the third stop the control always had; **a tapped pin surfacing its place as a card on the canvas** — the pins here are live, and tapping one at the map extreme raises the place with **nothing moving**: the pane's box is unchanged, so the camera does not shift and the map keeps every pixel. The card is the same `.place` row in a second host (one grammar, two hosts, the way the pin is the badge in a second form factor), carries the same way-in block so acting on a reference never needs the sheet to move, renders **only where the sheet cannot show the row** so it never doubles it, and clears Google's attribution by the attribution's own height — measured 13px in the file, and its `bottom` includes `--sheet-h` because the containing block is the split, whose bottom is the screen's. It **generalises the shipped `.map-ghostrow`**, which already surfaces a tapped pin's row on exactly that condition. A tap on the canvas clears the selection; a **row** tap normalises the sheet to `half`; **one motion idiom for the three objects that come and go** (the sort chip, the place card, the pre-prompt — a small rise and a fade at `--t-quick`, with the chip hidden via `visibility` rather than `display` so both directions animate while it still leaves the tab order and the a11y tree, and with stop-driven hiding animating where capability-driven absence stays unmounted); and the **geolocation pre-prompt as canvas furniture** — absolutely positioned inside `.map-split` so it costs the split no height and the camera never moves for it, a sibling of `.map-pane` and never a wrapper (a remount is a billed map load), one floating object over the canvas at a time, still **inline and not an overlay** (ADR-0109 session-105) — while the refusal notice stays in the list, because that one explains the list's order. **Renders through the app's real CSS, and is self-contained:** the shipped stylesheets are **inlined** in the app's own import order by [`mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs`](../../mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs) from the file's `APP-CSS:` manifest — **re-run it after changing any of those sheets** (`node mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs mockups/map-split-v2.html`; idempotent, and the file is in `.prettierignore` so formatting can't fight the generator). Three things are its own, each fenced and labelled: `mk-*` mockup chrome, the **Phase-2 delta** (the genuinely new CSS, written in the app's `map-*`/`wp-snapsheet-*` naming so it reads as the diff for `screens/map.css` and `ui/primitives/snap-sheet.css`), and the faked base canvas + hand-placed pins (the pin markup itself is `MapPane`'s verbatim; the day connector is drawn by the Maps API in the app, so unlike everything else here it has no shipped CSS to inherit and the file owns it). Two mockup mechanics worth knowing before reading its CSS: `[hidden]` only sets `display: none`, so the `:has(> .map-geoprompt)` rule needed a `:not([hidden])` here that the app — which renders the card conditionally — does not; and the sheet **animates** its height, so the budget re-measures on `transitionend`, since a reading taken in the same tick reports the old stop. **The numbers are a starting point, not a calibration:** `half`'s fraction, `peek`'s sliver and the flick threshold are legibility and feel, and the ADR hands them to the device pass — **still unspent after the build**, which shipped the derived defaults and never saw a rendered canvas. **Two ways the shipped app now reads differently from this file, both deliberate:** the minimum stop is named **`map`** in code (this file's ⟨הצצה⟩ toggle is that stop, under the retired stop's word), and while the place card is up the fit's **bottom** inset does not reserve it — carrying that would need a `MapPane` prop that flips on a tap, so ADR-0122's build log hands it to Phase 3 and this file does not draw it. Interactive: tap a pin, tap a row, drag the sheet's head, or focus the handle and use the arrows. State toggles: עצירה (הצצה · חצי · רשימה מלאה) · פריסה (שתי שורות כפי שנשלח / שורה אחת מעל הקנבס) · סינון פתוח · בקשת מיקום · האזור שהמצלמה שומרת פנוי · תכנון (קו יום ומסלול) · מסך (390×844 · 390×734 · 360×640).
- [`mockups/map-chrome-v1.html`](../../mockups/map-chrome-v1.html) — **the canvas's own chrome, restated** (Phase 8 of the map panel's third pass), promoting [ADR-0126](../decisions/0126-map-canvas-chrome-two-camera-controls-and-an-area-sort.md) (**Accepted; built session 150 — so this file is the built design's reference, not a proposal**, and its Phase-8 delta block is now a historical record sitting _above_ the real rules the manifest pulls in; re-rendering it against the shipped stylesheets measures the same 44×44 band and the same clear canvas per stop, which is the cross-check that what shipped is what was designed), which amends [ADR-0121](../decisions/0121-embedded-map-phase-6-design.md) §12 and [ADR-0122](../decisions/0122-map-split-controls-over-the-canvas.md) §1/§2. **Read this entry before mocking any surface whose design has since SHIPPED.** It starts from `map-split-v2.html` — the repo's layout-tree reference — but with one difference that bit immediately: Phase 2 is _built_, so everything that file carried as a hand-written delta now arrives here through the `APP-CSS:` manifest as app css, and the shipped rule `.map-split:has(> .map-geoprompt) .map-areacount, … .map-recenter { display: none }` **matched a node this file keeps mounted and toggles with `[hidden]`, hiding both canvas controls in every state**. `[hidden]` is not absence and a shipped `:has()` cannot tell the difference, so it has to be _undone_ by a labelled mockup-only override rather than written differently. Promotes: **two camera controls instead of one** — `recentre` is `if (me) focus(me); else reframe(points)`, the same tap centring you or re-framing the filtered set depending on a permission you cannot see, so locate becomes **locate-only** and routes to the same reason-first pre-prompt the near-me chip opens (which fills ADR-0122 §2's gap: at the `map` stop the chip is absent, so nothing on the canvas could ask for the permission at all), while "frame the filtered set" becomes its own control, absent when there is nothing to frame; **one horizontal furniture band, and the rule behind it** — the scarce axis on this surface is **height**, so canvas furniture grows sideways, which the file settles by measurement rather than taste: at 360×640 `half` the pane is 160px and a 44+8+44 vertical stack leaves **−3px** of clear canvas, i.e. it **overlaps Google's attribution** (ToS, ADR-0106 §B), where the same band laid out horizontally leaves 49px and costs 164px of an inline axis with 172 spare (390×844 `half`: 139px against 87px; 390×734: 90 against 38) — the rejected column is left **drawn** under a toggle rather than described; **the 44×44 floor met by geometry**, which also catches two shipped violations the reports did not mention (`.map-recenter` at **38×38**, `.map-areacount` at **64×26**) and forces the pill to move off the live region and onto its child, because `min-height: 44` plus a hairline is 46 outer and the box that must clear the floor is the tappable one; **`באזור` becoming a control without ceasing to be a readout** — `role="status" aria-live="polite"` stay exactly where they shipped and the live region **wraps** a `<button>`, which is `StatusBanner`'s own shape in this repo, so the count text exists once in the DOM and what the region announces is the button's own words rather than a copy, with the action carried by `title` (a description) so the visible `7 באזור` stays the accessible **name**, and zero rendering no button at all; and **the area as a SORT, not a filter** — a tap raises the sheet and orders the in-view places first, hiding nothing, a sibling of session 138's `sortByDistance` rather than a new mechanism, with two `.map-grouphead` headers (`באזור שבמסך`, `שאר המקומות`) because "in view" is not legible per row the way a distance chip is, and bounds snapshotted **at the tap, before the sheet moves**, since raising the sheet fires a fresh `idle` and a sort clearing on camera movement would kill itself the instant it was created. **Its third measurement is the prerequisite, and the file is built to fail visibly without it:** `areaCount` counts **ghosts**, which the sheet structurally cannot render, so the canvas here carries seven in-view pins of which three are ghosts and the readout says `7 באזור` where the day's list can offer four rows. Turn the area sort on and the list states the shortfall in session 144's own grammar (`3 מקומות באזור אינם ביום הזה` + `הצגת כל מקומות הטיול`); turn on `כל הימים` and the ghosts stop existing, the two counts converge and the note removes itself. The rejected answer — couple the count to the list and stop counting ghosts — is recorded in the ADR, not drawn, because it produces seven visible pins over a readout saying `4`. **A fixture bug worth knowing about, because it made the file prove nothing:** the first pass had all four pinned places also first in schedule order, so "in-view first" produced the order it started in; the hotel's pin was moved out of the viewport and the station's into it so the sort visibly lifts the station from last to fourth. **Renders through the app's real CSS, and is self-contained:** the shipped stylesheets are inlined in the app's own import order by [`mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs`](../../mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs) from the file's `APP-CSS:` manifest — **re-run it after changing any of them** (`node mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs mockups/map-chrome-v1.html`; idempotent, and the file is in `.prettierignore` so formatting cannot fight the generator). Its ⟨הרצועה⟩ panel reads the live DOM on every state change and prints the band's real box, what it leaves of the canvas, and what the column would have left instead, so a wrong claim in ADR-0126 shows up as a wrong row. Its own blocks are fenced and labelled: `mk-*` chrome, the **Phase-8 delta** (the genuinely new CSS, in the app's `map-*` naming so it reads as the diff for `ui/domain/map-pane.css` and `screens/map.css`), the faked base canvas, and the mockup-only overrides. **Not answerable here, and left to the device pass:** whether a crosshair and a corner-bracket frame read as distinct over real cloud-styled tiles, whether a 44px band is right or heavy at `half` on a 360×640 phone, and whether a pill among two circles reads as tappable at all. State toggles: עצירה (מפה · חצי · רשימה מלאה) · הכרום של הקנבס (כפי שנשלח / האפיון) · מיקום הצמד (רצועה מלבנית / עמודה אנכית — נפסל) · סדר הרשימה (לוח זמנים / לפי האזור שבמסך) · סינון פתוח · בקשת מיקום · האזור שהמצלמה שומרת פנוי · תכנון · מסך (390×844 · 390×734 · 360×640).
- [`mockups/map-search-v1.html`](../../mockups/map-search-v1.html) — **search overlays the map instead of replacing it** (Phase 10 of the map panel's third pass), promoting [ADR-0131](../decisions/0131-map-search-is-a-control-not-a-screen.md) (**Accepted; §1–§8b built session 158, so this file is the built design's reference for those — §9's long press and §10's errand are drawn here and still unbuilt**), which amends [ADR-0101](../decisions/0101-index-search-mode-and-header-titles.md) §2 and [ADR-0122](../decisions/0122-map-split-controls-over-the-canvas.md) §1/§2/§7. It continues the layout-tree lineage — `map-split-v2` → `map-chrome-v1` → here — so Phases 2, 3 and 8 all arrive as **shipped** css through the `APP-CSS:` manifest and the only hand-written block is Phase 10's delta, which is **one element**. **The one thing this file does that no other mockup in the epic has done: it draws the on-screen keyboard.** That element is what overturned ADR-0100 §3 and it had never been on a page, so ⟨מקלדת⟩ exists and it is where the design's whole argument is settled. ADR-0101 §1 killed search-in-place because "once the on-screen keyboard opens it covers most of the remaining screen, hiding almost every result" — true, and true because there the results sat **below** the field. Here the field is at the top of the split and the canvas is directly under it, so the keyboard eats the **sheet** and the **pins** survive: measured, at 390×844 `half` the keyboard's top edge sits at 945 against a pane bottom of 894, i.e. **the whole 250px canvas is still on screen**, against **0px** in the shipped state. **Both surfaces are drawn, which is the point** — ⟨שכבה מלאה⟩ renders the real `.modal-overlay[data-variant='full']` + `.search-overlay` at the size it actually covers (confined to the frame by a labelled mockup-only `position: absolute`, since `fixed` would cover the whole page), so the report and its answer are one toggle apart in the same tree. Promotes: **the query TAKES the controls row rather than joining it** — the phasing note's leading candidate was a permanent field in that row, and the ⟨שורת הפקדים במנוחה⟩ row of the budget panel is what killed it (**163.5px** spare in Trip mode at 390, but **12.8px** in Plan mode + day scope where the free `מסלול היום בגוגל` link rides along, and **−17.2px** at 360, i.e. the row already overflows there today, against ~150px a usable field needs) — so it becomes the **second occupant of the disclosure slot `סינון` already covers the row with**, one three-valued state, one shared `✕` whose label names whichever is open, and **44px inside the 46px row** so the touch floor is met by geometry and the split pays no height; **no match cue, because a match is not marked — it is what is left**, a query being a filter like every other control in that row; and **one clause for the trap**, which ⟨שינ⟩ + ⟨קידום הרוח⟩ draws both ways — search is scope-blind, so an out-of-day match is already `ghost aside` and arrives wearing the paint that means "not what you are looking at", and the fix is ADR-0130 §3's own paint/size split one axis over: the ghost **paint** stays (it is the answer to _which day_) and the subordinate **ratio** comes off, which also carries the dot tier and the camera's fit and deliberately **not** the amber cues or the day connector. **Two cross-effects it surfaced that a prop list would not**: `X באזור` follows the query for free (it counts `pins`, and the query filters `pins`), and a match with **no coordinates** has no pin at all — which together with a ghost's day living in its row is why opening search normalises the sheet to `half` from **either** extreme, ADR-0126 §7's rule in its fourth case and first two-directional one. **Two fidelity slips it caught in the file it inherited from:** ghost pins drawn with glyphs `MapPane` does not give them (a ghost has no fill for one to sit on), and the Map's retired one-off `.map-addbtn` (`＋ מיקום`) where the shared `AddLocationButton` (`＋ הוספת מקום`) now sits — the latter being the very affordance ADR-0131 §9 settles, so it had to read the way it ships. **What it deliberately does NOT do:** fix the standing day-scoped-grammar defect on this surface (`searchRows` reads `allUsages` but `renderList` blocks it with the day's `orderCtx` and no `forceDay`, so another day's hit is filed under `ללא יום`). That ships on its own branch; this file draws the rows **as the corrected renderer renders them** (the Friday match carries `שישי`) and does not design around the bug. **Renders through the app's real CSS, and is self-contained:** thirteen shipped stylesheets inlined in the app's own import order by [`mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs`](../../mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs) from the `APP-CSS:` manifest — **re-run it after changing any of them** (`node mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs mockups/map-search-v1.html`; idempotent, and the file is in `.prettierignore` so formatting cannot fight the generator). `modal.css`, `search-overlay.css` and `place-picker.css` are new to a map mockup's manifest, because the surface being **retired** has to be drawn as it actually ships in order to be judged. Its budget panel reads the live DOM on every state change, so a wrong claim in ADR-0131 shows up as a wrong row. **Not answerable here, and left to the device pass:** the real keyboard height and what iOS does with focus inside a `100dvh` PWA (the heaviest question in the file — the layout viewport does not shrink there, only the visual one), whether a 44px field reads as a field over real cloud-styled tiles, whether a promoted ghost reads as "this is what you searched for, and it is another day's" or as a contradiction, and whether a camera that does not move reads as quiet or as broken when every match is off-screen. **It also draws what the owner added across two correction rounds**, which is what turned the phase from a surface move into "where does a place come from": the **paid Google half sits in the sheet with no arm** — `PlaceResearch` re-parented, not rewritten, because a prediction carries no coordinates until the pick (ADR-0115 §2) and so cannot be a pin, in **both** modes now that ADR-0115 §6's Plan-only scoping _and_ §1's arm are withdrawn, differentiated by the two shipped group headers plus `.map-badge.result`'s dashed neutral pin rather than by a gate; ⟨לחיצה ארוכה⟩ drops a **nameless pin** and names it inside the shipped `.map-placecard`, the only add-a-place route that spends nothing; and ⟨שליחות⟩ draws the **errand banner**, which after the fourth correction is the **exception** path — `＋ הוספת מקום` answers in the picker's own sheet when the place already exists, and only reaches for the canvas when it does not. **And the `map` stop is where the file earns its keep on the second round:** the owner asked what searching does at a maximized map, the draft's answer was to shrink it to `half`, and the frame shows the better one — 516px of canvas untouched, the pins filtered live, and the field's **count as the button** that raises the sheet (ADR-0126 §4's live-region-wrapping-a-button, one more caller). **It also runs §8b's deferral rather than describing it** — ⟨לפי דרישה⟩ against ⟨בכל הקשה⟩, with a sentinel at the end of the trip's matches that tints while it is in view. Four trip matches at `half` fill the list, so nothing is fetched; scrolling to the end shows the skeletons and then the results, and the count goes 4 → 6. A query the trip cannot answer never fills the list, so it fetches with no gesture at all. At the `map` stop there is nothing to scroll, so nothing is fetched, which is correct rather than a gap. **The file's own measured table is what killed the count-threshold form:** a threshold would have to be 1, 2, 3, 4, 5 or 6 depending on the stop and the screen, so the fold is not a number. **And wiring it produced two build notes the prose did not have:** the reveal _animates_, so "did the list fill" is unanswerable on the frame the query changed (an argument for an `IntersectionObserver`, which re-fires as layout settles, over any hand-rolled snapshot); and the one scroll region is `.wp-snapsheet-body`, not the `.map-sheet-scroll` inside it, so an observer given the wrong root silently never fires. **And drawing that Google half in Trip mode is what caught the design's one real defect:** `.map-addmaybe` is painted in `--plan`, which was legitimate while research was Plan-only and is mode-identity-used-as-a-button-colour the moment it renders on a Trip-blue surface (root `CLAUDE.md` rule 4; `tokens.css`'s own comment says "amber/teal/plan are semantic, never CTAs"). It moves to `--cta` in the delta. Nothing in the ADR's prose would have caught it. State toggles: החיפוש (סגור · שכבה מלאה — כפי שנשלח · שורת שאילתה — האפיון) · השאילתה (ריקה · שי · שינ · זזז) · קידום ההתאמה שמחוץ ליום (מקודמת / כרוח כפופה) · החצי המשולם (בכל הפסקה / לפי דרישה — נפסל) · שליחות · לחיצה ארוכה · עצירה (מפה · חצי · רשימה מלאה) · מקלדת · סינון פתוח · תכנון · מסך (390×844 · 390×734 · 360×640).
- [`mockups/map-google-pins-v1.html`](../../mockups/map-google-pins-v1.html) — **search reclaims the app chrome, and Google's results become pins** (Phase 12 of the map panel's third pass), promoting [ADR-0132](../decisions/0132-search-reclaims-the-chrome-and-a-google-result-is-a-ring.md) (**Accepted; design only — nothing here is built**), which amends [ADR-0131](../decisions/0131-map-search-is-a-control-not-a-screen.md) **§2**, [ADR-0101](../decisions/0101-index-search-mode-and-header-titles.md)'s Alternatives and [ADR-0115](../decisions/0115-plan-mode-place-research.md) **§2**. **Read this entry for the one thing this file does differently from every other mockup in the epic: its frame is SPLICED out of the built `map-search-v1.html`** rather than continuing the hand-carried layout-tree lineage. Phase 10 is shipped, so its query row is app css now — and a re-typing of it would be exactly the fidelity slip session 135 warned about, one level up: not "the CSS without the layout tree" but "the layout tree without the shipped delta". The splice keeps the row that shipped, drops the nodes this file is not about (the retired overlay goes, which is also why the manifest is two stylesheets shorter than Phase 10's), and opens the query disclosure. **Its state is the phase's first finding, drawn: the field is OPEN and EMPTY.** The keyboard opens on **focus**, before a character exists, so the state the surface has to survive is one ADR-0131 never drew — which is why §1 keys the reclaim off the **disclosure being open** rather than off the `searching` predicate that (correctly) drives the list. **And it draws the keyboard under BOTH platform models, because assuming there was one is what produced the owner's screenshot.** Android **resizes** the layout viewport — modelled as the `.app` losing height while the frame keeps it, which is what resizing means — and iOS overlays it. Measured at `half` from the live DOM, chrome kept → reclaimed: **390×844 43px → 165px**, **411×914 (the owner's device) 61px → 183px**, **360×640 0px → 91px**. **Its ToS row is the reason the reclaim is a condition rather than an improvement, and it has two failure modes — one per platform.** At 360×640 on Android the pane is **28px** where the controls row (46) plus Google's attribution (22) need 68, so the attribution cannot be laid out at all ([ADR-0106](../decisions/0106-maps-and-places-epic-scope-and-phasing.md) §B). On iOS the pane does **not** shrink, so every number looks fine, and the keyboard **covers** the attribution where it sits at the pane's bottom edge — the harder of the two to notice, and invisible to ADR-0131's model entirely. Both go green under ⟨מוחזר⟩. **A third finding the file produced that no prose would have:** the safe-area insets are not on the shell, they are on the two nodes being hidden (`.header` pays `--safe-top`, `.nav` pays `--safe-bottom`), so hiding both puts the query field under the notch and the sheet's last row under the home indicator. ⟨אזור בטוח⟩ models a notched device — `env()` is 0 in a desktop browser, so this case can only be modelled — and the table then prints the real arithmetic: the chrome costs **324px** at 24/24 and the reclaim returns **276** of it, canvas 22px → 143px. **Its pin candidates are drawn over the resting canvas beside the trip's own pins**, which is the real reading environment (a query keeps the trip's matches on screen too), and the comparison is of the **silhouette** and nothing else — same glyph, same place, three shapes. ⟨ב⟩ the **ring** is the recommendation and what ADR-0132 §6 promotes: no tip (a tip claims _which building_), no hue (we do not buy place types, ADR-0115 §2), a `＋` (the only verb), sitting **on** the coordinate. ⟨א⟩ the dashed teardrop is drawn precisely so its rejection is visible — it collides with the **ghost rung**, which is also a hollow teardrop, and reads as a pin that has not finished loading; ⟨ג⟩ the dark inversion is distinct and reads as **more** important than the trip's own places, inverting the whole point. **What the file deliberately does NOT do:** filter anything. The query filter is Phase 10's, built and drawn there, and re-drawing it here would have meant inventing a fixture (a coffee query, a promoted ghost, three renamed results) whose only purpose was to decorate a chrome measurement. The rings are a silhouette comparison and the hint says so. **Renders through the app's real CSS, and is self-contained:** eleven shipped stylesheets inlined in the app's own import order by [`mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs`](../../mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs) from the `APP-CSS:` manifest — **re-run it after changing any of them** (`node mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs mockups/map-google-pins-v1.html`; idempotent, and the file is in `.prettierignore` so formatting cannot fight the generator). Every number in ADR-0132 is read from the live DOM here, so a wrong claim shows up as a wrong row. **Not answerable here, and left to the device pass:** the real keyboard heights per platform and screen (336/365/300px are approximations), whether a ring reads as "not yours yet" over real cloud-styled tiles and whether N of them read as a group or as noise, whether `＋` is legible inside a 28px ring at all, whether a chrome-less map tab reads as "still in the app" or as a modal that lost its way out (which is what §5's back rule answers in advance), and the owner's real safe-area insets. State toggles: כרום האפליקציה (כפי שנשלח / מוחזר — האפיון) · מקלדת (סגורה · אנדרואיד — מכווץ את הפריסה · iOS — מכסה מלמעלה) · תוצאות גוגל על הקנבס (בלי · א · ב — מומלץ · ג) · עצירה (מפה · חצי · רשימה מלאה) · אזור בטוח (בלי / מגרעת + פס בית) · מסך (390×844 · 411×914 — של הבעלים · 360×640).
- [`mockups/map-result-ring-v2.html`](../../mockups/map-result-ring-v2.html) — **an unsaved Google result, redrawn** (2026-08-06), promoting **[ADR-0168](../decisions/0168-the-search-answers-on-the-canvas.md) §2** — **Accepted and built the same day.** It **supersedes the ⟨תוצאות גוגל על הקנבס⟩ panel of [`map-google-pins-v1.html`](../../mockups/map-google-pins-v1.html)** and nothing else in that file: its two keyboard models, its ToS row and its safe-area arithmetic are untouched and still authoritative. **Why it exists: ADR-0132's device pass named three readings it could not take, and the owner has now taken two of them on a phone** — _"the `＋` in a circle sign for the results is not visually pleasing and feels out of place and amateur, especially in light mode. In dark theme it's better"_, and then, on this file's first draft, which answered in greys: _"I'm not sure that black/white palette is the best choice for us."_ **The finding is one line of a stylesheet nobody had put beside the mark.** `design/map-styles/waypoint-map-day.json` paints Google's OWN points of interest `pinFillColor #c9ccd4` · `pinGlyphColor #4b5568` · `pinOutlineColor #ffffff`, which is the shipped ring's three colours redistributed — so the result mark read as **basemap furniture**, and that is why no re-tuning of greys had an answer: the basemap owns the greys. The same file resolves the half of the report that looked like a contradiction — at night that POI is `#414b61` on a `#191e2c` ground, so the ring did not get better in dark mode, **its competition got quieter.** **Which is why this file's backdrop includes a synthetic Google POI**, drawn in the style JSON's exact three colours, past a hairline from the trip's own pins (a numbered stop, the amber next-stop cue, a ghost). A result mark is never seen alone — a query keeps the trip's matches on screen (ADR-0131 §4) and the basemap keeps its own labels — so "does it read as ours" cannot be judged from one swatch, and the collision is **seen** rather than argued. The terrain washes are the style JSON's too (`natural.base`, `natural.land.landCover.forest`, `natural.water`, per theme), not invented. **Five candidates × rest and selected × two themes.** ⟨ג⟩ the **teal donut** is what ADR-0168 §2 promotes: the canvas's own marker grammar (a fill, a `--card` keyline at `.pin-b`'s own 0.06 ratio, its shadow) with a `--on-fill` core where the glyph was, and selection **closing the core**, growing to full `--pin-base` and taking the `--card` halo plus a teal glow. ⟨א⟩ is what shipped. ⟨ב⟩ the **neutral donut** is the first draft's answer and is kept deliberately, because it is the right SHAPE in the same wrong FAMILY and sits visibly next to the POI. ⟨ד⟩ keeps teal at rest and lets selection jump to `--cta`, which is rejected on the render: the selected mark then reads as a different kind of object than its own resting state, and the heaviest thing on the canvas besides. ⟨ה⟩ the **dark inversion** is redrawn even though ADR-0132 rejected it on paper, because A-in-dark _is_ a dark inversion and the owner reports that one as the better of the two — it out-ranks everything in both themes, so the paper rejection survives the render. **Teal is neither new nor borrowed, and the argument is structural:** ADR-0028 reserves it for location, the design language forbids the category palette from ever being amber or teal, and a Google result is the one object on this canvas that is _only_ a location (no day, no time, no commitment, and no category, since the mask does not buy place types, ADR-0115 §2) — so a sixth category cannot collide with it later. Nothing in either basemap is teal. **Both themes mean two renders, not two panels**, and the file says why: `tokens.css` remaps dark mode on `:root[data-theme='dark']`, and `frontend/CLAUDE.md` records what happened the one time that block was split to make a variant addressable lower down — so a side-by-side would lie about the token layer. `?theme=dark` sets the attribute on `<html>` and the pass is two screenshots of one file. **Renders through the app's real CSS and is self-contained:** five shipped stylesheets inlined in the app's own import order by [`mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs`](../../mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs) from the `APP-CSS:` manifest — **re-run it after changing any of them** (`node mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs mockups/map-result-ring-v2.html`; idempotent). Every size and fill in its readout is measured off the live DOM, so a wrong claim shows up as a wrong row. **One trap it records because the first pass fell into it:** the marker cells were classed `cell`, which `App.css` already owns, so every mark drew inside a stray dark box — and overriding `.map-pin`'s `position: relative` threw the order badge across the row. **Not answerable here, and still the device pass's:** whether teal reads as ours over real cloud-styled tiles, and whether N of them among the trip's own pins read as a group or as noise — the two readings ADR-0132 owed that a hand-drawn basemap cannot settle.
