- [`mockups/map-pin-phase-v1.html`](../../mockups/map-pin-phase-v1.html) — **a booking's phase reads on the canvas** (Phase 11 of the map panel's third pass, session 191), promoting [ADR-0141](../decisions/0141-the-pin-says-which-transition-is-next.md) (**Accepted; built the same session**), which extends [ADR-0121](../decisions/0121-embedded-map-phase-6-design.md) §6's tag slot and carries [ADR-0063](../decisions/0063-category-time-behaviour-profile.md)'s transition vocabulary onto its sixth surface — the first one that is not a list. **Read this entry for the two claims the file refuted, both of them its own.** §B2's first draft rationed by vocabulary alone (`bracketed` is 2 of 9 categories, so a day has a handful of edges) and the measurement showed that is true of **one day** and false of a **trip**: in all-days every stay's two ends and every flight are on the canvas at once, giving 9 tag-to-tag collisions and one tag off the frame at both 360 and 390. That is what makes the neutral tag **day-scoped**, which turned out to be the narrowing ADR-0121 §6 had already made for the number. And the first pass counted only tag↔tag intersections, which said nothing about the failure the render made obvious — **a tag sitting on the neighbouring pin's body**, worse than two tags touching because it hides a pin; that is now its own column, split into total and _wrong-way_ (a lower-ranked tag over a higher-ranked pin, the only kind that is a defect). **§B1 is the number that decides the section:** the widest tag pair needs a 65px gap at a 40px pin (1.64× pin height), so **five fit across 360** — and it derives that from two measured widths then verifies it by rendering at threshold+2 (must clear) and threshold−6 (must clash), because a derivation checked in one direction is an assertion. **§A corrects a shipped comment:** `constants.ts` reports the tag reaching 1.10× the pin height per side, measured on `התחנה הבאה` — copy the app does not show; the shipped `היעד הבא` is 0.88× and the widest new word `צ׳ק-אאוט` is 0.90×, i.e. the same width, so the camera's inset does not move. **§D draws a loss rather than a design:** the same pin with and without the leading dot, in a reduced-motion frame, because `.nowstop` and `.nextstop` differ by 4.4% of pin height and the pulse that really separates them is killed globally — so the specimen without the dot is what replacing the word costs. §E is the hotel-changing day before and after; §F renders five rejected shapes, two of them with their cost as a number (the `לפני` prefix is +39% tag width). **Renders through the app's real CSS and is self-contained:** four shipped stylesheets inlined in the app's own import order by [`mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs`](../../mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs) from the `APP-CSS:` manifest — **re-run it after changing any of them** (`node mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs mockups/map-pin-phase-v1.html`; idempotent, and the file is in `.prettierignore`). Three mockup-local classes, all prefixed `mk-`, and one labelled override that is itself a recorded trap: **`.map-pin` ships `transition: --pin-u`, so §A's table at the 56px stop first came back with the 34px stop's numbers** — every read was one size behind, and the ratios looked reassuringly size-invariant for the wrong reason. [`map-place-becomes-v1.html`](../../mockups/map-place-becomes-v1.html) recorded this about the sheet; it applies to the pins too. **Left to the device pass:** whether `--muted` ink at ~10px holds over arbitrary tiles the way amber does, and the wrong-way occlusion above — 3 across the deliberately heavy day, 0 on the changing day and on any ordinary one. State toggles: גודל הפין (25–56, עם רצפה/תקרה) · רקע (בהיר / כהה) · מי מקבל תג (כל ההזמנות · כל קצה סוגר · קצה סוגר בסקופ יום — נבחר · רק שני הרמזים של היום).
- [`mockups/map-settle-from-canvas-v2.html`](../../mockups/map-settle-from-canvas-v2.html) — **settling an event from the canvas** (session 188), promoting [ADR-0139](../decisions/0139-settling-an-event-from-the-map.md) (**Accepted; built the same session**), which closes the gap [ADR-0137](../decisions/0137-the-pin-says-what-happened.md) opened and backlogged. **Read this entry for the one thing the file is for: §B, where it refuted its own author.** The recommendation carried into it was "drop the words `היינו שם?` from the cluster and let an amber tint carry the emphasis" — and at 9% with a hairline the emphasised row was **indistinguishable** from a row deliberately not emphasised. §B caught it only because it draws the two side by side, which is the whole argument for a section whose job is to show a difference: a §B in which both frames look the same is a finding. Fixed at 16% with a 2px ring at 42%, and the rejected 9% is kept as the middle frame. **§C draws something a reader could reasonably assume the opposite of:** `refs` is passed to a row when that row is SELECTED and `renderRow` serves both hosts, so the cluster is not a card feature — it appears wherever the selected row is (the card at the `map` stop, the list at `half`/`full`). **§D is the measurement, and it reports a COST rather than a confirmation:** the label wants 199px and gets 146 at 390 / 116 at 360, so a long event title truncates at both widths; dropping the ask-words returns 51px and does not cure it. **Three measurement traps it hit first, all recorded:** the card is `position: absolute` against `.map-split` so it escaped every frame and stacked at the document's foot; its entrance keyframe starts at `opacity: 0` with no fill-mode so a static capture sampled frame 0 and every card was invisible; and the first metric was the label's OWN width, which is content-sized and therefore identical at both screens — a number that cannot move is not a measurement. `scrollWidth` then lied about a shrunk flex item that ellipsises, so the natural width comes off an unconstrained clone. **Renders through the app's real CSS and is self-contained:** five shipped stylesheets inlined by [`mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs`](../../mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs) from the `APP-CSS:` manifest — **re-run it after changing any of them** (`node mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs mockups/map-settle-from-canvas-v2.html`; idempotent, and the file is in `.prettierignore`). Two mockup-only overrides, both labelled: the card's positioning and its entrance animation. **Left to the device pass:** whether 32×32 reads as a touch target in a row whose own target is all the remaining width, and whether an amber-washed reference row reads as "open question" rather than "error".
- [`mockups/map-settle-from-canvas-v1.html`](../../mockups/map-settle-from-canvas-v1.html) — **superseded by v2** (same session), and kept for two things v2 does not carry: its §B draws the rejected **card-wide `היינו שם?` strip** on a place with TWO events, which is what makes its failure visible rather than argued (it must settle both or pick one by clock, the reading ADR-0117 §5 forbids); and its §A carries the three-way toggle over WHICH events may be settled, whose middle option — passed-and-unanswered only — was rejected on a practical ground worth keeping: a settled row is no longer passed-and-unanswered, so its own undo would vanish the instant it was earned. Do not read it as current.
- [`mockups/map-pin-outcome-v2.html`](../../mockups/map-pin-outcome-v2.html) — **the pin says what happened there** (session 187), promoting [ADR-0137](../decisions/0137-the-pin-says-what-happened.md) (**Accepted; built the same session**), which keeps a promise [ADR-0117](../decisions/0117-map-place-outcome-states.md)'s Consequences made and Phase 6 never delivered. **Read this entry for what the file is FOR: it is a decision surface, not an illustration.** Three things were open when it was written — the placement, the skip symbol, and (after the owner's next reply) whether the mark joins the number or replaces it — so §A carries **six live skip candidates** that re-render every pin on the page, and §B draws the two placement systems side by side as **tappable** cards that the rest of the file then follows. Picking in §A/§B changes §C, §D and §E, so the page can never show a ghost read one way and a cluster read another. **Its most useful section is §A at the 34px floor**, which is where the symbol was actually decided: the arc and the chevrons turn to mush inside a 12px disc and the bar reads as _unmarked_, which is the state it has to be distinguished from. **§C's last two specimens exist because the first ones were flattering the design** — every early sample paired a green category hue with ✓ and a reddish one with ✕, so nothing could fail; the pairing that decides it is the **mismatch** (a green ✓ inside the reddish food outline), and it is now drawn. **§F draws four rejected designs rather than describing them**, including the two the owner reversed: the mark replacing the category emoji (v1), and the mark as a **second** badge beside the number. **Two findings the file produced that no prose would have:** white marks nearly vanish on a pastel pin body (`.pin-n` gets away with white because that badge sits on `--ink`), and `.pin-n`'s corner had been decided by its own `dir="auto"` resolving digits to LTR — so a logical inset for a second badge landed on the **same** shoulder and painted over the number. **Renders through the app's real CSS and is self-contained:** four shipped stylesheets inlined in the app's own import order by [`mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs`](../../mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs) from the `APP-CSS:` manifest — **re-run it after changing any of them** (`node mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs mockups/map-pin-outcome-v2.html`; idempotent, and the file is in `.prettierignore` so formatting cannot fight the generator). Only §E's two rejected variants and §B's ghost sub-variants are mockup-local, each prefixed `mk-`; everything else is the shipped `.map-pin` markup, so the file is a **preview** rather than a drawing and drifts the moment `map-pane.css` does. **Not answerable here, and left to the device pass:** whether a green ✓ ghost holds against dark park-green tiles (the weakest pairing on the canvas — the fallback is the coloured **disc** in the ghost's centre instead of a stroke, drawn here and a one-line change), and the `.pin-n.outcome` stroke weight at 34px. State toggles: מועמד לדילגנו (צלב — נבחר · לוכסן · מקף · קפיצה · שברונים · עיגול חוצה) · סימן על גוסט (קו צבעוני — נבחר / דיסק צבעוני) · מקום הסימן (מרכז לגוסט + כתף לפין מלא — נבחר / כתף בכל הדרגות) · גודל הפין (25–56, עם רצפה/תקרה) · רקע (בהיר / כהה).
- [`mockups/map-pin-outcome-v1.html`](../../mockups/map-pin-outcome-v1.html) — **SUPERSEDED by `map-pin-outcome-v2.html`** (same session). Kept for one reason: it draws the design the owner rejected, which is what the successor's Alternatives point at. It answered the wrong population (marking the **`behind`** tier while leaving the **`ghost`** silent, when the ghost was both the reported tier and the one with a free centre) and traded the category emoji for the mark on a filled pin (_"they should retain their icons so it's easier to distinguish"_). Its §E is still the clearest demonstration of why the badge has to be dark or coloured rather than white. Do not read it as current: the shipped `map-pane.css` it inlines has since changed under it.
- [`mockups/map-place-becomes-v1.html`](../../mockups/map-place-becomes-v1.html) — **a place becomes an event or a booking: one action on the way-in block** (Phase 14), promoting [ADR-0135](../decisions/0135-a-place-becomes-an-event-or-a-booking.md) (**Accepted; built 2026-07-31, session 185 — the footer, the scroll-into-view, the `EventForm` host and the one-idea consume all shipped as drawn**), which extends [ADR-0121](../decisions/0121-embedded-map-phase-6-design.md) **§8**'s way-in block and reads [ADR-0134](../decisions/0134-the-map-is-where-a-forms-place-comes-from.md) **§9** rather than its channel. **It draws the map and nothing else, and it used to draw more.** Sessions 181–182 also drew `EventForm`, a second host and the outcome screens here, because the code rule was designed inside this phase; session 184 moved all of that to [`event-also-booked-v1.html`](../../mockups/event-also-booked-v1.html) with the decision it belongs to ([ADR-0136](../decisions/0136-an-event-can-also-be-booked.md)). Two files claiming one design is how the older one starts to rot, and the manifest shrank from nineteen stylesheets to eleven when the form left. **It draws a rejected design on purpose.** The first pass hung two entries, `＋ אירוע` and `＋ הזמנה`, in the same grey `.map-ref` box as the references; the owner read it as "cluttered and very amateur looking". ⟨התפריט שנפסל⟩ keeps that state alive **so the panel can measure it** instead of asserting that one control is smaller: the menu costs **+92px** on the selected row, the single `.map-addmaybe` pill costs **+56px**, and a plain row is **73px** — the rejected shape cost more than adding a whole row to the list and this one costs less. **And the measurement found something neither design caused:** at 360×640 with the sheet at `half` the scroller is **153px**, and a place with **two** references is already **186px** as shipped, so the block overflows before this phase adds anything — which is why ADR-0135 §8 ships a `scrollIntoView({ block: 'nearest' })` with the control rather than treating the overflow as new. At `full` everything fits in every state. **Renders through the app's real CSS and is self-contained:** eleven shipped stylesheets inlined in the app's own import order by [`mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs`](../../mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs) from the `APP-CSS:` manifest — **re-run it after changing any of them** (`node mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs mockups/map-place-becomes-v1.html`; idempotent, and the file is in `.prettierignore` so formatting cannot fight the generator). **Measurement traps it hit and records:** the sheet **animates** between stops, so a panel reading one frame later printed `208px` for a viewport that settles at `153px` (transitions are cut inside the frame — a number taken mid-transition is the same class of lie as a selector matching nothing); `[hidden]` loses to any rule with its own `display`, so `.map-ref` (flex) and the footer were hidden in name only; and the row is read by `id`, never by `[data-place]`, which matches the **pin** too and puts it first in the DOM. **And a trap that is not a measurement one: a frame inherited from an older mockup is stale markup.** This file's chrome started as a copy of `map-errand-v1`'s (session 163) and four pieces had drifted by the time it was re-read against the components — the header's member cluster is now a real `<button class="avatars-btn">` wrapping `Avatar` (`.wp-av .av`, ADR-0133 §9); a pin's glyph lives inside **`.pin-b`** with `.pin-n` as its sibling; `PlaceBadge` adds `wp-placebadge` and its mark **only when interactive**; and `ListRow`'s `.wp-listrow-right` is a **sibling** of the open button, not a child. **The `APP-CSS` manifest keeps the _styles_ honest and does nothing for the _markup_**, so a frame more than a session old has to be re-read against the components before it is trusted. **Left to the device pass:** whether the pill reads as the row's primary action at 44px against a reference row above it, and whether 198px at `half` on a 360 is usable even with the scroll-into-view or whether a small screen should drop to `full` instead. State toggles: בלוק דרכי הכניסה (כפי שנשלח · התפריט שנפסל · האפיון) · המקום הנבחר (רעיון אחד · שני רעיונות · כבר אירוע) · עצירה (חצי · רשימה מלאה) · מסך (390×844 · 411×914 · 360×640).
- [`mockups/event-also-booked-v1.html`](../../mockups/event-also-booked-v1.html) — **an event can also be booked**, promoting [ADR-0136](../decisions/0136-an-event-can-also-be-booked.md) (**Accepted; built 2026-07-31, session 185, with §1 and §2 amended in place while building**). **Not a map file:** it draws `EventForm` on the day view, the surface that hosts it most, because the decision is `EventForm`'s and not the map's. **It records two rejected triggers, because both were wrong in instructive ways.** `＋ אירוע` / `＋ הזמנה` as two entries asks the app's question at the moment a traveller knows least. And a **confirmation code**, which two passes keyed on, is refuted by the schema this file quotes: `confirmationCode` is nullable and every `Booking` field but `type`/`title` is optional, so a table booked by phone has no number — and there was no fallback signal either, since the commonest booking (a restaurant) is also **soft** by `bookingDefaultKind`. **The reframe is the file's subject:** an event and a booking were never alternatives — a `Booking` has a linked event, so "this event is also booked" is exactly `event.bookingId != null`. You always create an event; one `יש הזמנה` toggle (the app's existing `aria-pressed` boolean idiom, no new primitive) says it is **also** booked. **⟨קטגוריה⟩ moves two things live, which is the point of drawing it rather than tabulating it:** the row's **default** (lodging and transport open on, food and sights off) and the derived **type**, with the kind toggle following the type — so `מלון` comes out `מחויב` and `מסעדה` comes out `גמיש`, which is §4 as a picture: booked-ness and commitment are different axes. Touch the toggle and the category stops moving it (`bookedTouched`). **Three states of the same form:** a new event, an existing one **converting**, and one **already linked** — where there is no control at all, only a statement with a way in, which is the shipped rule for a linked event's place and category one field wider. **And it draws what each save produces**, in a second frame: a soft `.wp-event` card, or the booking's **linked** card, plus the Index bookings row — which carries **no code**, because a booking made here with nothing typed is the ordinary case and the whole correction this design exists for. A conversion's card is marked **amber-changed rather than green-new**, since a conversion adds nothing to the day. **Measured** on the real `.modal-form` (never `.modal-card`, which is height-capped and scrolls, so it stops moving when its content grows): **482px** as shipped, **560px** with the row and nothing booked (+78px, ~16%), **642px** booked. +78px is what someone who books nothing pays, on every host — the number that matters, and the one a later "can this be tighter" change has to beat. Dropping the row's `field-label` was worth **20px** for a word the button already says. **Renders through the app's real CSS and is self-contained:** sixteen shipped stylesheets inlined by [`mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs`](../../mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs) (`node mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs mockups/event-also-booked-v1.html`; in `.prettierignore`). `event-card.css` and `list-row.css` are in the manifest because a faked outcome card would make the comparison worthless. **Two traps it inherited and re-hit:** `.wp-collapsible` opens on an **`.on` class**, not `[hidden]`, and it **animates** — so a panel reading it one frame after a toggle reported the open state as costing nothing; transitions are cut inside the frame. And `.field` is a column, so the `inline-flex` pill stretched full width and read as a disabled input until it was given `width: fit-content`. **Left to the device pass:** whether `יש הזמנה` is noticed at all by someone who would say yes (one chip in a seven-field form); whether defaulting it on for lodging and transport reads as helpful or presumptuous; whether +78px is worth it on the day view and the builder; whether the statement moving under the category pill reads as clever or unstable; and whether a booking with only a title feels finished or abandoned when opened later in `BookingSheet`. **Session 185 added the one question the category cannot answer**, on the owner's call after they caught a false sentence in ADR-0136 §2: it claimed a wrong `flight` guess was "one tap on the category the form was showing anyway", and there is no such tap — `EventCategory` has a **single** `transport` while `BookingType` has `flight`, `train` and `other`, and `BOOKING_TYPE_TO_CATEGORY` maps both transport types back to that one category. So `⟨תחבורה⟩` now also draws **three `.choice-pill`s** — `✈️ טיסה` · `🚄 רכבת` · `🚌 אחר` — inside the opened block, above the code, at the 44px floor and with **no** `field-label` (the pills say what they are, and the statement below names the result). Drawn rather than tabulated for two reasons the DOM answers and prose does not: the three pills fit on **one line at 356px** of a 390 phone, so the row costs **+54px** and not two rows; and picking `אחר` moves the **kind toggle to `גמיש`**, because `other` is not a span type — `bookingDefaultKind` is the one source of commitment (§4) and this row is deliberately not a second opinion about it. `📄` was tried for `אחר` first (it is `BOOKING_TYPE_ICON.other`) and rejected by the owner: all three pills answer "which transport", so a document among two vehicles reads as a different kind of answer, and the Index never shows that glyph here anyway since a linked booking's row borrows the **event's** icon. **The measurement grew a fourth row:** 482 → 560 → 642 → **696px** with the row booked _and_ the category transport. Two copy corrections came with the build, both toward shipped vocabulary rather than this file's earlier ad-hoc wording: the code placeholder is `קוד אישור · לא חובה` (the app's own `index.form.codeLabel`) and the statement names types through `t.index.bookingType[…]`, so `hotel` reads `לינה` and not `מלון`. **It now lags the shipped form on the transport pill in two ways, and neither is worth re-drawing** (the ADR is the authority; this is here so a reader does not take the drawing for the app): [ADR-0156](../decisions/0156-a-bus-is-transport-the-third-mode-the-picker-always-meant.md) renamed the third pill `🚌 אחר` → `נסיעה` and made it **hard**, so the `גמיש` this file draws under it is the old `other`; and [ADR-0136 §2](../decisions/0136-an-event-can-also-be-booked.md)'s session-187 amendment answered one of the device-pass questions below — `⟨תחבורה⟩` now opens the row **off**, so the +54px pills and the 696px fourth row are what a booked transport event costs, not what picking the category costs. State toggles: הטופס (סגור · כפי שנשלח · לא מוזמן · מוזמן) · הטופס פועל על (אירוע חדש · אירוע קיים · אירוע שכבר מקושר) · קטגוריה (אוכל · לינה · תחבורה · אתרים) · התוצאה (יום ביום · אינדקס) · מסך (390×844 · 411×914 · 360×640).
- [`mockups/map-errand-v1.html`](../../mockups/map-errand-v1.html) — **the map is where a form's place comes from, and a row tap commits** (Phase 13 of the map panel's third pass), promoting [ADR-0134](../decisions/0134-the-map-is-where-a-forms-place-comes-from.md) (**Accepted; design only — nothing here is built**), which amends [ADR-0131](../decisions/0131-map-search-is-a-control-not-a-screen.md) **§10** (reversed by the owner), [ADR-0129](../decisions/0129-map-camera-moves-like-a-camera.md) **§1** (split in half) and [ADR-0115](../decisions/0115-plan-mode-place-research.md) **§2**. **Spliced from the built `map-search-v1.html` rather than from Phase 12's file**, and the reason is worth knowing before mocking anything on this tab: Phase 12 DROPPED the sheet's Google rows and the errand banner because they were not its subject, and they are precisely this file's — so the frame comes from the last mockup that still carries them, with phases 10 and 12 both shipped so the query row, the chrome reclaim and the result ring all arrive through the `APP-CSS:` manifest as app css. **Its own delta is two rules.** **What it measures, and the first thing it found was that I was measuring the wrong axis:** a result row's actions live in `.map-right`, which is a `flex-direction: column` — so a second control buys **no width at all** and instead makes every row taller. Stacked, a result row is **106px** and the sheet at `half` shows **3** rows at 390×844 and **2** at 360×640; side by side it is **68px**, showing **6** and **4**. **Stacking halves the results you can see**, on the axis ADR-0126 declared scarce ("canvas furniture grows sideways") — so `.place.result .map-right` becomes a row, scoped, because a trip row's `נווט` + distance genuinely want the column. **And the measurement it was built to settle does not settle it:** a labelled Google button fits too (237px remain for name + address at 360), so the ADR rests the icon on a different argument — the row already carries one labelled verb, and two compete — rather than pretending a number decided it. Stating that is the point of measuring. **⟨מסגור⟩ draws the camera rule instead of describing it:** ADR-0129 §2's `focusBoundsFor` arithmetic runs over the fixture's real coordinates and the result is drawn as a box on the faked canvas, against the fixed zoom it replaces — **133×124px against 320×297px**, i.e. the difference between "this café, among these three" and "somewhere in Shinjuku". The rule itself is **already built**; what this file settles is who calls it (a row tap, not a pin tap) and what counts as a ring's neighbours (the trip's pins **plus the other rings**, since choosing between five cafés the useful context is the other candidates — while the rings stay out of the camera's `points`, because a query moving the camera is what ADR-0131 §5 forbids). **⟨שליחות⟩ draws the errand** in both shapes that matter: an event, and **a transport leg** — which exists to make visible why `target.field` cannot be optional, since a booking with two place fields can otherwise take the right place on the wrong side. In errand mode the verb **changes** rather than being added (`בחירה`, no `＋ אולי` and no shelf item — the owner's "only choosing one place"), the row tap still only **frames** so you can look before committing, and `נווט` goes **absent** because navigating somewhere is not the task while you are picking a place for a form — which also keeps the trip row at **73px in both states** instead of growing. **Two fixture bugs in its own panel, both recorded because they make numbers lie:** `[hidden]` lost to the new control's own `display: grid`, so the ⟨as shipped⟩ comparison hid nothing and both states measured identically (the same trap `map-split-v2` and `map-chrome-v1` hit, one layer down); and `[data-place="ramen"]` matches the **pin** as well as the row, and the pin comes first in the DOM — so "the trip row is 34px" was `MAP_PIN.MIN_H`. `:not(.result)` was wrong too: it matched a row the reveal had collapsed to zero (ADR-0120 hides in place). **A mockup that measures the wrong node is worse than one that measures nothing.** **Renders through the app's real CSS, and is self-contained:** eleven shipped stylesheets inlined in the app's own import order by [`mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs`](../../mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs) from the `APP-CSS:` manifest — **re-run it after changing any of them** (`node mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs mockups/map-errand-v1.html`; idempotent, and the file is in `.prettierignore` so formatting cannot fight the generator). `place-picker.css` is in the manifest because the surface this phase **retires** has to be drawn as it ships in order to be judged. **Not answerable here, and left to the device pass:** whether a 44×44 icon reads as "open in Google Maps" without a label and as subordinate to the verb beside it; whether the sheet dropping and then the camera framing reads as one movement or two (two separate animations, possibly needing sequencing); whether `בחירה` reads correctly on a row that already says `כבר בטיול`; whether a derived span means the right thing for a result whose neighbours are other candidates rather than the trip's plan; and whether the return lands where you left the form (the draft carries the values, not the view). State toggles: שליחות מטופס (בלי · אירוע · רגל של הסעה) · שורת התוצאה (כפי שנשלח / עם כפתור לגוגל) · פעולות השורה (זה ליד זה / מוערמות) · מסגור אחרי הקשה (נגזר · זום קבוע · שניהם) · עצירה (מפה · חצי · רשימה מלאה) · מסך (390×844 · 411×914 · 360×640).
- [`mockups/shelf-day-aware-v1.html`](../../mockups/shelf-day-aware-v1.html) — **the day-aware shelf + the Map's outcome states** (two frames, one design pass), promoting [ADR-0116](../decisions/0116-day-aware-shelf-and-idea-target-day.md) and [ADR-0117](../decisions/0117-map-place-outcome-states.md) (**both Accepted, shipped session 112**). **Frame 1 (Plan chrome):** an idea's **optional target day** (`MaybeItem.targetDate` — pencilled in, never a schedule: no time, no slot, off the timeline), the shelf as **two groups** (`לְיום הזה`, including that day's skipped soft events, then `רעיונות` with dateless first and out-of-day ideas **naming their day** per ADR-0085), ADR-0027's union rendered in **Plan mode** at last, the **skip vs park** split with park carrying the category + date it used to drop, a shelf card **dragged onto a gap chip** (free time, no displacement — dropping onto an occupied row stays out, it's a ripple decision), and the schedule sheet with a real **day field** (`WhenField variant="day"`, ADR-0083, replacing its bare `TimePicker`). **Frame 2 (Trip chrome):** the Map list's **three outcome states** — `היינו` (`--ok`), `דילגנו` (`--miss`, row quiet) and passed-but-unsettled (no tag, position is the claim) — with the block header renamed to the neutral `מה שמאחורינו` and `מה שלפנינו` appearing only when a behind block exists. Notes panels carry the reasoning, including why a stored target day beat a derived per-day "fit" and why merging skip into park was rejected. **Behind the shipped app on the drag itself (sessions 113-117):** the frame shows the drag as a card that changes style in place; shipped, the held card is a clone that follows the finger while its slot stays behind as a placeholder, a **skipped** card drags too, and an **empty day** grows its own drop zone. A row can also be dragged the other way, onto a shelf group (which parks it), onto a header day pill (which moves it to that day), or — session 123 — onto **free time**, which moves it there as an event on whatever day the chip belongs to; and the frame's ⠿ grip and ▲/▼ arrows are **retired**, since a row now drags on a hold from anywhere. The frame also draws gap chips only **between** events: shipped, the day's two **edges** carry one too (before the first event, after the last), which is where "add something before this" is dropped. Read ADR-0116's session-113/115/116/117/118/119/120/122/123 amendments for the gesture, not this frame.
- [`mockups/screens-v1.html`](../../mockups/screens-v1.html) — landing, lobby, join (paste-a-link + invite landing, ADR-0030), and trip-settings screens (plan-mode daylight chrome).
- [`mockups/zero-state-v1.html`](../../mockups/zero-state-v1.html) — the zero-state home (logged in, no trips): the dormant "board off" concept, two equal join/create actions (ADR-0024 §2).
- [`mockups/create-trip-v1.html`](../../mockups/create-trip-v1.html) — trip creation `/new`: three inputs, live soft-grammar draft preview, post-create invite prompt (ADR-0032).
- [`mockups/all-trips-v2.html`](../../mockups/all-trips-v2.html) — the "all trips" home (ADR-0033 + its 2026-07-16 revision): the landing when no trip is live, reached from a trip via the header ▾. Sectioned trip list (now/soon/past), a prominent indigo live-trip hero, mono date meta, create-only, offline state. Supersedes `all-trips-v1.html` (the flat-list original).
- [`mockups/trip-settings-v1.html`](../../mockups/trip-settings-v1.html) — the **trip-settings** screen (ADR-0039): supported-first scope, admin-governed edits (details form, promote/remove via a member action sheet, delete), mode-neutral chrome, admin/peer perspective toggle, and a side design-notes panel documenting what's deferred. Supersedes the trip-settings screen in `screens-v1.html`.
- [`mockups/top-bar-v1.html`](../../mockups/top-bar-v1.html) — **the in-trip top bar, from five rows to two**, promoting [ADR-0149](../decisions/0149-the-top-bar-is-two-rows.md) (**Accepted and built 2026-08-03, session 199** — the built chrome matches this file's numbers on every axis; **read its RENDERER, not its prose cards**, which are one draft behind and describe the ⟨ערימה בשבב⟩ menu variant the owner did not pick), which amends [ADR-0043](../decisions/0043-day-view-now-line-phases-and-archive-chrome.md) §4/§6, [ADR-0033](../decisions/0033-all-trips-home.md) §3, [ADR-0080](../decisions/0080-per-entity-sync-status.md) and [ADR-0133](../decisions/0133-the-user-is-a-surface-identity-ramp-and-a-reachable-roster.md) §9. **Renders through the app's real CSS and is self-contained:** five shipped stylesheets inlined in the app's own import order by [`mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs`](../../mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs) from the `APP-CSS:` manifest — **re-run it after changing any of them** (`node mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs mockups/top-bar-v1.html`; idempotent, and the file is in `.prettierignore` so formatting cannot fight the generator). `modal.css` is in the manifest for a specific reason: the exit is **played, not described** — tapping the chip inside the frame either raises a sheet through the shipped `.modal-overlay[data-variant='sheet']` (scrim and card on separate channels, `--ease-arrive` in, `.is-closing` reversed and briefer out) or replaces the screen through `.route-shell[data-nav='forward']`, so the comparison is between two **shipped behaviours** rather than two drawings. Its "before" frame is the real header, so every number the live measurement panel prints is a number the app actually has: **250px at rest, 321px off-today** at 390×844 with a simulated 44px inset. **Promotes:** two rows at **160px in every state and 108px condensed**; a **deck cue** for the exit (a card edge behind the trip glyph plus a `swap` mark) that is visible without a tap and **absent at one trip** — **the deck half was withdrawn on the 2026-08-03 device pass and only the `swap` mark shipped on:** at 20px the glyph fills its own box, so the edge lands on the emoji rather than behind it, and these frames default to Trip mode where it is white on indigo, never Plan mode where the same rule draws dark ink on a light chip. **The first of this file's own open questions to be answered, and answered no**; **mode in the day row**, icons-only, because that is what makes it survive the condense and work on the Map; the 42px day-scope ribbon becoming a fixed-width **anchor slot** (`יום 2/10` ⇄ `היום`, cross-faded in place) and the sync badges a **positioned** mark, so nothing in the chrome reflows; and **a surface declaring the condensed chrome as its resting state**, which the Map needs because `is-fullbleed` never scrolls. **Two answers it argued itself out of, both recorded rather than deleted:** the trip name as a **menu** (improves comprehension after the tap, and discovery cannot be fixed by something you must tap to discover) and a **back arrow** (fixes discovery but asserts a hierarchy ADR-0033's landing rule contradicts, since a live trip opens directly). **And a whole second shape drawn beside the first** — ⟨הסרגל מתפרק⟩: the strip back to the two tabs `DAY_SCOPED_TABS` actually names, mode out of the chrome, the identity line riding the body as a page title that scrolls away, measuring **Home 44 · Day 102 · Index 44 · Map 0**. It measures better and was **refused by the owner** for changing the navigation model; it stays in the file as the place to look if the two-row strip proves too tight. **Its costs are drawn, not hidden:** the strip narrows 358→182px, so the resting day window goes from **7 to 3**. **And measuring it found two shipped defects it did not introduce:** `MAX_TRIP_NAME_LENGTH = 18` exists by its own comment "to keep the header switcher pill to one line" and **already fails** at 390, and `useShrinkToFit`'s fit test **compares integers**, so a 1.8px overflow reads as 94 against 93 and the loop can stop a step early while the browser draws an ellipsis. **Four times its own instrument lied, and that is the entry's most transferable part:** `--safe-top` is 0 in a desktop browser (so neither header paid a notch and the faked status bar sat on row 1); the frame was 390px wide with a **374px viewport**, because the bezel is padding; a **transition in flight** makes every width a lie (the frame's own `width`, then the mode label's `max-width` over `--t-base`) so the read has to happen after they settle; and the integer-rounding above made the table print "fits" over a frame showing `…`. It also **oscillated** when the condense first shipped — collapsing frees 52px of body, which removes the overflow that triggered it — which is why a slack test is in the ADR as part of the decision rather than as implementation detail (the hysteresis that shipped beside it, 48 in / 12 out, was **withdrawn on the 2026-08-04 device pass**: a threshold turns a gradual gesture into a 52px step, and the collapse is now linked to the scroll itself and snaps when it stops). None of those five is visible in a static frame. **Not answerable here, and left to the device pass:** whether a 3-day strip window is usable on the ground, whether an icons-only mode pill reads as trip-vs-plan to someone who has not seen it before, whether the deck cue reads as "there are other trips" at 20px, and whether a 17px trip name is legible on a dark chrome outdoors. State toggles: מצב · היום שנבחר · סנכרון (תקין · אופליין · ממתין · לא נשמר) · החבורה (לבד · 5 · 8) · שם הטיול (קצר · מקסימום 18) · כמה טיולים יש לך · המעבר לטיולים (ערימה בשבב · ישיר · חץ חזרה — נפסל) · מיקום פקד המצב · פקד המצב · חלון חי · הצעה (שתי שורות · הסרגל מתפרק) · הלשונית (בית · יום-יום · אינדקס · מפה) · גלילה · מסך (390 · 360 · 430).
- [`mockups/user-settings-v1.html`](../../mockups/user-settings-v1.html) — **the user's own settings page + the reachable roster**, promoting [ADR-0133](../decisions/0133-the-user-is-a-surface-identity-ramp-and-a-reachable-roster.md) (Accepted, design): four views via the switcher — the `/settings` page, the picture picker, the roster sheet over the real trip chrome, and a **documentation view for the identity ramp** showing why it is not the `--cat-*` pin hues and what the amber/teal defaults look like beside it. **Bound to the app's css, not a copy of it:** it carries an `APP-CSS` manifest (`styles/tokens.css, App.css, screens.css`), so `.header.mode-chrome`, `.avatars`/`.av`/`.account-btn`, the ghost `.gear-btn`, `.new-head`, `.set-card`/`.set-member`/`.role` and `.sheet-*` are the shipped rules — re-run `mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs` after touching those files. That binding is what caught the first pass drawing a `⚙` emoji where the app renders `Icon`'s cog SVG (retired in U-11), and what exposed the two shipped defects in ADR-0133 §10. Only the genuinely new parts — the ramp tokens, the `Avatar` size ramp, the picker rows, the roster row — live in the file's own `<style>` block, marked as such. Its ramp still owes a **device + dark-mode** pass (§5). **Status vs. the shipped app (2026-07-28):** all four phases have shipped, so the picture view is no longer ahead of the build — the upload affordances it always drew are real as of Phase 4, though the shipped page renders them as a camera badge plus a stacked primary/links group (ADR-0133 §6/§12) rather than exactly the mockup's arrangement. Treat the mockup as the design reference for _grammar_, and the app as the reference for that group's final layout.
- [`mockups/row-menu-v1.html`](../../mockups/row-menu-v1.html) — **the `⋯` row menu, redesigned** (2026-07-29), promoting [ADR-0138](../decisions/0138-the-row-menu-is-one-surface-and-icons-are-ui.md) (**Accepted, built the same session**). Opened by two owner reports on one surface: **emoji drawn on controls** (`design-language.md`'s "emoji are content, icons are UI" is enforced today only for arrow/caret glyphs, so ✏️ 🗑️ 📥 🔄 👑 🚪 all sit outside the rule — and `BookingManageSheet`/`DocumentManageSheet` pass theirs as **inline literals**, not even through `ICONS`, so the backlog's sweep would not have reached them), and **options that do not earn their place** (`הקדם`/`אחר` in the Plan builder's menu). **Before | after, at 360px, rendered from the app's own CSS** (`APP-CSS` manifest: `styles/tokens.css, App.css, screens.css, ui/primitives/modal.css, ui/domain/list-row.css, ui/domain/place-badge.css` — re-run `node mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs mockups/row-menu-v1.html` after touching any of them), with the proposal's own delta fenced off in one hand-written block. **What it draws that a prop list cannot:** the destructive verb at real size, where `מחק` and `ערוך` differ by **text hue alone** (same fill, border, height, gap — and the emoji beside the red label keeps its own colours, which is the tell that it was never part of the state); the builder's menu at its **three lengths** (`הקדם` is omitted at the top of the soft list and `אחר` at the bottom, so `מחק` sits at a different y depending on which row you opened — the position toggle draws all three); and the shipped booking menu naming **nothing**, two anonymous rows over a scrim with the thing you are deleting behind it. **Promotes:** one component for all five menus (`RowManageSheet`; `PlanDay`'s hand-rolled `.row-actions` copy and `MemberSheet`'s `.ms-act` fold in), a **subject line** under every title, five new `Icon` shapes (`edit`, `trash`, `shelf`, `swap`, `calendar` — authored in the file exactly as they would land in `PATHS`), `danger` as a **partition** rather than a hue, imperative copy everywhere (`ערוך`/`מחק`, retiring `עריכה`/`מחיקה`), `הקדם`/`אחר` dropped, and `שבץ במסלול` added to the booking menu — the verb the row's own `לא משובצת במסלול` was asking for and could not reach. **Status vs. the shipped app: all of it is built, plus two things the file draws only after the fact** — the `הזז` step's own frame (added once it existed) and the retirement of `הקדם`/`אחר`, which the BEFORE column keeps so the position toggle still demonstrates the defect. What the mockup does NOT draw is the rest of the sweep the same ADR carried: the bottom nav, the toasts, and ~130 other call sites. **One thing the file itself caught:** the subject line's time range rendered `09:00–08:00` on the first pass — ADR-0118's isolate rule, missing — so the build takes it from `ltrIsolate` (`lib/bidi.ts`), never a hand-written `dir` in JSX.
- [`mockups/motion-primitives-v1.html`](../../mockups/motion-primitives-v1.html) — **the three systemic motion gaps, before/after** (2026-07-31, session 189; brief: [`planning/2026-07-31-session-189-motion-and-first-run-brief.md`](../planning/2026-07-31-session-189-motion-and-first-run-brief.md)). Three tabs, each with a faithful BEFORE reproducing the shipped CSS beside the proposal, because these are only legible by comparison. **G1** — `modal.css` cross-fades scrim and card as one and `Modal.tsx` unmounts on `onClose`, so **no overlay in the app has an exit animation**; the proposal splits the channels and adds an `open → closing → unmounted` state per the `.toast` precedent, with a per-variant arrival (sheet rises / dialog scales / `full` comes from the inline-end edge). **G2** — a direction-aware shell transition for the `.app` surfaces that hard-cut today, deliberately asymmetric (arriving travels 100%, leaving recedes 22% and dims) so it reads as depth rather than a carousel. **G3** — a shared `:active` press rule at two sizes, scale rather than colour so it needs no per-surface table and spends nothing from the semantic budget. Four proofs are wired as controls rather than asserted: reduced motion (where the `closing` hold must read `0`, not the token, or the overlay lingers), the RTL/LTR mirror off one `--dir` sign, `--ease-arrive` on/off, and the theme flip. Proposes exactly two token additions — `--stagger-step` and `--ease-arrive`.
- [`mockups/motion-trip-birth-v1.html`](../../mockups/motion-trip-birth-v1.html) — **the trip-creation payoff, two candidates** (2026-07-31, session 189). Today `submit()` succeeding is a state swap with no motion. The proposal needs no celebration vocabulary because the app already has one: the zero state renders the departure board **unpowered** and Trip Home renders it live, so **trip birth is the board's first departure** — the shipped `board-power`/`wp-board-glow` assets on a second trigger, with split-flap cells settling into honest content (the trip itself, departing in N days). That reading also settles the cinematic budget **without amending design-language's one-moment rule**: the same asset at the two moments a trip changes state, not a second indulgence. The draft card is a **shared element** travelling form→born and going dashed→solid, which is ADR-0011's grammar carrying its real meaning at the frame the trip stops being provisional. **A** (~1450ms, staged, recommended) vs **B** (~650ms, no board) are replayable beat by beat; confetti is drawn as the rejected alternative. Skippability is treated as part of the design, and the open question it names is the repeat viewing, not the first.
- [`mockups/motion-join-v1.html`](../../mockups/motion-join-v1.html) — **the invite join, arrival and stamp** (2026-07-31, session 189). Reads the boarding pass as an object: objects arrive, and they get stamped. Five beats — a ticket-shaped skeleton instead of a status paragraph, the pass arriving and **settling** (notches punching, countdown counting up, avatars landing as arriving people, the amber anticipation glow ramping rather than being warm from the first frame), the stamp landing on the server's success with the pass recoiling under it, the **tear along the perforation the shipped CSS already draws**, and expired/invalid as a **refused** pass rather than a paragraph that reads like a loading state which never resolved. Also surfaces a non-motion finding as a toggle: the anonymous Google detour auto-joins on return via `consumeJoinIntent()`, so the app decides while the user watches a redirect — `resume` shows the visible alternative. Notes two things it does not settle: the removed-member copy (ADR-0067's `TripBlock` must not reuse the expiry stamp) and whether the count-up should run on a re-open.
- [`mockups/document-viewer-motion-v1.html`](../../mockups/document-viewer-motion-v1.html) — **the document viewer opens and closes** (2026-08-02) — **its MOTION DESIGN is superseded by [`document-viewer-motion-v2`](../../mockups/document-viewer-motion-v2.html); its frame/origin/content findings stand and v2 inherits them.** Kept as the record of what was measured and why the first answer was rejected — owner verdict: _"choppy, not smooth, and not varied enough"_, all three correct and all three quantified in v2 §A. From an owner report: _"opening and closing documents doesn't have any animation. They just open/close. That looks amateur."_ The report is right about the close and half-right about the open, and the halves need different fixes: the open IS animated (`screens.css`'s `.doc-viewer { animation: fade 0.18s ease }`) but on the OVERLAY, which is the card's ancestor — so scrim and card fade as one flat image and the card never travels, exactly the coupling [ADR-0140](../decisions/0140-motion-foundations-overlays-arrive-taps-answer-routes-have-a-direction.md) §G1 removed from `modal.css` — while the close is nothing at all, because `onClose` unmounts the portal on the frame it runs. `DocumentViewer` is a lint-allowlisted bespoke portal, which is why G1 never reached it; with `.sheet-overlay` now consumer-less and `.confirm-overlay` gone, it is the **whole** of that ADR's remaining "legacy families" line. **Three beats:** scrim and card on separate channels with the card scaling from `.94` on `--ease-arrive`; an exit through the existing `useExitTransition` ([ADR-0144](../decisions/0144-the-anchored-panels-arrive-and-leave.md)'s hook, so no second close path); and a third the report did not name — the **content swap**, which is the beat specific to documents rather than to overlays. Written to **[ADR-0158](../decisions/0158-dark-mode-ships-and-the-ink-a-surface-carries-is-a-token.md) §16**, which landed the day before it: one THEME toggle writing `data-theme` on `<html>` — the same attribute `lib/theme.ts` writes — so the frames and the file's own chrome re-map together, rather than a side-by-side light/dark pair. Two of its findings are only reachable that way: the scrim (§F) and the letterbox (§B) are both values the two themes disagree about, and neither is visible from the light frame. **Rendering corrected the written proposal five times, and two of the five changed the design:** (1) the arriving image must **not** scale — `.doc-viewer-img` owns `transform` for [ADR-0062](../decisions/0062-zoom-disabled-except-image-preview.md)'s pinch handler, whose `originOf()` derives its focal point from a rect the animation would be mid-way through, so B3 is opacity only; (2) the origin needs **one** measured number, not two — the card is vertically centred, so `transform-origin: 50% calc(50% + var(--dv-origin-dy))` off the tapped row's centre needs no card measurement, no `--dir` and no RTL trap, and the unset default is the honest deep-link case; (3) "a min-height on the body" was wrong twice — it strands the [ADR-0052](../decisions/0052-document-viewer-hardening.md) §1 hand-off panel in an empty card, and a FLOOR does not hold a frame unless it exceeds where the content lands (it took the jump only 253 → 93px), so an image gets a **fixed** frame and the hand-off a floor, decided by the mime type before any bytes exist; (4) `block-size` is **inert** on a `flex: 1` child, so the fixed frame is `flex-basis` — note that `min-block-size` is honoured in flex, which is why the hand-off floor worked while the image frame silently did not; (5) the fixed frame **creates** a question nothing had to answer before — a content-sized frame never letterboxes, and `--paper` is a warm `#2e2a20` in dark, so the strip beside a scan reads as a stain; **settled 2026-08-05 by removing the band rather than recolouring it** — the frame is still reserved before the bytes, but at the picture's own ratio (delivered `width`/`height` for a place photo, the decode for a document), so nothing letterboxes and the jump stays at 0. **The measurement that reframes the scope:** the cold-fetch frame jump is **+253px on a 564px frame**, nearly half the screen and ~5× the distance the card travels arriving — so "beats 1 and 2 only" fixes the two movements you notice and leaves the biggest one in (measured after: image 253 → 0px, hand-off 126 → 15px). **§F puts the one visible half of the ADR-0079 Wave-2 question on the table** — the viewer's `0.72` scrim beside `Modal`'s dialog `0.45`, judged in dark especially; the rest of that cost is not a drawing (340px vs 480px, the dialog's padding, and `frontend/index.html:17` hardcoding `.doc-viewer` as the root of the ADR-0062 zoom exception), with ADR-0144's reuse-the-mechanism-keep-the-shape as the alternative. **Deliberately not drawn:** a travelling shared element (it fails `lib/trip-handoff.ts`'s own "same object at both ends" test, and three landing positions in this repo have already been written as constants instead of measured), a page-turn, and anything at `--t-cinematic`. **A trap it records for the next mockup that inlines `App.css`:** every lab class is `lab-`-prefixed because `.cell` is also the departure board's split-flap cell (`App.css:842`, `height: 26px`) — the unprefixed first draft collapsed every phone frame to 26px with nothing in the console to say so; `.cap`/`.go`/`.ghost`/`.hint`/`.tag`/`.sub` collide too. **What it cannot answer:** how the arrival reads under a thumb (the same device debt ADR-0140 recorded against its own durations), and how a real multi-megabyte scan decodes and paints mid-transition — the document here is a drawn SVG.
- [`mockups/document-viewer-motion-v2.html`](../../mockups/document-viewer-motion-v2.html) — **the document viewer, round two: the sequence, not the fade** (2026-08-02) — **Accepted and BUILT the same day**, together with the app-wide exit fix its §D turned up ([ADR-0140](../decisions/0140-motion-foundations-overlays-arrive-taps-answer-routes-have-a-direction.md) §8). Built as drawn, with `mount opens` chosen for §F's content beat and implemented as a compositing opacity layer rather than the animated `background` the file drew — colour is a paint property, and ramping it across the frame would repaint every frame. From the owner's verdict on v1, _"choppy, not smooth, and not varied enough"_. All three were correct and none was taste; v1 was instrumented and the numbers named the faults. **F1 — it is over in the first quarter and then nothing happens:** v1's card is at 87% opacity and 99.2% of full scale at 25% of its duration, and the remaining 75% is drift nobody can see. **F2 — no distance:** `scale(.94 → 1)` on a 252px card is **7.6px** of edge travel, a nudge rather than an arrival. **F3 — the card arrives before the room dims:** at 160ms the card is 40% opaque against an 11% scrim, because all four channels shared one start time and one duration; simultaneity IS the "not varied", since one event gives one thing to perceive. The answer is a **sequence** with four actors behaving like what they are — the scrim leads (atmosphere), the mount travels and settles, the chrome arrives _after_ the object it sits on so the card assembles rather than appearing pre-drawn, and the document keeps its own clock because its bytes land 40ms or 1.4s later. §A and §B run a live plot that samples every channel's computed value each frame: v1's three curves are one curve bunched into the first third, v2's are four separated ones. Every value is from the shipped ramp, no new tokens and no second `--t-cinematic` — `--t-deliberate` is a new _use_ of an existing rung, which is the right one for a large surface v1 was flying at sheet speed. **§D is the reason this file matters beyond the viewer, and it is a SHIPPED DEFECT.** Writing v2's exit found that an exit expressed as the entrance's keyframes played `reverse` **does not run at all**: same `animation-name` means the same animation retargeted, so it keeps its current time, which by the time anyone closes an overlay is far past the new `--t-quick` duration — the animation is already complete and `fill: both` paints the reversed END state on frame one. Driven against the real `modal.css`, a sheet open ≥200ms is at `translateY(200px)` with the scrim at `0` the instant `.is-closing` lands; it plays only if you close within ~140ms of opening (at 60ms: y=130, scrim 0.35). **Six rules do this** — `modal-scrim-in`, `modal-sheet-in`, `modal-dialog-in`, `modal-full-in`, and both `panel-open` rules in `screens.css` — i.e. every overlay in the app plus [ADR-0144](../decisions/0144-the-anchored-panels-arrive-and-leave.md)'s anchored panels, so [ADR-0140](../decisions/0140-motion-foundations-overlays-arrive-taps-answer-routes-have-a-direction.md) §G1's own premise ("no overlay in the app has an exit at all") is still true and its fix has never run. How it survived is legible in that ADR's build log — the durations were "verified by sampling computed styles mid-flight", and mid-flight is the one window where it works — plus jsdom has no stylesheet, so the unit suite only ever exercises the zero branch. The fix is one word per rule: give the exit its own `@keyframes` name, at the cost of ADR-0140's "an exit cannot drift out of sync by being written twice", which is a real benefit and the thing that has to be given up. v2's own build is the proof: `dv2-leave` was the only exit channel written with a distinct name and the only one that animated, while its chrome and scrim snapped. **§E is a second finding about vocabulary rather than about this surface:** `--ease-arrive`, added by ADR-0140 as "the only overshooting easing — an object that must _settle_", is `cubic-bezier(.22, 1.16, .36, 1)`, whose peak progress is **1.0075** — 0.04% over on v1's scale range, 0.3px across a 40px translate, measured live at 1.0009 against 1.0116 for a conventional back-ease (~13×). A settle nobody can perceive is not a settle. **The file deliberately does not change the token**: it is shared vocabulary owned by ADR-0140 and used by every overlay, the shell transition and the trip-birth sequence, so §E is the evidence and the question and the v2 sheet reads on the shipped curve either way. §F offers three treatments for how the page itself arrives (fade / a `clip-path` wipe / the mount opening from `--card` to `--paper`), none touching `transform`, which belongs to ADR-0062's pinch handler. Names two smoothness fixes that are not curves: `img.decode()` before insertion so a real scan never decodes on the animating frames (invisible here, since the stand-in is a drawn SVG), and `will-change` dropped on `animationend` so a portal that mounts per open does not leak a compositor layer. Inherits v1's settled findings — the stable frame, the measured origin, opacity-only on the image — and does not re-litigate them. Both themes via one toggle (ADR-0158 §16). **What it cannot answer:** how any of it reads under a thumb, and how a real multi-megabyte scan decodes mid-transition.

- [`mockups/map-onefinger-zoom-v1.html`](../../mockups/map-onefinger-zoom-v1.html) — **the canvas takes a one-finger zoom** (Phase 9 of the map panel's third pass, #17), promoting [ADR-0145](../decisions/0145-the-canvas-takes-a-one-finger-zoom.md) (**Accepted**). A **decision surface**, and specifically one built to be driven with a thumb rather than read: §A is the recogniser wired live, so the arbitration can be felt. **It earned its keep twice in one session** — the owner drove it and reversed two derivations, which is the whole argument for drawing a gesture before building it. Promotes: **the three-phase entry condition** (a tap that is passed straight through, a provisional second press that is the only thing taken from Google, and a commit past the slop), with a log naming what Google _would_ have received at each step; **the arbitration as a capture-phase guard rather than ADR-0122 §4's "both listen and the loser bails"** — that precedent is safe only because the sheet's losing drag renders nothing, where Google's pan writes the camera on the first move; **§A2's geometry**, which is where the brief's expected central problem dissolved (`.map-pane` is `bottom: var(--sheet-h)`, so the canvas and the sheet's drag region never share a pixel and were never going to fight — the real competitor is Google's double-tap, which is enabled); **§B's two anchors side by side with the thumb drawn**, settling centre-anchoring on the grounds that the tapped point sits under the finger and that ADR-0129 §1 already refused a lateral move bundled into a zoom; **§C the dot tier crossing live** on a real `[data-pins]` attribute so the shipped degradation css does the work, which also draws ADR-0123's invariant that a pin's SIZE does not change under zoom while its TIER may. **Two numbers the file moved:** the direction (down zooms in — the design had reasoned _up_ from the sheet's "up means more", and a finger overruled it) and the sensitivity (0.18 → 0.5 across two rounds). **Read §D's warning before re-tuning:** this file's canvases are 260px and 214px against ~501px shipped, and because the sensitivity is a **share**, the same constant is twitchier here than in the app — so §D's table is what to calibrate against, not the feel of this file. The canvas is a scaled grid, not Google's tiles, so nothing here speaks to how it reads over real imagery at 60fps.
- [`mockups/map-make-a-place-v1.html`](../../mockups/map-make-a-place-v1.html) — **a place is made, and named, on the canvas** (Phase 6(b) + 6(c) of the Maps & Places epic, the epic's last feature work, plus the naming/renaming capability raised on top of it), promoting **[ADR-0147](../decisions/0147-a-place-is-made-on-the-canvas.md)** — **Accepted and built (2026-07-30, session 197).** Two things in it are now history rather than spec, per **[ADR-0148](../decisions/0148-the-place-form-has-the-room-it-needs.md)** and its own mockup [`map-form-visibility-v1.html`](../../mockups/map-form-visibility-v1.html): the **6c source is gone** (§C's four sources are three, and the ring draft marker with them), and the card carries **one** quiet line rather than a hint and a coordinates row. Everything else here shipped as drawn. Renders through the app's real css via the `APP-CSS:` manifest (`tokens` → `App` → `screens` → `choice-grid` → `map-pane` → `snap-sheet` → `screens/map.css`); re-run `node mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs mockups/map-make-a-place-v1.html` after touching any of those. **The unifying claim it draws:** a place's NAME is the user's, and naming and renaming are ONE act in three states — and this is not a new policy but an existing one getting a surface, because `places.service.ts`'s `enrichExisting` already omits `name` on purpose (a user-authored name survives Google enriching the row with coordinates) while `createEnriched` states the other half out loud. **§A** the three flows sharing one field; **§B** the long press end to end, four frames (press → dashed pin + empty field → typed → full pin + a row tagged `על המדף`, because ADR-0112 makes a reference-less `Place` cache-only so the drop must also create the uncategorised `MaybeItem`); **§C** one form, four sources, and what it holds besides the name — **verified against the model: a `Place` has NO category and NO icon**, the pin's hue is always derived from the referencing entity, and `MaybeItem` is what carries `icon`/`category` (so the form needs no migration), against the recorded counter-decision that `AddIdea` deliberately ships an `IconPicker` and _no_ category picker (session 76 / ADR-0109 §11) — the map's case for differing being that here the category IS the pin's hue, so an unchosen one renders a restaurant green, which is misinformation rather than absence; it also carries the **icon-hierarchy correction** (the shipped chain is `chosenIcon(event?.icon) ?? BOOKING_TYPE_ICON[booking.type]`, i.e. the linked event's deliberate pick beats the booking's type glyph, not the reverse) and costs the two options for where a place icon could live; **§D** the three rejected placements, **measured in-file** (a `⋯` in `.map-right` takes the row to 113px and a control on the name line to 90px, against the 73px ADR-0134 §5 protects — plus ADR-0135 §1's recorded rejection of a second control in the way-in footer); **§E** one row, two hosts (the canvas card IS `renderRow`'s output, so the field reaches the sheet's list and the card with no second call site); **§F** what it spends from the budget (nothing: neutral control, no new floating object, zero height while unselected, `Icon`s not emoji, 44×44 via a `::after` so the target costs no layout). **Rendering against the real sheets caught six defects in the proposal itself**, which is the point of the binding: `hidden` is inert on the new `display:flex`/`grid` containers so three surfaces showed at once; the draft card had no surface at all (`.map-placecard` is positioning only — the card look comes from its `.place` child); a 44px button grew the selected row 4px and its negative margin overlapped the ellipsis-truncated name; the confirm/cancel overflowed the card because a grid child defaults to `min-width: auto`; the pending pin was drawn on top of Google's own sight icon (now the app's own **ring**, ADR-0132 §6's silhouette for a Google-sourced candidate that is not yours yet); and the uncategorised fallback hue is `leisure`, not the `services` the first pass guessed. **What it cannot answer:** whether a long press survives Google's own tiles on a real phone — simulated on a `div` here, and ADR-0131 §9's flagged device-pass item that could **block** rather than tune.
- [`mockups/map-form-visibility-v1.html`](../../mockups/map-form-visibility-v1.html) — **the place form's visibility under a keyboard, and every way out of it** (2026-07-30, session 198), promoting **[ADR-0148](../decisions/0148-the-place-form-has-the-room-it-needs.md)** — **Accepted and built the same day (session 198); every §C lever shipped.** Two reports off a real phone against the form ADR-0147 shipped: _"we must have total visibility, see how it's handled for search"_ and _"clicking outside should close it and deselect"_. Renders through the app's real css via the `APP-CSS:` manifest, and **lifts the phone frame, the two keyboard models and the safe-area modelling from [`map-google-pins-v1.html`](../../mockups/map-google-pins-v1.html)** so its numbers are directly comparable with [ADR-0132](../decisions/0132-search-reclaims-the-chrome-and-a-google-result-is-a-ring.md) §4's table. Every frame is built by **one builder with flags**, so "as shipped" and "proposed" cannot quietly drift into two different drawings, and every number is read from the live DOM. **The report looked like one bug and the measurement made it three.** §A reproduces the screenshot with a dashed box showing where the card _really_ ends: the card is bottom-anchored and grows upward, so a shrinking split clips it at the **top** — the actions survive and the content dies, which means you can commit a form you cannot read. §B is the measurement, and it is why this file exists: the card is 223px (243 with a hint), the chrome reclaim buys 122, and at the `half` stop with an Android keyboard the room goes 76 → 140 — **still 103px short**, with the missing part being the title and the name field. So _"give the form the reclaim too"_ is half a fix. **Two findings neither report contained.** (1) At the `full` stop `room` is **−38px, constant, keyboard or not** — so the pencil ADR-0147 added opens a form that cannot be drawn at all, in a state that same ADR argued could not arise ("the gesture can only start on visible canvas" is true of the two canvas gestures and false of the pencil). (2) **On iOS every layout number is healthy and the card is invisible** — the viewport does not shrink, so the card lays out at a full 564px of room and the keyboard is drawn over it; the table therefore reports _what is seen_ beside _what was laid out_, and they separate only on iOS. That is the same shape as ADR-0132 §4's iOS attribution failure, "the harder of the two to notice". **Two of the levers were then settled by the owner and are drawn as decided, not proposed.** §C2 draws the first as four before/after pairs from one builder: **opening the form normalises the sheet to `map` from every origin** — plain map, `half`, the full list, and the **search view** (which is not hypothetical, since a search result is the fourth source) — so "the sheet stands down" stops being a special behaviour of the form and becomes the same act as tapping `רשימה / מפה`. All four land at an identical 372px, which is what turns the card's budget into one constant per screen instead of a function of a draggable fraction; and the sheet returns to the stop it came from, which answers ADR-0147's rejection ("it takes away the list you were reading") rather than overriding it. The search pair also shows why that one path never broke: it already reclaims on `queryOpen`, so _"see how it's handled for search"_ was exactly right. The second settled lever is that **the chrome comes down while the keyboard is up** — with one correction recorded in §C: the trigger is _the form being open_, not the keyboard literally, because tapping a category pill blurs the field and drops the keyboard, so a keyboard-literal trigger makes the header and tab bar pop back in and the card re-lay-out on every pill tap. "For the duration of the form" contains "while the keyboard is up" and never flickers, and costs nothing when the keyboard is down (243 needed against 372/564 available). §C proposes **one guarantee then three levers**, in that order: the card is bounded by `--map-card-room` with its title and actions **pinned** and only the middle scrolling (which is not a new pattern — `EventForm`/`BookingSheet` are already a scrolling body with pinned `FormActions`; the place form is the only form in the app that did not carry it, because it was drawn as a card); the sheet stands down to `map` while the form is open (**+233px, the biggest single lever**, and at `full` a correction rather than an optimisation — ADR-0147 rejected this without a keyboard in the room); the chrome reclaim, whose state is called `queryOpen` when the shell only ever needed the _want_, not the cause; and the card's bottom clearing **the keyboard** and not only the sheet, which is the iOS-only half. §D is the full matrix — source × stop × keyboard × screen — and §E the dismissal table: the outside tap is not a feature request but a rule `frontend/CLAUDE.md` states and the shipped form breaks (one function for the ✕, back, Escape and the tap), with a transparent surface and **no scrim**, because a scrim says "the map is disabled" and the map is the thing you are naming a point on. **§C3 records the owner refusing the scroll** — _"some of the form is cut off, this shouldn't happen and I don't want to allow this"_ — and is where the file stops proposing and starts accounting: a scroll inside a 204px card is permission to cut, not total visibility, so the two levers that found the pixels are drawn with their arithmetic. **−20px** for one quiet line instead of two (they were never both load-bearing: a dropped pin has no address, and a result's marker had already said where it was), and **+52px** for the sheet not being shown **at all** rather than merely normalised — at `map` it is nothing but its own strip over a list you cannot see, and its view toggle actively contradicts a form that just moved you to the canvas. That takes the card to **223px for every source** against 256 in the worst target (360×640, Android keyboard), so the bound survives as a floor for a device nobody measured rather than as the answer. **§G is the removal**, added last on a third report: _"opening a create place form for every hit on a Google suggestion is very annoying, I prefer if we removed this one."_ It is the one thing in this file the file itself could not have found — **frequency is not a drawing** — so §G is an accounting rather than a comparison: the source, the phase's only paid gesture, the ring marker, `mapsPlaceIdUrl`, `nameOptional` and the free owned-sight rename path all go, ADR-0125 §6 stands **unamended**, and renaming is the pencil and only the pencil. **What it cannot answer:** the real keyboard heights (approximated per width, a device-pass question), and whether iOS's `100dvh` PWA behaves as the overlay model predicts — the one assumption the whole iOS column rests on.
- [`mockups/form-refusal-v1.html`](../../mockups/form-refusal-v1.html) — **how a form says no** (2026-08-04, session 201), promoting **[ADR-0150](../decisions/0150-a-form-refuses-at-the-field.md)** — **Accepted and built the same day.** From an owner report against the event form: _"the error for missing fields is nearly noticeable"_. Two sheets side by side, both live: **before** is the shipped caption above the save button — one problem at a time, naming no field, and in a real form sitting after a scroll; **after** marks each missing field where it is, nudges once, and brings the first into view. Self-contained (tokens inlined, not the `APP-CSS:` manifest) because what it draws is a handful of rules that did not exist yet — but it carries the app's **teal focus rule** deliberately, and that is what earned it: focusing the refused field repainted its border as the healthy focused one, a defect the unit tests could not see and this file showed in a second (ADR-0150 §4). Press `שמירה` on either side; typing in a marked field retires that field's mark only. **What it cannot answer:** how the nudge reads on a real phone under a real keyboard — the field is brought to centre of a scroll container this file does not model.

- [`mockups/the-way-back-is-its-own-route-v1.html`](../../mockups/the-way-back-is-its-own-route-v1.html) — **הדרך חזרה היא מסלול בפני עצמו · can a round trip come home a different way** (2026-08-24; building [ADR-0203](../decisions/0203-a-journey-has-one-date-and-its-arrival-is-a-clock.md) §6 at last and amending [ADR-0184](../decisions/0184-an-edge-can-be-a-window.md) §2, **Accepted and built**). Renders the app's real CSS (`APP-CSS:` manifest — 11 sheets; re-run `mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs` after touching any of them), both themes, 360/390px. **Three field reports, and the middle one is §6 arriving with a dimension the ADR never had.** **§1** the day-turn divider, which shipped with a 90%-transparent ground on a band that does not move — measured at alpha **0.1** against **1**, so the scrolling row read straight through it; centring the label is drawn as its own variant and **rejected**, because it moves the collision rather than ending it and turns a caption into an ornament. **§2** the spine: the way back as its own route, mirrored by default (one derived sentence, no second list) and diverging into its own stop list, **including a different NUMBER of them** — which `reversed` could never express, since it was one array read backwards and `legCount` was one number for both journeys. Interactive on the state (mirrored · diverged), the control shape (pills · a text offer) and both stop counts. **§3** a window read as one sentence at both edges: the reported `מ־ 08:00 מ־ 01:15` against `מ־ 01:15 עד 08:00`, and the measurement table **prints the actual word run each line renders** because a screenshot cannot be trusted to tell `מ־` from `עד` at that size. **§4** what §2 costs the route step, with an off-screen probe for the deepest form the feature can produce (`MAX_ROUTE_STOPS` both ways). **Measured:** the route step runs 306/358/462px mirrored and 438/490/594px diverged, and the true worst case — 3 stops each way — is **646px against ADR-0155's 675px**, so it fits with 29px spare and the mirrored default is what buys that margin. **What the render falsified:** place pickers drawn as `.place-picker > .pp-name` came out as blank white bars, because that is not the tree `PlacePicker` emits (it is `.pp-trigger > .pp-trigger-icon + .pp-trigger-label`) — the same for `ChoiceGrid`, whose pills are `button.choice-pill` inside a `radiogroup` and not a hand-rolled `role=group`; and a literal `←` in the derived-ends line, which is `Bidi_Mirrored` and flips in RTL, replaced with the real `NavArrow` inside `RouteLabel`'s own `<bdi>` pair. **Rejected in the file, with reasons:** centring the divider label; a text offer instead of pills (its revert must say `חזרה לאותה דרך`, and `חזרה` already names the section — one word, two meanings, adjacent lines); diverging the return's two ENDPOINTS as well (you fly home from where you landed, so an open-jaw trip is a different feature, declared rather than half-built); and a `ConfirmDialog` on returning to a mirror, unnecessary once the flag and the list are separate fields. **Left open:** the refusal mark's scroll target now that the end edge's two tokens are reordered — the mark is on the line's box, which is right, but the scroll aims at that box and wants a device pass.
- [`mockups/a-clock-follows-the-one-before-it-v1.html`](../../mockups/a-clock-follows-the-one-before-it-v1.html) — **שעה נמשכת מזו שלפניה · what does a clock offer when the moment before it is known** (2026-08-23; amending [ADR-0203](../decisions/0203-a-journey-has-one-date-and-its-arrival-is-a-clock.md) with §10 and two §3 corrections, **Accepted and built**). Renders the app's real CSS (`APP-CSS:` manifest — 11 sheets, `ui/domain/journey-field.css` among them; re-run `mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs` after touching any of them), both themes, 360/390px. **Five sections, from six field reports on the rail ADR-0203 shipped.** **§1** a layover's two clocks, unlabelled against captioned: _"the form doesn't explain what each time is"_, and the file's first contribution is that §3's original plan **cannot** work — `ConnectionBand` was meant to name them and it measures the _wait_, so it renders only once both clocks exist and is absent for exactly as long as the pair is ambiguous. **§2** the spine — the offered order, rotated to start one step after the previous moment and wrap through midnight, with the day turning **inside** the list; interactive on the anchor (⁦20:30⁩ · ⁦06:00⁩ · ⁦23:30⁩) and on whether the journey crosses zones, because a westward crossing keeps the same calendar day past 00:00 and a midnight divider would lie about it. **§2b** a shipped 44px miss the render found on `.tp-list button` (**36.1px**, on the control a traveller taps most while setting a journey). **§3** the node body as a column with a gap (**0px** of zone-chip air today against 8px, the number `WhenField` already spends) and the empty segment row that reserves 8px for a measurement it cannot take. **§4** the step seam (**0px** against the sheet's own 12px). **§5** the step's identity pinned, asserted by measurement rather than by eye — **−150px** from the scroller's top after 150px of scroll today, **0px** pinned — and why it must be **one** sticky box: two sticky siblings leave the container's gap between them and the rail scrolls through it, **24px** of it. **Interactive:** theme, width, §2's anchor, §2's zone case. **What the render falsified — and the third item is the file itself:** the obvious claim that rotation is always closer (for a 20:30 departure a 4h15 leg arrives 00:45, which the shipped 00:00-first order puts at row **3**; the honest claim is that the row stops depending on where midnight falls — **2–86 today against a constant 3** for a one-hour leg, across three anchors); a divider drawn with `.tp-list button`'s own class, which collapsed against the row above and read as a dead option; and **twice, the "before" columns were drawing the fix and grading it a win**, because the file was written with its change and inlines the sheets that carry it — first 8px of zone-chip gap in both columns, then 44px rows and 12px seams in both. Every baseline is now explicitly restored under `.bld-was*`. **Rejected in the file, with reasons:** filtering instead of rotating (`minTime` already filters, and that is _why_ the report exists — it is passed only on a same-day end, so a later day falls back to 00:00; filtering here would delete a legal 00:45 arrival); keeping the 00:00 order and only scrolling to the anchor (tomorrow's hours then sit **above** the anchor, so forward in time is backward on screen); the anchor as the first row (an arrival at its own departure is 24h later — it stays, but last); a divider at local midnight (the Tokyo→Honolulu case); passing the anchor to `WhenField` too — refused with a **count** as the argument, since `transportProfile` sets `inMotion: true` for flight, train and transit and all three also title from their route, so every in-motion span **is** a journey and what is left in `WhenField` is a stay and a hire, where anchoring a checkout on a check-in would be wrong rather than merely useless; and two sticky siblings instead of one header slot. **Left open:** `scroll-snap` behaviour on a rotated list, on a device — `.tp-list` already carries `scroll-snap-type: y proximity` and the divider is the first row that is not a snap target.
- [`mockups/a-journey-has-one-date-v1.html`](../../mockups/a-journey-has-one-date-v1.html) — **למסע יש תאריך אחד · why a departure and an arrival read as two flights** (2026-08-23; promoting [ADR-0203](../decisions/0203-a-journey-has-one-date-and-its-arrival-is-a-clock.md), **Proposed**). Session note: [`planning/2026-08-23-…`](../planning/2026-08-23-the-arrival-was-read-as-a-return.md).
