Renders the app's real CSS (`APP-CSS:` manifest — 15 sheets; re-run `mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs` after touching any of them), both themes, 360/390px. From an owner-relayed field report: _"because the departure and arrival of a flight both have a date and a time, they thought that the latter was the return flight."_ **The diagnosis is not only symmetry, and that is the file's first contribution:** `המראה`/`נחיתה` do not disambiguate either — a return flight also has a `המראה` — and `bs-leg-head`'s `RouteLabel` renders only when `multiLeg || twoLegs`, i.e. exactly not in the reported case, so neither block names the place it is about although the step before collected both. **§1** draws the shipped `WhenField variant="span"` tree beside the proposal at the same width; **§2** is the spine — one absolute date per journey, every later moment a clock plus a derived relative day in the existing `.vt-word` tone, drawn in three states (derived `למחרת`, the **Tokyo 21:00 → Honolulu 09:00** case that separates instants from wall clocks, and a hand-overridden `+2 ימים`); **§3** draws the journey as `day-join.css`'s own `.journey` with `ConnectionBand` unchanged, so a layover's wait is stated while you type it, in its `tight` state; **§4** puts the shipped **defect** beside the fix (an empty stop cannot be removed — `pp-clear` renders `{current && …}`, so the row that most needs removing has no control and tapping it launches a Map errand that unmounts the sheet); **§5** the single date suggestion, filtered by `readiness.ts`'s `reachesDestination` in its three states (way there · way home · internal hop, which gets nothing); **§6** the seeded-not-mirrored return, drawn both still-mirrored and diverged; **§7** the fold, per-leg against per-journey, and the frame that proves the cross-leg **refusal is designed out** (an "earlier" clock is simply tomorrow, so `legBeforeArrival` cannot fire — the frame was drawn as a _refusal_ frame first, i.e. the file contradicted its own design until it was rendered); **§8** a **place** suggested off the trip's existing legs, plus what the errand's `kind` filter already does and the gap its own comment names; **§9** a long journey — a filled node summarises to one line, drawn against all-open and against a step per leg, and it is what turns §7's "the common case fits" into "every case fits" (at `MAX_ROUTE_STOPS`: **783px** all-open against 675px of visible sheet, **437.5px** summarised, **548.5px** for the whole step). **Interactive:** theme, width, whether `באותו יום` always shows, pill vs pre-fill vs off, and whether `הלוך ושוב` is pre-offered — the last three are the device questions the file refuses to settle from a desktop. **Every duration, wait and day offset is DERIVED from the clocks plus each point's zone offset**, after the first draft's data disagreed with itself (a segment reading `3:20 שע׳` between two clocks 1:20 apart) — and deriving it found the real rule: **a day rollover is computed on instants, not wall clocks**, because Tel Aviv 23:40 → Reykjavík 23:55 is the same calendar day. **What the render falsified, eight items, listed in the file's header:** §7's central claim (a three-leg journey measured **708px against 675px** — over; the ladder is now 220/396/607px for 0/1/2 stops, with the whole step at **720.5px** at two stops, which scrolls); three touch targets short of the 44px floor (38 · 41 · 32); a rail that did not paint at all (`inset-block: 0` with no inline offset resolves to the _static_ position, so the line drew beside its column instead of through the dots); the self-disagreeing data above; a footer drawn with `btn-primary`/`btn-ghost` against the app's real `fa-primary`/`fa-secondary`, so it rendered as two native buttons — caught by looking at the **dark** render, not by reading; `מהטיסה לכאן` set in the mono class, which has no Hebrew (the exact trap `value-token.css` keeps a whole `.vt-word` tone for); and a second ✕ drawn beside the stop picker, which is precisely the copy ADR-0159 §5 refused — the fix is `pp-clear` itself made unconditional. **Where it now differs from the shipped app (2026-08-24):** §9 draws compaction from ONE stop up, and the app summarises from two — the threshold was corrected to match this file's own fold table, whose 0–1 row is dashes because those counts never overflowed. The heights and the ladder are unchanged and still the spec; only the count at which compaction switches on moved. **Rejected in the file, with reasons:** regularising the two boxes (ADR-0177 §1's own "tidying is not designing"), an arrival entered as a duration (the ticket says a landing time), a dotted rail in the badge column (ADR-0159 §3 already tried it and the owner read it on a phone), merging the where and when steps, a `ChoiceGrid` for the trip's edges, a "suggested" look on a seeded return, auto-running a seeded Places search (`PLACE_SEARCH_MIN_CHARS` exists so a paid call is never made unasked), bundling an airport dataset, and a cross-trip "home airport" — deferred with the schema's own reason quoted, and narrowed by the owner's note that once a trip holds one leg §8 answers the rest.
- [`mockups/a-day-turns-under-a-held-card-v2.html`](../../mockups/a-day-turns-under-a-held-card-v2.html) — **היום נרים, נעצר, ומסתובב · the edge-drag day step, take two** (2026-08-22) — **promoted into [ADR-0116](../decisions/0116-day-aware-shelf-and-idea-target-day.md) §2d and built the same day**, superseding `-v1`'s approach. Session note: [`planning/2026-08-22-…`](../planning/2026-08-22-a-day-turns-under-a-held-card.md). From the owner's rejection of what v1 shipped — _"you got this all wrong. Your design is very ugly, no I don't like the static 'peek' into the next/prev day"_ — and the model that replaced it, in their words: _"it starts dragging to the next day and stops and then if your finger stays then it completes the motion"_. **The diagnosis is a unit, and it is the file's reason to exist.** v1's lean travels 48px over the 700ms dwell = **1.1px per 16ms frame**, which is not slow motion but a static offset with a timer attached — so _"static peek"_ was a measurement, not a taste. v1's argument (the motion IS the progress indicator) was sound and unaffordable at that speed; the session had checked that the duration matched the dwell and never that the result was **perceptible**. Every phase here prints **px/frame** for that reason. **What it proposes:** three phases with a detent — ① the whole strip lifts `DRAG_DAY_LIFT_PX` (48px) over `--t-base` / `--ease-arrive` and **stops** (3.2px/frame, 2.9× v1); ② it rests there for the rest of the dwell (~460ms), nothing moving; ③ staying completes the turn on the swipe's own settle (350px over `--t-base` at 360px = 23px/frame). The affordance stops being "how long is left" and becomes **"the page is cocked"**. Total 940ms, the dwell unchanged. **What reading the code changed:** phase ③ is not new code — `useSwipePager` already owns the offset channel, both settle attributes, the timer and ADR-0200 §8's wait-for-the-arriving-page — so the build is an **extraction** ("a page turn can be _commanded_, not only dragged"), which deletes v1's `--peek-lean` and its extra `transform` term and puts the panes back on `--swipe-dx` where §7 had them. **Where it contradicts v1 on purpose:** v1's §3 rejected the whole-strip model on a measured 48px displacement of the row under the finger. That rejection was **over-weighted**, and this file says so with both readings side by side — the displacement is 48px during the hold and **0px** once the band is left, so it is never present at the moment anyone is aiming at a chip. **The play control is the point.** `הרץ את התנועה` runs the phases as real transitions on the real stylesheet, because a filmstrip cannot answer "is this fluent" — which is exactly how v1 was approved and then rejected. Sampled while playing: 0→48px over ~160ms, held ~320ms, then →350px. **Rejected, with the reason each would be re-proposed for:** v1 itself (right argument, 1.1px/frame); spending the whole dwell on the lift (same 1.1px/frame with more moving); a slow creep during the hold to keep the "how long is left" reading (the owner said _stops_); shortening the dwell (700ms is shared with the day pill's route, so it moves two mechanisms). **A defect in the file itself, caught by rendering it:** the measurement table multiplied `240ms` by 1000 and printed a **240000ms** lift — a token duration is legal CSS in either unit, and the app reads it through `lib/motion.ts` which a standalone mockup cannot import, so the file now carries a 6-line `ms()` parser. The table lied in the one column the whole file is about. **Where it differs from the shipped app.** The three phases shipped as drawn; two rules the app has now are **not** in this file, both from the same-day repair round after five reports — the band leaves at `DRAG_DAY_EDGE_PX + DRAG_EDGE_SCROLL_RELEASE_PX` rather than at the depth it entered on (hysteresis, so a finger near the boundary stops chattering), and the opposite band pays **half** the dwell inside `DRAG_DAY_REVERSE_MS` of a step (`DRAG_DAY_REVERSE_DWELL_MS`), because undoing a step you can still see is a correction. Neither changes what the motion looks like, which is why the file was not re-drawn for them. **A third rule does** and is also not drawn here, though the file's own frames happen to be right about it: the lift is spent **once per approach**, so the strip rests at LEVEL between days and a hold that turns several of them is one full-page turn per day with nothing in between. The file draws a single step from level, which is exactly that; what it does not show is the second and third day of a hold. Two things the build found that the drawing could not: `useEffect(() => stop, [stop])` is an unmount cleanup only while `stop` is identity-stable, and the moment it depended on the caller's command it ran every render and gave the lift back the instant it was taken (the command is now read through a latest-ref); and an e2e waiting for a stepped day has to poll **faster than the step** — the cadence is 940ms and the next turn is committed 240ms before the previous day appears, so `expect.poll`'s default ladder reported an arrival a second late (2026-08-23 at 1850ms, 2026-08-24 landing at 1880) and the spec acted on the day before last. Labelled mockup-only overrides are v1's, unchanged: `.day-peeks` positioned against the frame rather than a `fixed` window, and a static dragged clone wearing the shipped `--drag-ghost-opacity`. Controls: ערכת צבעים (בהיר · כהה) · מסך (⁦360×640⁩ · ⁦390×844⁩) · מרחק ההרמה (⁦40 · 48 · 64 · 80px⁩ — a feel call, shipped at the recommendation, device pass owns it) · האטה בהרמה (arrive · standard) · הרץ את התנועה.
- [`mockups/a-day-turns-under-a-held-card-v1.html`](../../mockups/a-day-turns-under-a-held-card-v1.html) — **מעבר יום בזמן גרירה · the motion for the edge-drag day step** (2026-08-22) — **promoted into [ADR-0116](../decisions/0116-day-aware-shelf-and-idea-target-day.md) §2c and BUILT the same day, as drawn** (owner: _"approved, let's build this"_). Session note: [`planning/2026-08-22-…`](../planning/2026-08-22-a-day-turns-under-a-held-card.md). From the third owner report in two days against one gesture: _"I think that we need some kind of an animation or something for dragging between pages. Something that looks polished."_ §2b had shipped the step itself — hold a dragged card at the day surface's inline edge and 700ms later the day changes — **in total silence, and then as an instant swap.** **The finding that made the proposal one line long:** the app already owns a page turn ([ADR-0200](../decisions/0200-a-day-steps-with-a-swipe-and-the-shell-stops-scrolling.md) §7's peek panes on `--swipe-dx`, landing on §9b's `--t-base`/`--ease-standard`), and the edge-drag is **the one day change that does not use it** — so the whole CSS delta is one term added to `.day-peek`'s existing `transform`, and the file's hand-written proposal block is 12 lines. **Four sections and one rejection, each carrying a measured number.** §1 the lean — the incoming pane comes to meet the finger over exactly the dwell, `linear`, so the motion IS the progress indicator (no ring, no bar, no new word); the four frames are the real motion sampled at **0 · 233 · 467 · 700ms**, which is only legitimate _because_ it is linear. §2 the turn, which is the swipe's own settle unchanged. §3 the rejected model (the whole strip leans) beside the proposed one at the same instant, where the two numbers that killed it are printed: the row under the finger displaces **48px** against **0px**, and — the geometric finding nobody would guess — **the gutter must be crossed before any of tomorrow is visible at all**, since `.day-peek` parks `--swipe-page-gap` (24px) outside the window, so a "12px hint" reveals 12px of page background and **0px** of the day it promises. §4 the trip's end (nothing moves, and `BEAT.REBUFF` refused on its vertical axis and on firing every time a drag crosses the band) and the abort (`--t-quick`/`--ease-exit`, declared on the destination state per `design-language.md`'s asymmetric settle). §5 **the risk that could have killed the design, and only a render could answer it**: the dragged clone is a full-width row under the finger, so it covers the revealed strip **completely** across — 24 of 24px — which looked fatal until the measurement was taken in the dimension that decides it. The reveal is the body's whole visible height and the clone is one row: **89% of it is never covered** (24 × 638px revealed, 68px of height crossed), and the crossed part still reads through `--drag-ghost-opacity` 0.78. A width cannot see that, which is `frontend/CLAUDE.md`'s "a height cannot see a clip" running in reverse. **Why the duration is not a motion token:** 700ms is longer than every token but `--t-cinematic` (600ms), which is budgeted to one moment in the product — and a token would lie, because the motion must end exactly when the day changes. It reads `DRAG_DAY_DWELL_MS` through `--swipe-dwell`, the mirror of the pager reading `--swipe-page-gap` out of CSS. **Three defects this file had while being built, each caught by the render and each recorded in it:** the lean was **added** to the parked offset instead of subtracted, so the "hint" pushed tomorrow further off screen (`translateX(-400px)` where −304 was wanted) — now written as one multiplication so the sign cannot be got wrong again; the displacement row measured **378px** because it compared rects across frames in different `justify-content: center` containers, which is a number about flexbox and not about the design; and the striped band drawn to _explain_ the edge was painted at `z-index: 14` **over the 24px reveal the whole file is about** — a mockup hiding its own subject, now a dashed boundary instead of a fill. **A shipped defect it found, unrelated to motion:** `screens.css`'s `.day-peek` declares its own `--peek-dir` (−1 RTL / 1 LTR) for the job `tokens.css`'s **`--dir`** already does and four shipped stylesheets already spend — introduced by ADR-0200 §7 the day before; the proposal's rule spends `--dir` and the duplicate goes with it. **Where it differs from the shipped app: it does not, in what it proposes — §2c shipped as drawn.** Three things the BUILD found that the drawing could not, all consequences of the peeks now mounting during a drag (§7 only ever mounted them during a swipe, when no drag is running): `useSelectionGuard`'s `release` removes `body.wp-dragging` from an unmount cleanup, so a pane going away took the class the real drag was using — and the lean's own stylesheet keys off it; `:not([data-preview])` on an ANCESTOR does not exclude preview descendants, because the panes live inside the non-preview host, so a pool-strip query matched three strips and `.first()` walked the day to the trip's first day; and the peeks' mount condition has to be `live || leaning`, because the pager's `live` is false in exactly the case the lean needs a pane. Also: an unregistered custom property computes to its token stream, so `--peek-lean` reads back as `calc(24px + 24px)` and a `parseFloat` assertion passes as `0 === 0` — the spec resolves it through a probe's `width`. Labelled mockup-only overrides: `.day-peeks` is `absolute` against the frame (the app measures `--peek-x` into a `fixed` window, which would escape a phone frame on a document) and aligned to `.body`'s own 16px padding after the first render reported the window 16px off the host; the dragged clone is a static stand-in wearing the shipped `--drag-ghost-opacity`, placed against the **measured** top of the held row rather than a constant; and the band's boundary is drawn because nothing paints it in the app, which is §4's open question. Controls: ערכת צבעים (בהיר · כהה) · מסך (⁦360×640⁩ · ⁦390×844⁩) · כמה ממחר נחשף (⁦16 · 24 · 32 · 44px⁩ — a feel call, shipped at the recommendation, device pass owns it) · הרוח (מוצג · מוסתר). **SUPERSEDED IN APPROACH by `-v2` the same day** — the owner rejected the shipped lean on sight (_"very ugly, no I don't like the static 'peek'"_), so the paragraph above about "§2c shipped as drawn" describes what shipped for one merge, not the app today. Its measurements stand and v2 builds on them; its choice of what moves does not. The one number it did not take is the one that decided it: **1.1px per 16ms frame**.
- [`mockups/shelf-crowded-v1.html`](../../mockups/shelf-crowded-v1.html) — **the maybe shelf when it crowds** (2026-07-31), **promoted and BUILT** — decided in [ADR-0151](../decisions/0151-a-suggestion-has-a-source-and-a-reason.md) + [ADR-0116](../decisions/0116-day-aware-shelf-and-idea-target-day.md)'s session-202 amendment, shipped in session 203 ([note](../planning/2026-08-05-session-203-the-shelf-crowds-built.md)). All five proposals below are in the app; §5's owner verdict was **build it as drawn**, with the device pass on how the tab switch READS still owed. Two corrections the build made to this file are worth knowing before reading it as a spec: the tile's meta is the fact ALONE (`0.3 ק״מ`) because the sheet's full sentence wraps at 140px and costs the tile the 8px it was drawn to save — this file's own code had that right, its header comment says "one-line title" and is stale — and the ranking partitions on spoken-for FIRST and sorts by distance within it, as `ranked()` here does, because the other reading silently reverses ADR-0116 §2's grouping. From two owner reports against the shipped shelf — _"some people are going to have tens of maybe items, so at some point it's gonna become hard to find and slot the card that you want"_ and _"the cards are really big compared to the events themselves"_ — plus a third the code volunteered: `GapFillSheet` is handed `maybeItems.filter((m) => !m.consumed)`, the whole pool, unsorted and unsearchable (`PlanDay.tsx:920`), on the one surface whose entire question is _which idea fits this slot_. **Its subject is N, not the card**, so the idea COUNT is the main toggle (5 · 18 · 40) and every number in the panel is read from the live DOM at the selected count — the failure can be watched arriving. **The root cause it names:** ADR-0115 put `＋ אולי` one tap from a Google result and ADR-0135 put `＋ שיבוץ ליום` on the map's place card, so supply rose an order of magnitude while the container stayed ADR-0116 §2's two strips — whose own Consequences deferred a filter row with the trigger _"earn it when the strip crowds"_. Report #1 is that trigger firing. **What it measures that a prop list cannot:** the position of the idea you are actually reaching for (nearest to the day's stops) — **3 · 18 · 18** shipped, **1** proposed, at every count and on both surfaces, which is the report restated as a number where every other row is a size; swipes-to-last **2 · 10 · 24** against **2 · 3 · 3**, the point being that it stops growing rather than that 3 is small; the card at **132px against a collapsed event row's 86px** (1.53×), taken to 76px (0.88×) and the whole shelf from 349px to 245px of a 640px screen; and an anatomy frame showing that 56px of the shipped card's 132 is padding and slack held open by `min-height` plus the action line's `margin-top: auto`. **Proposes five separable things:** a stable `orderBy` (there is none — `trips.service.ts:456` — so "the snapshot's order" ADR-0116 §2 relies on is Postgres's whim); a compact tile keeping the soft grammar and losing the per-card `＋ שבץ ליום` that a section hint already says; ranking the pool, which is ADR-0116 §1's own deferred "future sort" arriving now that `targetDate` exists; a gap sheet ranked against the slot it opened on, capped and searchable past a threshold, filling `.gapfill-m` — a meta slot styled in `screens.css` and never once rendered; and §5, the tail of the pool leaving the shelf for the Map's `אולי` facet, which ADR-0119 made the same union and which already carries day scope, chips, search, distance sort and `＋ שיבוץ ליום`. **Three things it got wrong while being built, each recorded in the file because each changed a conclusion:** it inset the strip by its 14px mask before counting visible cards and got "one" for both columns, which was over-strict — but the exercise produced the real finding, that the strip holds **two cards at any usable width** (three would need 102px, which carries no title), so narrowing the card is not the lever and §5's cap is; it measured the event row against a **wrapping** title, flattering the card at 1.29× until the baseline became the shortest row on the frame; and its first tile clamped the title to one line, which the render answered instantly with `מוזיאון…` and `תצפית מצפ…` — the height comes out of the action line, never the title. **Renders through the app's real CSS** (eight shipped stylesheets inlined by [`mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs`](../../mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs); re-run after touching any of them, and the file is in `.prettierignore` so formatting cannot fight the generator). Numbers verified identical with and without the webfont loaded. **Deliberately undrawn: the hold-to-drag gesture** — ten ADR-0116 amendments live in it with unit and e2e cover, and nothing proposed changes what a drop _means_, only how many candidates you passed to start one; redrawing it would imply otherwise. **What it cannot settle:** whether §5's handoff reads as "the rest are over there" or as being thrown off the surface mid-build (a phone judgement, ADR-0017's device pass); whether a 140px tile's two-line title truncates too much on the trip's real titles; and whether a capped strip reads as "the five best" or as "something is missing". State toggles: רעיונות במדף (5 · 18 · 40) · מסך (360×640 · 390×844) · תצוגה (היום · הצעה / הצעה בלבד).

- [`mockups/notes-screen-v1.html`](../../mockups/notes-screen-v1.html) — **פתקים · the screen** (2026-08-01, session 205), the first half of the design session [ADR-0152](../decisions/0152-a-note-is-one-entity-with-an-optional-host.md) owes — **Proposed, not yet decided.** The Index's third tile and the screen behind it: the tile's preview line (the LATEST note with its author — a note collection has no "next" and no type groups, but it has a newest, and that is the line that changes), the screen's **grouping** answered flat-by-recency with the number that kills the intuitive alternative (at 40 notes they fall across 22 hosts, so a host-grouped list is 1.4 rows per header — and grouping-by-host rebuilds, worse and 22 times, what the host surfaces already do), the row across **nine** states (body-only, title-only, title+body with the body DEMOTED rather than printed twice, url-only as an `ltrIsolate` island, no-category, an over-long host name, pending and failed writes), search mode, the row menu and its delete confirm, the editor with its one refusal marked on **both** fields that can cure it, the card tier drawn-but-not-wired, offline, and both empty states. Live: the chip filter runs the real `.wp-reveal` stagger, and §7 plays the one new transition the design owes — **keeping a card collapses it in place and the row arrives**, rather than flying it to a sorted position that may be off screen. Two corrections it records against itself, both from rendering rather than reasoning: `.idx-screen` needs its `.index` ancestor or every rule the screen depends on silently drops, and `.modal-head`/`.modal-body`/`.sheet-card` do not exist — a Sheet is `.modal-overlay[data-variant='sheet'] > .modal-card > .modal-title`. Icons follow ADR-0138 where the shipped Index does: the tile's mark is `<Icon name="clipboard" />`, not 📝, because a tile is a control; the category badge stays emoji, because a category is the thing's own face.

- [`mockups/notes-on-a-host-v1.html`](../../mockups/notes-on-a-host-v1.html) — **פתקים · on a host** (2026-08-01, session 205), the second half of the same session — **Proposed, not yet decided.** The mark and the reach on all five hosts, drawn against the **crowded** rows rather than clean ones. Three findings the brief could not have had, all from reading the code or measuring the render: (1) **`EventCard` has no `meta` prop** — it is built inside the component from `placeName`/`code` (`EventCard.tsx:148`), so the mark needs a new `notes?: number`; (2) the mark **cost 19px on a 102px row**, and the first fix measured identically because `flex-wrap: wrap` **wraps before it shrinks** — only `nowrap` + ellipsis returns it to 0px, which is a behaviour change for rows with **no** notes and therefore an ADR line; and its own consequence, flagged rather than shipped, that the ellipsis eats the **confirmation code** at the end of the line; (3) **`MaybeItem` has no detail surface at all**, the one real gap in ADR-0152 §6's rule, closed by giving the sheet the tile already opens the same `.note-sec` the detail surfaces get. The maybe tile's three options are measured against ADR-0151's own 8px refusal: corner **0px**, replacing the ranking reason 0px but selling last week's feature for this week's, both-at-once **+6px**. Leaves §B1 (the create-time fork, since `BookingSheet` has an inline notes field **today** and a note-as-row makes one save two dependent outbox ops) deliberately undecided — it is an outbox decision, not a layout one. _Superseded on one interaction (2026-08-07, session 219): the composer in this file commits on **Enter**, and the shipped one no longer does. The owner reversed that rule — Enter is a newline inside the note, only `＋` commits — and [ADR-0152 §6b](../decisions/0152-a-note-is-one-entity-with-an-optional-host.md) is amended in place. Everything else here still describes the build._

- [`mockups/notes-on-a-place-v1.html`](../../mockups/notes-on-a-place-v1.html) — **פתקים · on a place** (2026-08-01, session 206), the prerequisite phase 6 of [the notes build plan](../planning/2026-08-01-session-206-the-notes-build-plan.md) is gated on — **Promoted and built (2026-08-02, session 207).** The fifth and last host, and the one the plan's G1 first read as having no entrance at all: `PlaceRow` is not a `ListRow`, has no kebab, and its "detail surface" is the same row again inside `.map-placecard`. **Not a Map mockup** — everything unmeasured is row-level, so there is no canvas, only a hatched stand-in sized to the split so the card's bottom-anchored geometry has something real to be bounded by. **Almost nothing in it is a proposal:** since phase 5 shipped, `.note-mark`/`.note-sec`/`.note-item`/`.note-compose` are app CSS and arrive through the `APP-CSS:` manifest; the file adds **three rules** (the section as a full-width line inside the wrapping `.place`, the height bound's `:has` widened past the form card, and the pinned primary action below). **What driving it found, and it is not the "zero" the plan assumed:** the mark **can** cost one meta line, and the panel's threshold row builds the line up a tag at a time to say when — at 390px the cost appears at 3 tags (+9px) and is **0 again at 4, 5 and 6**; at 360px it appears at 5 (+17px). Non-monotone because the cost lands exactly where the mark is the item that tips the line and vanishes once the row wraps without it, and bounded at one line because the mark is a fixed-width last item rather than text that grows. **A semantic tag can never move** — that is what rendering it last buys, and it is the actual answer to the plan's question (1). Unlike the day card, `.map-m` has wrapped by design since it shipped, with six tags and no notes. **`שיבוץ ליום` is PINNED, and the owner's review is what caught it** (2026-08-01): the first draft let the whole row scroll, the primary action included — the same defect ADR-0148 §1 fixed on the form card, and worse here, because putting a place on a day is what the card exists for. Fixing it produced the file's second real finding: `position: sticky` on `.map-refs-foot` **where it lives today does not work** — measured at **−156px**, i.e. off the card, because a sticky element cannot leave its containing block and `.map-refs` sits entirely below the scrollport on a card scrolled to the top. So the proposal **moves the foot out of `.map-refs`** to be the row's own last child, which is also the truer description of it (the row's primary action, not a fourth reference); it then pins from the first pixel, 12px off the bottom at rest and after a full scroll, at both widths. A third render note came with it: a scroll container's padding is part of its scrollable area, so notes kept scrolling visibly through the 11px strip under the pinned foot while every number read healthy — covered by a pseudo-element rather than by moving the button. **§3 is the one that justifies the file:** at 360×640 with three notes the unbounded card overflows the canvas **top by 149px** — clipped, ADR-0148 §1's "commit a form you cannot read" arriving on the selection card — while the extended bound leaves it 54px inside and scrolling; at 390 it clears by 8px, so the failure only shows at the small width. Also measured: the section costs **+33px at one note, +123px at three** (390px), and the composer as `.map-draft-scroll`'s second child leaves the pinned foot **12px inside the card, unchanged** from without it. Toggles: רוחב (390×844 · 360×640) · סימון (מוצג · מוסתר). **What it cannot settle:** the composer with the keyboard up on iOS, where the viewport does not shrink and every layout number reads healthy while the card is invisible (`--map-kb-h`, ADR-0148 §4); every text-derived width, since a sandboxed browser cannot load Assistant — the thresholds above will move on a device, the shape will not; and whether the mark wants a tap target, the one question ADR-0153 §8 left to a finger. **Built as drawn, with two corrections the shipped code forced:** the grid variant anchors the identity row on `.map-badge` (the mockup wrote `.wp-place-badge`, which is not a class this app has — the badge's own is `.map-badge`, plus `wp-placebadge` only where it is the way in to the pin), and `NoteSection` gained a `.note-sec-list` wrapper, because the mockup's scroll rule needs the list to be an element and the shipped component rendered its items as bare children of the section. The owner's one open question is answered by taking this file's recommendation — **accept the extra meta line** (measured against the built CSS: +0 at 390px, +17px/one line at 360px, no semantic tag displaced either way). Re-measured after the build, at 360×640 with three notes: the card lands exactly on its cap (316px), the note list is the only scroller, and the section header and `שיבוץ ליום` are both inside the card.

- [`mockups/notes-and-documents-in-context-v1.html`](../../mockups/notes-and-documents-in-context-v1.html) — **פתקים ומסמכים · in context** (2026-08-08, session 225), the design half of [ADR-0172](../decisions/0172-a-linked-pair-is-one-context-and-a-place-may-inherit-it.md) / [ADR-0173](../decisions/0173-a-document-attaches-and-detaches-never-dies.md), drawn **after the model was decided and before #26 was built** — the owner asked for it mid-session and the ask was right: ADR-0172's decisions are invisible (which row holds a note, which host owns a write) and need no drawing, but ADR-0173 §5 puts a new slot on `BookingSheet`, a form ADR-0155 already measures at ~1565px against ~675px of visible phone. Four findings, all measured headless at 390×844 rather than asserted: (1) **the attach slot's EMPTY state cost 86px** with a header and two entrances, which is too much for nothing on that form — so variant **D** was drawn _after_ the measurement (one 40px control until something is attached, the header/list/two-entrance split appearing only once it does), saving **46px** on every form that attaches nothing, which is most of them; the chip itself is ~34px and linear, the same shape and the same reason ADR-0152 §6b rejected an open box per note. (2) **an inherited note marked with its source costs 2px per note** (111px flush vs 115px marked, two notes) — ADR-0172 §9 had deferred that distinction, and 2px is too cheap to defer given the argument is not decorative: deleting the booking destroys those notes and a booking delete has no undo, so a reader who cannot see where a note came from cannot see what threatens it. (3) **the mark costs 0px** on the crowded row, confirming ADR-0152 §6c at §7's new frequency — but it explicitly does **not** settle §6c's drop-the-place-name rule, because the name did not clip here and §6c already recorded why (its 174px is webfont-dependent and does not reproduce without Assistant loaded); recorded as "height only" rather than read as a refutation. (4) **the reworked delete dialog is 23px shorter** (203 → 180), the consequence line coming off the top and the count moving into a description line that already existed.

- [`mockups/attachments-and-event-preview-v1.html`](../../mockups/attachments-and-event-preview-v1.html) — **מסמכים מצורפים ותצוגת אירוע** (2026-08-08, sessions 231 + 232), the design half of [ADR-0174](../decisions/0174-an-attachment-is-marked-and-opened-and-an-event-has-a-read.md) — **§1/§2/§3/§5/§7 accepted and BUILT; §4 still open and unbuilt.** Drawn from three owner reports that turn out to be one shape: an attachment has no mark, no way in, and the surface a way-in would live on (an event's read) does not exist. **Eight sections, and session 232 rewrote it in place rather than superseding it, because ADR-0174 was still Proposed and nothing it recorded was a decision yet.** Interactive: a controls bar driving theme (בהיר/כהה) and screen (⁦360×640⁩/⁦390×844⁩), with one `#meas` table read off the live DOM and re-read on every control change — the shape `.claude/skills/design-mockups/scripts/render.mjs` drives. **What session 232 changed, and two of them changed an argument rather than a digit.** (a) **The file had no webfont link at all**, so every number session 231 recorded was measured on the sandbox's fallback face; Assistant / Secular One / JetBrains Mono are linked now. (b) It measured one phone; ADR-0017 calls 360 the design width, so the Plan builder row's naive mark turns out to cost **+0px at 390 and +14px at 360** rather than a flat +10px — `.bld-m`'s rebuild is still the prerequisite and is now a defect you would not see on the phone you tested on. (c) **§6 is new** — the lifted Trip hero, the surface the feature exists for and the one ADR-0174 never mentioned; three treatments measured (section 324px · chip-in-the-acts-row 300px · mark alone 258px), and at 360×640 the densest states are 46px/37px **over** the lift card's room, which the file had reported as fitting because `.hero-scroll` cannot overflow when `max-height: 100%` resolves against an auto-height card. (d) **Every sheet is the app's own `Modal`** now; the first pass hand-rolled an `mk-sheet`, which is a rule-8 violation inside a file whose premise is that it renders the shipped CSS. (e) **§4 draws the real `EventForm`** at its real height in a real phone over a real Plan day — 700px at 390 and 547px at 360, with 266px/419px below the fold — where the first pass drew a 497px stub that made today's surface look better than it is. (f) **§5 shows a six-variant range** for the attach control (all 40–48px, so height is not the axis) and lands on `.pp-trigger`'s shipped geometry; a filled `--cta` is refused because the form already has one and it is `שמירה`. (g) **§7 (picker + upload) and §8 (the reverse read, drawn once and deliberately not built) are new**, as are the stress cases — four attachments, a queued upload, a 62-char stored filename, a 44-char address on a marked row. (h) The doc/clipboard marks use `ui/Icon.tsx`'s own paths and stroke attributes; the first pass invented both silhouettes and rendered them filled. **The one thing it still does not settle** is §4 — whether the Plan row's tap stops opening the editor — which is a product call and the reason that section alone is unbuilt. **Where it now differs from the shipped app (session 233):** its §1 still draws the row's meta line carrying a place name and a confirmation code, and the app no longer does — the owner's call after a real code (`הזמנה #MEGAZIP-T141215488`) overflowed the row on a device is that a row shows **glyphs and no text at all**, which retired ADR-0152 §6c's composition rule and `eventMetaParts` with it (ADR-0174 §8). §1's finding still stands where it matters: the `.bld-m` rebuild into a nowrap line of elements is what the marks ride on. And §4 is now **built** rather than proposed — `EventDetail` ships, over a `DetailSheet` shell extracted from `BookingDetail` because the two came out identical.

- [`mockups/note-preview-v2.html`](../../mockups/note-preview-v2.html) — **פתקים · the preview, round two** (2026-08-02, session 208), from three more owner reports against the preview `note-read-surfaces-v1` had just shipped — **Proposed.** The reports were the dead link, "too compact for notes that are short", and "no reference to the host". Then the owner asked a fourth thing that turns out to come BEFORE all of them — _"I'm not 100% sure that notes should open from the bottom like other previews"_ — so that is §1 and the rest follows from it. **The link report is not in the file**: a defect with one right answer and no design in it, fixed in code the same day (a user-typed url has no scheme, and a scheme-less string is a RELATIVE href, so the tap re-entered the app — the whole window in the installed PWA; `lib/external-url.ts` supplies the scheme for the href, keeps the typed text on screen, and passes only http/https/mailto/tel, which also closes `javascript:` in a group-visible free-text field). **§1 draws three containers on the same note** — the sheet as shipped, the sheet stripped of its head, and an expansion in place — over the list they cover, so the ceremony is visible rather than argued: at 390px a short note costs **151px / 109px / +37px** and a long one **199px / 157px / +89px**, and the first two cover the list while the third pushes it. **What the render corrected twice, both invisible on paper:** the expansion's first draft printed the body under the row, which is the same sentence twice on every short note (the failure ADR-0153 §4 names for the row) — so it became **the row's own two-line clamp lifting**, plus one foot line, which is why a short note's whole cost is the foot; and the foot's host way-in then duplicated the host chip in the meta line, so the chip hides while the row is open. The foot is also the only place the way in CAN go: the meta sits inside the row's own `<button>` and buttons do not nest (`ListRow`'s own trailing slot is a sibling for the same reason, ADR-0139 §3). **§2** turns the host's name into the destination it always was — a caret and a 44px target under an 11.5px line — with the five host kinds tabulated against what exists (place and booking ship today via `useShowPlaceOnMap` and `?booking=`; event, document and idea are three copies of `Index.tsx`'s own `pendingBookingId` shape, not a new mechanism). **§3 answers the rule-8 question the expansion raises:** the notes screen's rows are the shared `ListRow`, and the expansion is a **sibling** of the row inside `.listcard` rather than a prop on the primitive — so bookings, documents and members inherit nothing and nothing is copied. Recommends the expansion, and leaves two decisions: whether `NoteDetail` disappears entirely or survives for a note reached from search, and whether to ship two destinations now or hold for all five.

- [`mockups/note-preview-v2.html`](../../mockups/note-preview-v2.html) — **פתקים · the preview, round two** (2026-08-02, session 207), from three owner reports against the preview that shipped that morning — **Promoted; owner's call taken on both open questions.** Reports: the link does not work, the surface is too compact for a short note, and the host is named but goes nowhere. **The link report is not in the file** — a defect with one right answer (a user-typed url has no scheme, so it is a RELATIVE href and the tap re-enters the app; installed, that is the whole window) fixed in `lib/external-url.ts` alongside. What the file is really about is a FOURTH question the owner asked while it was being drawn — _"I'm not 100% sure that notes should open from the bottom like other previews"_ — which comes before the other two and reframes them: `BookingDetail`'s grammar is an object with facts and verbs, and a note is a sentence, so the ceremony IS the compactness complaint. §1 draws three containers over the real list at 390px and measures them: **A** the sheet as shipped (151px short / 199px long, covering the list), **B** the sheet stripped of its head (109 / 157), **C** expand in place (**+37 / +89**, list intact). Two corrections the render forced, both duplication the paper version hid: C's first draft printed the body under the row, which on a short note is the row's own words twice — so C became **the row's two-line clamp lifting**, plus one foot line, and on a short note opening adds only the foot; and the foot's host way-in duplicated the row's host chip, so the chip hides while the row is open (the foot is the only half that can hold a tap target, since the meta lives inside the row's own `<button>` and buttons do not nest). §2 makes the host's name the way in — same words, a caret, a 44px target under an 11.5px line — with the five destinations tabled and their mechanisms named. §3 states C's only real cost and why it is zero: the expansion is a **sibling** of the row inside `.listcard`, so `ListRow` (shared with bookings, documents and members) never learns about it. **Owner's answers (2026-08-02):** the sheet goes — the host note SECTIONS are unchanged and keep displaying notes as they do, only the tap changes — and all five destinations are wired before the UI is built, rather than shipping the two that exist.

- [`mockups/note-read-surfaces-v1.html`](../../mockups/note-read-surfaces-v1.html) — **פתקים · the read surfaces** (2026-08-02, session 207), from three owner reports against the shipped build — **Promoted and built.** All three are about READING a note rather than writing one, and two of them are the same mistake made twice: **the app kept sending a reader into an author's surface.** An event's notes were only in its `⋯` menu (a list of verbs), and tapping a row on the notes screen opened the EDIT FORM. §1 measures why the menu happened at all — `.wp-event-actions` animates `max-height: 0 → 220px`, and with three notes the strip needs **235px, so it clips by 15px** — and proposes the cap's removal rather than a workaround: `grid-template-rows: 0fr → 1fr` is the content's own height, so there is nothing to guess and nothing to cut. **That is a motion change to every card on the day**, noted or not (the strip used to race to a ceiling and stop where its content ended; now it travels exactly its own height), which is an ADR-0140 line. §2 draws the preview the notes screen never had, in `BookingDetail`'s grammar (ADR-0053: a read-only sheet, facts, and ONE visible edit; the delete stays on the `⋯`). **Two corrections the render forced, both invisible on paper:** the first draft invented `.bs-facts`/`.bs-fact`/`.k`/`.v`, none of which exist in this app, so every key ran into its value unspaced — the real classes are `.bk-fact*`; and it printed the host in the head AND in a `מדבר על` fact, which stutters on every untitled hosted note, so the host now sits once in the head's type line and the fact below it is the way IN, carrying a caret and no name. The head never repeats the note's words either (ADR-0153 §4's rule for the row, applied one surface up): an untitled note's head says `פתק` and its words read in full below, unclamped, which is the entire reason the surface exists. **Promoted and BUILT the same day (2026-08-02, session 207):** §1 shipped as drawn (the cap removed, `grid-template-rows: 0fr → 1fr`, the notes under the verbs inside the expanded card) and §2 as `ui/NoteDetail.tsx` — with one thing the mockup got wrong and the render caught: the caret fact §2 drew as "the way IN" points at nothing, because **no surface in the app navigates to a note's host**, so it shipped without it. Amended into [ADR-0152 §6](../decisions/0152-a-note-is-one-entity-with-an-optional-host.md) (the 5b amendment retracted in place), [ADR-0153 §4](../decisions/0153-the-notes-surface-the-mark-and-no-mode-gate.md) and [ADR-0140 §8](../decisions/0140-motion-foundations-overlays-arrive-taps-answer-routes-have-a-direction.md) (the motion cost). The build also went one step past the mockup, for a reason the mockup could not see because it drew one screen: the preview is what a host's own note SECTION opens too, since its lines clamp exactly as the screen's rows do.

- [`mockups/notes-long-note-row-v1.html`](../../mockups/notes-long-note-row-v1.html) — **פתקים · השורה שאורכה לא נגמר** (2026-08-16), from two owner reports against the shipped notes screen over a real Iceland trip — **Promoted and BUILT the same day; owner's call taken (“go with option א, and fix the host surface too”).** The reports: _"I want to improve how the notes screen looks. It looks really bad with long notes"_ and _"Note descriptions formatting (new lines, spaces etc.) don't show in the note preview."_ **They are one defect, and the file turns on counting where a note's body actually renders** — four places, one broken: `.note-body-line` (body-only note, this screen) has both a clamp and `pre-wrap`; `.note-item-b` (a host's section) has `pre-wrap`; Home's hero preview is one line by design; and `.wp-listrow-meta`, which is where ADR-0153 §4 sends a **titled** note's body, is a shared `ListRow` class with **neither**. So the same misplacement produces the wall (no clamp) and the swallowed line breaks (no `white-space`). **The row in the owner's screenshot is closed** — it reads as a bug in the expansion and is not one. §1 reproduces it and measures it: **285px at 360 and 255px at 390, 16 rendered lines inside the meta line**, with the author and the elapsed time pushed to the end of that paragraph, i.e. the meta line has lost exactly what it exists to carry. §2 puts three answers side by side on the same note: **א** the body moves to its own clamped line under the title (**98.4px**, constant for every note regardless of length); **ב** the body stays in the meta and the meta grows a clamp (**75px**); **ג** a titled note is one line (**60px**). The whole list goes **679→493px** (−27% at 360, −24% at 390), and a body-only note costs **exactly 0px**, which is asserted in the table rather than claimed. **The recommendation is א, and its cost is one declaration:** `.wp-listrow-title > .note-body-line { flex: 1 0 100% }` — no new class and no new `ListRow` slot, because `.note-body-line` already clamps, already keeps the composer's newlines (ADR-0152 §6b's 2026-08-07 amendment) and already unclamps under `.wp-listrow.is-open`, and `.wp-listrow-title` is already a wrapping flex. The first draft added a `sub` prop to `ListRow` and it was thrown away under root `CLAUDE.md` rule 8. §3 is a cross-cutting fix in `list-row.css`: `.wp-listrow`/`.wp-listrow-open` are `align-items: center`, which is right for the fixed two-line rows the primitive was born on and wrong the moment one row can be four lines — the badge and the `⋯` float at mid-height, which is visible in the owner's screenshot; scoped to `.listcard`, with the trailing group's inset **derived** from the kebab's 30px-ink-in-a-44px-box rather than guessed. **Three things the render corrected, and the first two were wrong numbers that did not look wrong.** (a) The newline measurement first counted `Range.getClientRects()` and reported **32 lines against 61** for the same sentence — rects fragment per bidi run, so one line of Hebrew carrying `Aurora forecast` yields three, and the two probes were different font sizes on top of it; line counts are now **distinct rect tops**, and `height ÷ line-height` is not used either because `.wp-listrow-meta` declares no line-height and returns `NaN`. (b) Counting _lines_ then reported the four-line stress note as **+0 lines lost**, which is true and meaningless — what a swallowed break costs is **which words begin a line**, so the probe now asks it per authored break: **0→1 kept** for the note in the screenshot, **2→3** for a note typed as four lines. (c) Option **ב** turns out to delete the author and the time outright, since the clamp applies to the whole meta line and they sit at its end — an argument against ב that only appeared once a frame was drawn. **The open question the render also created:** with the breaks honoured, a first authored line that wraps consumes the entire two-line preview (the screenshot's note previews as `⁦החלון המעשי הטוב הוא בדרך כלל: 22:00–02:00⁩` and nothing else), which makes the 2-vs-3-line clamp a live device call rather than a decoration. Interactive: theme (בהיר/כהה), screen (⁦360×640⁩/⁦390×844⁩), and four feel calls as controls — clamp (2/3), the body's size under a title (13.5/12.5), chrome alignment (מרכז/ראש), and the category badge (עם/בלי, measured at 36px+11px and **recommended kept**: the screenshot's uniform glyph is data-dependent, and a note row without a badge stops looking like every other list in the app). **Rejected and recorded:** patching `.wp-listrow-meta` itself with `pre-wrap`+clamp — the shortest-looking fix for both reports, and it changes bookings, documents and members to repair a note, while still leaving the body at 11.5px `--muted`. **A separate defect the reading exposed, not fixed here:** `NoteSection` renders `noteTitleText(note)` = `title || body`, so on a host a titled note's body is invisible on every read surface but this row — counted against all four `noteTitleText` call sites, the other three being single-line by design. **Fixed in the same change on the owner's instruction**, so ADR-0152 §6 is amended alongside §4: `NoteSection` shows a titled note's title AND its body, which is where a structured note is actually read — that surface does not clamp and already carries `pre-wrap`, so the breaks land whole. **Where the build differs from this file:** §3's alignment fix is drawn scoped to `.listcard` and shipped scoped to a note's own `.note-row` — narrower, because bookings, documents and members cannot grow and the shared default would have moved their `⋯` to fix a screen they are not on; the shipped selector is `.wp-listrow.note-row`, since the rule it overrides is also one class and a tie would be settled by bundler emit order. Verified against the shipped stylesheets in a browser rather than against this file's own PROPOSED block: badge centre and kebab centre both land at 31px, and the clamp holds the body at 36.4px — which also caught this file's measurement trap a fourth time, where a rect count claimed the clamp was not applying and the box height said it was.

- [`mockups/note-full-screen-v1.html`](../../mockups/note-full-screen-v1.html) — **פתק · המסך המלא, הדרך אליו, ומה עושים עם Markdown** (2026-08-22), promoting [ADR-0202](../decisions/0202-a-note-gets-a-full-screen-and-markdown-is-a-subset.md) — **Proposed; design only, nothing built.** Four owner messages, and the fourth (_"we should also take embedded notes into consideration (notes for bookings, places, events...)"_) is the one that settles §1. **It does not take the in-place expansion back**: "clicking on a note expands it in place" reads as a defect and is ADR-0153 §4's measured decision (+37px short / +89px long, against a sheet's 151/199px that also covered the list), so the full screen is a **third container** and its way in must be earned — the row's tap is already spoken for. **§1 draws four candidates on BOTH surfaces a note opens on**, which is what kills three of them on facts rather than taste: a trailing mark has nowhere to live in `.note-item`'s two-cell grid; a `⋯` action has no menu to join (`NoteSection` renders none, and a place row has never had a kebab, ADR-0153 §8's 2026-08-02 amendment); and brackets on the body are a **button inside a button**, drawn as a real nested one so the render reports the inner control **hoisted out of** `.wp-listrow-open` rather than quoting ADR-0160 §4 about it. What survives is one more `.row-open-act` in the shared `RowOpenFoot` wearing `Icon name="frame"` — the glyph `FilePicker` already uses to mean "opens full screen" — at **0px**, its 44px floor arriving from the family's `::after` overlay and not from its 27px box. **§2** is `Modal variant="full"`, bar measured **56px** against `.search-overlay-bar`'s 56px, with `MediaViewer` refused in writing (what it brings is bytes: an object URL, a decode, ADR-0062's pinch, a fall back to "open in a tab" — a note has none of them). **§3** renders the whole subset at once and prices the alternative instead of dismissing it: `react-markdown` is **117,575 / 36,023 bytes** across **81 packages** (esbuild, react external, then `gzip -9`) against the proposal's **147 lines / 2,911 / 1,437** — and the argument that decides it is not the bytes but that CommonMark **joins a soft newline into its paragraph**, while ADR-0152 §6b made those newlines content on 2026-08-07, so a "correct" renderer would silently rewrite every note that exists. The parser returns **structure** (React nodes in the app), so the app's one group-visible free-text field has no injection surface to sanitize — a property, not a mitigation. **§4** is link detection, unconditional and through the shipped `externalHref`/`prettyUrl` pair, with the four refusals drawn: a filename (`passport.pdf`, which is why a bare host needs a scheme, a `www.` or a path slash), a time or a price, a phone number, and a `javascript:` link rendered as the author's words. **§5** splits three surfaces on two facts already in the CSS — _does it clamp_ and _is the body a `<button>`_ — so the row is flat text (**99.4px either way**, the clamp fixing the height), a host's section is shaped with **dead** urls (ADR-0153 §8's "a second tappable thing is a mistap" reaching a url found in prose), and the full screen is the only surface where a note's links are live, which is its second reason to exist. **Four things the render corrected, and two of them were the file's own numbers.** (a) A **shipped defect**: `externalHref` allows `mailto:` and nothing supplies it, so a typed email takes the `https://` branch and the `@` is parsed as HTTP **userinfo** — `tokyo-stay@example.com` → `https://tokyo-stay@example.com/`, host `example.com`, and `prettyUrl` then labels it `example.com`, so the address is not on screen at all; reachable today, since the url field is free text. (b) The flattened preview had to shorten its urls too — raw, it put `Google:` on one line and `/www.tabelog.com/tokyo/A1303` on the next, which is the owner's own 2026-08-02 complaint arriving on a different surface. (c) The foot **wraps** under a real host name once a third control joins it (51px against 27px in a browser with Assistant loaded), so `.row-open-lead` truncates rather than the verbs moving off the trailing edge — and the wrap point is **webfont-dependent**, exactly ADR-0153 §6c's caution, which the file records instead of picking whichever reading it preferred. (d) Shaping a note on a host makes the section **shorter** (312.5 vs 319.7px), not longer, which is the opposite of what was assumed while drawing it. **Two findings about the mockup harness itself, written into [`.claude/skills/design-mockups/references/pitfalls.md`](../../.claude/skills/design-mockups/references/pitfalls.md) because they are not about notes:** `tokens.css` has declared `html, body { overflow: clip }` since 2026-08-21 (ADR-0200 §1), so **every** mockup that inlines it now collapses to one viewport — §2–§5 laid out, never painted, no console error, and the measurement table still filling in (`a-day-turns-under-a-held-card-v1.html` is the other file carrying the rule); and `document.fonts.ready` resolves **before** a lazily-fetched `@font-face` exists, which is how this file's table reported the truncation fix as costing **nothing** (⁦38px⁩ against ⁦38px⁩) until it asked for the faces by weight. Interactive: theme · width · candidate (א/ב/ג/ד, with a per-candidate verdict line that changes with it) · note (ארוך/קצר/קישור) · Markdown מעובד/גלמי, which flips every frame on the page including §1's and §5's · and the two feel calls as controls, the prose size (⁦14.5⁩/⁦15.5⁩) and one heading step or two. Renders all four theme×width combinations with the webfonts loaded and no console errors. **PROMOTED AND BUILT the same day (2026-08-22), and the build differs from this file in four places** — all four written into [ADR-0202](../decisions/0202-a-note-gets-a-full-screen-and-markdown-is-a-subset.md) as in-place amendments rather than left as drift: §2's frame drops the `⋯` **and** the bar's host chip (ADR-0053 keeps a delete on the ROW's kebab, and ADR-0153 §4 already hides the row's chip once the foot names the host — so a chip a whole screen from the foot is that same stutter; the foot then names the host even when it cannot be reached, since it is the only place left that can); §3's heading map ships with `#` and `##` sharing the one size step, because `##` is the level people actually paste and giving the step to `#` alone flattened the common case; the way in reads **`תצוגה מלאה`** and not the shorter `מסך מלא` drawn here, since `FilePicker` already names this exact action that way and one meaning with two nouns is how a vocabulary drifts; and the host section's newlines are now a `<br />` per authored line rather than `white-space: pre-wrap`, which is what lets a blank line and a single newline finally differ — and moves the guarantee out of CSS that jsdom cannot see and into the DOM. **And four more from the owner's first look at the shipped screen** (same day, ADR-0202 §9), of which one is a defect this file helped cause: the prose container was drawn with `dir="auto"`, which resolves from the first strong character — so a Hebrew note opening `TL;DR` laid every line out left to right, and **omitting the attribute would have been better than adding it**, since the notes screen's row carries none and has always read correctly by inheriting the page. Nothing in this file could have caught it: its fixtures are Hebrew-first and none of them opens with Latin, which is worth remembering the next time a mockup's content is written to look right. Also: the prose reads at a new `--text-reading` step rather than `--text-body`, and a **hold** on either row body now opens the screen — the answer to a friction this file measured as zero and mis-scoped, since the cost of the foot's control was never pixels but distance down a long note.

_`notes-screen-v1.html` and `notes-on-a-host-v1.html` were **promoted** on 2026-08-01 (session 205) into [ADR-0153](../decisions/0153-the-notes-surface-the-mark-and-no-mode-gate.md), which closes the design brief's §A, §B and §D. Read the ADR for what was decided; the mockups remain the build spec and carry the measurements and the rejected options. `notes-on-a-host-v1.html` was revised twice during that review: the multi-note stack it first proposed was rejected for optimising the rare case (it added ~62px per note to the sheet, and opened a box nobody asked for) and replaced by a single composer whose committed notes collapse to one-line chips — 113px for three notes against 152px — each tappable to keep editing._

- [`mockups/booking-round-trip-v1.html`](../../mockups/booking-round-trip-v1.html) — **transport authoring, both halves** (2026-08-01; **promoted 2026-08-02 into [ADR-0154](../decisions/0154-transport-authoring-a-route-shape-a-round-trip-and-a-derived-pair.md), Accepted — the build spec, **built 2026-08-02**; the shipped form differs on one measured point, below**): a flight or train is bought as a round trip and written one leg at a time, and the two forms that can write one are each shaped wrong for it in a different way. Renders the app's real CSS (`APP-CSS:` manifest; re-run `mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs` after touching those sheets), so it is in `.prettierignore`. **Does not reopen [ADR-0047](../decisions/0047-booking-event-linkage-and-notes.md) §1** — a round trip stays two `Booking`s, because the timeline needs two independent hard events with their own instants, statuses and zones — so nothing here is an entity, a table or a migration. §1: a `כיוון אחד · הלוך ושוב` pair of `.choice-pill`s at the top of the **route** field, which reveals a second `WhenField` span and gives BOTH blocks a leg heading (in pairs or not at all — one-way is today's form plus the 44px control row, not byte-for-byte it — the file's own claim, corrected against a measurement); type, icon, code and kind stay one shared input, only dates and times are written twice, and one save writes two bookings inside one `withChangeGroup`. §2: the one new refusal, `החזרה יוצאת לפני ההגעה ליעד`, marked on the return's departure leg. §3: the pair is **derived, not stored** (mirrored route **or** shared confirmation code, nearest in time wins) — so it also catches legs written separately or imported from Gmail, which a `pairId` written by §1's control would miss; it surfaces as one ink (never teal — a sibling is not a location) fact last on `BookingDetail` and one statement, **not a fourth button**, on the delete prompt. §4 is the half [ADR-0136](../decisions/0136-an-event-can-also-be-booked.md) missed: its session-185 amendment found that `EventCategory` has one `transport` where `BookingType` has three, but not that transport also has a different **place shape** — so `EventForm`'s `יש הזמנה` row sends `placeId` with `type: 'flight'`, which `bookings.service.ts`'s `assertPlaceShape` (ADR-0048) **rejects with a 400**. Reproduced, not inferred: the payload is `{ type: 'flight', title: '…', placeId: 'p-nrt' }`. With no place it saves and produces a **route-less** transport booking, which then costs the route title (ADR-0059 §3), the location fact, the map pin, the per-endpoint zones (so a zone-crossing flight renders in the wrong zone), the Plan readiness count, and the ability to re-save it from `BookingSheet` at all (`routeRequired`). The fix: for a booked transport event the place field **is** the route field — the same two `PlacePicker`s, neither required, so ADR-0136's "requires nothing" is intact — plus a **swap** that makes the origin guess safe with one tap and no errand, which `BookingSheet`'s own route field should share rather than copy (rule 8). Carries a live DOM measurement of what the second span costs, the seven-row table of what is broken today and which fix closes each, an explicit **phase 0** (the 400 is a shipped bug whose fix is one conditional already written elsewhere, and it should not wait on any of this), and the boundary: **round trip stays out of `EventForm`** because that form authors one event on the day you are looking at, and the derived pairing is what lets a leg written there pair with a return written in `BookingSheet` anyway. Deliberately unsettled at the time, and both settled in ADR-0154 before the build: the note hosts on the **outbound** (§6), and the control **defaults off** (§4), because the control row costs 44px on every transport booking and the second leg a further 492px that only an explicit tap should buy. **§6 draws the same form stepped** (added 2026-08-01 on the owner's ask, so the round-trip design reviews whole in one file): the §1 proposal and a three-step `ui/primitives/FormSteps` version side by side at 390px with the **80vh fold line drawn** — the scrolled form is 1565px against ~675px visible, so the entire return leg sits below the fold. The steps are the form's own three subjects (which journey · when out · when back + what both share), and the section corrects an under-weighting in `form-steps-v1.html`'s recommendation: this form is a **middle case**, mostly chunking but carrying one genuine cross-step dependency — §2's `החזרה יוצאת לפני ההגעה ליעד` cannot be validated until the outbound leg is answered, which is branching, not layout. The recommendation is unchanged on the primitive (extract it from the two shipped surfaces regardless) and explicitly hands the apply-it-here call to a phone rather than to an argument. _Revisited 2026-08-02 against the notes work (ADR-0152/0153, phases 5-5b, merged after this file was written)._ Phase 5b settled the **principle** behind the note-host question this file had left open: on `EventForm`'s `יש הזמנה` path notes are written to the **booking**, because the server materializes the event from a seed (ADR-0093) and only the booking's id is client-generated — so a note hangs on the host whose id the client holds. Both round-trip bookings have client ids, so it narrows rather than decides; but it exposes a concrete defect, now recorded: `BookingSheet` sets `hostId = created?.id` and hangs the notes there, so two `createBooking` calls would silently put the note on **whichever ran last** (the return), by statement order rather than by decision. The recommendation stays the outbound and now has a basis — it is the journey that happens first and the one §3's derived relation calls the primary. The rule-8 panel also gains the two fresher precedents the notes work shipped: `NOTE_HOST_FIELD` states verbatim the one-line-addition property proposed here for `BOOKING_TYPE_PROFILE`, and `ui/HostNotes.tsx`'s header makes the `RouteField` argument word for word ("`BookingDetail` did it inline first, which was right for one host; documents and ideas would have been the second and third copy").

- [`mockups/car-hire-form-and-ambient-v1.html`](../../mockups/car-hire-form-and-ambient-v1.html) — **השכרת רכב · the form and the day's backdrop** (2026-08-04, session 211), from four owner reports against the build [ADR-0162](../decisions/0162-a-car-hire-is-transport-you-drive-yourself.md) had just shipped — **Promoted and BUILT** (2026-08-04) into [ADR-0163](../decisions/0163-a-hire-is-not-a-journey.md) — §4's owner verdict was **option א**, position with a per-type unit, and §1–§3 shipped as drawn. 0162 gave a hire its own _type_ and stopped there, so it is still **described as a journey**: the generic `RouteField` asks `מוצא`/`יעד` and offers `החלפת כיוון`, and the title derives from those two — which is why a hire taken and returned at one airport is named `נריטה ← נריטה`, and why the day's ambient strip prints a route. **Three of the four reports are one mistake**, and the file's job is to show that: §1 replaces the route field with `איסוף 🔑` plus a same-place/different-place toggle defaulting to SAME (no swap — you cannot reverse a pick-up and a return), §2 adds the provider field, and §3 makes a hire's title its **company**, which is the correction that deletes the route from every surface that receives only a title. **§2 is not a model gap and the file says so:** `Booking.provider` already exists in the schema and `BookingDetail` already renders it as `ספק` — no form has ever written it, for any type, so an airline and a hotel chain are equally unenterable. **The model does not move at all**: `fromPlaceId` stays the pick-up and `toPlaceId` the return, so ADR-0048's route-xor-single invariant and every reader of those columns (map pins, per-end zones) are untouched; the toggle is _derived_ (`same = from === to`). **§4 is the one that needs a decision rather than a fix**, and it is drawn as three options side by side because they answer different questions: **(א)** position with the unit per type (`יום 2 מתוך 5` beside the hotel's `לילה 2 מתוך 4`) — one grammar, and the unit already exists in 0162's `durationUnit` table, so it is a word rather than a component; **(ב)** the return deadline (`עד יום ו׳ 10:00`), which is what actually obliges you but makes the two ambient rows say different kinds of thing in one strip; **(ג)** `הרכב איתנו` as a fact about the day, the most readable and the furthest from the shipped row. **A prerequisite it surfaces rather than proposes:** `stayNight`/`stayNights` exist as **three** hand-copied derivations (`DayView`, `PlanDay`, `Home`), so whichever option wins, that derivation belongs in one place before it gains a per-type unit — the ADR-0096 shape, and the fourth such collapse this area has needed. **Renders through the app's real CSS** (nine shipped stylesheets via the `APP-CSS` manifest), so the ambient strip's teal tint, 12px radius and 11px meta are the shipped rule and not a drawing of it — verified by reading the computed style off the live DOM. Both themes, 360px frames (ADR-0017's narrow end). **What it could not settle, and how it was settled:** the no-company fallback is the **type label** (`השכרת רכב`), because a bare counter name does not say what the row is; whether §4's wording reads on a single-night hire is still a device judgement, and the strip only shows on strictly-middle days so a one-night hire never draws it at all. **Two things the build found that the drawing did not:** the journey-chain save path (`legBooking`) derived each leg's title from its two points, so a hire kept its route title on save until a payload test caught it, and the title-row preview was still showing the `בחרו מוצא ויעד` ghost — the last surface on the form asking a hire for a route.

- [`mockups/form-steps-v1.html`](../../mockups/form-steps-v1.html) — **multi-step forms: should a long form get `הבא`/`הקודם`, and which forms would profit** (2026-08-01; **promoted 2026-08-02 into [ADR-0155](../decisions/0155-a-stepped-form-is-one-primitive-and-it-commits-once.md), Accepted — the extraction approved, applying it to `BookingSheet` deliberately not**). Renders the app's real CSS; in `.prettierignore`. Written to answer a question raised by `booking-round-trip-v1.html` making `BookingSheet` long, and the answer is not the expected one. **The concept already exists in the app twice, unowned, and neither instance is a long form:** `ResolveSheet` and `RowManageSheet`'s `הזז` position step (ADR-0138 §8), both in `screens/PlanDay.tsx`, each hand-rolling step state and each carrying the same `useBackLayer(…, { remainsActive: true })` block with a near-identical comment — the parent-registration rule that is in `frontend/CLAUDE.md` precisely because it had to be got right twice. Those two are the primitive's justification and its first call sites. **But they are BRANCHING steps** (step 2's content depends on step 1's answer — you cannot draw the slots until you know which event moves), where a long form is **CHUNKING** (independent fields split across pages: a booking's code does not depend on its date). Chunking costs three things this app has paid to avoid, all named: [ADR-0150](../decisions/0150-a-form-refuses-at-the-field.md)'s "report every problem in one call" (paging manufactures the second save loop it exists to end), [ADR-0136](../decisions/0136-an-event-can-also-be-booked.md)'s "requires nothing" posture, and the ability to review a hard commitment before signing it. Carries a **head-to-head at 390px with the 80vh fold line drawn** (a sheet shows ~675px of itself on an 844px phone; the round-trip form measures 1570px, 2.3 screens, and the entire return leg falls below the fold) and a **scan of all nine form surfaces** with `<Field>`/control counts from source — of which exactly **one** (`BookingSheet`) is long enough to be a candidate, and only after the round-trip proposal lands, so the proposal would create the app's first form that needs paging. `EventForm` already solves the same problem with `Collapsible` (ADR-0098, four call sites); `CreateTrip` is deliberately minimal (ADR-0032); `MapPlaceForm` explicitly must not grow (ADR-0148 spent a session on its room, and a card that clips cannot host more). States the rule that must live **inside** the primitive or it breaks ADR-0150: a step gate reports every problem in the current step, and the save re-validates everything and **navigates to the first step carrying a problem and marks it**. Recommends: extract `ui/primitives/FormSteps` from the two shipped surfaces (standalone justification, deletes a copy, independent of any round-trip decision); do **not** step `BookingSheet` yet; try `Collapsible` there first. _Revised 2026-08-02 against the notes work (ADR-0152/0153), which lands two hard rules on the primitive and stales three scan rows (corrected: `EventForm` 5 fields/18 controls, `DocumentUploadSheet` 4/10, `DocumentManageSheet` gains `HostNotes`, plus a new `MaybeManageSheet`)._ **(1) `FormSteps` must never animate height.** The obvious step transition animates `max-height` so the sheet does not jump — which is exactly the trap ADR-0152 §6's amendment was written for: `.wp-event-actions` animates `max-height: 0 → 220px`, a fixed cap, clipping notes at about three, and raising it changes the motion of every card (ADR-0140). A step panel now contains a **composer that grows without bound** (a committed note collapses to a chip: `base + N×chip`), so the transition is `translateX`+`opacity` only and the sheet is allowed to resize. **(2) `FormSteps` must commit once, on the last step.** `verbs.create`/`schedule`/`book` now resolve to their created host so notes queue _behind_ it — the outbox is FIFO and a note that overtakes its host is refused — so a per-step "save and continue" would queue a note before its host exists, and would fail only offline. Single-commit is also what keeps ADR-0150's re-validate-everything honest. The composer belongs in the **last** step, with the code and the kind, since all three are shared across both legs. The 1570px measurement is now a **floor**, not a height.

- [`mockups/dark-mode-v1.html`](../../mockups/dark-mode-v1.html) — **dark mode, and what it found in light mode** (2026-08-02, session 209) — **Promoted into [ADR-0158](../decisions/0158-dark-mode-ships-and-the-ink-a-surface-carries-is-a-token.md), Accepted; the build is phased in its §10 and one question is deferred to Hero 2.0.** **Generated, not hand-written** (`mockups/build-dark-mode-v1.mjs`; DOM captured by `mockups/extract-dark-mode-dom.mjs` into `dark-mode-v1.dom.json`, both committed, both in `.prettierignore`). It takes a different approach from `tools/inline-app-css.mjs`: rather than inlining the app's stylesheets into hand-written markup, it reads the CSS from `frontend/src` at build time **and captures the DOM from the running app**, then renders each frame in an isolated `<iframe>` so the app's own `:root`/`body` rules apply exactly as they do in the product. Proposals are applied as CSS **overlays**, never by editing the captured markup, so "shipped" and "proposed" are literally the same screen. That shape exists because the first draft of this file was hand-drawn and the owner's review was that the designs "look outdated or even made up" — this cannot drift and cannot invent. **The premise:** the dark remap was complete and **inert** (`tokens.css` had every value; nothing anywhere set `data-theme`), so it had never been rendered once. Rendering it is the whole session. **Two of the remap's own recorded conventions are wrong.** (1) **On-fill ink** — both stylesheets state "the fill re-maps, the on-fill ink must not", which holds for a fill that keeps its **polarity** (`--amber` stays light, `--indigo` stays dark) and fails for every mid-tone one, since those were brightened for text legibility and white ink then stops working: **ten shipped controls go illegible**, worst `background: var(--ink); color: #fff` on `.choice-pill.on` at **1.20:1**. Fixed by one `--on-fill` pair (`#FFFFFF` light / `#12203A` dark — byte-identical in light) plus `--on-amber`, generalising the `--cta`/`--cta-text` one-off rather than adding a second (rule 8). (2) **`--plan-deep` is remapped as text and used as a fill** by `.prep`, so plan mode renders the **brightest surface in the dark app** (L\* 68) at **2.16:1** ink — inverting mode identity, since trip is meant to be the loud mode. Fixed by giving plan's hero surface tokens the way trip's hero already has `--board`/`--board-2`; an accent brightens on dark, a surface must not. **§4 inverts the expected result:** text contrast fails **four times in the shipped LIGHT theme and zero times in dark** (the remap brightened everything and fixed them by accident), while on-fill ink fails ten times in dark and zero in light — each theme was hiding the other's bug class. Also caught, and only by a real render: `.nav` is `rgba(255,255,255,.92)`, an `rgba()` the hex-only U-08 sweep never looked at, so the tab bar stays white in dark. **§5 answers an owner report** ("the avatar still carries the blue hue") by sweeping every colour declaration on a trip-chrome surface: **53, of which 4 are marked**. They are not wrong — they are correct _given a dark chrome_, and nothing records that dependency. Plan mode already hand-wrote a second copy of the whole set when its chrome went light (`.app[data-mode='plan'] .av { border-color: var(--card) }` is literally the ring the owner spotted), so the fix is a five-name **chrome token contract**, not a third copy — the shape ADRs 0078/0079/0094/0095 all exist to undo. **§6 is the open question:** light mode is ~220px of dark chrome above a dark hero, so it reads half-dark. Four renders establish that the **hero is load-bearing** (lighten it and the chrome inherits the prominence — in light mode darkness is the hero's only mechanism, since amber-as-a-ground was rejected in ADR-0105) and the **chrome** is what looks out of place; four grounds are then drawn and **indigo 12%** recommended, because it keeps the trip hue so mode identity survives on hue rather than luminance. Deliberately **not** shipped with the theming work: it moves a surface ADR-0028 names as mode identity, that ADR-0142 animates and ADR-0143 is choreographed against, and it belongs with the queued **Hero 2.0** line. §7 settles `/login` + `/join` as theme-**fixed** and marked; §8 places the toggle (three rungs, device-local, applied before first paint — a server preference structurally cannot beat the first frame).

- [`mockups/day-gaps-and-layovers-v1.html`](../../mockups/day-gaps-and-layovers-v1.html) — **the day's holes, and the ones inside a journey** (2026-08-02, two rounds the same day) — the **first mockup drawn in both themes** under the rule above. Two questions that turn out to be one slot: Trip mode's day list draws nothing between two events, while Plan mode draws the same gap as a violet `שבץ` **control** — so Trip takes the same slot (same flex row, same dashed hairline, same 9px margin) with the control removed, a `<span>` where Plan has a `<button>`, `--faint` and no hue.
