It reuses Plan's own `gapBetween` derivation and its `GAP_MIN_MINUTES` floor (the question "is there a hole here" is the same in both modes; only what you can do about it differs), but reads the length through `hoursPhrase` rather than Plan's rounding `gapLabel` — a statement has to be a measurement (`2:40`, not `שעתיים`). **§3 is the layover, and it is where round one failed**: v1 drew a dotted rail in the badge column, and the owner read it on a phone — a rail is a connector, so it has to touch, and that one kept the list's 9px rhythm on both sides and was cut in half the moment a now-line landed between the legs (the normal on-the-ground state). Round two draws three candidates and recommends **the journey block**: the two legs go _inside_ one container with a full-width amber band between them, so nothing is being asked to sit between two cards — there is one object with parts, which is what a connection is, and a now-line becomes a row of it. The band ignores the gap floor: §3c's **12-minute train change at Kyoto** is the case that settles why (a connection is presence, a gap is absence), and it also shows that none of this is flight-specific — everything keys off `carriesRoute`, true for `train` and `transit` since ADR-0156, with one word differing (`החלפה` vs `עצירת ביניים`). §4/§4b are the authoring half: a **stop** on `RouteField` (whose `pp-clear` is also its remove — an empty stop is not a state — and whose swap stays put and reverses the whole sequence), and **a leg is a step** in the already-stepped form (ADR-0155): `מה ואיפה` → `מתי · קטע 1` → `מתי · קטע 2` → `פרטים`, with the cross-leg refusal (a departure before the previous arrival) landing on the later leg's departure — the cross-step dependency ADR-0155 §5 calls the strongest argument for stepping. Rejected in the file, with reasons: the v1 rail (kept drawn, corrected, so the comparison is honest), an amber pill on a line (that is `.nowline`), a crow-flies distance in the strip (no routing by decision, ADR-0109 §7), a live countdown, edge strips, and a second ✕ on a stop. **Promoted and BUILT the same day into [ADR-0159](../decisions/0159-the-day-says-what-is-between-two-events.md)**, which settled the two open numbers per transport mode (24h/90min for a flight, 6h/20min for a train or bus) and left one thing open: legs that fall on **different days**, where the block has no adjacency to draw from.

- [`mockups/day-scheduling-grammar-v1.html`](../../mockups/day-scheduling-grammar-v1.html) — **the day's scheduling grammar: a move names a position, an event owns its length** (2026-08-04) — the design reference for [ADR-0161](../decisions/0161-a-move-names-a-position-and-an-event-owns-its-length.md), drawn in both themes. Four owner reports read together, three of which are one defect: a drag "replaces their timing" (swap a 1-hour event with a 2-hour one and each inherits the **other's** length), `שבץ` is inconvenient about the time, `החלף` is confusing, and elementary day-scheduling verbs are missing. **§1 is the whole argument in one pair of frames**: the app already disagrees with itself inside one gesture — `planReorder` permutes `{startsAt, endsAt, sortOrder}` **triples**, so a drop on a row trades lengths, while a drop on a **gap** goes through `slotFor`, whose docblock says the opposite in as many words ("keeps the length it already had"). One finger, two semantics, decided by what is under the pointer. **§2 draws the missing target rather than a missing feature**: you can only drop _between_ two rows where a ≥ 60-minute gap happens to exist, so the seam is that gap chip generalised to zero minutes — measured here at **18px while a drag is live and 0px at rest**, which is what lets it exist between every pair without crowding the list (five seams add 90px, and only while a finger is down). The frames also show the case that motivates it: the seams either side of a **hard** flight, which is not itself a drag source or a swap target (ADR-0011, unchanged). **§3 leans on chrome the app already has** — a collision after an unequal swap renders as ADR-0041's violet cluster with its seam tag and `הזז`, because the two alternatives both move rows nobody touched (absorbing the excess silently spends a gap left on purpose; shifting the tail moves dinner because you sorted the morning). **§4 is a generalisation, not an addition**: `ResolveSheet`'s before/after options and `BuilderRow`'s `הזז` peer list each already answer "where in the day" and neither is reusable, so the picker arrives with `.resolve-opt`'s exact geometry and adds only the free-time note and the `עכשיו` row — every row **shows the clock it computes**, at a measured 44px. **§6 is the section the owner rejected and it is drawn both ways**, because the rejected version is the argument for the kept one: the first draft put all five new verbs in the row's `⋯` sheet and rendered as **eight rows that scroll** (543px of a 640px screen), `מחק` below the fold, and `משך`/`דחה את שאר היום` colliding on one `clock` glyph — _"too much actions and it's becoming overwhelming instead of easy to use"_. What replaced it is one affordance instead of five rows: **the row's own time becomes a button** (`PlaceBadge`'s idiom one slot over, ADR-0121 §8), so the menu keeps its length at four rows and every element of a row does what it depicts. The frames also carry the measurement that decided the chip's shape — a `min-height: 44px` took the row from **58px to 75px**, so the chip keeps its natural **39px** and the target is a **55px** inset overlay: measured cost **+2px** at 360 and **+3px** at 390 of row height, and **16px** off the title, against the **58px** that killed a separate trailing control in ADR-0121 §8. An untimed row, which holds nothing at all today, gets `＋ שעה` in the same slot. A **third** round then took `שכפל` out of that menu too — _"is duplicate event that important to be one of only four actions? How does it work anyway?"_ — leaving the sheet **one row shorter than it is today** (322px → 267px), which is the real check on whether §6 is placement or hoarding. **§7 is the drag clone**, from a report in the same message (_"it should be somewhat transparent … it's hard to see where you're landing and read the controls"_) and it turns out to be three omissions rather than one: `opacity: 1`, `transform: scale(1.04)` (the clone is **larger** than the row it vacated, eating the very 18px §2 puts the seams in), and it sits **on** the finger so the target under the pointer is hidden by construction — while `.bld.dragging` fades the source to 0.45, so today the real row looks like the ghost. The lift is the fix that answers the report; both of its numbers are **toggles in the file, not decisions** (0.85 · 0.78 · 0.65 and 0 · 12 · 20), because "somewhat transparent" is judged with a finger on the thing. **§5 shows one component with two headers** (`מילוי הפער` / `החלפה`), since "which idea fits this slot" is what `GapFillSheet` has always been. **§7 is the ADR-0159 §1 amendment**, drawn as a before|after so the claim is checkable: the Trip-mode gap keeps `hoursPhrase`'s measurement word for word, gains a trailing `＋` and a 44px target, and takes **none** of Plan's violet or its `שבץ`. **One shipped defect this file found by being rendered:** `t.planDay.gapFillTitle` builds `מילוי הפער · ${start}–${end}` with no ltr isolate, so the shipped sheet's header reads **`18:00–15:00`** for a 15:00–18:00 gap — ADR-0118's rule, invisible to its lint guard because the guard reads `dir="ltr"` attributes and this is a template string in `he.ts`. **Renders through the app's real CSS** (ten shipped stylesheets inlined by [`mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs`](../../mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs); re-run after touching any of them, and the file is in `.prettierignore` so formatting cannot fight the generator). The only hand-written app CSS in it is the **proposed** block — `.bld-seam*`, `.slotpick-*`, `button.day-gap` — named `.bld-seam` and not `.seam` because `.seam-tag` already means the overlap tag on a cluster member. **Deliberately undrawn:** a proportional time-height day (unreadable at 360px, and the reason the list is ordinal at all), drag-to-resize (it needs that axis), and auto-arranging the day. **What it cannot settle:** whether an 18px seam is findable under a thumb on a real device and whether the seam labels read as promises or as noise mid-drag — both ADR-0017 device-pass questions. **§9 is not ADR-0161's**: two cheap wins for a workflow reported separately (thirty places added to the map, then wanting to slot them), taken as amendments to the ADRs that own the rules — an idea reaching its own pin (ADR-0121 §8's badge was on every host except the entity most likely to BE a place) and a `fits-a-day` suggestion so a dateless idea names the day it belongs to (ADR-0151's second strategy, which is the registry claim its §2 made, finally tested). Its value is the **third** measurement in the file, which took three attempts and was wrong twice: whether `יום 4 · 300 מ׳ מהמוזיאון` fits the 140×76 tile. With a short title the `min-height` floor absorbs the wrap and it reads as free; with all three cards in one `.shelf` a flex row stretches them to the tallest and reports three identical numbers — session 203's amendment #1 from the other side, and the rule it yields is **a strip is not a neutral place to measure a card**. Measured properly (long title, one strip per case) it is **76px → 84px**, exactly the 8px ADR-0151's own earlier amendment refused. So the stop name stays in the sheet, and so does the verb, since session 203 removed the tile's action line. State toggles: ערכת צבעים (בהיר · כהה) · מסך (360×640 · 390×844) · §7's ghost opacity (0.85 · 0.78 · 0.65) and lift (0 · 12 · 20px).
- [`mockups/place-enrichment-v2.html`](../../mockups/place-enrichment-v2.html) — **enrichment on the REAL card: what v1 forgot** (2026-08-05, session 214) — **Promoted into [ADR-0167 §9](../decisions/0167-the-badge-is-the-thumbnails-frame.md), which supersedes its own §3 for a committed place.** [`v1`](../../mockups/place-enrichment-v1.html) was **rejected by the owner as incomplete**, correctly: it drew a stripped-down row, while the shipped selection card carries an order counter, a lock, a rename, three meta tags, a notes section with its own header and list, one or two reference rows with their settle pairs, `שיבוץ ליום` and a delete — and is a **grid with exactly one scrolling track**, four of five rows pinned by the owner's rule quoted in `map.css` (_"only the notes themselves should be scrollable, everything else is locked"_). v1's 132px hero plus 3-line summary on **that** card measures ~538px, **64% of the screen**, which is the number that killed it. **§0 draws the card as it ships** precisely so the diff is visible rather than asserted. The owner's brief for the redesign was explicit — the summary must be included and _very easy to reach_ (1–2 lines, expandable), rearranging existing things is fair game, _"I'm not afraid of drastic changes, the app is not GA yet"_ — and the answer is two rearrangements rather than a corner: **hours ride the existing wrapping meta line** (`פתוח עד 17:00` beside the time and the area), which **measures 0px** because the meta line is 17px with or without it, where §7's own pinned line had been costing 19px when it fitted and **43px when the freshness tail wrapped**; and that buys **the summary a pinned two-line block under the identity**, measured at **64px collapsed and 108px expanded**, with the extra 44px borrowed from the **notes scroller** (the flexible track), which still holds 114px at a 420px cap. **The principle the file argues, and the reason the two surfaces diverge: enrichment is for DECIDING, and once you have decided it compresses.** §2 draws the deciding card — a Google result with no notes, no references and no schedule action, so the hero and summary have the room they are actually for (361px) — beside the majority case, a restaurant we know nothing about whose entire content is `עוד בגוגל` (112px). The committed card therefore gets **no hero at all**: the badge already carries the photograph at zero cost, and 132px of picture on a place you have already chosen is the least valuable block on a capped card. **The rejected alternative is drawn too** (summary _inside_ the scroller, section renamed `מה ידוע`): it costs no pinned height but makes the group's own writing share a region with fetched text, on a surface the owner specified personally — so the pinned block wins on that boundary rather than on pixels. **One defect it caught is a specificity fight, not a layout one:** `.summary`'s `-webkit-line-clamp: 3` silently overrode the two-line clamp because it is declared later, so the "two-line" block rendered three lines and measured 20px more than designed — a clamp that varies by state belongs on the compound selector. Drawn **dark only** (the card lives over the canvas, which is where the owner's screenshot came from) and **hand-written CSS, not the app's** — so re-check `.map-placecard`'s grid, `.note-sec` and `.map-refs` against the shipped stylesheet before building. Photographs are synthetic at real aspect ratios; whether one is legible at 40px is still the device pass.
- [`mockups/place-enrichment-v1.html`](../../mockups/place-enrichment-v1.html) — **a place says more: the thumbnail, the credit, the language marker and the way through** (2026-08-05, session 214) — **Promoted into [ADR-0167](../decisions/0167-the-badge-is-the-thumbnails-frame.md) (Accepted; design only, nothing built).** The design pass [ADR-0166](../decisions/0166-place-enrichment-is-a-multi-source-pipe.md) §10 gated Phase 1 on, answering all four questions it accumulated (thumbnail slot, attribution slot, the English-summary marker from §11.5, the Google way-through from §13). **Every name, summary, credit, opening-hours string and aspect ratio is real data from the [coverage spike](../planning/2026-08-04-session-213-place-enrichment-coverage-spike.md)** — 43 places, measured the day before — which is what stopped this being the mistake ADR-0115 §2 recorded (`plan-mode-v1.html` drew `★ 4.5` and `1.2 ק״מ` against a pipe that returns neither). **The photographs are the one thing that is not real:** the session could not reach `upload.wikimedia.org`, so each image is a CSS gradient at the **real file's ratio**, composed sky-above-structure so a crop's damage shows. Crop geometry and density are therefore honest; whether a real photo is legible at 40px is the single question the file cannot answer, and it is a device pass. **The design's whole claim is that the thumbnail needs no slot** — the row already leads with a 40×40 `r12` badge that is always filled and is already the way to the pin, so a photo fills its interior and the category hue moves from fill to ring; rows without an image (the majority, since Tokyo restaurants scored **0 of 7**) look exactly as they do today, which is what keeps the list from going ragged. **Two defects it produced by being rendered, both of which would have shipped:** the hue ring **did not survive the photo** — an `inset` box-shadow paints above the background and _below_ the children, so the image covered it and the badge silently lost its category, needing an overlay `::after` instead (plus a hairline, so a dark ring holds against a bright sky); and the credit line **orphaned itself to the wrong edge** — it is Latin, so `dir="auto"` made the whole element LTR and aligned it _left_ while every other line in the RTL card sits right, detached from the image it credits. That second one is the **mirror** of the bug [ADR-0118](../decisions/0118-numbers-in-hebrew-bidi.md) exists for, invisible to its lint guard (which reads `dir="ltr"` attributes), and it will recur on every enriched Latin string. **One measurement changed a decision and one confirmed the claim:** sweeping the hero 132 → 96px moves the card only 392 → 356px, because the height is five stacked elements and not the image — so the hero looked like the lever, is not, and keeps its size for 36px; and collapsed rows measure **69–71px**, so the badge-as-frame genuinely costs nothing. Other numbers the build inherits: a fully enriched card is **392px, 46% of a 390×844 viewport**, the empty one **134px** with a 44px refs block, and there is no horizontal overflow at 390. **§3 is the section to read first**, because it draws the majority case rather than the happy one: a restaurant we know nothing about, whose entire reveal is `עוד בגוגל` — the free deep link ADR-0166 §13 chose over a paid API fallback — with nothing drawn where a summary would be (ADR-0109 §7). **Drawn in both themes**, and dark is where the credit line earns its placement: it sits on the card under the image rather than overlaid on it, so it never fights a bright sky and needs no second solution when the surface inverts (ADR-0158 §15). The embedded Japanese run inside the Hebrew summary orders correctly under the browser's own bidi algorithm — worth checking, since an enriched summary is third-party prose that can carry any script. **Hand-written CSS, not the app's** (it does not go through `inline-app-css.mjs`), so the row geometry is a faithful copy of `screens/map.css` rather than the real thing — re-check `.place`, `.map-badge` and `.map-refs` against the shipped stylesheet before building.
- [`mockups/hero-lift-v1.html`](../../mockups/hero-lift-v1.html) — **Hero 2.0 · the lift, its three characters, and the answer to an empty tap** (2026-08-03, session 210) — **Promoted into [ADR-0160](../decisions/0160-the-hero-lifts-and-shows-a-horizon.md) (Accepted; design only, nothing built yet).** The first pass on the [Hero 2.0 brief](../planning/2026-07-28-hero-2-0-design-brief.md), whose backlog line has been open since session 148. It answers the brief's own "load-bearing" question with a **third word**: the owner's hero is not an expansion (a pane that grows and reflows Home under the thumb) and not the overlay the brief drew (a second surface rendering the same facts twice, which throws away the glance value the board exists for) — it is a **promotion**, one object gaining elevation. Three characters for that lift are drawn and none is chosen here: **א׳ הפתיחה** anchors its top edge and leaves the chrome alone, **ב׳ ההרמה** travels up over the chrome to the top of the screen, **ג׳ ההטיה** adds a 9°/46px swing upright toward the viewer on top of B's box animation. **Read this entry before drawing any motion set piece on a surface you have not measured: the panel rewrote two of the three characters.** Written from reasoning, B rose "to just under the chrome" — which measured **4px**, because the board is the first thing on Home, so B was A with extra steps and there is nowhere to rise to that is not _over_ the chrome; that promotes B from a tuning value into a real decision, since the chrome is where mode identity lives (ADR-0028) and [ADR-0158](../decisions/0158-dark-mode-ships-and-the-ink-a-surface-carries-is-a-token.md) §12 has just given light mode its own band. And C was a scale-up, which measured **×1.045** — the board is already near-full-width (358 → 374px), so a width-keyed FLIP has no scale in it to spend and the lift's whole visible budget is **height (×2.01) and elevation**; a literal "toward the eyes" therefore had to become a 3D swing. Neither was reachable without the numbers. Four more things it settles or surfaces: the lifted hero is still a **back layer** through `Modal`/`useOverlay` (ADR-0103's amendments plus [ADR-0090](../decisions/0090-back-is-computed-from-nav-state.md) leave no choice — what is rejected is the sheet's _grammar_, never the back contract, and `SnapSheet`'s register-nothing model would have left the tab on a system back with the hero up); `--t-cinematic` is **not available** (ADR-0140's budget rule reserves the app's one cinematic moment for Plan→Trip, so all three are drawn at `--t-base` with `--t-deliberate` on a toggle); the **rebuff** the owner asked for borrows three properties from `.is-nudging` (one shot, `linear` because the keyframe offsets _are_ the timing, symmetric so no `--dir`) and none of its gesture or its `--miss`, because a sideways shake means "you did something wrong" and nothing is wrong — and its trigger is **derived, not a variant check**, which §3c proves by rebuffing a perfectly valid `now` board whose event has no note, place, code or concurrent sibling; and the **settle control does not survive this ground as shipped** — `.wp-settle-btn` is `background: var(--card)` with `color: var(--ink)`, so on the board it is a paper chip in light mode and invisible in dark, which is ADR-0158 §15's "an inverted surface is the limit" arriving on a new surface. §4 draws the fix in both themes, and it invents nothing: the `board` density is the recipe `.wp-board-countdown`/`.code`/`.tlabel` already use, a low-alpha semantic fill with a brightened ink, so ADR-0139's rule holds (a fourth host adds a **density**; the words, marks and hues are not its to choose). **Also decided in the same round, and drawn in §2:** the exit is not the entrance reversed (`--ease-arrive` is scoped to entrances, and `--ease-exit` already accelerates into its end, which is the curve of something being set down), so what was left to pick was whether the return has a _landing_ — three candidates at three durations, with **ג׳ הנחה** recommended: the drop plus one 2.5% single-axis beat on the board that comes back, so the object visibly touches down instead of the flight merely stopping. That beat makes **three** one-shot answer classes in the app (`.is-nudging` when a form refuses, `.is-rebuffing` when a tap has nothing to open, `.is-landing` when a lifted surface touches down), all sharing the same discipline — one shot removed at `animationend`, `linear` because the offsets are the timing, symmetric so no `--dir`, duration from a token — which is one shared primitive rather than a third copy, on ADR-0139's own precedent. **§3א is chosen for the rebuff: the motion alone, no text** (the owner moved off §3ב's one-shot line — a line that appears and disappears on the app's loudest surface reads as a scolding). Three things fall out of that: no frequency question survives into the build, no new copy or translation exists, and the answer stays in the channel the question was asked in — you touched the surface, the surface moved. **One defect this file shipped and fixed:** v1 painted the lifted hero over Home the whole time, because the measuring pass writes a box and clears its own `visibility` — so measurement left the thing it measured on screen, `✕` and all. A closed layer is now `visibility: hidden` (not `display`, because the hero must stay measurable while closed — that is how its landing box is read off real content instead of written as a constant). The expanded hero's **content** is indicative only — the horizon's own questions, and what the `in-transit` / `group-split` / `free` variants each carry, are the next file's subject. Every pixel number in it is read off the live DOM and is **webfont-dependent**: re-measure on a device with the real fonts before any of it enters an ADR.

- [`mockups/hero-horizon-v1.html`](../../mockups/hero-horizon-v1.html) — **Hero 2.0 · what the lifted hero carries** (2026-08-03, session 210) — **Promoted into [ADR-0160](../decisions/0160-the-hero-lifts-and-shows-a-horizon.md) (Accepted; design only, nothing built yet).** The companion to [`hero-lift-v1`](../../mockups/hero-lift-v1.html), which owns the motion. Where that file decided _how_ the hero lifts, this one decides _what is worth lifting_, and answers the brief's sub-question 1 with a shape rather than a list: **the collapsed board shows two POINTS, the lifted hero shows a HORIZON with depth on each point** (`עכשיו → ועוד עכשיו → הבא בתור → אחר כך`). Only three things pass the "worth an interaction, not a reflow" test, because everything else is already on the collapsed board: **איפה** (the board has never carried a place at all — no name, no way to the pin, no Maps hand-off, which is exactly the affordance ADR-0121's 2026-07-28 amendment §4 backed off the _collapsed_ board for reading too loud), **פתק** (the note the group wrote, which the notes work handed here on purpose — ADR-0152/0153, and the notes brief's §C3 put no mark on the Board so this session could), and **הסדרה**. **Every hero is drawn at rest** — no lift runs — which is what lets four variants and four load levels sit side by side. **Read this entry before assuming a new tap target is free: §4 is a condition, not a tidy-up.** The brief's sub-question 4 guessed the board's two expanding things "most likely become one"; it is forced. If the whole board is the tap target it is a `<button>`, and `.wp-board-also-toggle` is a `<button>` inside it — and the file's first pass drew that nesting, at which point Chrome **closed the board at the nested button and reparented everything after it**, dropping the divider, `הבא בתור` and the day rail onto the page background in dark ink. A detached-tree probe in the panel now reports it as a number (**1 of 4 children left inside the board**), and the "before" frame is the board exactly as shipped today, so the section stays readable. After this the collapsed board has **no interactive child at all**, and the count survives as a readout because it must stay legible without a tap. Two per-variant rules the ADRs forced: `in-transit` **drops the settle verbs** — not a density question but a nonsense one, since a flight you are sitting inside settles itself by landing — and drops the day rail (ADR-0059 §2); and `group-split` **cannot collapse one of its equals**, because ADR-0041 §6 gives that variant its own existence precisely on there being _no_ primary, so expanding one row would manufacture the primary the variant denies. `free` does not lift, and the reason is not that it is empty: `GlanceCard` is already Home's "what could we do" surface two inches lower, so a hero lifting into the shelf would compete with a shipped one. **What the measurement settled:** the fit question, which is the one that could have made "lift" a euphemism for "second screen". At 390×844 (826px of room) everything fits except a three-way split (9px over); at 360×640 (622px) the thin/typical/full cases fit and the dense ones do not — heavy by 72px, a **two-way** split by 92px, three-way by 255px. So the common case is not a scroller and the group split is, on a small phone, in its own commonest form. Two questions it deliberately leaves open with the numbers attached: whether `אחר כך` earns its 28px (kept, on one condition recorded in §3 — one line, no place, no note, no control, no way in; the moment someone asks to add a hand-off to it, that is the request to turn the hero into the Day tab, and the answer is the tab), and whether the hero should size to its **content** or to the **screen** — a `% מהמסך` column shows `בטיסה` claiming 49%, so a content-sized hero leaves the backdrop nearly empty in the thin cases, and 415px vs 826px is the difference between "a card that was lifted" and "a screen". **Both were then decided (owner, same session) and are in ADR-0160 §12 and §8: `אחר כך` is kept on a stated condition, and the hero is content-sized** — a screen-sized one would put ~400px of empty dark board under a flight, and empty space on the app's one glowing surface reads as a load failure. Copy is read off `i18n/he.ts` rather than paraphrased (`הבא בתור`, `פנוי`/`זמן חופשי`), which also corrected two strings in the lift file. Pixel numbers are webfont-dependent — re-measure on a device before any of them enter an ADR. **§4 SHIPPED (2026-08-03, phase 1):** `.wp-board-also-read` and `.wp-board.is-tappable` now live in `ui/domain/board.css` and reach this file through the `APP-CSS` block, so the proposal no longer declares them and §4's right-hand frame is drawn by the app rather than by the mockup — which is what made it a check on the build rather than a picture of it. One consequence worth knowing before editing §4: its **left** frame draws `.wp-board-also-toggle`, a rule phase 1 **deleted**, so that block is kept locally and labelled as a historical record. Without it the before-frame renders a bare UA `<button>` and reads as broken instead of as the thing being replaced.

- [`mockups/hero-in-transit-v1.html`](../../mockups/hero-in-transit-v1.html) — **בטיסה · the lifted hero in transit** (2026-08-05, session 215) — **every recommendation was accepted and BUILT the same session; promoted into [ADR-0160](../decisions/0160-the-hero-lifts-and-shows-a-horizon.md)'s session-215 amendment (§K-§O) plus [ADR-0059](../decisions/0059-booking-presentation-on-home-and-index.md)'s. Read it for the measurements and the rejected options; the shipped behaviour is the ADRs'.** Four reports off a real device mid-flight (FRA→TLV) against the shipped [ADR-0160](../decisions/0160-the-hero-lifts-and-shows-a-horizon.md) hero — a `ניווט` chip on its own line, a progress rail that reads as the NEXT event's, no time-to-arrival anywhere, and the owner's own observation that the **collapsed** in-transit board reads better than the lifted one — plus two widenings from the owner: the same must hold for a train / bus / ferry but **not** for a rental car, and the zone shift could be said in words (`מזיזים את השעון שעה קדימה`). **Read this entry before treating any of the four as cosmetic: they are one structural cause.** `HeroLift` has no `in-transit` variant at all — it prints `עכשיו` with the amber blip where the board prints `בטיסה` with the teal one, labels the flight `קשיח` + `עד 22:15` where the board says `כרגע · בדרך` + a teal `נחיתה` chip, and receives the flight's rail as `foot`, which pins it BELOW the `הבא בתור` block. So report 2 is what that misplacement reads as (measured: the rail sits **258px** under the route it describes, against **36px** when it moves inside the point) and report 4 is the owner noticing the promotion dropped the grammar the collapsed state kept. **What measurement changed, and this is the file's own lesson twice over:** the `ניווט` wrap is not a near miss and not about that button — `.hero-row` is `flex-wrap: wrap`, and flex line-breaking uses HYPOTHETICAL sizes, so the decision is made before `flex-shrink` runs and the place name's `min-width: 0` + `text-overflow: ellipsis` are unreachable code in that row. The name wants **247px**, the two chips **153px**, the row has **308px** (360px phone) / **338px** (390px) — 70–100px short, and the failure looks like two different bugs by width (at 390px the name and `במפה` share line 1 and `ניווט` drops alone; at 360px the name takes line 1 and BOTH chips drop). `להזמנה` is a separate `hero-part` on top of that, so the screenshot's three stacked chip lines are two defects. The way out is the same measurement read forwards: the three chips TOGETHER are 247px, so **one action row under the name** fits the small phone with 61px spare (§1ג, recommended). The second lesson is in the panel: the first wrap detector grouped a row's children by rounded `top`, which counts a 15px name beside a 34px chip as two lines — it reported 3 lines where there were 2 and 2 where there was 1. Items on one flex line share a line BOX, not a top edge; the detector now clusters by vertical centre, and the wrong version's note is kept in the script. **What each section recommends:** §1ג the name on its own line + one action row; §2א the rail moves INSIDE the flight's point (the foot is for what belongs to the whole card, i.e. the day rail); §3 an inventory of every fact the app can honestly derive **with no network** — remaining/arrival/clock-jump IN, elapsed-and-percent and total duration LATER, and gate / seat / belt / delay / weather **OUT because the data does not exist** (`bookingSchema` has no seat field), which is rule 5 held to on the one surface where breaking it would matter most; §3ד the rail's middle slot stops printing `22:15` a second time (the end already does) and says `נותרו 1:39 שע׳` instead — free, and it improves the COLLAPSED board where most of a flight is read; §3ב-clock the shift in words plus the destination's clock now, in the lift only, with the pill left on the collapsed board (direction-signed: `+` is `קדימה`, `−` is `אחורה`, and a fractional zone falls back to the ladder's `2:30 שע׳` rather than inventing `וחצי`); §4 the lifted hero rebuilt as the collapsed grammar plus depth. **Two defects the file found that no report mentioned:** `t.board.inTransitLive` is the literal `בטיסה` and `TransitProgress` hardcodes `Icon name="flight"`, but `in-transit` fires for ANY bracketed transport between its ends — so a train reads `בטיסה` with a plane crossing its rail, and a **same-day car hire** reaches the same state (a multi-day one is ambient and goes to the stay strip). The fix needs no new table and no new SVG — the travelling mark is the event's **own glyph** (the board already holds it as `nowIcon`; the app has no `train`/`bus` icon to reach for anyway) and the live word comes off the same `ICON_TIME_PROFILE` that already gives a flight `המראה`/`נחיתה` and a hire `איסוף הרכב`/`החזרת הרכב`. §5 draws all four modes and takes the hire out of the journey grammar entirely — a held resource with a return deadline, which is [ADR-0163](../decisions/0163-a-hire-is-not-a-journey.md) §4's rule (**the verb and the unit belong to the span's own mode**) one surface over. And `איפה` in transit resolves to the flight's **own** place, i.e. the airport you took off from, so today's lifted hero offers `ניווט` backwards; mid-journey the only useful pin is the destination. **Fit:** the whole composition is **590px** — 71% of the 826px a 390×844 phone leaves, and 32px inside the 622px of a 360×640 — so nothing here turns the content-sized hero (ADR-0160 §8) into a scroller. **Left open on purpose:** `הבא בתור`'s countdown counts from the clock, so mid-flight it counts through the landing when the question is "how long after we land" — a change to what the board's countdown MEANS, not to this variant's layout. Every frame renders the app's real CSS through the `APP-CSS` manifest and every number under a frame is computed in-page; the pixel values are webfont-dependent, so re-measure on a device before any of them enters an ADR.
- [`mockups/place-pin-thumbnail-v1.html`](../../mockups/place-pin-thumbnail-v1.html) — **הסיכה נושאת את התמונה · the pin carries the thumbnail** (2026-08-05, after the enrichment surface shipped) — **Promoted; the owner took B, and B is built.** Answers the owner's fourth report on the live enrichment build: _"Map pins should also show the thumbnail (think how to do this aesthetically)"_. [ADR-0167](../decisions/0167-the-badge-is-the-thumbnails-frame.md) §1 made the badge the thumbnail's frame in the LIST and [ADR-0109](../decisions/0109-map-tab-design.md) §3 calls the pin "the badge on the canvas", so the canvas is the missing half. Draws three treatments against the app's **real** pin CSS (inlined, `APP-CSS` manifest) at the two sizes `pinHeightFor()` actually produces: **A** the photo fills the teardrop head with the category hue moving to a ring (the badge's own move), **B** A gated on the size the canvas resolves — photo at the map extreme, glyph at `half` — and **C** a second silhouette for photographed places (the badge's rounded square with a hue pointer). **Measured off the live DOM: A buys 21px of photograph at a 34px pin and 35px at 56px** (the list badge's interior is ~36px, so the map extreme is the size §1 already accepted a photo at, and `half` is a texture rather than a subject); **C buys 31% more** at the same pin height and costs the canvas its one silhouette. Two corrections the render forced, both recorded in the file because the paper version had them wrong: the head is **rotated 45°, so its client rect is √2 too big** (the first pass reported 28px where it is 21px — measure `offsetWidth`), and **a board mixing 34px and 56px pins is a state the app cannot be in**, since pin size is a share of the canvas (ADR-0123) and therefore per-STOP, not per-pin. The file's recommendation was **B** — one treatment, drawn where a photograph can be read — with the glyph keeping the `half` strip, where it carries more meaning than 21px of texture does; the owner took it (_"definitely not C, leaning B"_) and it shipped as a **`@container` height query** on the pane, so the gate costs no prop and no marker re-diff.
- [`mockups/a-time-without-a-position-v1.html`](../../mockups/a-time-without-a-position-v1.html) — **זמן בלי מקום ביום · a time without a position in the day** (2026-08-07, session 220) — drawn **against** an ADR, not for one: the owner's counter-example to [ADR-0171](../decisions/0171-a-time-can-be-a-floor-or-a-ceiling.md) §4, which that rule does not survive. `צ׳ק-אין מ-15:00 · נחיתה 21:00 · איסוף רכב 22:00`, and _"in practice we check in only AFTER the car rental… in other cases maybe we'll actually check in as soon as possible"_. §4 slides a `not-before` edge past the last **journey** that ends after its floor, and a car hire is `held`, not a journey ([ADR-0163](../decisions/0163-a-hire-is-not-a-journey.md) §4) — so it lands the check-in after the flight and **before** the car. **No repair helps**, because the two sentences describe the same data with two different answers, which is the finding: the premise to drop is that the edge has a position at all. So five treatments, each drawn against the **same three days** (the counter-example, an ordinary day nobody complained about, and 11 September) — because a treatment that reads well on one is not an answer, and a `.pair` cannot show that. **א** is today's instant interleave, right on exactly one of the three and therefore not a rule but a coincidence. **ב** is §4, kept drawn because a file without the side that was killed is not a comparison — and its middle column carries the distinction that would otherwise blur, since §3's `מ-15:00` wording applies whether or not the row moved. **ג** takes the edge out of the list into `.day-ambient`, the backdrop a multi-night stay's **middle** days already render as, so it costs no new grammar. **ד** leaves it a row and puts one dashed hairline above it reading `ללא מיקום ביום` — the `.day-gap` line saying the other thing a line between rows can say — reusing the tail untimed events already occupy ([ADR-0161](../decisions/0161-a-move-names-a-position-and-an-event-owns-its-length.md) §10). **And `ג+ד` is the frame the session actually landed on**, after the owner rejected the file's first conclusion: having generalized to one class, it had folded the class into one **category on screen**, which collapses `hard` and `soft` — the one axis [ADR-0011](../decisions/0011-hard-soft-event-model.md) forbids collapsing, and the axis this ADR's own §1 promises not to. So: **one derivation, two placements** — a commitment with no position (a check-in from 15:00, and the untimed hard booking drawn in the first column) reads at the **top** in the strip, an idea with no position stays in the tail. The strip's rows carry a `SettleControl` at a fourth density, because ADR-0164 counts a check-in in `נותרו היום` until it is settled and a host that cannot be ticked leaves that number stuck all evening. **ה** is the only one that can answer _when_, because it is the only one that stops asking one number to be two facts: the floor belongs to the **reservation**, the position to the **day**, and a human sets the second through the slot picker §4 of 0161 already built — and its cost is stated on the frame rather than buried, since [ADR-0047](../decisions/0047-booking-event-linkage-and-notes.md) §1 makes the Event the sole time authority, so a second number needs a nullable column and a migration. **§6-§8 are the owner's other half, and they grew a rule of their own.** A number is an ordering claim, so a stop with no position takes none — the slot stays for alignment, the mark leaves. **§7** then separates the one flexible meaning that keeps a position: a floor is open on the side you act (any time after 15:00, so the app would have to guess which), a ceiling is closed on it (before 11:00, full stop), so a deadline **intersects** with the day's first departure — two hard bounds, the earlier wins — and its second column shows the frames going identical when there is nothing to intersect against. **§8 is the owner's Iceland case**, which splits two claims the file had been treating as one: check out of Reykjavik by 11:00, fly, land in Tel Aviv, and numbering from the ceiling reads Keflavik → Ben Gurion → **the Reykjavik hotel**. The intersect earns the list position and cannot earn the number, because on a day with no departure to intersect against the order is simply unknown. Hence the stronger rule: **a stop number is only ever the index of a moment the app actually knows.** Which also catches a shipped defect nobody reported — an untimed event's place is numbered today (`place-usage.ts` gives it `prominence: 'edge'` with `at: undefined`, so `hasScheduleSlot` passes). **Three measurements read from the live DOM** at 360: `ד`'s line is **13px**, the whole of what it adds to a day; `ה`'s second number doubles the time slot **15px → 30px** and the row does **not** grow, because a transition row's height comes from its badge and not its text — what it does cost is **8px** of title width (38 → 46). **Renders through the app's real CSS** (eight shipped stylesheets inlined by [`mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs`](../../mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs); `screens.css` carries both hosts the file argues about, `.transition-row` and `.day-ambient`, so neither side is drawn from hand-copied values). The only proposed app CSS is `.day-unplaced` and one `.tr-floor` rule, both marked as such in the file. **Nothing here is decided** — the file exists so the owner can pick one, and ADR-0171 §3/§4 is reopened until they do. State toggles: ערכת צבעים (בהיר · כהה) · מסך (360×640 · 390×844).

- [`mockups/plan-row-title-overflow-v1.html`](../../mockups/plan-row-title-overflow-v1.html) — **הכותרת מקבלת 17% מהשורה · what a Plan-day row does when the title is longer than its column** (2026-08-09, session 236) — **Promoted into [ADR-0178](../decisions/0178-a-day-row-says-what-then-when.md) (Accepted 2026-08-09, including §3's fork; not built yet).** From a screenshot of Plan mode's day builder where a flight row's `קפלאוויק ← פרנקפורט` runs straight under `00:20–05:50`, and the owner's framing — _"handle this gracefully (preferably not folding text to three dots …), there could be even longer texts"_ — followed by a rejection of this file's own first draft: _"Your suggestions look really bad … Lets think outside the box"_. **That rejection is the most useful thing in the file and the draft is drawn in §6 rather than deleted**, because the trap is easy to fall back into: the draft answered the question as asked (how should text behave when it does not fit) instead of the question under it (how much width does it have), and its three flow rules turned a 78px overflow into `קפלאווי / ק` — a place name broken mid-word. **The reframe is §1's measured budget:** the row's content box is 302px at 360px, the badge (36) + lock anchor (22) + ⋯ (32) + gaps (40) + **the time column (124)** take 254, and the title — the thing the row is read for — gets **48px, 17%**, against a route asking for **129px**. Single-line tuning was then measured and killed by its own numbers: stacking the time's footnote and deleting the redundant `קשיח` chip take the title to **71px**, so the line is not short of calibration, it is short by a factor of two. **What it promotes: the row gets a second line, in both modes** — a three-column grid (`'badge title menu' / 'badge when menu'`, and the same on `.wp-event-face`), the title at **214px (71% of the row, 4.4×)**, the time keeping its identity as `button.bld-time` and ADR-0161 §7's rule that a row's time is what you press to move the event, gaining room to read as a sentence (`00:20–05:50 · 3:30 שע׳ · ⏱ +2 ש׳`) and `justify-self: start` so the day scans down one column — which the trailing-time layout is defended for and does not deliver, since its position moves with every row's height. **The height objection is closed by measurement, not argument:** the row is **69px against today's 88px**, because the `קשיח` chip already wraps to a second line inside `.bld-t`, so two lines' height is paid today and spent on a tag. **§3 is the fork left to the owner** — hard/soft is drawn three times (anchor lock, chip, border), two go, and the survivor moves into the when line where ADR-0011 says it belongs (`🔒 00:20–05:50`), leaving a soft row marked by its dashed border and the absence of a lock rather than by the word `גמיש`; every frame carries a hard row and a soft row side by side so it can be judged. **The `…` survives as a backstop and only that**, at two lines, and the file counts how often it actually fires (`scrollHeight > clientHeight`, read from the DOM): **0 of 4** across the stress set including a 24-character unbreakable Icelandic place name. **Four shipped defects it names.** `.route` is `display: inline-flex` (screens.css:2799) — default `nowrap`, two single-word `<bdi>` children, so it has no break point and no shrink point at any width and a 16-character route spills the moment the row is narrower than it; `route-display.tsx`'s own header comment claims the opposite in as many words ("Nothing here truncates: both layouts wrap rather than clip"), which is why no threshold, test or review ever caught it; the same atom sits in `EventCard` behind a comment reading "anything else may wrap freely" and is **already overflowing there too** (−37px on the reported flight); and the obvious cheap fix is actively harmful here — lowering `ROUTE_INLINE_MAX_CHARS` falls back to a destination-primary title whose origin returns as `meta`, which the Trip card renders and the builder row does not (`PlanDay.tsx:1773` passes `title` alone, since ADR-0174 §8 took names off this row deliberately), so on the reported surface the origin is not moved to a second line, it is deleted. **Renders through the app's real CSS** (seven shipped stylesheets inlined by [`mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs`](../../mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs); re-run after touching any of them, and the file is in `.prettierignore` so formatting cannot fight the generator). **Both day surfaces change together**, on the owner's instruction (_"Make sure that your design for plan and trip mode's are aligned"_) and because the day card is not latent but **already broken**: measured with the same flight, its title box is 97.8px against a 129px route — **−37px**, and an earlier reading of "69px of headroom" had measured a route long enough to trip `routeDisplay`'s destination-primary fallback, i.e. the short title the fallback makes rather than the reported one. Under the proposal both land at **214px / 213px**; what stays different stays different on purpose (Plan's time is a `button` and wears ADR-0161 §7's hairline chip, Trip's is a readout; 36px badge against 40px) — the layout is aligned, not the density. **The correction the owner caught mid-session is recorded in the file rather than quietly fixed**, because how it was found is the lesson: the first drawing spanned the ⋯ down both rows and left the when line the title's column alone — 214px for a 247px sentence — so it painted over the ⋯ at 360px and not at 390px, and the measurement table reported `0px` because every reading watched `.bld-main`. Two rules came out of it and both are now instruments in the file: **a fix that moves an element measures the element it moved**, and **an overlap check is two-dimensional** (the first re-measure still said "23px" from an x-axis comparison, which cannot tell "passes beneath" from "collides with"). The sweep now runs over every pair of boxes in every frame at both widths in both modes, and it immediately caught a second collision in §3's own control frame. **Deliberately undrawn:** `.bld-icon` (32px) / `.bld-bd` (36px), each owned by another ADR and not needed for the route to fit — recorded as where the width is if the row ever needs more. **What it cannot settle** (ADR-0017 device pass): whether a soft row still reads as soft from the dashed border alone, whether the title-aligned time column really scans better than the trailing one — §2's central claim and a claim about the eye — and whether 214px of title reads as calmer or emptier. State toggles: ערכת צבעים (בהיר · כהה) · מסך (360×640 · 390×844) · ההצעה (שורה שנייה · רשת ביטחון), the last two independent so the layout and the backstop are priced separately.
- [`mockups/booking-row-crowding-v1.html`](../../mockups/booking-row-crowding-v1.html) — **שורת ההזמנה צפופה · what a booking row actually has to say** (2026-08-09, session 237) — **Promoted into [ADR-0179](../decisions/0179-a-booking-row-says-what-then-when-and-the-code-is-a-read.md) (Accepted **and built** 2026-08-09).** From three owner screenshots of the Index bookings screen taken hours after ADR-0178 shipped, and its two questions: make the row read like the event row, and _"think if all information is necessary to be on the card row itself instead of only being on the booking preview"_. **The first question is nearly answered before it is asked** — this row has drawn two lines since it was built — so most of the file is the second one. **§1 is the measured budget**, drawn as a live bar whose segments are the widths they measured: `.wp-listrow-right` is `flex: 0 0 auto`, so the trailing slot is sized by its own content, and the first thing in it is `.code` — the one **unbounded** string any `ListRow` host puts there (documents pass a byte size, notes a glyph). At 360px the code takes **133px** and the title is left **43px · 13%**; ADR-0178's number for the same defect was 48 of 302. **§2 is the audit**, one table row per fact with its verdict: badge, title, lock, day, clock, duration and the note/doc marks stay; the 🔗 glyph, the type chip, the confirmation code and (on start edges) the transition verb go — each drawn removing alone in §2 so it is judged separately rather than as a package. **§5 is the fork**, all three code treatments drawn side by side (off · on the when line · kept trailing) and answered **off** by the owner; the rejected arms stay drawn so the rejection is checkable. **§3/§4 are the result and the stress set**, §6 is the other two `ListRow` hosts rendered unbroken so "nothing forks" is a render rather than a promise. **Four rules exist only because the render disproved the draft**, and each is commented at the rule that fixes it: `flex-wrap: wrap` **stacks** rather than wraps (flex distributes negative space only within a line — 0178's wrapping zone pill has nowhere to shrink to, text has an ellipsis), so the two rows carrying marks hit four bands at 360px; a single shrinkable `verb · day` span let the `…` eat **the day** rather than the verb, which is backwards; verb + duration together still overflowed by 23px and truncated the verb past the point it distinguishes check-out from check-in — on the one row it was kept for — so the annotation slot holds one, which on a closing edge is a **correctness** fix as well (`5 לילות` beside a check-out time reads as five nights still to come); and the marks had to leave the meta line for the title line, which also returns the row to agreement with the day card. **It corrects an instrument ADR-0178 made mandatory:** that collision sweep reads `getBoundingClientRect`, and a wrapped **inline** element's bounding box is the union of its fragments — it claims the ragged space beside every line where it paints nothing, and here it reported `9×10 · .bk-dur ↔ .icon` on the **shipped** row, which is exactly the "passes beneath" vs "collides with" confusion the sweep exists to prevent. The version in this file reads `getClientRects()` (one rect per fragment, the bounding box for a block) and skips same-element pairs; **copy this one, not 0178's**. The line counter went wrong twice for related reasons and the second version **flattered the proposal** — worth reading its comment before writing another. Final measurement across ten rows, both themes, both widths: title **43 → 184px** at 360px (13% → 56%), worst row **9 text lines → 2**, the five-row list **593 → 304px**, ellipsis fires **nowhere**, sweep clean. **Renders through the app's real CSS** (seven shipped stylesheets inlined by [`mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs`](../../mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs); re-run after touching any of them). **What it cannot settle** (ADR-0017 device pass): whether a muted day beside a full-ink clock reads as hierarchy or as something switched off, whether losing the verb on start edges is felt on a screen of ✈️ rows, and the open question it deliberately does not fold in — whether `עוד 33 ימים` is the right fact for an index at all, or whether a date (ADR-0176) reads better when the question is "when is my flight". **Where it now differs from the shipped app** (ADR-0097 requires this to be stated): the file draws the separator as a rendered `<span class="bk-sep">·</span>`; the build made it a `::before`, following both day rows, so the dot stays out of the accessibility tree — and that change is what surfaced a render-only defect the mockup could not have (`dir="auto"` on the clock resolves the box to LTR, so the pseudo landed on the wrong side of it; the isolate moved to an inner `<bdi>`). The file also draws its own `.bk-lock`, where the build collected the mark into the shared `HardLock`/`.hard-lock` (rule 8 — the day surfaces already had two byte-identical copies). Both are noted rather than retrofitted: a mockup is the dated record of what was decided, not a live mirror. Measured title width agreed to within 2px (184/214 drawn, 182/212 built). State toggles: ערכת צבעים (בהיר · כהה) · מסך (360×640 · 390×844) · קוד אישור (יורד · בשורת המתי · בקצה) · סדר שורת המתי · משך · פועל המעבר — the last four independent so each content call is priced on its own.
- [`mockups/currency-becomes-a-feature-v1.html`](../../mockups/currency-becomes-a-feature-v1.html) — **מטבע הופך לפיצ׳ר · where money lives on the Trip-mode Home, and what the tap opens** (2026-08-09, session 238) — **Promoted into [ADR-0180](../decisions/0180-currency-is-derived-and-a-rate-is-a-glance-card.md) (Accepted 2026-08-09, every fork answered — _"I accept your recommendations"_; **built in full the same day**, §9's per-card line being the owner's pick).** Session note: [`planning/2026-08-09-session-238-…`](../planning/2026-08-09-session-238-where-money-lives-on-the-home.md). Six sections against one question, since the backlog itself named the contested part: _"the Home's card budget … not the arithmetic."_ **§1 draws four whole Homes in the real shell** (`.app` → `.header` with `.hdr-top` + the shared `wp-daystrip` → `.body` → `.nav`, so the fold is a measured line at `640 − 116 − 71 = 453px` rather than an assumption) and **reverses this file's own first draft**: a fifth quick-access tile costs **+82px** (a second tile row, 115→197px) against the standalone card's **76px including its section heading**, and at five-in-a-row the value renders `¥100=…` (96px of content in a 44px box). What survives the numbers is stronger — **[ADR-0045](../decisions/0045-trip-home-real-data-only.md) §4 already wrote the answer**: `מבט מהיר` was removed for being _fixtures_, not for being a section, and its §4 promised "Weather / FX return as themselves … as their own glance cards". So the file promotes **the section's return with a `RateCard` as its first real tenant** (named so, because `GlanceCard` now means the day-at-a-glance time rail), weather as the second, **the section absent when it has no cards**. The rejected tile is drawn as option ג׳ rather than deleted. One objection closes purely on measurement: the glance rail is **already 228px below the fold at 360×640**, and the card — sitting after it — moves it by **0px**. **§2** is the freshness/absence set (טרי · בן 3 ימים · לא נטען מעולם) and the decision that "as of" is the **source's publication date, not `fetchedAt`** — an ECB reference rate is published once per TARGET day, so a Sunday rate dated Friday is _the current rate_ and gets no second staleness cue; what is kept from [ADR-0166](../decisions/0166-place-enrichment-is-a-multi-source-pipe.md) §6.4/§14 is serve-stale-never-block and a refresh riding a request already happening. **§3** is the converter as `Modal variant="sheet"` (amount 44px, currency chip 44px as a `ValueToken`, swap 34px visible / 44px `::after` target, sheet 278px against a 453px body), drawn over the real Home so the card it opens from is visible behind the scrim — and where the **home** currency, which nothing stores today, is chosen. **§4** is the currency picker in the shipped `.zp-*` geometry unaltered, over `Intl.supportedValuesOf('currency')` — **159 codes read live in the page** against `CURRENCY_OPTIONS`'s hardcoded 5. **§5** swaps the settings `<select>` for the zone field's own trigger and rewrites `t.settings.derivedHint`, which currently promises this feature in the future tense. **§6** reads the minor-unit exponent from `Intl.NumberFormat(...).resolvedOptions()` in the page rather than typing a table: **0** (JPY, ISK) · **2** (ILS, USD) · **3** (KWD, BHD), so the drawing cannot bake in `lib/money.ts`'s two-case assumption. Also decided here: **no money hue** (the rate is a mono numeric run; the `▲/▼` delta — the one thing with a budgeted hue — is dropped for needing stored history), and **the rate's base is a design call**, stated at the smallest power of ten clearing 1 (`¥100 = ₪2.43`, not `¥1 = ₪0.0243`). **Three shipped defects found by rendering, none about currency:** (a) `.quick`'s `repeat(N,1fr)` is not N equal columns — `.qa` has no `min-width: 0`, so at five tiles the row measures `56 · 68 · 81 · 56 · 110` and pushes **81px off the inline-start edge**; four tiles happen to fit, so it is latent, and the file has a control to see the break and the one-line repair; (b) `.set-tz-trigger` renders at **40px**, under ADR-0017's 44px floor, and §5 gives it a second call site; (c) its name says `tz` for what is now a two-instance shape. **Two costs the reuse itself carries**, invisible in `ZonePicker`'s source and found only on screen: the row's two text columns swap roles between instances (a zone is a short name + long id, a currency is a long name + a 3-char code, so `דירהם של איחוד הנסיכויות הערביות` wrapped and then `AED` was the thing ellipsised — hence a `[data-kind='currency']` modifier that leaves the zone instance untouched), and `Intl` returns the **code** as a narrow symbol when there is no distinct one, rendering `ALL ALL` until the comparison strips the bidi marks an RTL locale wraps it in. **Revised the same day (second pass), after the owner opened it on a phone.** **§7 added**, drawing the **whole `UserSettings` screen** (header → identity → display → account → sign-out; `avatar.css` + `choice-grid.css` joined the manifest) — its first pass drew the display card alone and invented its contents as a `.lab`/`.val` pair where the shipped theme control is a label-only `.id-row` plus a real `ChoiceGrid`, which is `pitfalls.md`'s "real CSS over an invented tree" landing on this file, caught by the owner against the running app. Drawing the neighbours is load-bearing: the section's claim is _which hint belongs to which card_. The content is the account-level currency: `User.preferredCurrency` in `UserSettings`' existing `תצוגה` section, which **amends [ADR-0133](../decisions/0133-the-user-is-a-surface-identity-ramp-and-a-reachable-roster.md) §7 by that section's own condition** ("a switch that does nothing is worse than a thin page" held until this slice gave it two readers) exactly as the theme toggle's return did, and whose one trap the frame draws: its neighbour's hint promises _"הבחירה נשמרת במכשיר הזה"_ and currency is account state, so the row needs a hint of its own. **A defect the owner caught and the file could not see:** `align-items: baseline` on the converter's currency chip put its contents **13.0px** above the chip's own centre — an inline SVG has no baseline, and on a flex line taller than its content `baseline` parks the group near the top rather than centring it. `center` takes it to 0.0px. The instrument is the lesson: every reading in the table was a width or a height, and the chip's width and height were right the whole time — so the file now measures **each child's centre against its container's centre** and prints the `baseline` counterfactual beside it by forcing the old value and re-reading. **And the drawing caught its own source out:** §3's pair control offers `ISK↔USD` against a `שער יציג · ECB` line, and the ECB turned out to cover only 30 of the app's 152 codes — 17 of its own 57 destinations unpriceable — which is what turns "support all currencies" into a rate-source decision rather than a picker one. **§8 added (third pass), answering "do we want a small refresh button?" with _yes to the action, no to a button_.** A reference rate is published once per business day, so a standing control reliably does nothing — ADR-0133 §7's own objection — and worse, implies a liveness the "as of" beside it denies. The rule is already in this codebase: `ErrorState`'s header says _"the retry button only renders when the caller can actually recover"_, applied here to a **value** instead of a dead end, and consistent with `SyncBadge`'s _"silent when synced"_. After the owner's steer (_"a glyph with no text, and let's rethink the placement"_) there is **no separate control at all — the date is the button**: `נכון ל־6.8` gains the app's existing `reset` glyph and a hairline when a press could change it, and nothing else. Two measured consequences the frames carry: the line must **not** reflow when the control appears (as a `border-block-end` the hairline took it 17px → 18px; painted as a `box-shadow` it is 17px either way, target 45px), and the busy state spins the **mark** while the date stays put (§6.4's "never a spinner where a fact used to be") — **anticlockwise**, which the owner caught on the moving version and the file then measured rather than eyeballed: sampling `reset`'s arc, the angle about its centre runs 151° → 85° → 21° → −139°, monotonically decreasing into the head bracket at the top-left, so the glyph points that way. Not the shared `@keyframes spin` (`+360deg`) and not a duplicate of it — `.spinner` is a symmetric ring with no head, so **a ring may spin either way; an arrow may not.** Not on the card, for a structural reason before an aesthetic one: the card is a single `<button>`. Four frames — bidding on today's rate (no control) · behind (control) · in flight · offline (no control). **§9 added (fourth pass), once the coverage check picked the provider.** ExchangeRate-API's terms require **visible, linked** attribution on any surface showing a rate — free in the converter sheet, which already ends its rate line in a source, and not free on the Home card, which ADR-0180 §3 fixed at three elements. A structural constraint narrows it before taste does: the card is a `<button>`, and an `<a>` inside one is invalid markup, so any placement _inside_ the card silently drops the link. Four frames — none · the existing `.sec-title .hint` slot · a line under the card · inside the card. Measured: the section slot costs **+0px and zero new CSS**, the per-card line **+21px**, and both clear the 44px floor through an `::after` overlay. The in-card option **dies on the render rather than on the argument** — it wraps to three lines and forces the rate itself onto two (`¥100 =` / `₪2.43`). The file recommends the per-card line despite costing more, on the scaling test: weather is the section's second tenant from a _different_ source, and one slot in a section heading cannot carry two sources honestly. State toggles: ערכת צבעים (בהיר · כהה) · מסך (360×640 · 390×844) · טריות השער · צמד (JPY↔ILS · ILS↔KWD · ISK↔USD, the 0/2/3-decimal cases) · `min-width:0` מתוקן/כמו היום · מסגרות §1 פרושות/חתוכות בקיפול. **Where it differs from the shipped app:** §1–§9 all shipped, so the file is now a record of _why_ rather than a proposal — with one difference worth naming, since it is the kind that ages a mockup silently: §3 draws its rate line ending in `שער יציג · ECB`, and the shipped converter carries no source line at all. The attribution moved to §9's per-card line under the Home card, and the provider is ExchangeRate-API rather than the ECB; the glance rail is drawn in its no-hard-anchor variant (its shorter form), which makes every fold claim conservative; and two labelled mockup-only overrides re-anchor `Modal`'s fixed overlay and `.zp-list`'s `52vh` to the frame box, since neither is the device viewport on a page full of frames.
- [`mockups/map-stop-traversal-v2.html`](../../mockups/map-stop-traversal-v2.html) — **המציץ מחליף את הפס · the peek replaces the rail** (2026-08-10, session 240) — **promoted into [ADR-0182](../decisions/0182-a-day-is-a-sequence-you-can-step-through.md)'s 2026-08-10 amendment, which supersedes its own §5–§7.** Supersedes v1's rail; **v1 stays** as the dated record of what was decided before the owner saw the numbers. Written because v1 §7 measured its 50px rail at **0px visible** on a loaded card, and the owner drew the conclusion that invites: _"do we even want the rail if it adds that much pixels. Maybe we should do the wanderlog approach, show just a little of the next card. And lose the circularity, maybe it's not that important."_ **The trade is explicit and is the whole file:** v1 §6 already drew this carousel and rejected it on **one** argument — a snap scroller is a line with two ends and wrapping means jumping `scrollLeft` under live momentum — so giving up wrap changes one input rather than the taste. **§1** the track: `.map-placecard` keeps its job (position, the measured `bottom` off the sheet and Google's attribution, z-index) and stops being the card's box; the card is a slide in it, `scroll-snap-type: x mandatory` with `.map-facetstrip`'s own snap/edge-mask precedent from the same stylesheet. Measured at 360×640: track **342px**, selected slide **286px**, peek **28px** per side. **The peek is cheap because of the card's own rule, not an optimisation** — every heavy block (way-in list, summary, notes, documents, foot) is revealed by selection, so a neighbour is the 73px `.place` row the list already renders and only the centred slide is the tall card. **§1** also draws the **`כל הימים`** case as its third frame — one full-width card with **no track**, because with no day the sequence is empty by construction (`buildPinOrderIndex` opens `if (!onDate) return new Map()`), so this is not a disabled control but a layout that only exists when the day gives it members; a lone slide inside the track would sit narrower than the card with empty gutters. **§2** the ends: no peek on a side **is** the end, which is what replaces wrap (**decided: no wrap** — _"the ends are ends"_, reversing the pre-session "navigation wraps") — nothing to time, nothing for `prefers-reduced-motion` to drop, no amplitude to bound against the 8px gutter. **§3** the loaded card, i.e. v1 §7's question: this proposal **adds no pinned row**, so it has nothing to be clipped — and the file is explicit that it therefore does **not fix** v1 §7's two bugs (the foot still shows **3 of 44px** here, and **0 of 104px** on today's full-width card); it stops _adding_ to them, and both backlog lines remain prerequisites. **§4** the slide density, after the owner's call: _"I want them all to be expanded the same way (as if you clicked on the place pin), that makes it look more balanced."_ It is also a correction — with collapsed neighbours the peek showed the edge of a **64px** row floating at the bottom of a **209px** card, a sliver rather than a card edge; equal density measures neighbour **209px** against the selected slide's 209px. It needs `expanded` to split from `selected` (today one fact: `Map.tsx` passes notes/refs/summary only for the selected row, and `.map-rename` is CSS-gated on `.place.selected`), and deliberately does **not** make them all look selected — that would trade the imbalance for an ambiguity about which card you are on. It also costs this file its own cheapness argument: **three full cards are mounted**, not N, since a peek shows one neighbour either side — a mounting cost on a screen that re-renders every second, not a pixel cost; the proposal still adds **0px** of pinned height. **The arrows are gone** (owner: _"no arrows, only peek"_), so there is no `.map-stopnav` and nothing added to `.map-refs-foot`; the earlier 38px measurement stands as the reason and is recorded in the notes. **§5** an inventory of what was deleted rather than restyled: the rail (50px → 0px), the gesture apparatus (no `setPointerCapture`, no slop threshold for a device pass, no `touch-action` puzzle, no `armClickSwallow`), the indicator **and its contradiction** (a peek asserts no ordinal, so it cannot disagree with the badge under it), and the wrap nudge with its 8px ceiling. **Two corrections the render forced on this file's own first draft, and they are the valuable part.** (a) **The bound must move from the card to the slide** — `overflow-x: auto` with `overflow-y: visible` is not available (the used value becomes `auto`), so a track scrolls on both axes; unbounded, the slide measured **422px inside a 315px track**, its top **53px above the split** and the identity row **82px behind the controls row**, which is _worse_ than the rail and is exactly ADR-0182 §9's second failure mode. `align-self: stretch` on the selected slide is what binds it, since a percentage `max-height` computes to `none` against an indefinite-height parent — the same pair `map.css` already writes one element out. (b) **The "0px" arrow seat is not 0px**: `.map-refs-foot` is `flex-wrap: wrap` with a 16px gap and two 44px verbs, and the slide is narrower than the card by the peek, so the foot breaks to two lines — **44px → 82px, i.e. 38px** against the rail's 50px, which is not cheap enough to justify putting navigation in the row that holds delete (ADR-0157 §2). (c) A third, smaller one: **the file had to centre the selected slide itself** (`useCenterSelected`'s job in the app), and until it did, every peek reading came off a track resting at `scrollLeft: 0` — the table printed a peek of **−238px**, a negative number being the file catching itself, the same class as v1's seam reading an entrance animation. State toggles: ערכת צבעים · מסך (⁦360×640⁩ · ⁦390×844⁩) · עומס הכרטיס (מינימלי · הזמנות מרובות · הכל) · מציץ (⁦12⁩ · ⁦20⁩ · ⁦28px⁩, a feel call the device pass owns) · חיצים (בלי · בפוטר). **Where it differs from the shipped app: §1–§5 all shipped on 2026-08-11**, so the file is now a record of _why_ rather than a proposal — with two differences worth naming. The shipped track keeps `--map-peek` at 20px (this file's middle control) and adds `MAP_TRACK_SETTLE_MS`, which the file has no equivalent of because a static page has no scroll to settle; and the shipped `.map-placecard` bound is **unconditional**, where this file inherited the three-way `:has()` gate and worked around it on the slide. **REVISED 2026-08-13 (session 261)** for ADR-0182's device pass: the `APP-CSS:` block was re-inlined (it had drifted past three `map.css` amendments, including the one that made the card's bound unconditional) and the selected slide gained `.map-cardclose`, the card's new close control. It is what measured the two claims the pass needed: the neighbours are **286px wide, the same as the selected slide**, so the control's fourth grid column comes out of the title's space and not the card's; and its 44px target overlaps `נווט` by **3px**. It also measured the empty space in the field report — emptying the selected slide's note list leaves its box at **315px, unchanged** — which is what identified the stretch rather than the bound as its cause. Labelled mockup-only overrides: a `.mk-marker` standing in for Google's `AdvancedMarker`, a painted ground for the `google.maps.Map`, `animation: none` on the resting frames (`map-furniture-in`'s frame 0 is `translateY(6px)`), and a reconstructed v1 rail in §3 for the side-by-side.
- [`mockups/map-stop-traversal-v1.html`](../../mockups/map-stop-traversal-v1.html) — **מעבר בין עצירות במפה · stepping through a day's stops from the selected place card** (2026-08-10, session 240) — **Promoted into [ADR-0182](../decisions/0182-a-day-is-a-sequence-you-can-step-through.md) (Accepted 2026-08-10, all three forks answered as recommended — flexible interleaved, the dot rail, the nudge at 6px; not yet built, and a real-device pass is owed).** Session note: [`planning/2026-08-10-session-240-…`](../planning/2026-08-10-session-240-stepping-through-a-day-of-stops.md). Backlog **J**, field report **#25 / ADD-04** (the addendum's own #22–#26 numbering, unrelated to the Map epic's internal #1–#23). Six sections. **§1 reverses the brief's own premise about the data shape**, and it is the load-bearing finding: [`lib/map-pins.ts`](../../frontend/src/lib/map-pins.ts)'s `buildPinOrderIndex` already builds the traversable sequence — `stops` → sort → collapse adjacent same-Place connection moments into **`merged`** → filter to `numbered` → return a `Map<placeId, number>` — so **`merged` carries the owner's one-connection-one-stop rule already and is a local `const` that never leaves the function**; the work is a small extraction (`buildDayStopSequence`, consumed by `buildPinOrderIndex` itself), not a second itinerary algorithm, and **three** call sites already want it (`buildPinOrderIndex` internally, `screens/Map.tsx`'s `orderedStops`, `mapsDayRouteUrl`). And **`placeDayEntries` is _not_ a tail primitive**: its `DayPlacement` is a three-way split on the **hard/soft** axis ([ADR-0011](../decisions/0011-hard-soft-event-model.md)) whose two unpositioned buckets go to **opposite ends** of the day view, so concatenating "its tail" would put an unpositioned hard commitment last where the day view puts it first — and it is event-shaped (`UnplacedRow` carries a `TripEvent`) where a map stop is place-shaped. Drawn as two rails (the day's raw moments with the reason each drop drops, then the derived sequence) over a fixture day holding every case at once: a landing + next-leg departure that **collapse**, a **later return** to that same airport that does not, a floor (`מ-15:00`), a stay's middle night (excluded — `PIN_TIER.ambient` is already _"backdrop, not a stop"_), and two `idea`-tier places that `hasScheduleSlot` filters out at step one and that therefore form the tail. **§2** draws three arrow placements on the real `.map-placecard` → `.place` tree, and two die on the render rather than the argument: **ב׳** (inside `.map-t`) truncates `שוק צ׳אטוצ׳אק` → `שוק צ׳אטו…`, because the name is the row's one flexible column and it already shares it with the lock mark and the rename pencil; **ג׳** (floating on the canvas) has **8px** of gutter to live in, so a 44px-floor control there covers the card and the way-in block. **§3** paints the touch geography over the real split and reports the one structural finding the brief expected to be worse: `.map-placecard` is a **sibling** of `<MapPane>`, not a descendant, and `useCanvasGestures` listens on the **pane** — so a pointerdown on the card never reaches the canvas recogniser and the fifth gesture does not join the four-way arbitration. What is real: **8px** gutters, a **30px** seam to `SnapSheet`'s `touch-action: none` top region, **146px** of free canvas left for pan/pinch/long-press. The seam decides the mechanism — a finger starting there is on a region the browser will not hand back, so the swipe takes `setPointerCapture` on recognition, which is **ADR-0145 §A2's existing arbitration** ("both drags take capture at drag start") rather than a new one, plus `touch-action: pan-y` for `maybe-card.css`'s session-114 reason. **§4** draws the indicator on the stop where the numbers genuinely disagree: numbers are dense over _known_ moments only, so the airport you return a car to is pin **5** and the **6th** thing traversed, and a `6 · 8` readout beside a badge stamped `5` is the screen-contradicts-itself defect [ADR-0121](../decisions/0121-embedded-map-phase-6-design.md) §6's 2026-08-06 amendment closed. Recommends the **dot rail**, which claims order and nothing else; its first draft marked the tail with hollow rings (reasoning from the `idea` tier's dashed pin) and the render says a 1px ring inside a 5px circle is not a distinction at phone scale, so it is a gap and a hairline. **§5** is last → wrap → first. **§6** draws the rejected `scroll-snap` carousel, which is refused on a decision already taken rather than on taste: a snap scroller is a line with two ends, and wrapping means jumping `scrollLeft` while momentum is still running. State toggles: ערכת צבעים (בהיר · כהה) · מסך (⁦360×640⁩ · ⁦390×844⁩) · זמן גמיש (משובץ · בזנב, which is fork ⓐ) · מיקום החיצים (א׳ · ב׳ · ג׳) · מחוון מיקום (פסי נקודות · מספרי · בלי) · מספר עצירות (⁦8⁩ · ⁦20⁩) · דחיפת הגלישה (⁦4⁩ · ⁦6⁩ · ⁦8px⁩) · סף החלקה (⁦24⁩ · ⁦36⁩ · ⁦48px⁩) · כפתור החיפוש (כמו היום · מתוקן) · הרץ את התנועה. The last two number groups are **feel calls the device pass owns**, shipped at their recommendation. **A shipped defect found by rendering, unrelated to traversal:** [`screens/map.css:88`](../../frontend/src/screens/map.css) opens a selector list and puts a 17-line comment **inside** it, so the list parses as `.map-controls > .map-search-btn, .map-controls > .map-controls > .map-querystrip` — the second matches nothing, and the first, which the rule was never about (its own comment says search _"sits at the row's fixed end"_), quietly collects `flex: 1 1 auto; min-width: 0`, outranks the button's `flex: 0 0 auto; width: 34px` on specificity, and stretches a 34px square to **178px**. The query strip escapes only because the whole block is **duplicated** at line 216 and that copy is well-formed, which is the fingerprint of how it happened; a control shows the break against the one-line repair. **Three more things only rendering could say.** (a) **The wrap nudge has a ceiling of 8px** — the card is `inset-inline: 8px` inside a `.map-split` carrying `overflow: hidden`, so a larger translate is not an overshoot but a clipped corner; this file's own first draft shipped 10px and clipped by 2px, and the table now prints the overhang. (b) **The card is 177px, not the 94px the first draft drew**: `.map-right` is a COLUMN holding the distance _and_ `נווט` (which is why the shipped row is 73px, as its own comment states), and selection reveals the way-in block — so the 30px rail is a fifth of the 146px left above it, which is the honest price of option א׳. (c) **A measurement taken in the same tick as the paint read the entrance animation, not the resting box**: `.map-placecard` carries `map-furniture-in`, which starts at `translateY(6px)`, and the seam printed ⁦24px⁩ against the settled DOM's ⁦30px⁩. **Revised after the owner opened the rendered file**, and the correction reshaped §2 rather than restyling it: _"the bottom rail is rounded but the place card itself is rounded as well · that makes it look awkward and not related."_ Structural, not cosmetic — the rail was a **sibling** of `.place`, and `.place` is a self-contained card (own ground, `border-radius: 16px`, hairline, floating shadow, and `.selected`'s 2px ring, which stopped halfway down and made the fault visible), so nothing outside that box can read as part of it.
