The rail moved **inside** `.place` as its last child and became the **fourth tenant of a grammar the card already had written three times** — `.map-refs`, `.place > .note-sec`, `.place > .docr-sec` are the identical five declarations, and `.place` is `flex-wrap: wrap` precisely so a full-width line can exist. So the file's hand-written CSS **lost** declarations rather than gaining them: ground, radius, shadow, clip and the ring are inherited. This is the reuse rule landing on this file from the far side — a footer was a _new mechanism_ where the app already had a _line_. Two things the move surfaced that the source hides: those five declarations now appear **four** times in `map.css` and want one `.place > .place-line` class (rule 8, flagged and not taken here), and their `margin-top: 8px` is invisible spend — `.place` is `gap: 11px` and flex gap applies between wrapped lines, so those blocks already sit 19px apart and the margin is a third separator; the rail drops it and reads identically. The measured price moved with it: **the rail costs 50px** (a 39px box plus the 11px wrapped-line gap) of a card with **~126px** of free canvas above it, and the card's existing `max-height` arithmetic turns an over-tall card into a scroll rather than a clip. Also dropped in the same pass: a hairline tick beside the dot rail's tail gap, invisible at 5px for the same reason the hollow ring was — **two marks tried, the gap kept**. **§7 added after the owner's second question** — _"there could be cards with enrichment data or multiple bookings of events so they could be much higher, did you take that into account?"_ — and the answer was **no**: §2–§6 all draw the minimum card, and the loaded one breaks two ways, neither caused by the proposal and one of which makes the proposal invisible. **(a)** With `.note-sec` or `.map-hero` present, `map.css` caps the card and `.place` becomes a grid where only `.note-sec-list` scrolls; at 360×640 with three references plus enrichment the **pinned rows alone** overrun the cap by **83px** — note track **0px**, `.map-refs-foot` (`שיבוץ ליום`, `עוד בגוגל`) **10 of 44px**, and the proposed rail **39px tall with 0px visible**. [ADR-0148](../decisions/0148-the-place-form-has-the-room-it-needs.md) §1's "the shortfall becomes a SCROLL instead of a clip" holds only while the shortfall is in the note list; nothing bounds the pinned rows. **(b)** The `max-height` fires only on `:has(> .map-draft)`/`:has(.note-sec)`/`:has(.map-hero)`, so a place with several bookings and no notes and no enrichment is bounded by nothing — anchored to the split's bottom it grows **up** past the floating controls row, which paints after it and therefore over it, putting **~15px of the identity row behind the chips**: ADR-0148 §1's own "worst way round". Both are filed as backlog lines that **block J**. §7 also carries the method lesson: six of the file's seven sections measured **heights**, and a height cannot see a clip, so §7 intersects each section's rect with the card's clipping box and draws the cut line on the frame — `frontend/CLAUDE.md`'s "reading a rect and calling it visibility", landing on this file. And it surfaced the ADR's one open item: arrows inside `.map-refs-foot` cost **0px** (it is already pinned, already present on every selected row, children already 44px) and measure **16.9px** from the nearest verb against ADR-0157 §2's 16px — held back only because it reduces the overrun (83→44px) rather than removing it, and because the foot holds the schedule and delete verbs, which a stop-to-stop arrow is not. Controls: עומס הכרטיס (מינימלי · הזמנות מרובות · העשרה · הכל) · מיקום הפס (שורה נפרדת · בתוך הפוטר · בלי). **Where it differs from the shipped app:** everything in it is a proposal — nothing here is built. Labelled mockup-only overrides: a `.mk-marker` wrapper standing in for Google's `AdvancedMarker` (which is what positions a `.map-pin`; the pin's own tree and stylesheet are the shipped ones), a painted canvas ground standing in for the `google.maps.Map` the app renders, `animation: none` on the resting frames for (c) above, and §5's wrap frame **parked at its own keyframe's peak** with a negative delay and `paused` — the real keyframe sampled, since a static capture otherwise reads frame 0 where the displacement is zero.
- [`mockups/sw-update-prompt-v1.html`](../../mockups/sw-update-prompt-v1.html) — **where a "the build under you was swapped" notice lives** (2026-08-10), promoting [ADR-0181](../decisions/0181-a-swapped-build-announces-itself.md) (Accepted; **built the same day**). Backlog F-13. §1 the notice over the in-trip chrome against the app as it is (which shows **no signal at all** — the registration was the plugin's four-line default, so a rebuilt SW claimed every open tab silently); §2 the two occupied lower lanes drawn rather than asserted, the real `.toast` at `bottom: 78px` above the tab bar, which is the whole argument for putting a **persistent** notice on top; §3 the three `StatusBanner` tones side by side, `neutral` chosen because nothing failed. The banner is the shipped `StatusBanner` tree with one added slot — the `FeedbackAction` its own `types.ts` always claimed it shared — so the file's hand-written CSS is **two rules**: the `.app-update` mount and the `.fb-banner-action` verb. **Measured:** banner 45px over a 116px header (it covers row 1, leaves the day strip and body clear); the verb's box 27px, reaching ADR-0017's 44px floor at **45px** through ADR-0161 §7's `::after` overlay rather than a `min-height` that would have grown the banner; mount 326px of 360px. The three-word copy is a measurement too — longer wrapped to a second line at 360px. State toggles: ערכת צבעים (בהיר · כהה) · מסך (360×640 · 390×844) · מצב (עם הודעה · בלי הודעה). **Two things only rendering could have said.** The proposal's own cost is one of them — the banner sits exactly on the header's identity row, hiding the trip name and the passive sync badge ADR-0149 §7 protects, which is what made the placement an owner question with the alternatives drawn rather than a detail. The other is a defect in the harness: a top-level `const chrome` in this file's script collided with the browser's `window.chrome` global and threw at parse, leaving every frame blank — while `render.mjs` reported **"no console errors"**, because it listens for `console` and not for `pageerror`. Renamed here; the renderer's blind spot is recorded in the ADR. **Where it differs from the shipped app:** nothing yet — §1–§3 all shipped. One labelled mockup-only override: `.phone` carries a `transform`, which makes the frame a containing block for the `position: fixed` mount; on a device that containing block is the viewport and the transform does not exist.

- [`mockups/category-on-a-booking-and-a-note-v1.html`](../../mockups/category-on-a-booking-and-a-note-v1.html) — **קטגוריה בעריכה · why you cannot re-file a booking or a note** (2026-08-12) — **Promoted into [ADR-0183](../decisions/0183-what-a-thing-is-is-editable-and-the-statement-is-the-control.md) (Accepted, and built the same day).** From one owner report (_"When editing booking and notes, you're unable to update the category"_) plus two calls made against this file's own first draft, both of which changed the shape it ships with. **Reading the code split the report in two:** a note has `Note.category` and a booking has none and must never gain one (ADR-0038's 2026-07-19 amendment made `Booking.type` the sole authority, after a ⭐ badge on a hotel stored a non-lodging category and silently broke nights, check-in/out bracketing and the ambient backdrop) — so the booking half is "the TYPE is not editable", and **no ADR ever decided that it isn't**: session 221's note carries it as a premise (_"has never been editable, so on an edit there is no question to ask"_), and one `isCreate` at `BookingSheet.tsx:945` is the whole of what withholds a grid and an `onChange` that both already exist. §1 tables where a category lives on all six entities and which of them can be edited today. **The owner's two calls, and the principle they add up to:** _"I don't want a full step just for choosing the category… most edits will be changing other stuff"_ killed the first draft's return of the type STEP on edit (a step is what you pay on every pass through the form; a rare edit should be paid for only by whoever makes it — create stays at four steps, edit at three, session 221's arithmetic untouched), and _"the edit already has a category on top, so it should probably become clickable — for both notes and bookings"_ made the shape **one** mechanism instead of two: the statement already at the top of the editor becomes the control, and the chooser reveals in place through the shared `Collapsible`, costing **0px** until it is touched. §2 draws it on the booking, §3 on the note, and §3b draws the two rejected shapes so the rejections are checkable — a `Modal` sheet over the form (a second layer over a sheet for one of eight options with no search, hiding the form the choice returns to) and `ValueToken` (ADR-0177), which is the right rule for the wrong case: the primitive declares its open/focus mark in **amber for every host**, and its only `kind="word"` host today is a duration, i.e. time — a booking type is not, and lending it amber dilutes rule 4's budget. **§2c answers the owner's third call** (_"what does it mean to change from a hotel booking to a restaurant… we should probably pop up a warning"_) **yes on the warning and no on its wording.** Yes it needs more weight than in-grid text, so it is a `ConfirmDialog` — but not "reset and refill everything", because counted off the real axes `hotel → restaurant` drops **three** things and carries **eight** across, and a prompt that overstates gets dismissed unread while one that understates is the ambush. **A fourth call then cut the copy** (_"really short and no need to list everything that will be deleted"_), so the dialog is **three words** — `חלק מהפרטים יימחקו.`, the owner's own wording and the more accurate of the two drafts (the fields are genuinely deleted on save, not merely failing to carry over) and landing on `manage.plainBody`'s existing pattern of one future-tense sentence with a full stop; no itemised losses, no `consequence` row — and the in-grid banner the previous draft had added is **gone**: one warning, one place, short, because a banner and a dialog carrying the same sentence is the duplication session 246 was written to delete. The itemisation survives as §2c's matrix, **documentation rather than copy**, and that is what earns the short sentence: a reader can check that "חלק מהפרטים" is three of eleven. One thing the shortening surfaced rather than trimmed: the cancel could not stay `ביטול`, since this dialog fires from **שמירה** inside a form that already has its own discard confirm, so `ביטול` there reads as "abandon the edit" — it becomes `המשך עריכה`, verbatim what `common.discardCancel` already says for that intent. The build therefore needs a **boolean** (`switchIsLossy`), not a list to phrase — and the cut measures smaller twice, halving the confirm card (196.5px → 99.8px) and taking the opened form from 704.9px to 649.3px, i.e. 9px over a 360×640 screen instead of 65px. The three: the end clock and the spread across days (`span`→`point` turns a four-night stay into a point), the room number and the WiFi (`BookingSheet.tsx:633-635` **already** prunes these when `!isHotel`, correctly and silently — and the WiFi is one of the Home's three quick-access shortcuts, so the loss shows two surfaces away), and conditionally the derived round-trip pairing, which `useRoundTripPartner` already knows about and which a route→no-route switch ends because ADR-0154 §3 pairs on a mirrored route. The matrix is **computed** from `BOOKING_TYPE_PROFILE`, not listed, so a ninth booking type answers by existing, and the mechanism the build needs off it is a single boolean rather than a list. **The confirm sits on the TAP, and a fifth owner call reversed the previous draft to get there** (_"does it make sense to first remove the fields from the form, then after the user went through the whole form and decided to save, only then you'll warn?"_). That draft argued save-time on "nothing is lost until the save" — true of the stored data, **false of the form**, since the tap is what takes the route field, the span's end and the whole hotel block off the screen, making the tap the last moment the warning's subject is visible. Its other two reasons also fail: the "minefield" barely exists because the dialog only fires on a lossy switch and a near-empty create form loses nothing (and `changeType` already preserves an end a human typed), and "the app confirms at the destructive action" is the argument FOR the tap once the destructive action is located properly. **The sharpest tell is the cancel path** — at the tap `ביטול` is clean (nothing changed, the grid keeps the old type), while at the save it lands you back in a form whose boxes are already gone with nothing saying how to restore them; a broken cancel is a sign the confirm is at the wrong moment, and it is why the label moved twice (`ביטול` → `המשך עריכה` → `ביטול`), which is evidence rather than fussing since the label could not be settled without settling the moment. §2c draws both, and the frames carry the argument: the recommended dialog sits over the room number and the WiFi block **still on screen**, the rejected one over a form where they are **already missing**. Refusing a lossy switch is rejected too: it blocks exactly the report, and leaves the user with the delete-and-recreate that loses more than any switch. §3 also closes the half of the report that has nothing to do with hosts: **`ChoiceGrid`'s `onChange` only ever SETS**, so once a note has a category there is no way back to none — answered by one leading pill carrying a sentinel value, which is verbatim the shape `IndexNotesView`'s `הכל` chip already ships (`NOTE_CATEGORY_ALL`), so it costs zero CSS and zero change to the primitive. **Three findings that are defects rather than design**, all recorded here for the build: (1) **the wire cannot express a type change at all** — `updateBookingSchema` is `createBookingSchema.partial()` with the three place FKs `.optional()` rather than `.nullish()`, so `null` never reaches the server, `bookings.service.ts:116` merges `before.fromPlaceId` under the NEW type and `assertPlaceShape` throws 400; §5 tables the payloads, and its shipped twin is that **clearing a booking's place is already a silent no-op today** for the same reason; (2) **`NoteSheet:132` omits `.category-pills`**, so the note editor's pills render at the Index FILTER density — **28px against 35px**, measured, and the fourth call site this change needs is what turns the fix into the `CategoryField` extraction rather than a fifth copy; (3) **`HostNotes`' `NoteHostRef` is hand-built at five call sites and three omit `category`** (`BookingSheet:1349`, `EventForm:951`, `DetailSheet`) while `buildNoteHosts` derives it correctly in one place — which is why the editor cannot state the inherited value at all, and while in there `buildNoteHosts` drops `Place.category` on the strength of an ADR-0147 comment **ADR-0165 superseded**. **What the render found and reading did not:** at 360×640 the booking form with the grid revealed measures **686px against a 640px screen**, and the reveal-into-view that saves it is free _because the statement is a `<button>`_ — `BookingSheet.tsx:931`'s existing `onFocusCapture` → `scrollIntoView` catches a focusable row where a `<div>` would not. `NoteSheet` is the one editor without that pattern, which is hand-rolled three times (`BookingSheet:931`, `EventForm:673`, `DocumentUploadSheet:116`); lifting the three onto one place is recorded rather than smuggled into this change. Proposed CSS is one block of ~30 lines of which **four rules are `.bs-type-row`'s shipped rules moved out of `.booking-sheet` scope** so a second editor can wear them; the net new is a button reset, the 44px floor (the row measures 39px today and 44px proposed, a 5px cost) and the `--muted` caption that names where an inherited value came from. Live: theme, screen (the frame is the real screen height, so "does the opened panel still fit" is readable off the page), the booking type across six of the eight (the loss statement recomputes from the real `BOOKING_TYPE_PROFILE` axes and disappears when a switch drops nothing), the note's host across all five plus a general note, and the one thing left open — whether a note's 44px statement row is worth it on a form whose point is to ask nothing (ADR-0152 §6b) — which the shape toggle prices against the always-open field. Inlines the app's real CSS; in `.prettierignore`. **Three reports came back off the built surface** (session note §7), and the middle one is why the file's §5 was worth drawing: the update payload **never carried `type`** — correct for as long as the type was not editable, and it survived making it editable, so every other field saved and the category silently did not. Making a field editable is two changes and the second is invisible; every test passed because every test asserted on the form rather than on the payload. **The shape toggle's fork is now decided, and the app diverges from §3 accordingly** (2026-08-13, ADR-0183 §4's amendment): the collapsed statement row is the **edit's** shape, a create wears the always-open field this file drew beside it, and the category is the **first** field in both — so read §3 as the edit and §3's `צורה` alternative as the create.

- [`mockups/a-day-starts-and-ends-at-a-hotel-v1.html`](../../mockups/a-day-starts-and-ends-at-a-hotel-v1.html) — **יום מתחיל ונגמר במלון · where a stay is named in the day, and how many times** (2026-08-26) — **APPROVED AND BUILT 2026-08-26** ([ADR-0209](../decisions/0209-a-stay-is-named-once-in-the-day-it-belongs-to.md), and [ADR-0206](../decisions/0206-a-travel-time-belongs-between-two-points.md) §AI with it). Two differences from the drawing, both from the build: the shipped `StayRow` measures **⁦54px⁩** where this file drew ⁦60px⁩ (so every per-day total here is ⁦6px⁩ optimistic per stay row), and §3's `אחרי סגירת החלון` rides the **`OVERRUNS`** arm rather than the quiet one — because the build found that the _fit_ was measured to the window's opening too, so that state was unreachable until the fit moved to the close. Owner, off the shipped M6a build: _"when we know that a day starts or ends at a hotel (most days), the hotels should be added as rows at the start and end of the days … then the 'checkout until X' / 'check in from Y' could be quieted and not appear as rows."_ **Reading the code reframed the ask from an addition into a subtraction, four times over.** (1) The owner had answered this exact question the other way **one day earlier** — [ADR-0054](../decisions/0054-ambient-span-events-off-the-day-schedule.md)'s amendment records _"sequence only, no new rows"_ — and its three amendments since are all attempts to make the stay legible on the **map** instead, which is the evidence the map-only answer did not cover it. (2) [ADR-0206](../decisions/0206-a-travel-time-belongs-between-two-points.md) §AD made "no rows" incoherent on this surface specifically: the list draws `נסיעה · ~7 דק׳ · יציאה 06:18` **out of** the stay you woke in, a visible leg whose origin row does not exist. (3) A stay is already named **twice** on one screen — in `.day-ambient` and as a `צ׳ק-אאוט · עד 09:40` row — and the band's own comment explains that its sentence is borrowed from the day's _placed_ entry (`edgeSentence`) so the two cannot print two clocks, i.e. **the band entry is already a bookend row in everything but position**. So nothing is minted: the bookend is `TransitionRow`'s shipped tree, which end it takes is `dayBookendStays`' `{woke, sleeps}` that the map already reads, the sentence in the clock's slot is `ambientSpanLabel`'s own, and the file's whole proposed CSS is **three rules**. (4) _"warn me if I miss check-in"_ half exists and is not what this adds — `hero-booking.ts` already computes `missed` off the **clock** (`CHECKIN_GRACE_MIN`, ADR-0184 §3), a board countdown that fires once it is too late; §3 is a **prediction** from the day's own travel, readable at breakfast. **§1 draws the five shapes a day comes in** (middle night · check-out · check-in · change of hotel · no hotel) as a control, before and after. **§2 IS WHERE THE OWNER KILLED FOUR DRAFTS IN A ROW, AND THE ROUTE IS THE RESULT.** Draft 1 left the check-out at its bound and let the map carry "you started here" (ADR-0054's own call) — _"it says that we have to do svartifoss at 6:30 but the checkout row sits below it, which makes no sense"_: the row lands on ⁦09:40⁩, below a stop that ran to ⁦09:00⁩ and **above a journey departing at ⁦09:27⁩**. `day-entries.ts`'s `edgeAt` is why, and the rule is right — it pulls a ceiling back to `Math.min(atMs, …departures…)`, "a check-out cannot sort after your flight left", where a departure is a booked transport group and M6a added one the app **derives**. Draft 2 gave the check-out ROW the day's first position: killed by two counter-examples (_coffee at 08:00 then a check-out at 11:00_; _a check-in at 15:00 then an attraction and dinner_). Draft 3 merged the row and its journey: better, still read as a return trip. **All three were the same mistake — each tried to position the moment of checking out, which the app does not know.** Draft 4 splits the two facts apart (**where the day starts and ends** is certain and gets a position; **when you check out or in** is a bound and gets a quiet annotation positioning nothing) and authors the residual as a **stop** at the stay's own place, the owner's own way out. Draft 5 then takes the clock off the stay row entirely — _"why don't we get a `נסיעה` row after the morning and before svartifoss?"_ — so every leg stays an ordinary `JourneyBlock` and the day draws a leg one way. **The proposed CSS went four rules → two across the drafts, and each removal was the correction.** Also refused on the way: a `חזרה למלון` button on the stay row (it renders an _offer_ as a _statement_, present on the day you do not go back and absent from the day you do) and the `תחילת היום`/`סוף היום` label (redundant with the position and with the bound). **§3 produced two more defects, both M6a's and both now [ADR-0206 §AI](../decisions/0206-a-travel-time-belongs-between-two-points.md)** — a leave-by counted back from a check-in **window's opening** as though a floor were a deadline (`יציאה 16:18`, to arrive the instant the door opens, nothing due until ⁦20:00⁩), and a leave-by with **no clamp against its own origin**, landing at ⁦16:18⁩ inside a stop running to ⁦16:40⁩ — which §AH2's newly widened tolerance is what uncovered. So a leg into a floor or a window states its **arrival**, never a departure. **MEASURED:** every day that names a hotel twice names it once, and the two that named it most get shorter doing it (check-in ⁦422px⁩ → ⁦358px⁩, change-of-hotel ⁦675px⁩ → ⁦615px⁩ with ⁦4⁩ → ⁦2⁩ mentions); a check-out day is a wash (+⁦4px⁩); a hotel-less day is untouched to the pixel. The **middle night costs ⁦+159px⁩**, gaining four boxes it has none of today — and ⁦74px⁩ of that was available from draft 3's fold and is refused, because the day would then draw a leg two ways in two adjacent holes. Interactive: theme, width (⁦360×640⁩ · ⁦390×844⁩), the day shape (five), §2's one-row-vs-two, and §3's arrival state; the measurement table sweeps **all five shapes** off a hidden pass rather than only the selected one, because an edge day and a middle night move in opposite directions and one number would have read as the verdict. **Open and the owner's:** the two labels (`בוקר` / `לינה`).

- [`mockups/an-edge-can-be-a-window-v1.html`](../../mockups/an-edge-can-be-a-window-v1.html) — **קצה יכול להיות חלון · an edge can be a window** (2026-08-13, session 257) — a booking edge with a RANGE rather than an instant: check-in between 17:00 and 21:00. Owner brief: _"I don't want the default to have both the check-in start and check-in end time. I want a separate click or something to enable this feature. So it won't be that prominent, but it will be available."_ **Reading the code reframed the ask before anything was drawn:** [ADR-0171](../decisions/0171-a-time-can-be-a-floor-or-a-ceiling.md)'s `edgeMeaning` already derives `not-before` for a held span's start, so `15:00` on a check-in ALREADY means `[15:00, end of day]` and renders `מ-15:00`. This is not a new axis, it is **closing the open end of a window the app already draws** — which also settles the scope with no type check, since the control belongs to any edge whose meaning is not `exact` (a hotel's two ends and a hire's two, from `midSpan.kind === 'held'`). **§1 prices the two opt-in affordances against each other, and both are made of shipped primitives** — א is a second `ValueToken` in the `.wf-line` that already exists (`.vt-empty` is already the dashed absence, and `whenField.rangeFrom`/`rangeTo` are already the words), ב is the `ChoiceDisclosure` + `Collapsible` that shipped the day before. Neither is a new mechanism, so the comparison is purely cost: measured at 360, the whole when-block is **156.5px** under א and **216.5px** under ב, i.e. **ב costs 60px to everyone who never uses the feature** — and that is ב drawn for ONE edge; symmetric it is two rows. א's chip is 31.8px tall with a **45.8px** touch reach through `ValueToken`'s `::after`, clearing ADR-0017's floor without the line growing. **§2 is the behavioural consequence, and it falls out of ADR-0171 §10a's own test** — the width of the window. A floor is unpositioned because it is open on the side you act; a **closed** window is placeable, so the row leaves the `.day-ambient` strip (52px, measured) and rejoins the list. §10b's map rule is untouched: a stop number indexes a moment the app KNOWS, and a window is not a moment. **§2b is the owner's correction and it is a real trade, not a preference.** The app's two row shapes answer "where does the time go" oppositely and deliberately — `.transition-row` is flex with `.tr-time` at the trailing edge (`flex: 0 0 auto` · `nowrap`), `.wp-event-face` is a grid whose areas are `'badge title' / 'badge when'`, i.e. the time UNDER the title. For a single time it does not matter; a range is roughly twice the width, and every pixel it gains comes off `.tr-title`, the one element that ellipsises. Measured against a long hotel name at 360: at the edge the title gets **165.5px** against **210.5px** with a bare time — the range costs the name **45px** — while under the title the name gets the full **259px** and the row grows **55px → 75px**. Same shape as ADR-0171's own 38→46px finding, five times the size. **Decided in session: the range goes under the title and a bare time does not move**, so no shipped transition row changes and the 20px is charged only where a window was authored; every other section draws it that way and §2b keeps forcing both, because without both there is no number. **§2c is Plan, and it is in the file because forgetting it is the defect ADR-0171 §10e already had to fix once**: both modes read one `placeDayEntries`, so a closed window regains its position in **both**, `UnplacedCommitment` in Plan is the same row without the settle pair, and — the non-obvious half — **a window holds a position and still bounds no gap**. A position is an ordering claim, a gap is a duration claim, and the actual check-in moment stays unknown, so §5 is untouched and the row is transparent to `freeBetween` exactly as a deadline already is (§10d). The Plan gap measures ⁦18:15→20:00⁩ straight through it, identical in both columns. **§3 is the hero, and it names a shipped inconsistency**: `hero-booking.ts`'s `CHECKIN_GRACE_MIN = 120` is a stand-in for the ceiling nobody authored, and it already disagrees with ADR-0171 §6 (which keeps a floor pending until settled or the day ends) — nobody noticed because neither number is real. A ceiling replaces the guess and creates the state the hero exists for, **the window is about to shut**, drawn on the existing `.wp-board-countdown` with a different unit word. It also makes a lodging edge **missable for the first time**, which is the file's only proposed CSS: one rule, `.tlabel.missed`, in the board's own low-alpha-fill recipe and the app's existing `--miss`. **§4 is why the control is gated even though the model is not.** `edgeMeaning` reading an authored window generalises to any event for one branch; the FORM does not, because an ordinary event already draws `18:00–20:00` as a duration and a window draws the same pixels meaning the opposite — the first two frames are deliberately identical. A held edge is immune because its row already carries a word (`צ׳ק-אין`) and a check-in has no duration. **A bidi finding, and the first draft of the file had it backwards** — which is the render earning its keep: `.tr-time` carries `dir="auto"` and a digits-only run has no strong character, so `auto` falls back to `ltr` and a range is safe there. What breaks is `UnplacedCommitment`'s `.as`, which renders `${label} · ${when}` with **no `dir` at all**, so a strong Hebrew character leads and `17:00–21:00` renders reversed. §2's third column draws both. The build rule is ADR-0118's own: isolate the **numeric run** (`ltrIsolate`), never trust the container's direction. **Built 2026-08-13 as [ADR-0184](../decisions/0184-an-edge-can-be-a-window.md).** א was taken on its own measurement (60px charged to everyone who never uses the feature), §2b's placement was decided in session, and §4's gate shipped exactly as drawn — the model reaches an ordinary event and the control does not. What the file still holds open is what the ADR holds open: whether a NARROW window should bound a gap, and ADR-0017's device pass on the two new pieces of CSS. Renders through the app's real CSS (14 shipped stylesheets inlined by [`mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs`](../../mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs)). State toggles: ערכת צבעים (בהיר · כהה) · מסך (⁦360×640⁩ · ⁦390×844⁩) · חלון בטופס (כבוי · דלוק, and כבוי is the default the brief asks for) · שעה בלוח (⁦16:00⁩ · ⁦17:30⁩ · ⁦20:30⁩ · ⁦21:30⁩).

- [`mockups/document-types-in-one-row-v1.html`](../../mockups/document-types-in-one-row-v1.html) — **סוגי מסמך בשורה אחת · what eight types do to a picker and to a badge set** (2026-08-13, session 260) — two owner reports against [PR #586](https://github.com/assafmanor/waypoint/pull/586), which grew `DOCUMENT_TYPE` from four to eight: _"הזמנה and אחר use the same emoji - big no no"_ and _"there used to be only one line of categories to select from, now there's 3. It looks ugly and is hard to use."_ **Promoted and BUILT the same day** as [ADR-0052](../decisions/0052-document-lifecycle-view-manage-and-feedback.md) §6/§7/§8's 2026-08-13 session-260 amendment, which withdraws two decisions the session-259 amendment had just made (§2's `🧾` and §4's card grid). Both halves are the same failure: #586 answered "there are more types now" by **scaling what was already there** — a 4-up card grid became `columns={3}` over three rows, and a badge set chosen to be distinct at four gained a fifth paper glyph — and never asked whether either shape survives eight. **Reading the code answered the second report before anything was drawn:** the app already runs this exact control at a LARGER count. `CategoryField` (ADR-0109 §11) puts **nine** `EventCategory` options in one horizontally-scrollable row — `Field` › `.category-pills` › `ChoiceGrid layout="pills"` — and `ChoiceGrid`'s own doc comment names the reason ("too many options for a fixed grid on a narrow phone"). So **§1 proposes the third host of a shipped mechanism and costs zero new CSS**, and the measurement that settles the scale is off-frame: the eight document pills scroll **713px** against the nine shipped category pills' **806px** at the same width, because the Hebrew document labels are the shorter set. **What the render found, and it is the reason the file exists.** The report said "ugly and hard to use", which sounds like taste; at ⁦360×640⁩ it is arithmetic. `.booking-sheet` caps at `80vh` with `overflow-y: auto`, and the grid makes the upload body **652px against a 488px cap** — so the sheet opens with **140px to scroll before `העלה` is reachable** and the קובץ tiles, the one field the form cannot be saved without, are cut off; the pill row is **0px**. The picker itself goes **202px → 38px** (−164px, 81%). Second render finding: **the ⁦44px⁩ touch floor is free here** — at `--floor=1` the row is 46px against the grid's 202px and still 0px of scroll — so the only argument left against ADR-0017's floor is that `.category-pills` is shared with `EventForm`/`NoteSheet`/`MapPlaceForm` (whose card height is arithmetic, ADR-0148 §1), which is a scope question for the owner and not a room question. It is therefore a **control**, not a decision this file pretends to have made. **§2 is the badge collision, drawn at the size a badge is actually read at** — 36px `.wp-listrow-badge`, in a strip of all eight, because a badge is never read beside its own label, it is read beside the seven others. 🧾 and 📄 are two white pages of the same silhouette, and the pair is not new: `packages/shared/src/icons.ts:70` already holds `'🧾', '📄'` adjacently as two options for one idea in the _services_ icon group. Recommended candidate **🛎️** (unused anywhere in the repo, no silhouette twin in the set, and it covers all of what הזמנה means — hotel, table, RSVP), with 📅 · 📑 · 🔖 · today's 🧾 as toggles, since a glyph is a feel call a device pass owns. 📅 is argued against rather than merely unpicked: `Icon.tsx:170` already retired it from its two jobs. **🛎️ was taken** (owner, 2026-08-13). Rejected in the notes panel: a ⁦4×2⁩ grid (solves three rows with a shape that has no second host, no self-centring, and eight card widths set by `בריאות`), `disclosure` for the upload form (#586 just stopped guessing a type, so the collapsed state would be "כלום" plus a tap), and re-glyphing `other` instead of `reservation`. Renders through the app's real CSS (10 shipped stylesheets inlined by [`mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs`](../../mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs)); `lib/useCenterSelected.ts` is transcribed into the file rather than described, so the selected-pill centring in the עריכה state is drawn and not asserted. State toggles: ערכת צבעים (בהיר · כהה) · מסך (⁦360×640⁩ · ⁦390×844⁩) · מצב (העלאה ללא בחירה · עריכה של כרטיס) · גובה גלולה (⁦35px⁩ · ⁦44px⁩) · התג של הזמנה (🛎️ · 📅 · 📑 · 🔖 · 🧾). **§§3–4 answer a third report that arrived mid-file** — _"Also add search like bookings and notes have (where category titles are also shown)"_ — and the parenthetical is the whole design question, since documents state the category on screen already, as a **group heading**. Put to the owner, who asked for all three readings drawn with a recommendation: **א׳** chips replace the headings, **ב׳** chips and the headings both, **ג׳** search only with the headings moving into the results. One thing the parenthetical settles by itself, and it is not the fork: the chip **density**. The two shipped rows disagree — `IndexBookingsView` renders worded pills, `IndexNotesView` renders them `compact` (glyph + count, the word surviving only as `aria-label`, ADR-0122 §2) — and "where category titles are also shown" is the bookings one, so all three shapes draw worded chips. **Decided in session: ב׳ — the chips come and the group headings stay** (owner, 2026-08-13), against a recommendation of א׳; all three stay drawn, because what was compared is the record. The recommendation's numbers, which are also the price of the call: the `.filter-row` is **32px in all three** (its height comes from the 32px search button, not from the pills), so ג׳ is not the cheap option it sounds like — it pays exactly what א׳ pays for a row holding one button — and the same twelve documents measure **733px flat against 928px grouped**, i.e. the group shape costs **195px** in six headings and six card frames, growing with every type that has a document. א׳ also states the type **once** where ב׳ states it twice (a selected chip leaves one group whose heading repeats it), and it is the only one of the three that leaves all three Index screens the same shape. א׳ would have **reversed ADR-0098 §2**'s "the documents screen keeps its present type-grouped list as-is" and retired the plural `t.docs.group` copy; ב׳ keeps both, which is the other half of why it is cheap to build. **The call made §4 simpler than either draft of it, and that was not in the forecast:** because the screen keeps `.doc-group`, the results reuse **the screen's own grouped renderer** rather than a shape of their own — which deletes the file's only proposed CSS beyond the optional touch floor (a `.gt` re-padded for life inside a `.listcard`, which only א׳'s single flat list ever needed), so **the whole search ships zero new CSS**. **The one non-obvious part of building ב׳** is written into the file rather than left to the build: a group that empties is NOT `groups.filter(g => g.docs.length > 0)` — that is the `.filter()`-instead-of-reveal one-off ADR-0120 exists to end, and it pops a heading and a card frame out with no collapse while the rows beside them animate. `RevealRow` is exported and until now used only by its own test; a `.doc-group` wraps in one with `visible={countVisible(rows) > 0}`, measured collapsing to **0px**. **§4 draws both results shapes — the chosen one and the rejected one — and neither is a new mechanism:** `RevealList` already takes `renderBefore` for exactly this and the Map is its first consumer (`Map.tsx:2674`), with the heading derived from the **visible** rows only — otherwise a group whose rows all filter out keeps a heading over nothing. That one placement is the file's only proposed CSS beyond the optional touch floor: a heading emitted inside the list container needs the card's own inline padding and the hairline `.map-grouphead::after` already draws, six lines under `.index .listcard .gt`, a selector the shipped `.doc-group .gt` cannot reach. **The search TERMS are drawn as settled, not forked:** a booking's `matchesQuery` already searches the type label singular AND plural AND a synonym table (`index-bookings.ts:92`, which is why its placeholder says «או קטגוריה»), so a document's terms are the same shape and typing `כרטיס` finds every ticket — including in א׳, where no heading says the word. `SearchOverlay`'s own header comment anticipated this consumer in as many words: _"a future document search … reuses this rather than growing a second one-off"_.

- [`mockups/tasks-row-and-refusals-v1.html`](../../mockups/tasks-row-and-refusals-v1.html) — **משימות · השורה, שני הסירובים, והרצועה בשני הבתים** (2026-08-15, session 271) — the tasks design session's deliverable, promoting [ADR-0188](../decisions/0188-a-tasks-tick-is-a-sibling-and-the-leading-element-says-who-owns-the-outcome.md) (**Proposed; design only — nothing here is built**) and closing the [tasks design brief](../planning/2026-08-15-tasks-design-brief.md)'s **§A, §B and §D**. Its §C/§E/§F are deliberately out of scope, on the precedent of ADR-0153 deferring its own §C. **Reading the code moved the question before a pixel was drawn:** §A calls a task row "the first managed list row with an interactive element that is not the ⋯" and that is **false** — `PlaceBadge` already is one, via `ListRow`'s shipped `onShowOnMap`, and the app solved it with a `role="button"` **span inside** the trigger (`PlaceBadge.tsx:112`), which is a third option §A does not list. So the brief's sibling conclusion is right for a reason it does not give: the parser finding binds a real nested `<button>` only, and what rules out the span is ARIA plus having to swallow the row's tap on every press. §1 draws all five candidates (nested button · span-in-trigger · leading sibling · trailing beside the kebab · `SettleControl` at `compact`) and **reproduces ADR-0160 §4 live rather than quoting it** — written as a real nested button, the row reads back **4 children instead of 3**. **Two defects the render found in the file's own first draft, both invisible in source:** `border-radius: 50%` clips the _hit region_, so the 44px control answered only **83%** of its box and a corner tap **opened** the task instead of completing it (fixed by taking `.wp-listrow-kebab`'s rounded-square recipe → 95%, probed with a 2px `elementFromPoint` grid); and the lead slot needs `z-index: 1` because it uses the kebab's negative-margin overhang while sitting **first** in DOM order, where the kebab is last. **§3 is the risky claim and it does not resolve on pixels** — the two readings of the brief's §2 measure identically (364/364 at 360, 306/306 at 390), so the file says so instead of dressing a preference in a number; the argument is that the literal reading puts an **inert button** at the row's most prominent position on three of five rows. The proposal amends the brief's §2: the two kinds stay one noun, one list, one sort, **except the one element that is a verb** — manual leads with the tick, automatic with the derivation's own badge (PlanHome's `CHECK_ICON`), title columns landing **7px** apart and title blocks at **215 vs 195px**. The two refusals are answered in the `⋯` sheet, where the question is asked: the verbs are **absent**, never disabled, with `RowManageSheet`'s existing subject slot stating the reason once above them. **§5 found that `.chk-row` is `ListRow` written a second time** (`screens.css:1319`) inside a `.checklist` card that is `.index .listcard` renamed — so §D is a deletion, not an addition — and the first draft of that convergence **broke at 360px and not at 390**, `.chk-cta` leaving the automatic title **101.8px** against the manual row's **195px**; the CTA button is therefore deleted (ADR-0061 §1 survives it — `ListRow` has a tap where `.chk-row` is a `div`) and the rejected variant is kept as an off-frame probe so the number outlives the claim. Both Homes are the same `.checklist` card and the same row: Trip carries **manual only** (an automatic task's deadline is departure, so mid-trip all five would be permanently overdue), capped at 3 with an overflow row, **242px**; Plan carries the converged list with a toggle between readiness-first and pure urgency, so the burial the screen's own order would cause is visible rather than argued. Interactive: theme · width · how many are due (0 · 1 · 3 · 7) · Plan's order · and the tick and kebab both press. **Renders all four theme×width combinations with the webfonts loaded and no console errors** — so ADR-0152's fallback-font caution does not bite here, and the file says that rather than repeating the warning by rote. **Own CSS is deliberately small**: one new `ListRow` slot (`.wp-listrow-lead`) plus six `.tsk-*` rules. Two labelled mockup-only overrides, and the first of them **shipped wrong in the first draft and the owner caught it** — recorded because it is this catalog's own recurring failure: §3's menus are `RowManageSheet` → `Sheet` → `Modal variant="sheet"`, which portals, so a static page has to draw them inline. The first draft drew a bare `.modal-card` with invented `.modal-head`/`.modal-body` children that exist in **no stylesheet**, and the sheet's `padding: var(--space-4)` lives on `.modal-overlay[data-variant='sheet'] .modal-card` — an ancestor that was never drawn — so both menus rendered at **0 padding**. Same class as `notes-screen-v1.html`'s own `.index` correction and exactly `references/pitfalls.md`'s "real CSS over an invented tree is not the app": the class names were right and the nesting was not, so it looked plausible and was measuring nothing. Now the real tree (`.modal-overlay[data-variant='sheet'] > .modal-card > .modal-title`), with only the **layer** undone — position, scrim, entrance animation — and the padding, max-width and rounded-top-only radius all arriving from the app; a probe row in the measurement table reads the computed padding back (**16px**) so a regression is visible rather than plausible. The second override is `.app { height: 100vh }` undone inside a frame, while `.body`'s 92px nav tail is deliberately **kept**. **Left to a device pass:** whether a 26px ring reads as pressable under a thumb, whether control-versus-badge reads as "who owns this" unexplained, and the final band cap.

- [`mockups/task-editor-and-read-v1.html`](../../mockups/task-editor-and-read-v1.html) — **משימות · הטופס והקריאה** (2026-08-15) — **Accepted and BUILT**, promoted by [ADR-0189](../decisions/0189-the-editor-uses-the-idiom-the-app-already-had-and-a-task-is-read-where-it-sits.md) ([build note](../planning/2026-08-15-tasks-editor-built.md)). Two places the shipped app deliberately differs from the drawing, both argued in the ADR: `Choice` grew a **`lead?: ReactNode`** rather than the `AvatarPerson` §3 proposed (the unassigned option is a person-shaped absence and no `AvatarPerson` can express it), and the open-foot was **generalised into `RowOpenFoot` + `.row-open-*`** rather than reused under its note-era names. The running app measures **44px** for the flag and **45.7px** for an assignee pill, against the file's 44 and 46. The half [ADR-0188](../decisions/0188-a-tasks-tick-is-a-sibling-and-the-leading-element-says-who-owns-the-outcome.md) did not cover: that ADR designed the row, the two menus and both Home bands, and phase 1 built an **editor** anyway from `NoteSheet`'s shape without rendering it. Drawn against four owner reports on the shipped build ([session note](../planning/2026-08-15-tasks-editor-design-session.md)). **Reading the code moved two of them before a pixel was drawn:** _"the important button is really ugly"_ has a measurable defect under it — the app already ships the boolean-in-a-form idiom (`EventForm`'s `יש הזמנה` row, ADR-0136 §1: `.field` + `ToggleChip tone="cta"` + **`size="touch"`**, whose stated job in `toggle-chip.css` is the ADR-0017 floor) and phase 1's chip has none of the three, so it measures **29px against 44**; and _"של כולנו isn't a good description"_ is a **name collision with phase 6**, not a nit — brief §6's three states are _nobody_ ("one of us"), _one person_ and _everyone_ (`completedBy[]`, phase 6), and phase 1 gave the first state the third's word. A third report, _"no way to see the task info"_, turned out stronger than reported: `body` is written by the editor and rendered by **nothing**. **What the render found and reading did not:** §3's first draft grew a **second scroller** — a bespoke `flex-wrap` grid of 60px avatar columns, which at a realistic roster (five people + unassigned) measured **156px across three lines** against the shipped pill row's 21px. Redrawn as what it should have been from the start — `ChoiceGrid layout="pills"` with a person where the glyph goes, so the scroll, the snap, the edge mask, `useCenterSelected` and the radiogroup ARIA all arrive from the primitive and `Choice` grows exactly one optional field — it is **48px, one line, 639px of scroll width**. It also establishes that **a new host is not bound by the app's deferred 44px pill debt**: `choice-grid.css` records that `.choice-pill` is 36px "here AND on the shipped category selector" and that raising it moves three shipped surfaces, so the new row scopes its own sizing the way `.category-pills` already does and measures **46px against the shipped 28px**. §4 answers the read with ADR-0153 §4's shipped idiom rather than a new surface — the row **opens where it is** (`.note-open-foot` reused verbatim, and the proposal is to rename it rather than copy it), **+46.3px** against **216.5px** for the detail sheet drawn beside it and rejected. The whole form goes 419px → 470px, and the row's details mark costs **0px**. Interactive: theme · width. (It also carried a live three-way picker for the word for the unassigned state — removed once the owner answered, with the two losing candidates and why they lost kept in the notes panel; a control for a closed fork is a fork that gets re-opened.) **Renders all four theme×width combinations with the webfonts loaded and no console errors.** **Two errors in its own first draft are recorded in its header rather than quietly fixed**, because they are this catalog's recurring failure: the avatars were an invented `.avatar.av-md` tree that rendered as bare initials with no circle and no hue (the real one is `.wp-av.wp-av-sm` + an inline `var(--id-*)`), and §1's "before" frame was drawing the _proposed_ assignee row, so the pair differed only in which option was lit. Both are `references/pitfalls.md`'s "real CSS over an invented tree is not the app", and both looked plausible. **The owner answered all three open forks on 2026-08-15 and the file was re-rendered against each** (session note): `חשוב` not `חשובה` (a label does not inflect for `משימה`; the "before" frames keep `חשובה` because they are the record of what shipped), **`לא משויך`** for the unassigned state — **and its default comes back**, which reverses the file's own first draft, because the word is what changes the answer: a presumed `של כולנו` is a **claim** that can be false, a presumed `לא משויך` is a **description of the form's state** that cannot be, and what is saved is identical either way. And the Index order **`הזמנות · משימות · מסמכים · פתקים`**, ordering the three below the spine by whether a tile can be LATE, with bookings keeping the lead it has held since ADR-0047/0049 — the earlier `משימות`-first recommendation was dropped against `Index.tsx`'s own comment that a task's prominence comes from the Home bands rather than from chrome. **A render finding from the revision itself, kept because it is the same failure as the two above:** making the word a single constant made the **"before" frames render it too, erasing the phase-6 collision §3 exists to show** — one shared renderer is what keeps a pair honest and also what lets one edit change both halves. **Still owed:** the ADR that promotes this, and two device-pass questions — whether a 38px avatar in a pill reads as a person at arm's length, and whether a selection ring reads as "chosen" beside an avatar carrying a strong hue of its own.
- [`mockups/a-task-row-that-matches-its-neighbour-v1.html`](../../mockups/a-task-row-that-matches-its-neighbour-v1.html) — **שורת משימה שמתיישרת עם השכנה שלה** (2026-08-16) — **Accepted and BUILT** ([ADR-0191](../decisions/0191-a-task-marks-its-host-and-lives-in-a-section-the-host-already-has.md) §5 reversed and §6–§8 added · [session note](../planning/2026-08-16-tasks-host-surface-corrections.md)), which promotes this file. **Seven owner reports on a host's task section after phase 4 merged** — it was drawn for four, a fifth arrived mid-draw, and two more (the Map card's overlapping sections and the assignee painting over the deadline) turned out to be defects rather than design and were fixed against the app rather than here. Supersedes nothing; it **reverses** [ADR-0191](../decisions/0191-a-task-marks-its-host-and-lives-in-a-section-the-host-already-has.md) §5 and proposes a new §6, so it is that amendment's design reference rather than a new ADR's. **§2 is the load-bearing one, and the app's own numbers are what make it an argument:** both section rows span 31→330, but the task's text starts at **x=276** against the note's **x=316** — a **40px** indent from the tick's `ListRow` lead column — with the title at **700/13.5px** against **400/13px**, a clipboard icon in one section header and none in the other, and `·` spaced in one meta line and not the other. Four differences, one of them structural. The mockup reproduces the 40px exactly and then closes it: **0px** of text-edge delta, the task row falling **61px → 34.8px** onto the note row's own height, **and the notes paying nothing** (34.8 → 34.8). Three row candidates are drawn (א shared lead column · ב the tick over the gutter · ג the tick replacing the 2px rule); א is the default. **§3 is the cost switching creates:** `.tsk-tick` is the SCREEN's 44px box, which is exactly why a `.note-item` looked broken with one dropped into it, so the section gets the same control at a second density — clearing ADR-0017's floor through [ADR-0177](../decisions/0177-a-when-reads-as-a-sentence.md)'s `::after` reach, measured at **44px** of touch against a 20px paint. The size stays a control, not a decision. **§1 answers a report that turned out wider than it read:** a `done` event does not merely keep its `tsk-mark` — its open task is sitting in the Trip Home band, so a settled host generates live obligations on the landing screen. The rule chosen is **stop counting, stay readable**: no mark, out of the band and the Index count, still listed struck in the section — which is what a settled task already does, so no new vocabulary is spent. **§4** measures the preview-sheet complaint at **177px → 55px** before the maybe sheet's own verb. **§5 is OPEN and says so**, drawing (ג) `＋ משימה` opening the real editor staged on save against (ה) a host form carrying no task path at all. **And §5 carries a correction to its own first draft, which is the part worth reading:** it argued that "a note has no life outside its host and a task has its own screen and tile" — **wrong, because notes have an Index tile and a screen too**, and the owner said so. What survives is narrower and is all §5 now rests on: a note **is** its body, so a free-text composer omits nothing from it, while a task's **deadline** is what puts it on the band, so a title-only composer systematically produces the weak kind. **§6 is the fifth report** (_"in the tasks page, linked tasks don't show their host"_) **and it designs nothing:** the notes screen already carries `.note-host` + `NOTE_HOST_ICON` + `useNoteHostWayIn`, `noteHost()` reads only the five FKs so it widens to `HostedRow` in one word, and the tasks screen never picked any of it up — rule 8, not a drawing. The one real constraint drawn there: **a task row has no badge slot** (ADR-0188 §1 — a row with a `lead` has no `badge`), so where a note says its host **twice** a task says it **once**, and the only slot is the meta line that already carries a deadline and an assignee. Measured at 360 on the worst case (an `important` dated task, the longest host name): the chip costs **1px** of row height and takes **32%** of the meta line, under `.note-host`'s own 47% ceiling — it fits without wrapping. Renders all four theme×width combinations, webfonts loaded, **no console errors**. Interactive: theme · width · row candidate · tick size. **One drawing defect this file hit and fixed, worth knowing for the next mockup:** putting `class="app"` on a frame's body inherits `App.css`'s `min-height: 100dvh` and stretched every frame on the page to the viewport, so four sections rendered as tall empty phones — the app tokens a frame needs come from `:root`/`[data-theme]`, never from the shell class. **Where it differs from the shipped app:** §5's fork was resolved to **(ג)** and the form's control is QUIETER than a read surface's (owner: the form is "not necessarily the main add point"), which this file does not draw; and the screen's meta line is **two rows** — deadline on the first, host chip and assignee on the second — which the owner proposed after seeing the four single-line candidates rendered, and which beats all of them (1px against the accidental wrap, and the chip gets 89px instead of 73 so a host name reads whole).

- [`mockups/a-task-in-the-hero-horizon-v1.html`](../../mockups/a-task-in-the-hero-horizon-v1.html) — **משימה באופק של הכותרת המורמת** (2026-08-16) — **Proposed; awaiting owner sign-off.** The tasks brief's **§E**, the last of its three open questions and the one phase 5 cannot start without: _"What a task looks like beside `איפה` / `פתק` / `הסדרה`, and whether it is a read, a completion, or both."_ Half is already settled and this file does not reopen it — brief §13 made the slot a **read**, and the owner was offered the tickable version and declined — so it answers only what it looks like. It promotes an **amendment in place to [ADR-0160](../decisions/0160-the-hero-lifts-and-shows-a-horizon.md) §3/§13**, never a new ADR beside them, which brief §11 requires by name. **Read this entry before assuming §3 still admits three things: it admits four already.** §T (2026-08-08) added an attached **document** without renumbering §3, so a task is the SECOND fourth-thing and §T's own answer is the precedent this file has to argue against rather than inherit — §T made the document a **chip** in `hero-acts`, and §1 refuses that for a task on §O's own definition of that row (_"every way out of a point, in ONE row"_): a task has no destination, so an inert chip in a row of actions is the promise §H already said a surface cannot keep. **And a second thing reading found: §13's own second bullet is FALSE in the shipped app.** It forbids a note on the **next** event — _"deliberately unbuilt and named so it cannot arrive quietly"_ — and `HeroLift.tsx` has rendered `<Note point={next} />` since the hero's first build (`git log -S` → `9b284e57`, #449). It arrived quietly on day one and no amendment recorded it; the correction belongs in this amendment whether or not a task reaches `הבא בתור`. **What is decided without a fork:** a `hero-part` block after `פתק` and before `הסדרה`, in `.hero-note`'s geometry exactly (§2, measured at **0px** of line-start delta — the number ADR-0191 §5 shipped wrong at **40px** and reversed on a device); **no host chip**, because on this surface the host IS the point and a chip repeats a title three lines up, which is the defect ADR-0188 §1 already removed once; **nothing on `אחר כך`**, enforced by `HeroThen` carrying no id (ADR-0160 §12's condition, which §T confirmed holds with no code); **nothing on a settled host** through `isOnSettledHost` (ADR-0191 §6 — the same set the bands and the Index tile already read), worth **68px** of card; and **nothing interactive at all**, which pays the brief's §A constraint at zero cost — ADR-0160 §4's nested-`<button>` parser finding cannot be reached by a block with no control, and there is no touch target to hold against ADR-0017's 44px floor. **Three things the render found that reading did not**, and two of them are this file's own first draft rather than the app's: the **assignee face wrapped onto a line of its own** at 360px, because the first draft used one `flex-wrap: wrap` line with `margin-inline-start: auto` — ADR-0160 §O's own finding recurring in the same file (flex breaks lines by _hypothetical_ size, so `min-width: 0` and `ellipsis` are unreachable), which produced §2b's fork and its answer, **the deadline on its own line**, exactly what the host section already ships for the identical crowding (ADR-0191 §8); the first measurement **reported 18px of drift that is not drift** — it compared the note's text edge against the task's _title_ edge, and the 18px is the `important` star, which the shipped section row inserts identically, so the table now carries the two as separate rows; and **the face costs 0px**, measured, because it rides the title line's own height. **The ink finding is the one worth quoting, and it is light-mode only** — which is why looking at the dark screenshot was not enough. `.tsk-due` is `var(--amber-deep)` and `.tsk-due.late` is `var(--miss-deep)`, paper inks: on the board in LIGHT they measure **2.93:1** and **2.44:1**, the first under WCAG AA's 4.5 for 11px text and the second under even 3.0; in DARK the same tokens are lifted and measure 6.15–7.39, i.e. fine. That is [ADR-0158](../decisions/0158-dark-mode-ships-and-the-ink-a-surface-carries-is-a-token.md) §15 arriving exactly as it did for `SettleControl` in ADR-0160 §11, and the fix invents nothing — the board already holds `#f0c785` (10.13:1, `.hero-clockshift`) and `#f0a09b` (7.82:1, `.wp-settle.board .wp-settle-btn.skip`). **Fit, and the honest framing of it:** the crowded two-point hero drawn here is **722px against 622px of room** at 360×640 **with no tasks at all**, i.e. already 100px over before the block arrives — the class §8's own table tabulates and the reason the one scroller exists. So the claim is the modest one: the block adds **87px across two points**, and choosing "three" adds **40px** more, which at 390×844 is what turns a card fitting by 17px into one over by 23px. **Three forks are left OPEN for the owner and are controls rather than decisions this file pretends to have made:** §3 the mark glyph (checkbox · bare ✓ · none — recommending the checkbox, the same one the section header and the host mark already carry non-interactively, with the risk named that a checkbox on a read-only surface can read as pressable, which a desktop screenshot cannot settle); §4b the assignee face (recommending it — ADR-0190 §6 says a task says who owes it, against ADR-0153 §4's reason for the note having no author face, and it measures free); §5 how many (recommending one plus `ועוד N`, the rule `פתק` already follows). Also proposes generalising `.hero-note-more` to serve both blocks rather than declaring a second identical rule — two consumers on day one is rule 8's threshold. Renders all four theme×width combinations, webfonts loaded, **no console errors**. Interactive: theme · width · line shape · mark · count · face · overdue. **Two new strings only** (`משימה` and `ועוד N משימות`); the deadline words are `t.tasks.due`'s, reused. **DRIFT from the shipped app, 2026-08-16 — §3's mark.** The owner signed off the **ticked** checkbox here and it shipped; a device pass then reversed it (_"the lifted hero shows a tick icon for tasks, this reads as 'task complete' and is misleading"_) and the hero now draws `checkbox-empty`, the same box minus its ✓. **This file is deliberately not retrofitted** — it is the dated record of what was decided then (ADR-0097), and its §3 control still offers the three candidates as drawn. The reasoning lives in [ADR-0160](../decisions/0160-the-hero-lifts-and-shows-a-horizon.md) §U3's amendment: the glyph names a NOUN on the section header and the host mark, and a STATE beside one task's title, which is the distinction this file's "same role at a third surface" argument missed. Note the knock-on for §3's own fallback: the bare `✓` drawn in §3ב is no longer available as the answer to the reads-as-pressable risk, since it is the mark that caused this.

- [`mockups/what-is-missing-when-there-is-nothing-missing-v1.html`](../../mockups/what-is-missing-when-there-is-nothing-missing-v1.html) — **כשאין מה להשלים** (2026-08-16) — **Accepted and BUILT.** Promotes [ADR-0193](../decisions/0193-what-is-missing-counts-everything-open-and-the-plan-hero-lifts.md) §3's third amendment and its new §6. Three owner reports, two of them about one screenshot: _"when everything done it should show some empty state"_, _"on this screenshot it shouldn't even show an empty state - we said limit to 5 and there's two!"_, _"the completed and the X more also look bad"_ — clarified by _"when there are no items there's just a stripe"_. **Read this entry before touching how the checklist folds.** **The stripe is not an ugly empty state, it is a card with nothing in it**: `.checklist` is a 1px-bordered box, and holding only a collapsed `Collapsible` it measures **2px** — with **0 of 2** open tasks visible, because both were due in 16 days and the near/far split classed both as "far". So §1 replaces the split with a **cap**, and the argument is structural rather than a preference: _the first N of a list is never zero, where a predicate can empty it at any length_. It is also a deletion — `soonIds`, `isNear`, `nearTasks`, `farTasks` and `tasksDueSoon` leave `PlanHome`, and the inline list and the lifted hero end up running the same rule off the same derivation. §2 draws the empty state three ways and the recommendation is **inside the card**, which is not decoration: it is what makes the card unable to render with nothing in it, closing the stripe from the other side. §3 draws the two controls three ways on the finding that **they are not the same kind of thing** — `עוד N` continues the list you are reading, `N שהושלמו` opens a different one — so two matching pills state a peerage that does not exist. **A shipped defect the measurement found:** those pills are **22px painted and 22px tappable**, half ADR-0017's 44px floor, and had been since they were written; the winning layout's continuation row is a real 44px and its quiet link clears the floor through an `::after` overlay (`ValueToken`'s idiom) rather than by growing. Measured: 2px → 157.3px, empty block 136.3px against the old hint's 15px, and the chosen control layout costs **+51px** of section against the pills. Interactive: theme · width · section state (הדיווח · רשימה ארוכה · הכול מוכן) · control layout. Renders all four theme×width combinations, no console errors. **Where it differs from the shipped app:** nowhere in kind — the build re-measured the empty state in the running app (136px, `--ok` at `#4cbf85`) rather than trusting the drawing, and the copy is the owner's pick (`אפשר לנשום` / `אין מה להשלים לפני היציאה`) rather than the file's first suggestion.

- [`mockups/the-plan-hero-lifts-and-the-checklist-counts-everything-v1.html`](../../mockups/the-plan-hero-lifts-and-the-checklist-counts-everything-v1.html) — **הכותרת של תכנון מתרוממת, והרשימה מפסיקה לשקר** (2026-08-16) — **Accepted and BUILT** (2026-08-16). Promotes [ADR-0193](../decisions/0193-what-is-missing-counts-everything-open-and-the-plan-hero-lifts.md) and supersedes [ADR-0160](../decisions/0160-the-hero-lifts-and-shows-a-horizon.md) §H, with the [session note](../planning/2026-08-16-plan-hero-lift-design-session.md). One owner message, two reports: _"the home readiness should also show non automatic tasks … right now it says all done where it isn't true - that's misleading"_ and _"we should create a lifted plan hero for upcoming tasks"_. **Read this entry before touching Plan Home's list: the app already renders manual tasks there and the WINDOW is the bug.** `PlanHome` builds its converged list from `tasksDueSoon`, which requires a `dueAt` and admits only overdue-or-within-7-days — so an **undated** task and anything a week out are invisible on the one screen whose countdown reads `בעוד 47 ימים`, and `tasks.ts`' own docstring says why that window exists (_"the right rule for a band you read ON the day"_), which Plan Home is not. A second silent fault settles the repair: `completedManual` has **never** had a date window, so an undated task is invisible while open and appears under `הושלמו` the instant it is ticked — the two halves ask different questions, and only widening makes them agree. **§1** the reported sentence, gated on `converged.length === 0`; the file carries three trip states and the third (`באמת סגור`) exists so that deleting `הכול מוכן 🎉` is visible as a loss rather than a tidy-up — measured, today's frame prints it above **5 open tasks with no checklist card on screen at all**. **§2** two numbers with two names (a second bar is drawn as §2ג and rejected: no fixed denominator, and two stacked bars read as one measurement split in half). **§3** urgent + checks inline, the remainder behind one **44px** row (`CollapseToggle` at a second density). **§4** the lift, banded against the **departure** — the week-keyed alternative is drawn as the left frame and rejected as Trip Home's window returning to the screen §1 just proved it wrong for. **§5** the skin. **Interactive:** theme · width · **trip state** (הדיווח · באמצע ההכנות · באמת סגור) · the far-task collapse · the skin fork, both options kept in the control after the decision so the device pass can still see the loser. **A shipped defect found by reading, not drawing:** `.tsk-more` — Trip Home's `עוד N משימות` overflow row — has **no CSS rule in any stylesheet** and `App.css` has no global `button` reset, so it renders as a **bare 19px UA button** inside a `.checklist` card. §3's left frame draws it, pinned to the mid-preparation fixture whatever the state control says, because in the reported state nothing is due within a week and the bare button would have sat alone in an empty card. **Four measurement bugs this file made and then fixed, all in its own contrast harness, and the fourth is the one worth copying:** stopping the ancestor walk at the first non-transparent background read a **16%-alpha pill fill as opaque salmon** and reported 1:1; a gradient ground with no `background-color` was walked straight past, so the card was measured against the light **page** and the rejected skin reported 3.36:1 (it is 5.02); a translucent ink was read as opaque; and once §5's ramp became long and gradual, **any single stop was wrong too** — the last flatters every row near the top, the first flatters none. The harness composites alpha in both directions and **interpolates a `180deg` gradient at the subject's own vertical position**; copy that function rather than the idea of it, and note that **Chrome drops `180deg` from the computed value** because `to bottom` is the default, so a regex requiring it matches only the authored string. **The skin took three rounds and each changed the technical answer** (all three are in the session note): the file recommended the board-ground option on measurement; the owner chose violet and supplied the fix that removed the file's four ink overrides (deepen the ground, keep the shipped ramp); then _"much much much more gradual, and like violet to dark violet"_ **reversed that conclusion**, because a ramp's top _is_ bright violet and no cool grey survives the upper half however deep the bottom gets. The final shape is one card-wide `linear-gradient` with **no seam**, one new surface token `--plan-surface-3`, and `--on-plan-*` — which is not a new ink family but a **name for the six white-alpha literals `.prep` has always carried** in `screens.css`, tokenised per ADR-0158 §3. `--on-plan-faint` is 0.80 because 0.76 measured _exactly_ 4.5:1 on the top band label. The overdue pill likewise inverts to solid `--miss` with white ink, because on the head's bright violet only a near-white ink clears AA at all. Renders all four theme×width combinations, webfonts loaded, no console errors. **WHERE IT NOW DIFFERS FROM THE SHIPPED APP** (not retrofitted — a mockup is the dated record of what was decided then). Two of its sections were amended the same day on the owner's report against the built screen, and the file still draws the superseded form: **§3's collapse is drawn as a full-width `.chk-more` row at the foot of the card** and shipped that way, then moved into the section head as a `.chk-toggle` — the row was reported _"really ugly (what's this font? Sizing?)"_, and the cause is worth carrying forward because it traps every future caller of `CollapseToggle`: `.wp-collapse-toggle` sets the `font` SHORTHAND to `inherit`, which resets `font-size`/`font-weight` at equal specificity, and `tasks.css` loads before `collapsible.css`, so the row rendered at 16px/400 instead of 13px/700. **And §4 is drawn with five date-keyed bands** (`דחוף · מה חסר · לפני היציאה · בזמן הטיול · ללא תאריך`), which are retired: the lift shows one list in `orderTaskRows`' order capped at 5 with `ועוד N`. The bands' own cost is the reason — they put `ללא תאריך` last regardless of `important`, so a flagged undated task fell below every check, bending the one ladder ADR-0190 §2 set. **Every pixel number is webfont-dependent** — the card measures 543.8px here and 427px in the app under the amended §4, which is the figure to re-check on a device.

- [`mockups/a-form-has-bands-and-one-content-section-v1.html`](../../mockups/a-form-has-bands-and-one-content-section-v1.html) — **לטופס יש פסים, ולתוכן המצורף יש מראה אחד** (2026-08-16) — **Accepted and BUILT** (2026-08-16) — it promotes [ADR-0192](../decisions/0192-a-form-has-bands-and-its-content-sections-have-one-look.md), with the [session note](../planning/2026-08-16-form-bands-and-one-content-section.md). Two owner reports on the event/booking form in one file, because they are one form: _"notes and tasks … are formatted differently, let's align them to the same style (preferably to the tasks style)"_ and _"every time we add a new field or section we just append them to the end, now it looks very messy … let's decide on a new ordering (+ an instruction to think from now on where we add things)"_. **§1** the content band — the gap is four axes, not one (header typography, add control, inset, separation) and a **fifth on an edit, which is the worst of them: the notes are TWO blocks with two headings where the tasks are one**, which is the only reason the string `labelMore` exists. The proposal is one section shape for all three, with the notes' composer as the last row of a single `פתקים` section. **§2** five bands — `מה · איפה · מתי · הזמנה · מצורף` — with two moves: **the place goes above the time**, because it is what derives the zone the times are read in (`EventForm.tsx:418` — type 19:00, then pick a Tokyo place, and the same wall-clock is a different instant), which is also the order `BookingSheet`'s steps already run; and **`סוג` joins the time band**, since hard/soft is a claim about the time (ADR-0011). **§3** the cost of that second move, paid in a sentence rather than a control. **Four things the render found that reading did not, and two are this file's own drafts rather than the app's:** the first draft wrapped every block in mockup chrome, so the two frames differed by four `flex` gaps and four label lines and it reported a **fake 134.7px saving** — the band rail now rides the block the app already renders and adds no box, and the residual is printed as a measured row rather than argued; the first draft also dropped the composer's `Field` **shell** along with its label, which silently strips `field.css`'s border, padding and `min-height: 56px` from the textarea, so **half the measured "saving" was an input shrinking** (the honest figures are **−0.7px on a create** and **−48.7px on an edit**); the `.sec-h` extraction — `.note-sec-h .t` and `.doc-sec-h .t` are already byte-identical twice, so notes must not become a third copy — **left `.add` behind**, and `＋ משימה` rendered as a native button; and the `סוג` sentence's extra clause costs **0px at 360** but **17px at 390**, the opposite way round from the guess, because at 360 it already wraps. **Two dead classes it names rather than fixes:** `tsk-sec-quiet` (ADR-0191 §7's quiet form density, specified and never written — on the backlog) and, found the same day and **fixed** in a separate PR, `.tsk-tick-sec`. Interactive: theme · width · מצב (יצירה · עריכה, both always drawn in §1) · הזחת המחבר, which is a feel call and therefore a control rather than a decision this file pretends to have made. Renders all four theme×width combinations, no console errors. **Where it differs from the shipped app:** the build scoped the `סוג` clause in the derived sentence to the **create** only — a spec found that ADR-0136 §4 counts an existing event as touched, so on an edit the booking toggle never moves the kind and the file's §3 frame announces a change that would not happen. **Read its numbers from `scripts/render.mjs`** — every height depends on Assistant actually loading, and a fallback face reports this file's own blocks 2–7px shorter while staying internally consistent.

- [`mockups/a-third-mark-on-a-host-row-v1.html`](../../mockups/a-third-mark-on-a-host-row-v1.html) — **סימן שלישי על שורת מארח** (2026-08-16) — **Accepted and built** ([ADR-0191](../decisions/0191-a-task-marks-its-host-and-lives-in-a-section-the-host-already-has.md) · [build note](../planning/2026-08-16-tasks-phase-4-built.md)), which promotes this file: the glyph is the **checkbox**, the mark counts **open only**, all **five** hosts carry it, and §5's section ships on every one of them in the order documents → tasks → notes. What the tasks brief's **§F** owes before phase 4 (hosts), whose own words are that the cost is _"measured rather than argued"_. **Reading the code closed most of §F's literal question before anything was drawn:** two marks ALREADY coexist — `NoteMark` (ADR-0153 §6) and `DocumentMark` (ADR-0174 §1) share a meta line on four row types, added under `DocumentMark`'s own stated rule that they are "not the same promise" — so "can a task and a note both mark one row" is answered by shipped code, and only the THIRD is open. **And §F's premise expired:** it inherited "these lines are already measured as full" from ADR-0152 §6c, whose composition rule was **retired 2026-08-09** — the meta line is the sync badge and the marks and nothing else, so there is no text left to compete with. **What is actually open: the glyph** (`NoteMark`'s rule is one silhouette per noun and tasks' silhouette is `check`, but `check` is the completion control's mark — and that collision SHARPENED on 2026-08-16 when the automatic row lost its badge, leaving `check` on a task row as the tick and nothing else); **open-only or all**, which is the one **neither existing mark can answer** because neither has a settled state; **what three marks cost**; and **which hosts**, since two row types carry both marks today and two carry one. **Three findings the render produced and reading did not.** (1) **The cost is per GLYPH, not per mark** — a bare ✓ and a checkbox cost **0px** of row height, the ring costs **+1.8px** because its `inline-grid` moves the line box; that turns §1 from a taste argument into a priced one. (2) **The two host rows are not built the same:** `.wp-event-m` is the hardened `flex` line §6c rebuilt, with every child on one row, while `.wp-listrow-meta` is a plain `display: block` that never was — so "what does a third mark cost" is two questions, not one. (3) **This file's own first draft invented `.wp-sync-badge`**, a class the app does not have, so §3 measured "with the badge present" **without a badge** and reported 15px where the real `SyncBadge` is **19px** — `references/pitfalls.md`'s "real CSS over an invented tree is not the app", in the section built to avoid exactly that. The corrected run reproduces ADR-0152 §6c's 19px. All three glyph candidates measure **20.8px** wide, identical to `NoteMark` — so width is not the axis; reading is. **Revised the same day on four owner reports**, and the revision is most of the file's value. **(1) The event row was an invented tree** — `.wp-event-when` matches no rule in the app — so it is now `EventCard`'s real one (the face is a button, the badge is `PlaceBadge`'s `role="button"` span, the title has its own clamp, the time is `.wp-event-time`), and the row measures **90px** rather than the 71px the invented version reported. **(2) The ring candidate does not sit on the line, and that is §1's answer rather than a bug to fix:** measured **2.5px** below the `NoteMark` beside it and **+1.8px** of row height, where both other candidates sit at **0** and cost **0**. The cause is structural — `.note-mark` and `.doc-mark` are `inline-flex` boxes whose first child is an `.icon`, so their baseline is that icon's text baseline, while the ring is a flex container with no text whose baseline is synthesized from its bottom edge, and `vertical-align` cannot reach it because it is a flex ITEM.
