Left mis-aligned on purpose so §1 is chosen against what it looks like. **(3) §4 was two invented shapes and is now the FOUR real ones**, which is a much stronger finding: `.wp-event-m` is §6c's hardened `nowrap` flex line, `.wp-listrow-meta` is a plain `display: block` that never was hardened, `MaybeCard`'s mark sits in a **corner** opposite the `✕` (corners are finite and both are taken), and the Map place row's tag line has `Map.tsx`'s own comment that the note mark is _"LAST, deliberately: it is the item this line drops to the next row first"_ — so a third mark joins the end of that queue. "What does a third mark cost" is four questions. **(4) A new §5 for SEEING AND ADDING a host's tasks**, which §F never asks and phase 4 needs — §F is the mark only, while phase 4 is "five hosts × (create, read)". Its first draft drew three abstract "readings" and communicated nothing (owner: _"I didn't understand how are tasks for the other entities are created. I want to be able to add (and view!) tasks for events, bookings"_); it now draws the **real surface** — `NoteSection`'s tree on a booking's detail, with a `משימות` section beside the `פתקים` one, its own `＋`, and real ticks. Three readings on that one surface: a section of its own (the notes model verbatim), one merged "what is here" section, or no section at all with the host reached from the task instead. **And the drawing produced a finding prose had missed:** a task in a host's section is a **`ListRow`, not a `.note-item`** — `.note-item` has no lead slot because a note has no completion control, so a 44px `.tsk-tick` dropped into one floats beside the words. The tasks section therefore holds the same row the tasks screen does (tick, star, deadline, assignee all arriving built), at the cost that **two sections on one surface are not the same row shape** — which is §5's actual decision, named rather than smoothed over. **A second alignment report** (owner, twice) traced to the mockup using the **ring** as its default in every section outside §1 — i.e. every frame was showing the one candidate the measurement had already rejected. The default is the aligned `.icon` candidate now; §1 alone still compares all three, with the ring left mis-aligned there on purpose. Verified across all 12 meta lines in the file: only §1's ring frame exceeds 0.6px of spread. Renders all four theme×width combinations, webfonts loaded, **no console errors**. Interactive: theme · width · **what the mark counts**. **Where it now differs from the shipped app:** nowhere in kind — the build re-measured every §5 claim in the running app rather than trusting the drawing, and the two that moved are worth knowing. The booking row's three marks measure **0.6px** of baseline spread live (the drawing's number for the same pair), and the mark's open-only count was verified end to end by ticking one of three tasks and watching the row read **2**. The one thing the drawing could not show is the **selector collision** the build found: sharing `.note-sec` alone made `querySelector('.note-sec')` start finding the tasks section, so the shipped root is `.note-sec.tsk-sec`.
- [`mockups/tasks-open-questions-before-phase-2-v1.html`](../../mockups/tasks-open-questions-before-phase-2-v1.html) — **משימות · מה שצריך להכריע לפני שלב 2** (2026-08-16) — **Open questions; nothing here is decided, and that is the point.** Phase 2 (automatic tasks) was about to be built and reading its design first turned up **five questions nobody had answered**; a **sixth** arrived from the owner's phone while the file was being drawn. Deliberately does **not** redraw what IS settled — the automatic row's shape ([ADR-0188](../decisions/0188-a-tasks-tick-is-a-sibling-and-the-leading-element-says-who-owns-the-outcome.md) §4), the two refusals as absences in the `⋯` (§5), the CTA deletion and the missing sync column (§7). **§1 does the tasks SCREEN carry automatic rows at all** — brief §3 says they render "on the tasks surface", brief §13 and ADR-0188 §6 name only Plan Home; if the screen carries them, `שלי` can never match an untouched check (no row → no assignee), `הושלמו` can never find a derived-done one (no row → no status), and the Index tile counts **7 instead of 3**, so a brand-new trip reads "4 משימות פתוחות" before anyone wrote one. **§2 the order**, which is the file's sharpest number: an automatic task has no `dueAt`, so pure urgency (the screen's settled order, brief §13) drops the first check **183px** down the card at three manual tasks, against **0px** automatic-first — and ADR-0188 §6 **already rejected that exact burial on Plan Home**. The manual-count control (0 · 3 · 8) is the whole argument: at 0 the two orders are identical, so the choice only matters at volume. **§3 what the tap does from the Index** — ADR-0061 §1's CTA-does-the-thing was written about Plan Home, where all five actions close over PlanHome's own state and **three of five are cross-screen**; three readings drawn (navigate away · sheet over the Index · open-in-place with the verb in the foot, which reuses ADR-0189's idiom and is the only one that contradicts "a derived row has nothing to read"). **§4 the completed-collapse would exist twice** (`CollapseToggle` + `.chk-done-sum` vs the `הושלמו` facet chip), which ADR-0188's retirement list never mentions. **§5 `.chk-ppl` retires with nowhere to go** — three readings, measured. **§6 is the owner's, from a real device:** _"the members are prominent in the creation form but you can barely see it in the main tasks view"_. ADR-0188 §3 decided that deliberately ("a name in the meta line, not an avatar") on the ground that an avatar would be **"a second identity system per row"** — **and that ground expired when [ADR-0189](../decisions/0189-the-editor-uses-the-idiom-the-app-already-had-and-a-task-is-read-where-it-sits.md) put `Avatar` in the editor for this same field**; the row would now be reusing the system the feature already established. Four readings, and the title column is where the cost lands: name-only **201px**, mini-avatar+name **201px** (free), avatar in the trailing slot **167px** — which visibly wraps a long title in the frame itself. **Three render findings §6 produced that reading did not:** this file's own first CSS comment claimed a mini avatar costs no height and was **wrong** — an 18px circle in an 11.5px line box took the row 61px → **68.5px**, a tax on every row including ones with no assignee, and two passes of negative block margin landed it at **61.5px against 61px** (half a pixel, written as measured rather than rounded to the claim); the 167px title column's wrap confirms §6ג's number visually; and the mini-avatar reading fixes something that was never reported — today an **unassigned** task's meta says _nothing at all_, indistinguishable from "assigned to someone whose name did not fit", where the editor's own glyph says `לא משויך`. Interactive: theme · width · **how many manual tasks the trip has (0 · 3 · 8)**. **Renders all four theme×width combinations with the webfonts loaded and no console errors.** **ANSWERED and BUILT — [ADR-0190](../decisions/0190-a-readiness-check-is-a-task-row-and-the-checks-sit-inside-the-urgency-ladder.md)** ([build note](../planning/2026-08-16-tasks-phase-2-built.md)). **The file is deliberately NOT retrofitted**, because §2's answer is a **third order it does not draw**: shown automatics-first and pure-urgency, the owner rejected both — _"prioritized or due, overdue tasks should be on top"_, then _"First but also important above them"_ — giving **urgent → checks → the rest**, in ONE list rather than the two cards drawn here. That is the file working as intended rather than failing: it made both candidate orders concrete enough to be turned down, and its 183px burial measurement is what ruled out the settled one. The rest landed as drawn — checks on both surfaces under `הכל` alone, Plan Home keeping its collapse, the passport pips deleted, and §6's mini-avatar reading (free: 201px title column, 61px row).

- [`mockups/a-task-that-holds-a-checklist-v1.html`](../../mockups/a-task-that-holds-a-checklist-v1.html) — **משימה שמחזיקה רשימה** (2026-08-18) — sub-tasks, from the backlog's own line (_"a task holding a checklist of its own"_, owner ask 2026-08-15), which names its three questions so they are not re-derived and requires the third to be answered **together with the tasks brief's §C**. **Promotes [ADR-0196](../decisions/0196-a-task-can-hold-a-checklist-and-the-exclusion-is-paid-once.md) (Accepted; **BUILT 2026-08-18** — see its build log and the [build note](../planning/2026-08-18-sub-tasks-built.md))**, which closes brief **§C** — the last of its six open design questions — and amends [ADR-0193 §2](../decisions/0193-what-is-missing-counts-everything-open-and-the-plan-hero-lifts.md) in place on whether a child counts (it does not). [Session note](../planning/2026-08-18-sub-tasks-design-session.md). **§1 what forgetting the exclusion looks like** — the same nine rows split in two places, 10 rows and `הכל · 10` against 5 and `הכל · 5`, because the backlog line predicted children surfacing "as peers on the screen, in the Index tile's count and in both Home bands" and drawing it is cheaper than arguing it · **§2 the parent row at 0/5 · 2/5 · 5/5** · **§3 the children in the open region** · **§4 the Trip Home band and a host section**, which is where the design is actually tested because a checklist does not fit there · **§5 brief §C, collapsed and open** · **§6 the refusals, measured**. **Reading the code moved the question before a pixel was drawn, and this is the file's load-bearing paragraph:** the exclusion is **not 23 filters, it is one boundary** — six derivations in `lib/tasks.ts` already carry a per-call-site `isManual(task)` guard, and **ADR-0193 §2 was amended precisely because one surface forgot it**, so the Plan hero answered "how many are open" with a different number than the Index tile. Splitting children out at the state boundary makes **nineteen of twenty-three derivations correct unchanged** and derivation twenty-four correct by default. Three more findings from the same audit: **`taskCountFor` has ZERO production call sites** (the live mark path is `hostCountForContext`, at all four), **`openTaskCountsByHost` is correct against children even handed the raw array** (its loop `continue`s when no host FK is set — held up by the refusal on a child's host, not by the split), and **the client-side cascade has a hole** (`dropHostedForHostChange` guards on `change.entityType in NOTE_HOST_FIELD`, which `ENTITY_TYPE.TASK` is not in, so a deleted parent's children would sit orphaned in Dexie until the next cold sync). **What it proposes:** one table (`Task.parentTaskId`), a parent's status **derived** through the exact predicate `automatic-tasks.ts` already ships (`status` is the derivation's answer **unless** the row says `dismissed`), a **read** rather than a control at the parent's leading edge — the same `.tsk-tick` box, same 44px, same ✓, its ring filled to the fraction by a masked `conic-gradient` in `--ok` — and a `2/5` count in the meta line, which is the **only** element small enough to be on every surface. **§C is closed by the same two elements:** an `everyone` task's arc is the GROUP's progress and the ✓ inside it is YOUR answer, so it reads to you first and to the organiser second with no second identity system (ADR-0153 §4); the collapsed rows are deliberately indistinguishable, and the **open region** is what says "items or people" — same region, same row shape, measured at **134px against 134px** for five either way. **The one place it argues with a shipped decision:** ADR-0188 §4's reversal (_"makes no sense that the indication for a complete task is different"_) is about a difference the reader cannot see the cause of, and a task with a checklist prints its cause inside the differing element. **Measured:** parent row **61px** against the leaf's **62px**, lead box **44×44** in both, parent title **219px** at 360 (ADR-0188 measured 195px for a shipped manual row), a child's tick **44×44** of touch from `.tsk-tick-sec`'s shipped recipe, the open block **172px** with its foot, and the refusals at **298.8px against 134px** for five children carrying everything a root task carries. **`2/5` needs no bidi isolate here** — rendered both ways in the real Hebrew context the paint order is identical, since a `CS` separator between two `EN` runs takes the digits' direction — and gets one anyway, because ADR-0118's rule is about the run. **Two defects the render produced, both invisible in source and both recorded in the proposal block:** a percentage in a **radial-gradient colour stop** resolves against the gradient's ray rather than the box, so the arc's first draft drew a **pie chart with a 4px hole** (and a pie at 4/5 is indistinguishable from done); and the count's modifier class was called `.gap`, which `App.css` already ships as a global `display: flex` — the count became a block-level flex box, dropped to its own line, and took the parent row from 61px to **89px**. **Rejected in the notes panel:** a second table, per-derivation filtering, a stored `done` on the parent, a **bulk tick** on the parent (refused on harm rather than cost — everything is LWW, so a bulk un-tick erases four other people's ticks in one press), a ring-around-the-✓ (ADR-0191 §1 refused that shape for the **mark**, on a baseline cause that does not reach the `lead` cell), a progress **bar** (ADR-0193 §2's own argument), the number inside the ring, `.tsk-sep`'s `·` (inked `--line`, and a class with zero production consumers), depth two, and a child with its own host. **§7–§9 ADD THE AUTHORING HALF (2026-08-18, round two)**, on the owner's reading that the first version drew the read side and left authoring as a label in a foot: _"how do you create sub tasks? How do you decide whether a task is one step or has multiple sub tasks? How do you edit them? Add/remove etc."_ They are **live** rather than drawn — type and press Enter to add a step, tap a step's words to edit it in place, press the face to cycle who owes it, ✕ to remove it — because the question is about a flow. **§7 there is no mode to decide:** a task becomes a parent when it gets its first step and stops being one when the last is deleted, both `subtasks.get(id)?.length`; a stored flag would permit a checklist with no steps, and nobody classifies a task before they know whether it has parts. So `＋ משימה` is in **every** open row's foot including a task with none, or nothing could get its first one. **The composer is `useNoteComposer` at a second host, and the reuse has an argument rather than a resemblance:** ADR-0191 §7 refused a title-only composer for a TASK because a task's deadline is what puts it on a Home band, so a title-only box "systematically produces the weak kind" — a step has no deadline **by refusal**, so it genuinely is its title. **Enter commits**, deliberately the opposite of ADR-0152 §6b's rule for a note and on that rule's own reasoning. **§8 the composer row IS the step's editor** (`reopen(index)` verbatim) carrying the assignee chip and the ✕, which is what keeps the READ row unchanged and `.note-item` a two-column grid, so the notes section sharing it pays nothing; a step being edited keeps its own tick rather than the add row's `＋`. No reordering, named as deferred. **§9 the editor holds the same list, and it was drawn twice** — the first version invented a two-field form and the owner replied with a photograph of the real one, so the field order, labels and controls are now read off `TaskSheet.tsx` and `he.ts`. The checklist goes **fourth** (after `מי אחראי`, before `פרטים`): not first, because most tasks have no steps and a variable-height field would push the fields every task uses below the fold; before `פרטים` rather than after, because both answer "what does closing this involve" and the structured one should be the one you reach for. **The empty state is a reveal control**, this form's own idiom (`עד מתי` rests as `הוספת תאריך`). Measured with the `75dvh` cap restated as 75% of the FRAME — `dvh` measures the page in a mockup — **300px** below the fold with five steps against **157px** with none, so the sheet already scrolled and the sticky action bar keeps `שמירה` reachable. **§13 (round three): the word is `תתי משימות`** — the owner's call over the first draft's `משימות בפנים`, reversing this session's own one-noun-including-the-word rule, which was right about the entity and could not name a **field inside a task's own editor** where `משימות` is ambiguous with the task being edited. **And "scroll the new sub-task into view" needed a change in a shipped PRIMITIVE** (owner: _"if needed even change the primitive"_): `.form-actions` is `position: sticky; inset-block-end: 0` INSIDE `.modal-form`'s own scrollport and nothing in the app declares `scroll-padding` except the Map's inline peek, so `scrollIntoView({ block: 'nearest' })` in **any** sheet-form parks its target under the bar. **A/B'd live in this file, and it is a control you can press:** **+15px** of clearance with `scroll-padding-block-end: var(--form-actions-h)`, **−53px** without — the composer entirely beneath a 55px bar, the same number at three added steps and at six. Latent for `EventForm`, `BookingSheet`, `DocumentUploadSheet` and `TaskSheet`; `.body` needs none of it, since `.nav` is a sibling of that scrollport rather than inside it. **The first attempt at that A/B reported "no difference"** because it wrote the override onto the element and `paint()` rebuilds the DOM before the scroll runs — a comparison that rebuilds its own subject measures nothing. **And one shipped string retires, with the code saying so itself:** `titlePlaceholder` is `משהו אחד שצריך לעשות`, and `he.ts` gives the reason for `אחד` — _"it is the model's own bound, since a task holding a checklist is a separate feature nobody has built"_. On a create the steps stage — the **fourth** consumer of a pattern the app runs three times, needing one type widening on `writeStagedTasks`' `where` rather than a fifth hook. **TWO MORE SHIPPED-CSS TRAPS, the first reported by the owner before the file was re-read** (_"some css issues with line alignment (the + and the text field for instance, the specific sub task editing)"_): **`App.css:1037` and `field.css` both define `.field` and only one is reset** — the `margin-top: 18px` App.css carries reaches every `.field` in the app, and inside a grid row that is a shove rather than a gap, putting the composer's box **9px** below the `＋` in all three places it appears; and **a primitive that styles by element has to be out-specified** — `.field input` is (0,1,1) against a bare `.tsk-kid-in` at (0,1,0), so `border: 0` lost and the row painted a rounded box **inside** a rounded box (ADR-0195's `.tsk-tick:hover .icon` one surface over). Measured after both: the `＋`, the box and the assignee chip share one centreline to **0px**, the box's edge sits **0px** off the step text's column, and the composer row fell from 55px to **35px** against a step's 20px. Also measured: the foot's `＋ משימה` at **53px** on a **38px** foot, the editor's field at **175px** for five steps and a box against the open region's 172px. **Where it differs from the shipped app:** §1–§13 are now **built**, so `.tsk-arc`, `.tsk-ring`, `.tsk-count`, `.tsk-kids` and the composer row all exist and this file is a reference rather than a proposal. **Two of its drawings are now out of date and the app is right, not the mockup** (2026-08-19 owner reports, amended into ADR-0196 in place): the parent's lead is drawn here as a READ and ships as a **control** whose press settles the whole checklist, and the checklist's indent is drawn at 58px where the app derives it from the parent's title (54px minus the lead cell) after 58px measured 7px short of any alignment. What it still draws ahead of the app is **§5**, the brief's §C — `assignedToAll` is not in the model yet (owner's call to ship it after), so nothing renders the arc for an `everyone` task. **Renders all four theme×width combinations with the webfonts loaded and no console errors.** Interactive: theme · width · §1's two readings · §2's fraction (0/5 · 2/5 · 5/5) · the deadline/count separator (a control, because `.tsk-sep` is inked `--line` and barely a mark) · §5's own tick in both frames, so "legible to me first" can be pressed rather than asserted · and the whole of §7–§9, which share one live checklist. Left to a device pass: whether a 2.5px arc at 26px reads as a fraction under a thumb, and whether the count alone carries a parent at `0/n`, where the ring is the same **1.21:1 / 1.33:1** hairline already on the backlog.

- [`mockups/a-tick-that-is-seen-v1.html`](../../mockups/a-tick-that-is-seen-v1.html) — **סימון משימה · הביט שנראה, והשורה שממתינה לו** (2026-08-17) — seven candidate beats for a task's completion control, from the owner's _"please design a nice tick animation for the task rows (in the tasks and the home screens)"_ and then, on the first render, _"I want more exciting animations! … some playing with the tick sign"_. **ANSWERED and BUILT the same day — [ADR-0195](../decisions/0195-a-tick-is-answered-once-and-the-row-waits-for-it.md)**, which this file promotes: the owner took **ה · הקפיצה** plus both corrections, and the shipped control is `ui/TaskTick.tsx` (the beat, the hold, and a flush-on-unmount) with the keyframes in `ui/tasks.css`. **One thing the build did NOT take, recorded in §6 in place:** the hover repair shipped as a **deletion** rather than as the drawn `@media (hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)` hint — six probes disagreed about whether a hovered pseudo-element's colour reads back at all, and a rule nobody can measure is the `className`-with-no-rule risk in a different hat; it was mouse-only on a phone-primary app either way. **Where the file now differs from the app:** it still draws all seven candidates and both rejected timings, which is the point of keeping it — only ה ships, and the `mk-*` classes it is drawn under do not exist in the app at all. **Reading the code moved the question before a pixel was drawn:** the tick has **no motion at all** today (`tasks.css` swaps `::before`'s background and flips the ✓ from `opacity: 0` with no transition, no animation and no token), and on **every surface the ask names the row leaves in the same frame** — `taskMatchesFacet` returns `false` for a settled task under both open facets, so the tasks screen collapses it over the same `--t-base` the beat would take, and neither Home band goes through `RevealList` at all, so the row **unmounts**. The only surface a ticked row stays on is a host section. So the deliverable is a **pair**: a beat, and a **hold** — and the hold is already written, because `lib/one-shot.ts`'s `playBeat` returns _"the duration it will take, in ms — 0 when nothing will animate"_ precisely so a caller can sequence the departure after it. The proposal is therefore a **fourth `BEAT`**, not a mechanism (root rule 8). **And it must be `playBeat` rather than a CSS rule on `[aria-pressed='true']`**, which cannot tell "just ticked" from "arrived already done" and would play the beat on every settled row at mount — ADR-0140 §6/§7 one control over; §3 renders that failure beside the latched version. **The file's method is what lets a still image judge motion:** every option is drawn twice, live and as a **filmstrip** frozen at seven sampled times via `animation-play-state: paused` + a negative `animation-delay`, so the browser samples the real curve rather than a drawing of it. **Two numbers sampled that way are why the loud half exists rather than the quiet half being merely modest:** the ✓ under `--ease-standard` is **61% drawn at 60ms of 240** and then crawls (a decelerate is right for an object arriving and wrong for a pen moving, so the draw is `linear` with its timing in keyframe offsets — `lib/one-shot.ts`'s own rule 2), and **`--ease-arrive`'s overshoot peaks at 0.073px on a 26px disc** — the app's only overshooting curve is a fifth of a device pixel at tick size, so liveliness cannot be bought with an easing here and every loud candidate is `linear` with real interior stops. Options: **ב** the draw · **ג** the stamp · **ד** the flood from the centre · **ה** the pop (**the recommendation** — 14%, measured **3.408px** against 0.073, one keyframe, no extra element, peaks at 29.6px inside the 44px box) · **ו** the coin flip (fill switching as a **step** at the halfway point, and the mark deliberately not riding the rotation because at 180° a ✓ is a mirrored glyph) · **ז** the flick (an overshoot as an **angle**, the one channel legible at 15px) · **ח** the ghost, whose cost is named because it is the only permanent one — a second `<Icon name="check">` at every tick forever so a 240ms flourish can play. The **ripple/ring is drawn nowhere and rejected in the notes**: an expanding ring is the "pulse means live" vocabulary, and a ghost of the glyph is the glyph's own. Rejected too: putting the beat on the `<button>` — an animation outranks author normal declarations, so a `transform` there would take `tokens.css`'s app-wide `:active` press-scale away for the beat's duration with the finger still down. **A SHIPPED DEFECT THE RENDER EXPOSED, and not about motion at all: the OPEN tick's ring measures 1.21:1 in light and 1.33:1 in dark** against the 3:1 floor a graphic control owes — `1.5px solid var(--line)` is a 10%-alpha hairline on the surface it sits on (composited before measuring; reading the rgba directly reports it as opaque). That is a measured answer to ADR-0188's **own** open device-pass question _"whether a 26px ring reads as pressable under a thumb"_, and the standing candidate cause of the two 2026-08-16 tick reports that did not reproduce. Not fixed here — it is a token decision, and `--line` has ~200 consumers that are genuinely hairlines. **A build call recorded rather than decided:** the tick's `<button>` is hand-copied at three call sites (`IndexTasksView`, `TaskBandRow`, `AutomaticTaskRow`), so the beat wants `TaskTick` extracted rather than the handler added three times — the shape this feature has already paid for twice (`.chk-toggle`'s font, `.tsk-who-row`). §5 draws the **reduced-motion trap** so it is not written: the dash length belongs in the beat's rule and the OFFSET in the keyframe, or a killed animation leaves a done task with no ✓ at all, permanently, with nothing able to fail (jsdom loads no stylesheet). **ANSWERED, ROUND TWO (2026-08-17): the owner took ה and corrected two things, both folded in.** **Un-ticking gets no beat at all** — it is a correction, not an achievement, so the fill drains and the mark fades over `--t-quick` on `--ease-exit`, with no beat and therefore no hold; the first draft had reused `is-ticking`, which on an open control plays the OPEN state's entrance, i.e. the pop in reverse. **And the asymmetry is one rule rather than a flag, because a transition is read from the DESTINATION state** — declared on the open state it governs done→open and is absent going the other way, so nothing needs to know the direction and the beat stays a pure entrance (measured **0.14s** against **0s**; first measured wrong, since reading `transition-duration` off the button reports 0s for both — the transition lives on `::before`). **And §6 is new: the lingering "selected" outline is a stuck `:hover`, and `:focus-visible` is innocent.** Probed rather than reasoned: `:focus-visible` is matched by neither a click nor a tap (so the focus ring stays), while `:hover` survives a tap until you tap elsewhere — and `.tsk-tick:hover .icon { opacity: 0.4 }` plus `:hover::before { border-color: var(--ok) }` then paint **a ghost ✓ inside a green ring on an OPEN control**, rendered beside two untouched rows and indistinguishable from done. **That closes the 2026-08-16 report that "did not reproduce"** — that session read `aria-pressed` and the fill, both correct the whole time, which is exactly why it found nothing; a report about a control's APPEARANCE after an interaction is not answered by asserting its STATE. Repair: the hover stops borrowing the status colour and the state's glyph, gated on `@media (hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)` — **the app's first such query**, against ~40 unguarded `:hover` rules now backlogged as one pass. One specificity trap recorded because the obvious spelling is wrong: `.tsk-tick:hover .icon` and the shipped `.tsk-tick[aria-pressed='true'] .icon` are both (0,3,0), so an unscoped override wins on a hovered DONE tick and takes its ✓ away. Interactive: theme · width · which option §4/§5 play · the **hold** (0 · 140 · 240 · 400) · a **×3 slow motion** that moves the tokens themselves, so the measurement table reports the slowed values instead of hiding a multiplier. **Renders all four theme×width combinations with the webfonts loaded and no console errors.** Left to a device pass, as controls rather than decisions: the hold, the pop's 14% height, and whether a drawn ✓ on a 26px disc reads at all.

- [`mockups/notifications-in-settings-v1.html`](../../mockups/notifications-in-settings-v1.html) — **התראות במסך ההגדרות · מכונת המצבים, ההעדפות ורשימת המכשירים** (2026-08-21) — the notifications epic's **only design phase** (1b), promoting amendments to [ADR-0197](../decisions/0197-a-notification-is-a-derived-obligation-and-the-sweep-is-its-clock.md) §7.1 and [ADR-0198](../decisions/0198-we-notify-what-you-can-still-miss.md) §6. Session note: [`planning/2026-08-21-notifications-phase-1b-designed.md`](../planning/2026-08-21-notifications-phase-1b-designed.md). Manifest: `tokens.css, App.css, screens.css, ui/primitives/choice-grid.css, ui/primitives/form-actions.css, ui/feedback/feedback.css` — re-run `inline-app-css.mjs` after touching any of those. Six sections against one question, and the phase list already named the contested part: _"the honest answer for several of those is an instruction rather than a control."_ **§1 draws the whole screen** (תצוגה → התראות → מפות אופליין) because the claim is about the neighbours: the device card and the preferences card have **opposite persistence**, so ADR-0180 §2's one-hint-per-card rule makes this two cards in one section, **235px** at 360px including heading and hints. **§2 is all seven states** — askable · granted-and-subscribed · granted-not-subscribed-here · denied · iOS-in-a-tab · unsupported · no-server-keypair — and its rule is that **where a blocker cannot be cleared there is no control, not a disabled one**, with the seventh state removing the section entirely because an absent keypair is a property of the deployment. **§3 is the rule-8 section, and it starts from a grep: `role="switch"` returns zero hits in `frontend/src`**, so the four booleans this surface needs have no primitive; measured against the same four, a two-option `ChoiceGrid` costs **+80px** and the `.set-edit` verb **+48px** _once ADR-0017's floor is honoured_ — as shipped it measures **25px**, so a fourth frame had to be drawn to show that **ג was only cheaper by being illegal**. **§4 rejects `ListRow` on a fact rather than a preference** (it requires `onOpen`, and a registered device has no screen to open) in favour of the map-storage rows six lines up the same file, and the list renders **only when a device other than this one exists**. **§5 places the second door inside `TaskSheet`** under the deadline field: `StatusBanner` already carries `action` + `onDismiss` (ADR-0181), so the door costs **zero new infrastructure**, against a sheet-over-sheet measured at **169px vs 55.7px, ×3, plus a back-stack layer**, and against a banner on "the screen after", which `HostTasks` and `TripHomeTaskBand` can never reach. **§6 decides the build order rather than the drawing:** phase 3 shipped with `NOTIFICATION_KINDS` empty, so all three category switches are inert today — and ADR-0133 §7 already ruled on that once — so the device card ships when built and the preferences card ships **with the kind it gates**, `notifyGroup` not drawn at all until phase D is decided. **Two defects the render found and the source did not:** (a) the file's first draft wrote its own `.set-note`, which **already exists** in `screens.css` with one call site (`TripSettings`'s peer-managed line, `lock` glyph included) — and because the shipped rule is `display: flex`, the `<b>` inside the iOS instruction became a second flex item and the paragraph rendered as two ragged columns; the collision is what identified the right component, so §2 generalises that one-off from a line to a paragraph (two properties) instead of adding a second beside it; (b) the device row **cannot be one line** — at 360px it ellipsised the device _name_, 116px of label against 296px on two lines, for 9px of height, and only with names a real `userAgent` produces (`Galaxy S23 Ultra · Chrome`, not `Mac`). Also decided: the switch's on state is **`--cta`/`--cta-text`**, never `--ok` or `--teal` (the colour budget has no member for "this setting is on"), its 44px target comes from an `::after` overlay exactly as `ValueToken`'s does, and its thumb carries a **hairline rather than the iOS knob-shadow**, which is invisible on a dark track. The iOS instruction gets **no glyph**: `Icon`'s `share` is three connected nodes and iOS draws a square with an up arrow, so pointing at ours would be a lie in the one place the file promises truth. State toggles: ערכת צבעים (בהיר · כהה) · מסך (360×640 · 390×844) · הפקד הבוליאני (מתג · ChoiceGrid · כפתור מילה) · מכשירים (אחד · שלושה). **One shipped defect to fix where it lives, not here:** `.set-edit` renders at **25px** at its existing call sites — the map-storage delete buttons on this very screen — the same class as the `.set-tz-trigger` 40px defect `currency-becomes-a-feature-v1` found. **Status vs. the shipped app (2026-08-21, revised the same day):** **built**, together with ADR-0198's phase A — a switch and the thing it switches had to ship together, which is §6's own recommendation taken. Three differences worth naming, since they are the kind that ages a mockup silently: the preferences card renders **only while this device is subscribed** (a category switch on a device that receives nothing narrows nothing, which the drawing did not distinguish); the device list is fed by a response carrying **no endpoint at all** (`POST /notifications/subscription` returns the row id and the client compares ids — the mockup did not have to decide how "this device" was identified, and the answer turned out to be a security boundary); and only ONE category switch exists, `notifyTasks`, since `notifyObligations` waits for phase B. The 25px `.set-edit` defect this file found is **fixed** in `screens.css` by the overlay technique §3 measured. `PushDebugPanel` stays behind `VITE_PUSH_DEBUG` for the one thing the product surface deliberately will not do: read back the raw endpoint.
- [`mockups/a-hard-event-answers-the-hold-v1.html`](../../mockups/a-hard-event-answers-the-hold-v1.html) — **אירוע נעול עונה לאחיזה · מה עונה לגרירה שלא תקרה, ולמה הטקסט נבחר בכלל** (2026-08-21) — promotes [ADR-0199](../decisions/0199-a-hard-event-answers-the-hold.md) (**Accepted; built 2026-08-21** — so this file is the built design's reference rather than a proposal; what shipped differs from it in nothing but the controls, whose defaults — ⁦4px⁩ and `--t-base` — are the values that went in). From an owner report that reads as two asks — a hard event should animate to say it will not drag, and hard-row + Trip-day text should stop selecting — and is one hole: `user-select: none` and `useHoldToDrag`'s `selectstart` cancel both ride on `dragProps`, which `PlanDay` gives only to soft rows. **Four sections, each mapping to an ADR section:** §1 the hold answered in a _refusal_ mode that ends the gesture at once (before/after, and the frames take the real 500 ms hold); §2 the same hard row under all three beats side by side — `is-pinned` against the shipped `is-nudging` and `is-rebuffing`, which is the argument for a fifth `BEAT` rather than a reuse; §3 the lock pulse on and off, the point being that `hardLock` renders **inside** `button.bld-time`, so the pulse is a redirection to the control that does move a hard event; §4 the one `user-select` rule that replaces three, with the computed value of every row type printed live. **It inlines eight real stylesheets** (`tokens`, `App`, `screens`, `form-errors`, `beats`, `when-line`, `place-badge`, `event-card`) and reproduces the real layout trees — `.body > .builder > .builder-main > .bld` for Plan, `.wp-event > .wp-event-face` for Trip — because both beats it argues against are shipped CSS and had to be played, not described. **Interactive:** theme, width, the beat (נעוץ · ניעור · הדיפה), its amplitude (3 · 4 · 6px) and duration token (`--t-quick` · `--t-base`), the lock pulse, and the rejected amber ring; every row on the page also takes the real press-and-hold with the 8px slop cancel, because the amplitude and the duration are feel calls a desktop screenshot cannot settle. **What the render exposed, and it is not this design's:** the control §3 points at misses ADR-0017's floor — `button.bld-time` renders 27px tall and its `::after` touch overlay (`inset: -8px 0`) comes to **43px, one short of 44**; ADR-0161 §7 chose the overlay deliberately but never measured where it landed. Backlogged. **Measured, not asserted:** the beat's 4px default is 44% of the row's own 9px `margin-bottom`, which is what bounds the amplitude from above, and the `user-select` row of the table reads `auto → none` for a hard `.bld` and a `.wp-event` and `none → none` for a soft `.bld` — the row that already worked, losing nothing.

- [`mockups/install-is-three-doors-v1.html`](../../mockups/install-is-three-doors-v1.html) — **where the app asks to be installed, and how hard** (2026-08-24), promoting [ADR-0204](../decisions/0204-the-install-ask-is-three-doors-and-a-permanent-home.md) (Accepted; **built the same day** — the proposed block shipped as `frontend/src/ui/install.css`). Owner: encourage the switch to the PWA, _"not too invasive"_ and _"really encourage switching"_ at once. §1 the ask as a `StatusBanner` on the existing `.app-update` mount, in its two unprompted moments, drawn beside the full-screen interstitial it rejects; §2 the `Sheet` in its three platform bodies (Chrome's real one-tap `beforeinstallprompt`; iOS Safari, which has no API and therefore **teaches**, its button reading `הבנתי` because no button there can install; and the in-app browser, where installing is impossible at all — not an edge case for a link-only-join app whose invites travel in a chat); §3 the permanent settings row, beside the `NotificationSettings` sentence it lets stop repeating itself; §4 the blocked want; §5 the budget as a ladder. **Measured** at 360×640: the ask **55.7px** (9%) against the interstitial's **640px** — **11.5×**, which is the argument against it; sheets 274px (Chrome) · 334px (iOS, taller because it teaches a gesture) · 293.6px (in-app); settings row 49px, over ADR-0017's 44px floor. The 55.7px is the same figure `PushAskBanner`'s own header cites, which is the check that the tree here is the real one. Interactive: ערכה (בהיר · כהה) · מסך (360×640 · 390×844) · פלטפורמה (Chrome · iOS Safari · דפדפן מוטמע) · **תקציב פניות (1 · 2 · 3)**, the last being the one number the file does not pretend to have settled. **Two things only rendering could have said.** The proposal's own duplication: the first pass gave the sheet a `.modal-title` _and_ an `.inst-head`, so it said "Travelive" twice in adjacent lines — the fix is `Sheet`'s `ariaLabel`, which `RosterSheet` already uses for exactly this. The second is not a new finding but a re-hit of one `pitfalls.md` already records twice — ADR-0200's `overflow: clip` arrives with the inlined sheets and clips the mockup itself, so every section below the first viewport measures correctly and never paints, silently. Recorded here only because it was walked into: the pitfalls list is worth reading _before_ the first render, not after the symptom. **A third, found while building against this file:** four token names in the hand-written block did not exist (`--ink-dim`, `--font-display`, `--text-title`, `--sunken`). An unknown custom property is invalid at computed-value time rather than an error, so those lines simply did nothing and the render still looked plausible — the sheet heights above are the corrected ones, and they moved by 30-33px. Grep the token before you spend it.

- [`mockups/the-days-lines-read-as-a-route-v1.html`](../../mockups/the-days-lines-read-as-a-route-v1.html) — **קווי היום נקראים כמסלול · how the day's routed lines read on the canvas** (2026-08-25, M7b of the routes epic), promoting [ADR-0206](../decisions/0206-a-travel-time-belongs-between-two-points.md) **§AC** (amended in place). Session note: [2026-08-25](../planning/2026-08-25-the-days-lines-read-as-a-route.md). **Nothing here is built** — the owner's instruction was "we'll have to mockup all of these before building", so this file is the proposal and the ADR records it as such. Four reports off the shipped app after M7 (#706) and its follow-up (#707), and **two of them turn out to be deletions rather than features**: the amber first leg is an orphaned workaround (§AB2's third arm existed only because Plan mode drew nothing, and §AB5 removed that reason **in the same PR**, so deleting it restores §D8's own wording), and leg numbering is already answered by ADR-0121 §6, which put the order on the pins for the exact reason the owner was reaching for. The other two are **one** problem — "which line connects what" and "the line stops beside the stop" are both about a leg's **end**, because the router snaps every endpoint to the nearest routable edge and therefore _always_ leaves a gap there, usually sub-metre and occasionally hundreds. **The render is what paid for the file, three times.** (1) **The obvious answer to telling legs apart is wrong**: a "collar" — a constant gap before each pin — is invisible on a line that is **already made of gaps** (the shipped dash is `[2, 2]` at weight 2.5, i.e. 5px on / 5px off, so a 9px collar is **1.8×** a gap the eye already discards), and the two frames are indistinguishable on screen; the failed candidate stays drawn beside the measurement that kills it, and the answer is a **solid dot** at each leg end — the one mark a dashed line cannot accidentally produce — with its cost stated (a **third** source/layer pair in `DayConnector`, a `circle` layer over the trimmed endpoints). (2) **The pins and the lines were in mirrored coordinate spaces**, so no pin sat on its own route: `inset-inline-start` resolves to the _right_ edge under `dir="rtl"` while SVG `x` is always from the left — **a map canvas is the one surface in this RTL app that is not RTL**, and its markers and geometry must share one _physical_ space (`map-split-v2.html` has the same mirroring, harmless there because its connector is decorative). (3) **`§D8` rendered as `D8§`** in this file's own Hebrew captions — the Bidi-neutral `§` that §Z5 already recorded from the first routes mockup, fixed with ADR-0118's isolate, and worth noting that a _documented_ trap still reached a new file. Promotes: **no amber in Plan mode by default** (§AC1); **a selected stop marking the leg that ARRIVES at it** in amber with the departing leg weighted and the rest at `line-opacity` 0.45 — arriving rather than departing because §AB2 already took that choice for "which leg belongs to a stop", and prominence by weight/opacity because root rule 4 has no hue to spare (§AC2); **an endpoint dot** (§AC3); **no leg labels** (§AC4 — drawn anyway, and measured: all three land on the drawn line itself, since a leg's midpoint is on its own path); and **an approach stub** for an off-network stop — the unrouted remainder in the leg's own hue, thinner and dotted, ending at the endpoint dot so it never touches the pin, saying "this part we do not know" rather than stitching a confident straight line across ground nobody walks (§AC5). **Revised on the owner's review of this file:** the stub was drawn per leg END, so an interior off-network stop got **two** — a tail from the arriving leg and one from the departing leg, meeting near the pin in a V (_"which looks a little off… you should render only one line"_). It is now **one per STOP**, running to the **arriving** leg's endpoint, because a stop meets the network in one place and two tails were the same fact drawn twice — a double claim, not merely a busy picture; §AB2 already makes the arriving leg a stop's canonical one, so the two answers cannot disagree, and the departing leg is the fallback only for the day's first stop. The two-tail version stays in the notes panel as a rejected candidate. **The ground is real even though the canvas is faked:** the frames are painted with the actual `earth` from `lib/map-style.ts` (`#eee8dc` day, `#343027` night), so the contrast rows are measured against the surface the line really crosses and reproduce §Z5 §M3's numbers exactly (⁦4.5:1⁩ for `--amber-deep` on the day ground, ⁦1.72:1⁩ for flat `--amber` — the measurement that makes the pair a pair — and ⁦7.01:1⁩ on the night ground). **Renders through the app's real CSS and is self-contained:** the shipped sheets are inlined in the app's own import order (`styles/tokens.css` → `App.css` → `screens.css` → `screens/map.css` → `ui/domain/map-pane.css`) by [`mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs`](../../mockups/tools/inline-app-css.mjs) from the file's `APP-CSS:` manifest — **re-run it after changing any of those sheets**. The pin markup is `MapPane`'s verbatim; the **lines have no shipped CSS to inherit at all**, because they are a MapLibre GL layer painted from `MAP_CONNECTOR` in `constants.ts`, so the file's SVG is the design surface and every `stroke-*` property is labelled with the `line-*` paint property it becomes. Interactive: theme · screen (⁦360×640⁩ · ⁦390×844⁩) · the collar radius and the stub threshold, both **feel calls shipped as controls** with defaults rather than as decisions this file pretends to have made.
- [`mockups/a-travel-time-between-two-points-v1.html`](../../mockups/a-travel-time-between-two-points-v1.html) — **זמן נסיעה בין שתי נקודות · what the app says once it knows how long it takes to get there** (2026-08-25, M3 of the routes epic) — **Reviewed 2026-08-25; superseded for its §2/§3/§4 by [`a-travel-time-between-two-points-v2.html`](../../mockups/a-travel-time-between-two-points-v2.html), and still the record for its §1 (the board's countdown swap and its threshold) and for the measurement work that ruled the width arguments out.** Draws all five things [ADR-0206](../decisions/0206-a-travel-time-belongs-between-two-points.md) §M demands before any of §V1 is coded, on one scenario with one set of numbers (a ⁦2:40⁩ hole, ⁦40⁩ minutes of it walking, ⁦2:00⁩ free — §V1.1's own example), so the five sections cannot disagree. Interactive: theme, width, **the swap threshold** (⁦10·20·30·45⁩), the leave-by buffer, the unit wording, the gap-line candidate (A–D), the polyline weight and colour, and D8's one-solid-vs-all-solid. **Read this entry before assuming §Z1's board swap is new work: it is a third arm on a ternary that already has two.** `Home.tsx:452` shows ADR-0184 §6 already swapping that same box — `{...formatCountdown(mins), unit: t.board.closesIn}` — because the unit slot says what the minutes are left OF; `לסגירה` is the precedent, and it settles the leave-by's word (`ליציאה`) as well as its mechanism. **Four of the five sections shrank on reading**, and two things only the render could say. (1) **`--amber` solid on the DAY map ground measures 1.72:1** — under the 3:1 a graphic owes what it crosses, and the same failure `MAP_CONNECTOR`'s own comment records for its first value (1.01:1 on the night land). The night ground is fine at 7.01. So §D1's "solid + amber" cannot be one value: `--amber-deep` on the paper-coloured ground (4.5:1), `--amber` on the dark one — no new hue, and the per-theme-pair-in-TypeScript shape `MAP_CONNECTOR.COLOR` already has. (2) **`§` is Bidi-neutral, so `§D8` renders `D8§` in Hebrew prose** — found in this file's own frame label, same class as the `~40` that renders `40~` without `ltrIsolate` (§2e measures the character's x, it does not deduce it). Both fixes are ADR-0118's: isolate the run. **What the measurements ruled OUT is as useful as what they ruled in:** all three unit words fit the ⁦74px⁩ countdown tile (the cliff is at `דקות ליציאה`, ⁦79.8px⁩), and all four gap-line candidates keep ≥⁦54px⁩ of dashed rule even at the worst case — so neither choice is a width question. What is measured is candidate D's rhythm cost (⁦58px⁩ per hole against ⁦29px⁩) and, in §1e's rejected two-countdown frame, ⁦11px⁩ off the `הבא בתור` title and a second line at ⁦360⁩ — which fits at ⁦390⁩, and is exactly why ⁦360⁩ is the design width. Also settled: **`GAP_MIN_MINUTES = 60` means a 45-minute hole renders nothing today**, so the travel line ignores the floor exactly as `ConnectionBand` already does ("a connection is presence, a gap is absence"); **there is no walk/bike/car icon in `ui/Icon.tsx`**, so the mode control is words in the existing `ToggleChip` (`.wp-chip`, three entries, ⁦51px⁩ target on a ⁦29px⁩ paint) and a gate-refused mode takes `.wp-chip.provisional`'s dashed border rather than a failure; and **§1e's collision — a shutting check-in window and a live leave-by in the same minute — which ADR-0206 never mentions.** One box, so the nearer number wins. Recommends the threshold **⁦30⁩ minutes of time-to-leave**, the number in the sentence that defines the product. Its own CSS block is ~50 lines on purpose: four of the five sections spend a mechanism that already ships.

- [`mockups/a-travel-time-between-two-points-v2.html`](../../mockups/a-travel-time-between-two-points-v2.html) — **הדרך היא חלק מהיום · the journey is an object in the day, not a note about a hole** (2026-08-25, M3 review round 1) — **§1 BUILT 2026-08-26 (M6a of the routes epic, ADR-0206 §AF); §3's three tiers built by M6c/M6d (ADR-0207/0208); §4 still awaiting M8.** Revises `-v1` on the owner's review of it and supersedes its §2/§3/§4; v1 remains the record for the collapsed board's countdown swap and its 30-minute threshold, which this round did not reopen. Four notes, four changes. **(1) "much more route oriented … a real visual thing … we need this crystal clear."** v1 made the travel a run of text inside the gap strip and measured it against how much dashed rule survived — the wrong question. v2 makes it a **block** between the two cards (mode mark in the day's own badge column, duration, leave-by, the leg's **real shape drawn small** from the same `decodeShape` array the map uses, and the mode chips on it), so the day reads `place · journey · place` — ADR-0206 §V1.3's own sentence. **It absorbs the free-time statement rather than sitting beside it**, which is what keeps ADR-0159's one-slot rule: measured, 58px against 87px for a strip-plus-block, with no fact dropped. **(2) "real paths for every two adjacent stops, not just the current one."** v1 drew one real path and three straight dashed segments; v2 draws every leg's geometry and lets §D8 ration only the **solid amber** — measured, one solid leg is 3.7× less amber than all-solid, and the straight-line shortcut also **under-reports distance** (‍9.9% on the drawn legs; the magnitude is the drawing's, the sign is a fact). **(3) "How will the app know whether you're on time or late?"** — v1's `7 · באיחור` claimed a fact it did not have; a settle mark is a record written when convenient, not a sensor. v2 says only what the app has — `זמן היציאה עבר ב-17:15`, a fact about the schedule rather than about the person — and gives it the affordance the app already ships: **`בדרך`** (`t.actions.onWay`, on the day row since ADR-0161), **which today writes nothing** (`verbs.ts:1361` is a toast). Tapping it turns the leg teal, the board's journey grammar. **(4) transit as a declarable mode with a warning** — agreed, drawn, and shaped so it amends §D9/§Z3 rather than breaking them: **declared, never estimated.** §D9 forbids a control that promises an answer and gives none; this one promises **no** answer up front, and its whole value is silencing a wrong one (Senso-ji → Tokyo Station is 73 min walking against 25 by train). It takes the existing `ticket` icon and `.wp-chip.provisional`'s dashed off-state, four chips fit one row at 360 (239px of 312px), and the cost is stated: a declared leg carries no leave-by, **so the board's countdown swap does not fire for it.** The one thing not from the code is **three proposed `ui/Icon.tsx` glyphs** (walking/cycling/driving), drawn on the real 24-grid at the real stroke weight and labelled as a proposal — v1 refused to mint them and "crystal clear" reverses that call. **Round 2 (same day) added two things.** **The route thumbnail is measured out of the day list:** at 46×26 the geometry survives (5–7 of 6–7 points at a 1px tolerance) but the closest pair of four real legs differs by **3.1px inside a 46px box**, and the render shows all four reading as "a wiggly line" — one bit, repeated at every hole of the surface ADR-0206 calls the densest in the app. The same 46px take `formatDistance` plus `PlaceBadge`'s existing show-on-map pin, and **the map still draws a real path for every leg**. **And a correction this file has to carry, because the first version of it was wrong:** the app _does_ have position. ADR-0006 separates **own-device location — "IN for v1 … always available (privately, on-device)"** — from member-to-member sharing, which is the only part deferred; ADR-0205 §8's "Not member GPS" is about the second. `lib/useGeolocation.ts` ships and has fed the Map's `קרוב עכשיו` since ADR-0109 §6. So §3d draws **three tiers**: no permission (the cautious sentence, which stays the floor), a fix at the last stop (the mark is _earned_), and a fix already on the route (**no mark at all** — the state that deletes a wrong nudge rather than adding a right one). Bounded by three code facts: the hook is one-shot rather than `watchPosition` (a stated battery decision), the fix is never persisted or sent, and an iOS PWA has no background position — so it is "the app knows when you open it", never "the app watches you". Using it is a **new capability for this surface and wants its own ADR**. Measured with it: `--teal` is 3.35:1 on `--card` — over the 3:1 a graphic owes, under the 4.5 a sentence does — so the hue rides the pin glyph and the words stay `--muted` at 5.57:1 (the app already spends teal as ink on two-word labels in `.wp-event-act.go`, at that same 3.35:1). Two mockup-only defects the render caught: a flex row with `overflow: hidden` reports `scrollWidth === clientWidth` on a visibly clipped line (the first draft printed `202px מתוך 202px` for a truncated line — sum the children instead), and isolating a whole `hoursPhrase` reverses it, because `1:55 שע׳` has Hebrew inside the LTR isolate — isolate the numeral, leave the unit in the Hebrew flow. **What the build changed about §1, both on measurement rather than on taste (ADR-0206 §AF4/§AF5):** the free-time run rides only the arms where the departure is still ahead or already behind — `זמן היציאה עבר ב-17:15 · פנוי לפני שעתיים` is ⁦219.70px⁩ of ink in the meta line's ⁦180.75px⁩ box at 360, so `text-overflow: ellipsis` was eating the free time on exactly the arm that matters (the drawing had already carried the mark alone on both urgent states, so this confirms §1 rather than revising it); and `עדיין כאן` moved from the meta line to the acts row, where it costs zero extra height, after measuring ⁦187.09px⁩ in the same ⁦180.75px⁩ box. `.day-trv-modes` is the one part of §1 not built — it is M8's, and `.day-trv-acts` is shaped to take it as a one-line addition. **And §1's absorption of the free-time strip is REVERSED in the shipped app** (2026-08-26, owner's call — ADR-0206 §AH3): the drawing states what is free inside the block, and the app now states it on the quiet strip below, because the block is about the leg and free time is about the hole. The measurement above is the argument that should have been read that way at the time — a line that can only hold both facts on half the arms is a line holding two subjects — and the drawing is **not** retrofitted (§AE6), so this note is the record. The shipped cost is ⁦88px⁩ per hole where the drawing's absorption measured ⁦58px⁩, minus the holes whose remainder falls under the ⁦15⁩-minute floor and render no strip at all. Two words in §1 are also stale: the free-time run reads `X דק׳ פנויות` (not `פנוי לפני X`) and a leg that does not fit reads `חסרות X לדרך` (not `הדרך ארוכה מהפער ב־X`). **ROUND 3 (2026-08-27, M9 of the routes epic) adds §5 — the day's own verdict — and touches §1–§4 not at all.** ADR-0206 §M named five gates and this was not one of them, so §V1.7's "this day does not fit" had never been drawn; it is extended into this file rather than given its own because the verdict only stands on a surface where every leg already states its own case, which is §1. **§5a draws the three day states and the finding is what is NOT in two of them:** a day that fits and a day nothing could be measured on are the SAME silence, because §D4 says a reader must not be able to tell "not computed" from "not computable" — so there is no positive state at all, and the fit stays a three-way discriminant (`FITS`/`OVERRUNS`/`UNKNOWN`) in code even though one arm draws. Measured as a DOM fact: both silent, and the unmeasured day renders **0** journey blocks against the other two's **3**, so it reads exactly as it did before the epic. **§5b rejects `--miss` on the day**, measured rather than asserted — it puts a third `--miss` box on a screen that already has two without adding a fact, which is ADR-0206's own Consequence ("felt as help, not as refusal") arriving as colour; amber, because what is missing is **time** (rule 4), at `--amber-deep` (5.49:1 light / 5.84:1 dark, where plain `--amber` is 2.1:1 in light — the failing theme is what picks the token). The row is **`.day-ambient .ambient` at a second density**, not a new region: 18 declarations, 35px, on a strip the day already has for facts true of the whole day. **§5c is the slot picker** (`dayPositions`), the one half of §V1.1's correction never applied — `פנוי 3 שעות` where the chip on the same hole says `פער של שעתיים`; corrected, 2 of 5 positions move and a hole the walk eats loses its offer entirely (`earnsChipAt` on the corrected number, §AG5), while the day's edges and a position joined around a moved row keep the raw hole because they have no adjacent pair to ask about (§D4). **Three things the render changed or caught.** (1) The first copy — `שתי דרכים לא נכנסות ביום · חסרות 35 דק׳ בסך הכול` — measures **314.9px in a 308px box** and lost the end of the count; both words are recoverable from context and the trim lands at 233.9px. (2) **The measurement harness under-reported it twice**, and each way looked comfortable: `scrollWidth` on an ellipsised child reports the CLIPPED width (§1's own trap, one level down, and the fix is an off-screen ghost carrying the computed font — a `Range` is not reliable inside `overflow: hidden`), and summing only the two text halves drops the glyph and one gap, 24px of 308. (3) **`measurements.md` from `render.mjs` is snapshotted before `document.fonts.ready` re-measures**, so its numbers are the fallback font's; this file's own table settles ~35px wider — read the live page, not the artefact. **And the design finding that is about code rather than pixels:** `daySequenceFits` (M2) is NOT the verdict's source despite being what M9's card names. It measures raw stop times, and the day surface has since grown five per-leg gates living only in `dayJourney` — a flexible arrival (§AI1/§AJ1), a declared leg (§AA4), a leg out of a bed with no departure window (§AF3), a sub-minute hop, and the PAST arm — so a verdict built from raw stops re-commits §AJ1's bug one scope up and calls a day impossible over the one leg nobody can be late for. The shipped number is a roll-up of the arms the rows already render. Recorded as ADR-0206 §AN. **And one owner call taken ON this section** (2026-08-27): _"No shape on the day row · I prefer מרחק, ומגע אל המפה, and it's what we mostly have today (minus the touch for map)."_ That **confirms** §1e's recommendation — the route thumbnail stays out of the day list — and adds the half of it that was drawn and never built, so §5's frames draw the journey block's distance carrying a teal pin to that leg on the canvas. It shipped on **both** day surfaces (ADR-0159 §1 forbids them differing about a fact) as a `role="button"` span rather than a `<button>`, because the block's face is itself a button once the mode disclosure is offered and `PlaceBadge` had already solved that exact problem; ⁦69.3px⁩ wide, a 44px `::after` target, and the face stays ⁦56px⁩ on one line. **§5's trail is its own renderer, not §1e's `distTrail`** — that one is the dated M3 drawing with different class names, and a mockup is never retrofitted (ADR-0097).

- [`mockups/where-a-route-shows-up-v1.html`](../../mockups/where-a-route-shows-up-v1.html) — **איפה מסלול מופיע, ומה הוא עושה · the coverage sweep** (2026-08-25, M3 review round 3) — **Proposed; awaiting owner sign-off.** The companion to `a-travel-time-between-two-points-v2.html`: that file decides **what a leg says**, this one answers the question the owner's review found missing — _"what does clicking on a route do? the differences between dayview and planday, in the map — is a route clickable … There are areas of the app that you didn't take into consideration at all: like the day timeline glance."_ The gap was structural: ADR-0206 §M named five things to settle, the session settled those five, and settling five questions is not designing a feature. The full inventory — **15 surfaces, 8 gestures, 10 states** — is in the session note's §10, where every row is decided, owned by a named milestone, or explicitly out; this file draws the three that needed a decision rather than a sentence. **§1 the GlanceCard rail**, which was missed entirely and commits §V1.1's exact overstatement one elevation up — it draws free time as the emptiness between blocks, so a two-hour hole that is really 1:20 plus a 40-minute walk _looks_ like two hours. **The fix is not a redesign** (the owner asked): the rail is proportional, travel is proportional, and `.seg` already carries the `min-width: 6px` floor a short leg needs — measured, a 40-minute leg is 13.3px of a 300px rail and a 12-minute one sits _on_ the floor, so the rail can say "there is a walk here" but not how long, which is the same compromise it already makes for short events. On this day **16% of the "free" emptiness is walking**. A hatch rather than a fill, because the rail's fills are events and a journey is not one. **§2 Plan mode**, where the difference from Trip is not cosmetic: `FreeSlot` is a **control** (ADR-0161 §2), so Plan does not display a gap, it _offers_ one — sized by `gapBetween`, which knows nothing about travel — and would hand you a 45-minute slot a 40-minute walk eats, which is §V1.7's own complaint arriving through a control. The chip now offers what is left (`＋ שבץ 120 דק׳` with a line saying why). **§2's `חור` is `פער` in the app** and its `tight` state's sentence is `חסרות X לדרך` (owner's calls, 2026-08-26 — ADR-0206 §AG/§AH4); the drawing is not retrofitted, so read the strings off `i18n/he.ts`. **And the render refused this section's first idea:** the infeasible case was drawn on Plan's seam, which is `display: none` outside an armed drag (`screens.css:2297`) — the frame rendered empty — and making it permanent would undo the rule that comment defends (~90px added to a four-event day _at the moment a drag arms_, moving every target under the finger). So the fact lives on the leg, which is always on screen. **§3 the map's gestures.** A tap on a route line does **nothing in v1, as a decision**: `handlePaneClick` hit-tests DOM children (`PANE_CHROME`) and a MapLibre line layer is not one, so a tap there currently reads as a canvas tap and _clears the selection_ (ADR-0122 §7); making it tappable needs the app's **first `queryRenderedFeatures`** and a ±10px tolerance box measured at **59% of a pin's width**, running the length of the line, on the surface ADR-0121 §9 says pins must win. What selects a leg is the row, at a 56px target. Also settled: the Map's **stop-traversal track** (ADR-0182 §1) says each step's cost, and the shipped whole-day `mapsDayRouteUrl` and a per-leg `mapsDirectionsUrl` are **two different promises that both stay** ("take me along the day" vs "take me to this leg"). **§4 draws the ten states once** — not-yet-fetched, offline, gate-refused, no place, same place, past day — and none of them is an error, per §D4.

- [`mockups/the-mode-set-and-transit-declared-v1.html`](../../mockups/the-mode-set-and-transit-declared-v1.html) — **ארבעה מצבים, סימן אחד, וקו שאינו כביש · the mode set drawn once, as a set** (2026-08-27, M8a of the routes epic) — **Adopted (2026-08-27) and BUILT in M8b** ([ADR-0206 §AM](../decisions/0206-a-travel-time-belongs-between-two-points.md)); the app now matches it, with two corrections noted at the end of this entry. Three cards had each asked for part of one icon set — [ADR-0206](../decisions/0206-a-travel-time-belongs-between-two-points.md) §AA3's walk/car/bicycle glyphs, §AA4's תחב״צ mark + suppressed-duration row + (2026-08-27) the declared leg's own straight segment, and §AK's **composited** warning mark — and M6e's card said the set "gets drawn once". Drawn separately they would have disagreed the way ADR-0139's three settle affordances did, on **vocabulary** rather than geometry, with every test green. Interactive: theme, width, glyph size (⁦24 · 21 · 16px⁩), the תחב״צ candidate (bus · `ticket` · word), the mark's corner / halo / size (⁦13 · 15 · 17px⁩), the mode row's shape (glyph · glyph+word · word), and the transit dash rhythm + line-cap. **Five things reading the code changed, and the first reframes the milestone.** (1) **The three mode glyphs already ship** — `ui/Icon.tsx:238-245`, minted by M6a off the v2 mockup's own _proposal_ frame, so §1 is a confirmation pass on shipped assets and §Z5 §M5's word chips had to be **drawn back by hand** (`.msq-was`), the "grades its own homework" trap. (2) **`ticket` is not free.** §AA4 and `Icon.tsx:63` stand it in for a declared תחב״צ leg, but `constants.ts:1520` is `booking: 'ticket'` and four screens spend it on that meaning (`Index.tsx:234`, `Home.tsx:1107`, `IndexBookingsView.tsx:246`, `HeroLift.tsx:308`) — two meanings behind one glyph is the drift ADR-0138 exists to end, and its own `sync`/`swap` comment says so. **So one new asset is minted: `transit`, a vehicle from the FRONT** (body · windscreen band · two headlights · two mirrors, same 24 grid, same `stroke-width: 2`) — front-facing because every other member of the set is a side view, so a side-view bus differs from `driving` only by a cabin arch against a box, ~3px at 21px. The mirrors are what make it a bus rather than a train, and תחב״צ is buses **and** trains, so rail was the narrower claim. (3) **`warn` is not free either, once §AK claims it.** The v2 mockup's §4 drew the transit "no estimate" line with `icon('warn')`; §AK makes `warn` the mark for "this journey does not fit", so both meanings would land on the same block in the same release. Dropped: the **missing** duration is the statement, and `בלי הערכת זמן` labels the absence so it does not read as missing data. (4) **§AK2's own precedent does not exist.** It says to start from "the avatar-hero badge (ADR-0133 §6/§12)" — `ui/primitives/avatar.css` has no badge, pseudo-element or overlay, and those two sections are the picture page's states and the upload's trust class. What ships is `PlaceBadge`'s corner mark (`.wp-placebadge-mark`, [ADR-0167](../decisions/0167-the-badge-is-the-thumbnails-frame.md) §1 + §11.2/§12), and it is the **better** precedent: a corner mark on a 32–40px rounded badge, which is `.day-trv-ic`'s box exactly, whose own owner review already answered §AK3.1's loudness question ("a bare teal pin, not a pin in a teal disc"). (5) **The un-routed fallback and the transit segment are the same geometry by construction** — `MapPane.tsx`'s `MapDayLeg.path` is documented as "the routed path, or the straight segment where none has arrived yet" and both take the connector's dashed paint, so the one confusion §AA4 forbids cannot be prevented by shape. **What the drawing settles.** The mark takes **no hue of its own** (`currentColor` off the tile, which the block's tone already tinted) — that is §AK3.1, and it is why a day that has ended can take the same mark in its own quiet tone; it hangs at the tile's bottom-inline-end at `size / -2.6`, ⁦15px⁩ on a ⁦38px⁩ tile (⁦39%⁩, against `PlaceBadge`'s ⁦42-53%⁩), overlapping the tile by only ⁦38%⁩ of itself and **not clipped** by `.day-trv`'s `overflow: hidden` (⁦4.2px⁩ of slack — ADR-0167 §11.2's trap, measured rather than assumed); the block is **⁦58px⁩ with the mark and ⁦58px⁩ without**, which is the whole claim of §AK2. §AK3.3/§AK3.4 collapse into one rule instead of a table: **the mark says the JOURNEY does not work, not that the clock moved** — `OVERRUNS` and `arrivesAfterClose` take it, `PASSED` and `ON_WAY` do not, and a finished day takes it quietly. **And the render found that transit × mark is UNREACHABLE**: a declared leg carries no duration, so it can never overrun, never arrive after a close and never pass a leave-by it does not have — the fourth block in §2a is a demonstration that the composition is glyph-agnostic (what a fifth _routable_ mode will need), labelled as such, and §AK2's "8 with תחב״צ" is really **7**. **The canvas:** the declared leg draws its own straight segment in the route's amber pair at `ROUTE.WEIGHT` ⁦3.5⁩ with a **long dash** (⁦10.5px⁩ on / ⁦4.2px⁩ off) and the route's own end dots — **three channels** against the fallback (hue ⁦4.50:1⁩ / ⁦7.01:1⁩ amber against the connector's ⁦3.01:1⁩ / ⁦3.25:1⁩ ink on the real `earth` ground, weight ⁦3.5⁩ vs ⁦2.5⁩, rhythm long-dash vs ⁦5/5⁩) and one against the routed line (dashed vs solid). **The weight and rhythm do NOT follow §D8's amber ration** — the ration governs hue and opacity only, or a declared leg that is not the asked-about one would fall back to reading as the fallback. **A rendering trap the measurement caught:** `line-cap: round` adds half the stroke at each end of **every dash**, so ⁦3.5px⁩ comes off a ⁦4.2px⁩ gap and the line reads nearly solid — i.e. it asserts the path it exists to disclaim. The declared segment takes **butt caps**, deliberately unlike `ROUTE`, whose own comment explains why it is round. **Two measurements of this file's own were wrong before the render corrected them**, and both are recorded rather than quietly fixed: an intersection computed one-sided reported **180% of the mark** inside a box it overhangs (in RTL the inline end is on the _left_, so a one-sided subtraction measures the tile's whole width), and the mode row's chips were compared against `getBoundingClientRect().width` — **which includes the row's ⁦12px⁩ inline padding on each side**, so glyph+word read as "fits" while it visibly clipped a control. **That is §AA4's own error** ("four chips fit one row at 360 — 239px of 312px", measured in the same wrong box). Against the real inner box glyph+word is ⁦327px⁩ of ⁦308px⁩ and wraps; **glyph-only is ⁦194px⁩ of ⁦308px⁩**, leaving ⁦114px⁩ so a fifth mode cannot break it, and it is the only shape that reaches ADR-0017's ⁦44px⁩ (a labelled chip paints ⁦31px⁩, a word-only one ⁦29px⁩ — §Z5 §M5's row never met the floor). So the mode row is **`.wp-chip.touch` squared**, the word moving to `aria-label`, plus one `flex-wrap: wrap` as a guard: `.wp-chip` is `flex: 0 0 auto`/`nowrap` inside a block that carries `overflow: hidden`, so a row that does not fit **disappears** rather than growing. **Stated costs of a declared leg, so the build does not discover them:** no duration, therefore no leave-by, therefore the board's swap does not fire and the day total skips it (§AA4) — and, new here, **the free-time strip below it states the RAW hole**, because there is no journey duration to net out (§AH3 moved free time to the strip; this is what happens to it when the block goes quiet). The distance stays: ⁦2.7 ק״מ⁩ is true and useful, and what disappears is the walking number that was wrong. Rejected, with reasons: the glyph-per-mode-per-state matrix; `ticket` as the fourth mode; a side-view bus; a train front; `warn` in the "no estimate" line; a hue of the mark's own; round caps on the declared segment; drawing nothing for a declared leg; a dashed border on the declared block (the hard/soft grammar — a declaration is a fact a person gave, not a provisional); and inferring transit from a leg's length (§AA4/§Z8 — only a person declares). **§5 is a second round, on the owner's review of the first** (2026-08-27): _"All glyphs that have a direction should have RTL variants. For example the person should be facing left and not right if the app is in Hebrew. The bike as well."_ Recorded in **[ADR-0138 §10](../decisions/0138-the-row-menu-is-one-surface-and-icons-are-ui.md)** rather than in ADR-0206, because the rule is about the icon vocabulary — §AA3 is only what made it visible, a walker and a bicycle being the app's first `Icon` entries that depict a person moving. **One declaration and no new mechanism:** `scaleX(var(--dir))`, off the token `tokens.css` already calls _"the one place a direction is named"_, with `NavArrow` as the precedent one layer down — and the sign lands the opposite way from `NavArrow` (these glyphs are authored right-facing, the set convention and what ships), which is exactly why the rule reads the token instead of writing ⁦-1⁩. Verified off the computed matrix rather than the custom property: ⁦scaleX -1⁩ in RTL, ⁦scaleX 1⁩ in LTR. **A named `MIRRORED` allowlist beside the existing `FILLED`/`ROTATE`, never a rule of thumb — and `clock` is the witness**, since mirrored it reads a different time in an app whose whole subject is time (`check` is the second: a mirrored ✓ is a tick drawn backwards). **Symmetry is measured off the rendered path**, 240 samples via `getPointAtLength` matched nearest-neighbour to the set mirrored about ⁦x=12⁩ — nearest-neighbour because mirroring reverses every subpath's traversal order, so an index comparison reports a huge error for a perfectly symmetric glyph. The threshold is not a judgement call: across all ⁦58⁩ entries the symmetric ones top out at ⁦0.33⁩ (`calendar`) and the asymmetric ones start at ⁦1.74⁩ (`members`), an empty band. **Two results worth carrying.** Only **2 of the 4 mode glyphs** have a facing — `driving` and the new `transit` are symmetric, and the front view §1c chose for LEGIBILITY is what makes `transit` need no RTL variant either, one decision paying twice. And **"asymmetric" and "has a direction" are different sets**: ⁦30⁩ of ⁦58⁩ are asymmetric, only ⁦9⁩ have a facing, which is the whole reason the list is explicit. **The audit is app-wide, so the file carries all 57 shipped paths, extracted mechanically** — an audit over the 11 glyphs the drawing happens to use would report on 11 and claim 57. It names **7 further candidates** (`exit`, `undo`, `external`, `navigate`, `search`, `bracket`, `ticket`; two the same class as `NavArrow`, two a real argument) and leaves them to a backlogged sweep rather than widening the card. Also rejected here: redrawing the paths to face left (`NavArrow` authors RTL-first; for a mode set the convention is the other way and the paths already ship). Note the constraint §10.3 records: `Icon` writes its `dir` rotation as an INLINE transform, so a glyph cannot be both rotated and mirrored — no member is today, and the unit test should assert the two sets are disjoint. **§6 and §7 are a third round, on the owner's two follow-ups** (2026-08-27), recorded in [ADR-0206 §AL10/§AL11](../decisions/0206-a-travel-time-belongs-between-two-points.md). **§6 — the mode row is a DISCLOSURE, and the question found a hole rather than a preference:** §3 drew it always-visible, never said when it appears, and never measured the cost. It is ⁦55px⁩ per block (⁦58⁩ → ⁦113⁩), so a four-hole day pays ⁦452px⁩ against ⁦232px⁩ collapsed — most of a ⁦640px⁩ screen for a control most days never touch. §Z5 §M5's "selected or next leg only" does not work either: the day LIST has no leg selection (that is the Map's model, §AC2), so every leg but the next would have no way to change its mode, while the override is keyed on a place PAIR and is set while planning. So: the caret is `.wp-event-chev` re-pointed (`rotate(180deg)`, `--t-base`), the container is `ui/primitives/Collapsible` (rule 8), and it registers **no back layer** — a pane OF the row, not a layer over it, the `SnapSheet` distinction. **And almost all of it already ships: `button.day-trv-face` is in `day-join.css` NOW and is dead code** — nothing renders it (`DayJoinRow.tsx:184` is always a `<div>`) and `DayJoinRow.test.tsx:171` asserts its absence as _"is a statement and not a control"_; the component's own docblock explains why the acts row is a SIBLING of the face, which is the shape that was holding this open. Measured: the collapsed block is ⁦58px⁩, identical to a statement block with no mode row. Two things change deliberately — that spec falls (§AH3's rule cuts the other way here: the mode IS about the leg), and `Collapsible`'s ⁦0.32s⁩ literal is visibly out of step with the caret's ⁦--t-base⁩, which the primitive owns. **§7 — a mode switch issues no request, and that is the design:** `useDayTravel` defaults to `modes = TRAVEL_MODES`, so one matrix per day carries all three and a switch is a cache read; its docblock says so in as many words, and M8b asserts it with a network spy. The cold window belongs to the DAY, not the switch (a warming answer per ADR-0187, a peek that must not reach out, offline), and there the answer is §D4's absence. **A spinner is refused and was already refused once** — `where-a-route-shows-up-v1.html`: _"on a day with five holes that is the loudest thing on the screen"_ — with ADR-0140 §6 rationing loops independently; noted as an OPEN call, since that file still awaits sign-off. **What the question surfaced:** "no number yet" and "the gate refused this pair" render identically, which is §AA4's polyline confusion one surface over. Split by where they are said: the chip carries availability only (dashed `.wp-chip.provisional`, still tappable at ⁦44px⁩, the tap landing on §D4's chip per §Z5 §M5), and the absence is said on the block. Also rejected: `רחוק מדי להליכה`, which reads best and is sometimes **false** — `sameTravelCluster`'s docblock says a point in no cluster answers `false`, so an isolated place refuses walking at any distance and a 2 km stroll would be told it is too far; the recommendation is `אין הערכה ל<מצב> כאן`, one template off `t.travelMode`, with `כאן` carrying the pair-not-mode difference. **A process note the file records about itself:** an edit dropped the `>` from the face tag and swallowed every block's children, and the renderer reported no console errors while the measurement table filled in with numbers off malformed boxes — one of which was written up as a finding before the screenshots caught it.
