Left deliberately open for a device pass: the mark's ⁦15px⁩, which is a control here and not a decision. **Two of this file's predictions were wrong and the build corrected them, recorded in [ADR-0206 §AM6/§AL10](../decisions/0206-a-travel-time-belongs-between-two-points.md) rather than quietly fixed.** (1) `DayJoinRow.test.tsx`'s _"is a statement and not a control"_ did **not** fall: the disclosure is opt-in per host (`modes` is an optional prop, absent on a read-only archive and on a leg whose ends do not both resolve to a place), so a block given no `modes` is still exactly that statement — the mockup draws the row always-visible and could not see the prop. (2) Suppressing the estimate is **not** the whole of a declared leg: `dayJourney` answers `null` with no duration, so the block — and the mode control on it — disappeared, making the declaration a one-way door. A declared leg is a journey with no duration rather than an absent journey, so it has its own `DECLARED` arm, and the distance the entry above promises comes from one derivation (`distanceFor`: routed where there is an estimate, the crow-flies floor otherwise, which is the same claim the straight segment makes).

- [`mockups/a-route-is-on-its-way-v1.html`](../../mockups/a-route-is-on-its-way-v1.html) — **מסלול בדרך · what a hole says while its number is being computed** (2026-08-28, off two field reports) — **Adopted and BUILT in the same change** ([ADR-0206 §AU](../decisions/0206-a-travel-time-belongs-between-two-points.md)). Two reports, and the second is the diagnosis: _"I've added two stops to my trip, and no route or time estimations"_ and _"perhaps the route is not automatically triggered when you add stops … because I left the app and came back after some time, and then I had a route."_ Interactive: theme, width, the row's state (מחשב · הגיע), the wait **copy** (three candidates) and whether the duration slot holds a sized placeholder at all — the last two are controls precisely because they are feel calls this file does not pretend to have settled. §1 → §AU1, §2 → §AU1's ranking, §3 → §AU2. **Three things reading the code changed, and the first reframes the file.** (1) **The app had already written this down and filed it as deferred** — `docs/backlog.md` carried it in as many words (_"the version that fixes it without waiting reserves the row's slot … which needs a state the journey block does not have today (§D4 has no 'asked, not yet answered') and a decision about what such a block says while it is empty"_), so this is an open decision being taken rather than a new idea. (2) **The row was not "empty", it was ABSENT — and so was the mode control**, since the switch is a disclosure ON the block: the one leg whose mode you would want to change is the one leg offering no way to change it. §AM10 had already fixed exactly this for a gate-refused mode and the same argument was simply never applied to the pending case. (3) **The arithmetic says the single retry was always going to miss** — a cold day is three matrix calls paced ⁦1/s⁩ by `PolitenessLimiter` against a `Retry-After` the server floors at ⁦2s⁩ — which is the whole of "I left the app and came back". **What the drawing settles.** The block is **⁦40px⁩ computing and ⁦40px⁩ answered** — measured by rendering both variants into an off-screen probe in the same tick rather than asserting it, because the whole point of a sized placeholder is that nothing moves when the matrix lands (§AF7's shift, closed for this case). The mode row measures **⁦44×44px⁩** and meets ADR-0017 through `.wp-chip.touch`'s own `min-height`. **A spinner is refused for the third time**, and the precedent is in this catalog: `where-a-route-shows-up-v1.html` (_"on a day with five holes that is the loudest thing on the screen"_) and §AL11, now joined by §D6 — the app has one live mark and `.nowline` is it. `ui/feedback/Skeleton` is the shared idle treatment and brings its own reduced-motion behaviour, so the proposed CSS is **five declarations**. **Rejected, with reasons:** a crow-flies distance during the wait (§AM10 already ruled it — _"a crow-flies number that later becomes a routed one is a figure that changes under the reader"_ — and `dayTravelTotal` reads these journeys, so the header would climb leg by leg); a spinner; leaving the hole empty and trusting the next refresh (the reported behaviour, and it takes the mode control with it); and **lowering walking's ⁦15 km⁩ ceiling**, which was the easy answer to the first report and is backwards — §Z8 raised it deliberately because _a group walks a long way on purpose_. The default and the ceiling are two different questions: ⁦2.5 km⁩ guesses, ⁦15 km⁩ limits, and the band between them is every leg you can still switch to a walk by hand. **Two defects the render caught, both this file's own and both recorded rather than quietly fixed.** The mode chips measured **⁦22.3px⁩** tall and were about to be written up as an ADR-0017 failure — `ui/primitives/toggle-chip.css` was **missing from the `APP-CSS:` manifest**, so the file was measuring an unstyled control and would have reported a defect the app does not have. And the measurement probe was parked at `left:-10000px`, which in this RTL document extended the page's scroll width to ⁦11320px⁩ and turned every screenshot into a ribbon; a `fixed` box is out of flow and contributes nothing to the scroll extent. **§3's threshold was superseded the next day and the file is NOT retrofitted** (2026-08-28, [ADR-0206 §AV1](../decisions/0206-a-travel-time-belongs-between-two-points.md)): its note reads _"מעל ⁦2.5⁩ ק״מ נסיעה, מתחת הליכה"_, and the rule is now **⁦10 minutes⁩ of walking time**, with ⁦700 m⁩ of crow as the floor under it for a leg whose duration has not arrived. Reported off the Iceland trip: Bjólfur stands ⁦1.4 km⁩ above Seyðisfjörður as the crow flies and ⁦12 km⁩ on foot, so the crow instrument this file drew called a ⁦4:18⁩ ascent a stroll. **What §3 still shows is exactly what it was drawn for** — that a leg's mode is derived per leg and switchable in one tap, and that the two frames disagree about the mode while agreeing about everything else; only the number in its prose is stale. §1 and §2, which are what the file exists for, are untouched.

- [`mockups/a-day-has-three-shapes-v1.html`](../../mockups/a-day-has-three-shapes-v1.html) — **ליום שלוש צורות · why the car pick-up, the check-out and the drive all look the same** (2026-08-28), promoting [ADR-0210](../decisions/0210-a-day-is-points-lines-and-envelopes.md) — **APPROVED AND BUILT 2026-08-28**, the same day (the owner's instruction was to mock it up and approve first, then _"approved, let's build this"_). **Do not re-run `inline-app-css.mjs` on this file.** Its inlined block is the sheet **as it stood before the build**, which is the only thing that makes its before/after columns mean anything — refreshing it makes BOTH columns draw the fix and the file silently reports a win it never measured (`pitfalls.md`'s "grades its own homework"). ADR-0210's build log is the record of what shipped, including **four things the drawing could not catch**: a block with more than a line in it needs its card back (which amends §3's `on-way` arm in place), `.day-trv.on-way`'s fill still painting a square-cornered teal band on the now-borderless arm, the `warn` corner mark left anchored to a tile that no longer exists, and `StayRow`'s `edge` being made required rather than defaulted. Session note: [2026-08-28](../planning/2026-08-28-a-day-has-three-shapes.md). Owner, against a real Iceland day: _"I feel like the car pick up, check in/out, drive... They all look very similar visually."_ **Read this entry before adding a row to the day list.** The report is true and **the sameness was decided on purpose, three times** — `StayRow` reuses `.transition-row`'s geometry _"deliberately … because it is the same kind of row"_, `.day-trv` is _"deliberately the same shape language as `.journey`"_, and `.day-trv-ic` joins the day's _"ONE leading edge for icons"_ — three locally-correct reuse calls (ADR-0064 / ADR-0206 / ADR-0209) whose **sum nobody drew**. That is why the file proposes a rule about the KINDS of row a day has rather than a new look for one row: a fourth local decision is how a fourth near-identical box gets built. **§1 is the census and it is byte-identical, not merely similar** — the transition row and the stay row report the same `12px` radius, the same resolved background and the same ⁦32×32⁩ badge because they are the same class, and ⁦5 of 6⁩ rows are a rounded amber-tinted badge-led box. **Hue is ruled out in one line** (rule 4: all three are bound to time; and teal is already spent INSIDE the journey block on the `on-way` arm, so a teal drive would erase the one state the traveller switches on by hand), which leaves **silhouette** — the axis `.day-ambient .day-total` already spends (_"the one row in this strip that is NOT a card, and that is the decision"_). Promotes: a **point** keeps the box, takes a circular badge, and its clock gains a hairline box open on the side time runs free, drawing `edgeMeaning`'s `exact`/`not-before`/`not-after`/`window` — whose finding is that **a car pick-up and a check-in are BOTH `not-before`**, i.e. two of the three things reported are the same thing to the model while a flight is categorically different and renders identically anyway; a **line** loses the box for a dashed track in the same badge column that **overshoots the ⁦10px⁩ gutter by ⁦10px⁩** so it touches both neighbours (measured — this is precisely why it is not ADR-0159 §3's rail, which floated because it kept the list's rhythm), ⁦58px⁩ → ⁦40px⁩ against a ⁦70px⁩ event card; an **envelope** loses the box AND the amber for a bracket opening into the day (`dayBookendStays`' `{woke, sleeps}` already says which way). **The render paid for the file four times.** (1) The bracket had to move from the badge to the ROW — around the ⁦34px⁩ badge it read as a stray corner, at the row's height it embraces the row. (2) **A false finding, caught and reversed:** candidate ד was reported as truncating a long hotel name off a screenshot that had **no webfont**, so every glyph was the fallback's wider one; under the real Assistant it fits, and the row now measures the BOX each candidate gives the name plus a genuine stress case where ג and ד clip and א and ב do not. `pitfalls.md` warns about this exactly and it still landed. (3) **A specificity tie decided a design question** — `.day-trv.miss` (0,2,0) out-specified the proposal's `.bld-trv` (0,1,0) so a missed leg kept its box by accident, and looking at it settled the arm rather than exposing a bug: `miss` keeps box and tile and drops the track, `on-way` loses the box and rides the track. (4) A stray corner on candidates ב/ג from the same class of tie. **Also drawn, deliberately not claimed:** the hire is named twice one row apart with the same clock (`Iceland Car Rental · איסוף הרכב · מ-00:00` over `איסוף הרכב · קפלאוויק · מ-00:00`) — the duplication ADR-0209 §1 removed for STAYS and never applied to hires. Interactive: theme, width, the drive candidate (א–ד), the stay candidate (א–ד, where **ד is the live zero-CSS alternative** and its cost is measured), the bound mark, the badge shape, the day scenario (רגיל · **עמוס** · Plan — Trip and Plan both, per ADR-0159 §1), and three feel numbers with the recommendation as the default. Renders through nine shipped stylesheets via the `APP-CSS` manifest, so every tint and radius in the "before" column is the shipped rule and not a drawing of it.

- [`mockups/the-gap-is-not-free-time-v1.html`](../../mockups/the-gap-is-not-free-time-v1.html) — **הפער אינו זמן חופשי · what the hero's now-slot should say when nothing is running** (2026-08-29) — **Accepted and BUILT the same day** ([ADR-0211](../decisions/0211-a-gap-has-a-character.md); [session note](../planning/2026-08-29-the-gap-is-not-free-time.md)). Owner, with a screenshot: _"not always we want to show that we have free time, for example on this screenshot we're on the way … Also at night or early in the morning it should show different messages. On the empty state (no upcoming events), etc."_ The screenshot is the witness — the card prints `זמן חופשי` in its title while its own journey line, 40px lower, prints `נסיעה · בדרך`. **The finding: `זמן חופשי` isn't a derived state, it's `Board`'s final `else`** (`Board.tsx:929-936` picks `in-transit → group-split → now → 'free'`; `:441-446` renders it as the branch nothing caught), so it's a positive claim resting on an absence — which is exactly what ADR-0208 and ADR-0207 already forbid everywhere else on this surface, and the same shape [ADR-0160](../decisions/0160-the-hero-lifts-and-shows-a-horizon.md) §S named from the other side when it fixed the _silence_ the same `else` caused. Proposes one closed set of five characters, first match wins, each standing on something already in the code: **on the way** (the `בדרך` device mark), **at the stay** (`travelOrigin → wokeIn` **and** the clock outside `DAY_WINDOW`), **day's done**, **empty day**, **open** (the honest gap, which finally gets `עד HH:MM`). Night and early morning are the _same_ member — only which side of `DAY_WINDOW` you're on differs, and that's a claim about the clock rather than the person. **Three things reading the code turned up.** "Night" needs no new constant: `DAY_WINDOW` is ⁦07:00–23:00⁩ and `dayProgress` clamps, so at ⁦02:40⁩ the rail's knob pins at ⁦0%⁩ under a label reading `עכשיו` — the board telling somebody in bed they're standing at ⁦07:00⁩. The `free` branch renders no `.wp-board-now-meta` while `now` does, so the board leaves a slot empty for a fact `GlanceCard` is carrying two inches lower (visible in the owner's own screenshot). And the next slot **already crosses midnight** — `deriveNow` is handed the whole trip's events with no date filter, so at ⁦22:40⁩ the board is already showing tomorrow's ⁦07:00⁩ flight with nothing saying it is tomorrow (`סוף היום` fires only when the trip has no timed event left at all). **Measured:** the recommended on-the-way draw costs ⁦+0px⁩ (words only), the night board ⁦+3px⁩ (the ⁦23px⁩ rail off, a meta line on), the gap's meta line ⁦17px⁩ in an existing slot, and **zero** new controls — so nothing to measure against ADR-0017's ⁦44px⁩ floor. Proposed CSS is one declaration. **What the render withdrew:** the draft rejected §1's option ב׳ (`בדרך ל־BBQ Mirage`) as ADR-0118's bidi trap; probing the rendered title character by character reports the order correct — a Latin run at the _end_ of a Hebrew line reads LTR within itself, and ADR-0118 is about a run in the _middle_. ב׳ stays rejected on the two grounds that survived (it names the destination twice ⁦28px⁩ apart, and `ל־` breaks agreement before a definite article), and the probe ships as a measurement row. **§5 and §6 are a second round, on the owner's follow-up** (_"at night when the day is over the hero should also have some kind of a lookahead … Should it be on the lifted hero? I'm not sure"_) — and they **withdraw this file's own larger claim**, which the first draft had already written into a session note, a backlog line and a PR description. `deriveNow` (`lib/time.ts:312`) carries no date filter, so the lookahead **already exists on both elevations** and `heroHorizon` is passed `shownNext` itself. Proved with a throwaway `vitest` against the real `deriveNow`/`heroHorizon` rather than by reading a second time — six assertions, listed in the session note as what the build should pin; reading `Board.tsx` alone is what produced the wrong claim, which is root `CLAUDE.md`'s "count the call sites" rule paid the hard way. So the answer to the question is not "add a lookahead" but **the lookahead is silent about which day it points at**, and what is missing is three things and no new slot: no day token on `.wp-board-next-meta` (the app ships exactly that fix as `endDay`, §M); `אחר כך` stopping at midnight because `thenAfter` filters `dayEvents` (the same function handed the whole trip finds tomorrow's second stop); and `סוף היום` in both slots on the trip's last day. **On "should it be on the lifted hero": both, and ADR-0160 §1 already made the split** — the collapsed board carries the point, the lifted hero the depth on it, and it already resolves place/note/file/tasks for a cross-day next. §12 is not strained: `אחר כך` stays one quiet line, measured ⁦30px⁩ against §12's stated ⁦28px⁩ (the difference is the loaded webfont), the lifted card going ⁦369px⁩ → ⁦399px⁩ with nothing else added. **One fork left open for the owner, and the file doesn't pretend to have decided it:** how `בדרך` is drawn (א׳ the destination becomes the now point and `הבא בתור` moves on — ⁦+20px⁩, takes the countdown off the screen, and pulls `אחר כך` into the second slot, which is ADR-0160 §12's Day-tab competition; ב׳ the full sentence; **ג׳ the recommendation** — `כרגע` / `בדרך` alone). Interactive: theme, width, and §1's three-way fork with the recommendation as the default. Renders four theme×width combinations, webfonts loaded, no console errors. Also rejected in the file, with reasons: `ישנים` as the night title (a claim about a person, no sensor — ADR-0207 §2); a separate "about to leave" state (the countdown already swaps to the leave-by, ADR-0206 §Z1, and a passed clock is not a claim about a person); and any suggestion content on an empty day (`GlanceCard` is Home's "what could we do" surface, ADR-0160 §10).

  **WHERE IT NOW DIFFERS FROM THE SHIPPED APP** (not retrofitted — a mockup is the dated record of what was decided then). The owner chose §1's option ג׳ and the build shipped it, with **three deviations recorded in ADR-0211's build log**. (1) The file draws the live badge as `לילה` / `בוקר` / `היום` **and** repeats the same word in the label ⁦20px⁩ below; the app keeps the badge at `עכשיו` except on `on-the-way`, where the swap is the shipped transit costume rather than a repetition — and there it swaps **both** badge and label to teal, which the file drew and the first build missed. (2) The file's `at-the-stay` title is a resolved place name; the app uses the stay's own title, because `.stay-strip` one surface down already renders `<b>{stayNow.title}</b>`. (3) §5's `יום פנוי` frame draws `יום 3 מתוך 12` in its meta row — **not built**, and not decided either way; the trip-position read is a separate question the ADR does not take.

- [`mockups/the-now-line-is-inside-something-v1.html`](../../mockups/the-now-line-is-inside-something-v1.html) — **מחוון עכשיו · where we are in the day, pointed at** (2026-09-02), promoting **[ADR-0217](../decisions/0217-the-now-marker-points-it-does-not-separate.md)** (**Accepted and BUILT 2026-09-02**, §§1–5; [session note](../planning/2026-09-02-the-marker-points-it-does-not-separate.md)). Owner, against both day surfaces: _"the now line appears before the event, giving the wrong impression that the event isn't taking place yet"_, and then, against this file's own passes in turn: _"We don't **have** to keep a row saying what's now. We can do an arrow or idk… prominent enough so that people immediately and intuitively understand"_, then _"just make sure to you fix the issue where the playhead is going over the text… Other cases looked great as is"_, then — after a translucent band was tried in its place — _"I actually preferred much better when it didn't go over the event rows, like you originally did."_ **§1** the defect, drawn on both shipped hosts — `DayView` puts the line above a running row and `lib/share-now-line.ts` puts it below, so two surfaces from one decision disagree (`docs/backlog.md:44`), and the card itself already says `עכשיו` two rows away. **§2** the model, over the shipped `styles/day-track.css` strip: a day is a continuous partition, and a moment can be inside **two** rows at once (ADR-0041), which is why an index cannot answer it. **§3** the proposal — a solid amber **arrow** in the day's margin at the exact height of `now`, with a ⁦2px⁩ rule that disappears behind the row holding the moment: **the occlusion is the sentence**. Three shapes were drawn before that stood: a rule occluded only by a CARD's background struck through a gap's label and a leg's two lines (ADR-0210 §3/§4 took the box away from both on purpose); cutting the rule at the row's edge left two ⁦16px⁩ stubs (_"totally cutoff"_); a translucent band over everything solved both and was refused for crossing the event rows. The answer was shipped one selector over — `.day-trv-ic .icon`'s `--screen` halo, which already stops the rule behind the leg's glyph — now carried by the gap's label and the leg's **inner** text runs. **§4** four answers to "longer events look longer", with §4א (true proportional height) refused by measurement. **§5** eight edge cases, each pinned to its own instant: envelope, cluster, leg, day head, day tail, point, midnight, settled, empty day. **§6** Trip and Plan. **§7** the four drawings that lost, kept because their reasons are measurements.

  **Interactive:** theme, screen (⁦360×640⁩ · ⁦390×844⁩), **a clock scrubber plus six presets** (every frame is drawn at that instant — this is how §5's cases are reached), mode (טיול · תכנון), and four feel controls: the rule behind/over/none, the arrow at ⁦14⁩/⁦18⁩/⁦22px⁩, the wording (`1:10 שע׳` vs `נותרו 1:10 שע׳`), and §4's two px-per-minute numbers.

  **WHAT RENDERING IT FOUND.** One defect in our own tooling, **fixed in this change**: `scripts/render.mjs` measured its first theme×width pass in the fallback typeface, because `document.fonts.load()` resolves when a face is fetched and not when it is applied — one `.wp-event-timemeta` read ⁦105.8px⁩ in pass 1 and ⁦123.4px⁩ in pass 3, so the same box was reported as ⁦72px⁩ **and** ⁦91px⁩ in one `measurements.md` with no error either way. A throwaway `page.screenshot()` before the loop fixes it (Playwright's screenshot does wait for fonts). Three more were defects in this file's own drafts and are recorded in its header: a time chip at a card's leading edge lands on the ⁦40px⁩ badge in RTL; an off-frame probe at a hand-set ⁦328px⁩ missed a wrap boundary the live ⁦326px⁩ card crosses; a nested row's arrow stood ⁦32px⁩ further in than its parent's until `--now-bleed` was overridden per brace; a measurement row printed a hard-coded `2` for the rule's thickness behind a `?? 2` fallback, because `querySelector` cannot return a pseudo-element (it reads `getComputedStyle(el, '::before')` now); the journey block was drawn with invented tone classes (`ahead`/`done`) where `DayJoinRow` takes `time | miss | on-way`, and deriving `on-way` from the clock would have shown the playhead flipping the traveller's own switch; and Plan's dashed core came out at full strength across the row, because a `repeating-linear-gradient` cannot carry a second axis of alpha.

  **WHERE IT DIFFERS FROM THE SHIPPED APP.** §§1–5 shipped and the app draws them; **§4's proportional gaps (the file's §4ג) did not** — deliberately, per ADR-0217's build log §5 — so the day in §4ג is the only frame here the app does not produce. §7's four rejected drawings are of course not shipped either; they are the dated record of what lost. And the build found something the drawing could not: there were **three** now-marks in the app, not one — `DayView`'s `.nowline`, `PlanDay`'s `.nowref` and the shared reader's own copy — so what shipped is one component (`ui/domain/NowMarker.tsx`) replacing all three, taking a formatted label rather than a `Date` precisely so the shared reader can take it next. Everywhere §3 and §6 draw — the app still renders `.nowline` as an interleaved row. §1 and §7א are the shipped marker, drawn deliberately as the baseline.

- [`mockups/the-shared-reader-gets-the-playhead-v1.html`](../../mockups/the-shared-reader-gets-the-playhead-v1.html) — **המחוון בדף הציבורי · the shared reader gets the app's playhead** (2026-09-02), promoting **[ADR-0217](../decisions/0217-the-now-marker-points-it-does-not-separate.md)'s second 2026-09-02 amendment** (**built the same evening**). Owner, when the marker was first built: _"my next task to you would be to (design first of course) and then build the corresponding now line for the live sharing screen"_. **The file asked for this by name:** `lib/share-now-line.ts` deviates from `nowLinePlacement` on purpose — START where the app compared END — and closes with _"unify it with the app's when `nowLinePlacement` grows its `inside` shape"_. **§1** the defect, and it is the deviation's own half that nothing had written down: the start-based rule puts the marker BELOW every row that has begun, so at ⁦14:30⁩ on a day with a ⁦10:00–16:00⁩ tour and a ⁦14:00–15:00⁩ shrine the page says two things happening right now are behind you — the original report with the sign flipped. **§2** the mechanism, and it adds no rule: `lib/now-inside.ts` is unit-agnostic, so it takes `dawnOrder`'s **minutes** here and epoch ms on a day surface; a span needs BOTH labels, so a point cannot hold a moment by construction rather than by rule, and midnight is free because `dawnOrder` adds a day at both ends. **§3** the ground is the day BODY's `color-mix(--paper 34%, --card)` and never `--screen`, drawn wrong-vs-right. **§4** the arrow against an ⁦11px⁩ gutter, with `.sh-day`'s `overflow: hidden` making the bleed a ceiling rather than a preference. **§5** six cases including the three refusals, all of which stand. **§6** the caption question this page raises and the day surfaces do not.

  **Interactive:** theme, screen (⁦360×640⁩ · ⁦390×844⁩), the arrow at ⁦15⁩/⁦18⁩/⁦22px⁩ and the boundary room at ⁦6⁩/⁦9px⁩ — the two feel numbers, defaults shipped as the recommendation.

  **WHAT RENDERING IT FOUND, and it changed the answer.** §6 was drafted recommending that the **mark** carry the clock here, since `.sh-event` has no `עכשיו` chip and ADR-0217 §1's "no caption" rests on the row having one. Drawn, that is unusable: the gutter is ⁦11px⁩, so a chip at the arrow's height lands **on** the row's own title and clock — the exact defect the owner rejected twice while ADR-0217 was being drawn. The file keeps it as §6ג, refused in a picture, and the shipped answer is §6ב: the **row** says the word through `.sh-event-now`, so §1's premise becomes true on every surface instead of gaining an exception.

  **WHERE IT DIFFERS FROM THE SHIPPED APP.** §1's left column is the page as it was and is kept as the "before"; everything else shipped the same evening. §6א and §6ג are the two drawings that lost.

- [`mockups/where-the-marker-stands-when-nothing-holds-it-v1.html`](../../mockups/where-the-marker-stands-when-nothing-holds-it-v1.html) — **גבולות המחוון · where the now-marker stands when no row holds the moment** (2026-09-02), promoting **[ADR-0217](../decisions/0217-the-now-marker-points-it-does-not-separate.md)'s 2026-09-02 amendment** ([session note](../planning/2026-09-02-the-marker-points-it-does-not-separate.md)). Owner, from a phone at ⁦00:17⁩ on a day whose first event is ⁦02:00⁩ — the boundary form, hours before anything starts: _"I'm not sure about the day start and end boundaries. It looks kind of awkward sitting there hugging the event card with no space at all."_ **§1** the defect at four instants, and the measurement reframes the report: the day list separates rows with **bottom margins only** (`.wp-event` ⁦10px⁩, `.day-gap` ⁦9px⁩, nothing carries a `margin-top`), so a zero-height mark lands ⁦0px⁩ above the row below it **everywhere** — head ⁦11 · 0⁩, **between two ordinary cards ⁦10 · 0⁩** (reached whenever a hole is under `GAP_MIN_MINUTES`, since `gapBetween` is floored and a ⁦20⁩-minute hole draws no row), tail ⁦10 · 12⁩. The between-cards case was never reported because a rule on a card's top border reads as the card's own edge; a fix aimed at "the edges" would have missed it, and the tail needs nothing. **§2** the number is not ours to choose — `.day-unplaced` (ADR-0171 §10a's other line-between-rows) already takes `margin: 9px 0`, drawn beside the mark in one list. **§3** four candidates at the reported instant: א room, ב the head hole as a real gap row, ג room + the clock, ד no mark at all. **§4** the five cases with the recommendation applied. **§5** Trip and Plan (ADR-0159 §1 / ADR-0043 §5).

  **Interactive:** theme, screen (⁦360×640⁩ · ⁦390×844⁩), the candidate (א·ב·ג·ד), the room in px (⁦6⁩·⁦9⁩·⁦12⁩·⁦14⁩ — a feel call defaulting to the app's own ⁦9⁩), and the mode. Every frame carries `.sec-title` above the list and the shelf heading below it.

  **WHAT RENDERING IT FOUND.** `.nowline-chip` is a ⁦12%⁩ translucent fill — built to sit BESIDE a hairline, never on one — so `.now-here > *`'s z-index lifted the clock above the rule without making it opaque and **the rule struck straight through the digits**, which is the exact defect the previous round spent three drawings on. The answer was one rule down in the same sheet: `--now-ground` and the ⁦3.5px⁩ halo the gap's label already carries. Two more were this file's own: the proposed rule was written **unscoped**, so the "as shipped" columns wore the fix and the table reported the defect and the repair as the same ⁦10 · 9⁩; and the tail frame drew the mark **above** the sleep bookend where `DayView` renders it below (the bookends are not in `merged` at all), which is a different sentence — "the night is still ahead" rather than "everything the day listed is behind us".

  **WHERE IT DIFFERS FROM THE SHIPPED APP.** §3ג shipped the same evening (`--now-room: 9px` on `.now-here.edge` plus `.nowline-chip` grounded by `--now-ground`), so §1's four frames are the "before" and refreshing them would make both columns draw the fix — `inline-app-css.mjs` is deliberately not re-run on this file. §3's א/ב/ד are the drawings that lost. **One thing this file drew and the build had to decide:** §5 renders Plan's chip in `.nowline-chip`'s own amber and says out loud that a live-coloured clock on a drafting table is the question — the app ships it re-inked, `color: var(--plan)` on the chip and `background: var(--plan)` on its dot, colour only.

  **AND WHY THE FIRST MOCKUP COULD NOT HAVE FOUND THIS.** Every frame in `the-now-line-is-inside-something-v1.html` starts at the day's first ROW — `.sec-title` is in that file's inlined CSS and never once in its rendered tree, and neither is `.day-unplaced` or the shelf heading. **A boundary is a statement about both of its sides**, so a frame cropped to the rows can draw the day's head and show nothing wrong with it. Read as a rule for the next mockup of a list, not as a note about that one.

- [`mockups/a-flight-is-a-line-v1.html`](../../mockups/a-flight-is-a-line-v1.html) — **טיסה היא קו · how a flight, which is a movement AND the day's strongest commitment, gets drawn** (2026-08-29), promoting [ADR-0212](../decisions/0212-a-flight-is-a-line-that-is-also-a-commitment.md) — **APPROVED AND BUILT 2026-08-29**, the same day (_"other than that I take your recommendations, let's build this"_) — the ADR's build log records four things the drawing could not catch and one it got wrong. Owner, the day after ADR-0210 shipped: _"we've recently added a new design for driving/walking etc. between stops. Now I want to add a similar design for flights … (but distance why not)"_, then correcting his own caveat in the next message — _"of course we have times, because the flights have estimates when filling it"_. **Read this entry before treating the drive's answer as transferable to a flight.** It is not an extension of ADR-0210 but an **amendment** to it: §1 there hands the box to commitments on purpose (_"the only committed mark in the list"_), so drawing a flight as a line spends the mark that ADR reserved twenty-four hours earlier — which is why this file draws **four candidates and measures each** instead of assuming. ADR-0210 names a flight only in passing and only as a **point**; a flight is both families at once, and that is the question it never had to take. Measured on the owner's own day with the app's own `haversineMeters`: ⁦5,292 ק״מ⁩ of it moves by air against ⁦69 ק״מ⁩ by road, and the ⁦69⁩ is the only part drawn as movement. **The grammar it spends is already decided twice and mints nothing** — `DayConnector` reserved _"solid + amber"_ in writing and ADR-0206 §D1 spent it on the routed map line, so dashed = a line we guessed, solid = a line somebody booked, and a flight's track is the second. **The owner's first correction became the file's sharpest rule:** ADR-0206 §D5 read from its other end says do not hedge what you were **told**, so a drive is `נסיעה · ~52 דק׳` and a flight `טיסה · 4:20 שע׳` — same row, opposite confidence, and the tilde is the entire proposal (no new class; `.day-trv-hd` and `.day-trv-dist` verbatim). Crow-flies is not a floor here but the **right** number, a great circle being roughly the flight path, and `TRAVEL_GATE` still refuses flights so no provider is asked anything. **His second correction, mid-session, killed the file's own first draft:** _"it shouldn't show that there's a gap, unless the gap it's between flights (in layovers)"_ — so every frame from §2 on draws a **layover**, the card candidates group inside `.journey` (ADR-0159's container, reused rather than re-invented), and the track spans the **run** rather than one leg. §1 keeps the gap chip because §1 is the defect. **What the render found and the reading got wrong: the two icon columns are ⁦5px⁩ apart, not the ⁦4px⁩ the sheets say** — `--trv-track` is `12+19` against the event badge's `15+20`, plus `.wp-event`'s `1px` border that the face's padding starts inside — so a track through both is not a line but two parallel ones, which kills the recommended candidate until they align. `.day-trv-ic`'s own comment claims the day has _"ONE leading edge for icons"_: true enough for two badges sitting still, false for the first thing that had to travel between them. It is a control in the file and §2 of the ADR. **A second defect the drawing nearly shipped**, same class as ADR-0210's specificity tie and caught the same way: the bead wrapper's z-index rule named `.wp-event` only, so the moment two legs grouped inside `.journey` it stopped matching and the track painted **over** the cards. **Also measured:** ב costs ⁦0px⁩ (the card is the height it already was — ⁦90px⁩ at 360, ⁦71px⁩ at 390), א is ⁦38px⁩ but deletes the lock, the confirmation code, the document/task marks and the detail sheet from the day surface, ג is ⁦95px⁩ — the tallest row in the day, spent on the two words already in `routeTitle` — and ד is ADR-0210's already-rejected "low line" shape; `dayTravelTotal` sums unconditionally, so a flight takes the day's total ⁦69 ק״מ⁩ → ⁦5,362 ק״מ⁩, **78×**, and its `navigate` glyph becomes the second false claim of its own docblock; and `formatDistance` has never seen four digits, printing `2931 ק״מ` with no separator. Interactive: theme, width, **the icon column** (as-shipped vs aligned — the control that decides ca

  **A THIRD CORRECTION CAME DURING THE BUILD, and it is the one to read before drawing UI COPY in this repo at all.** The file's layover band said `קומה`. The owner: _"don't call it קומה, call it עצירת ביניים, like the other vocabulary used on the app"_ — and `t.day.join.word.flight` has read `עצירת ביניים` since ADR-0159, with `החלפה` for a train and a bus (one word per transport mode), while `t.day.join.text` orders the parts `word · place · length`, which is not what the drawing did either. **The drawing had invented vocabulary the shipped copy already had**, which is precisely the failure the `APP-CSS` manifest prevents for stylesheets and nothing prevents for strings. The file now COPIES the composer, the way it already copies `formatDistance`; the fix was to the mockup, not to the app.

  **WHERE IT NOW DIFFERS FROM THE SHIPPED APP** (not retrofitted — a mockup is the dated record of what was decided then, and `inline-app-css.mjs` is deliberately NOT re-run on this file: its §1 is the "before" column and refreshing it would make both columns draw the fix). §1 draws the gap chip between two flights, which is the defect; the app now draws `עצירת ביניים`. §2's candidate ב is drawn with the file's own `.fl-bead`; the app ships it as `.day-thread`, and the alignment control's "aligned" position is simply what the app now does. §5's three treatments are drawn as three options; the app ships the split. The build also **generalised past flights** — `spendsSpanInMotion` replaced "is it a flight" everywhere, so a train and a bus get the thread, the distance and the air total by being carried — and the ⁦5px⁩ turned out not to be a rule that moves on its own: `--trv-track` positions the track while the glyph sits at the face's padding plus half the mark, so the column moved as one thing (face ⁦12px⁩ → ⁦16px⁩, mark ⁦38px⁩ → ⁦40px⁩). The thread is **Trip mode's only**; Plan gets both facts and no line, because its rows sit inside reorder/drag machinery no drawing has covered.ndidate ב), and the track weight. Renders six shipped stylesheets through the `APP-CSS` manifest, four theme×width combinations, no console errors.

It established preset-first sharing and independently configurable sensitive fields, but is superseded by `an-itinerary-is-shared-from-every-trip-v2.html` for entry points, live-link management, and PDF export.

Sharing is reachable from both the trip header and every All Trips card. The link opens without sign-in into a compact trip overview and expandable day spine. Summary, Full, and Everything projections alter the same stable page; live, stale, and unavailable states make link behavior visible. - [`mockups/a-shared-itinerary-feels-like-an-invitation-v2.html`](../../mockups/a-shared-itinerary-feels-like-an-invitation-v2.html) — the **superseded second public shared-itinerary reader** exploration (2026-08-29, supersedes the first round and is superseded by v3's real daypart sections; [ADR-0213](../decisions/0213-a-shared-trip-changes-emphasis-and-print-is-its-own-rendering.md); [session note](../planning/2026-08-29-itinerary-sharing.md)). Detail levels change emphasis rather than merely hiding rows: Summary is an inspiring route-led overview with morning/day/evening/night rhythm; Full quiets the route composition and foregrounds times, places, travel legs, and map links; Everything appends only owner-selected operational details. An owner cover is optional, the truthful fallback is the trip's own route, and stock imagery is refused. Travelive stays quiet until a friendly closing invitation. Interactive: theme, 360/390px width, projection, link state, and day expansion. Rendered in all four theme×width combinations with fonts loaded and no console errors; Summary's route hero is 199.8px and visible actions meet the 44px touch floor.

- [`mockups/a-shared-itinerary-is-printed-v1.html`](../../mockups/a-shared-itinerary-is-printed-v1.html) — the **superseded first compact print/PDF exploration** (2026-08-29; [ADR-0213](../decisions/0213-a-shared-trip-changes-emphasis-and-print-is-its-own-rendering.md); [session note](../planning/2026-08-29-itinerary-sharing.md)). It is a dedicated fixed-light A4 renderer, not a screenshot of the public page: Summary targets one page, the nine-day Full reference is two pages, and Everything moves selected sensitive fields into a labelled appendix. It uses restrained daypart marks, a dense two-column day grid, break-safe days, written URL, page count, generated-at time, and a QR position. The checkered QR is intentionally a mockup placeholder; production must generate a real code for the same written URL. Interactive review controls cover projection and review theme. The generated two-page PDF was rendered back to images and inspected with no clipping or overlap; unscaled DOM measurements are 50px day headers, 36px event rows, and 41px daylight rails.

- [`mockups/a-shared-itinerary-is-organized-by-the-day-v3.html`](../../mockups/a-shared-itinerary-is-organized-by-the-day-v3.html) — the **superseded** public shared-itinerary reader round (superseded 2026-08-30 by `a-shared-itinerary-tells-the-trip-v4.html`, which keeps its day spine and daypart sections and rehauls what a ROW says) (2026-08-29; supersedes v2's daylight-row proposal; [ADR-0213](../decisions/0213-a-shared-trip-changes-emphasis-and-print-is-its-own-rendering.md); [session note](../planning/2026-08-29-itinerary-sharing.md)). Owner: _"The morning noon afternoon evening row is meaningless … the schedule should split to the parts of the day"_ and later requested an LLM/skills-ready infrastructure with no sensitive model input and no approval step. Morning/noon/afternoon/evening/night/flexible are now non-empty schedule sections, not decoration. Day titles and synopses switch between automatically published generated narrative and a deterministic route/event fallback; the grouping and privacy projection do not change. The mockup records the narrow `SummaryNarrativeInput` boundary and explicitly refuses a generic agent runtime in sharing v1. Interactive: theme, 360/390px, projection, generated/fallback narrative, link state, and day expansion. All four theme×width renders load fonts with no console errors; daypart headings measure 30px, Summary rows 38px, and day headers 76px. **Built 2026-08-30** as `frontend/src/screens/SharedItinerary.tsx` — with one correction: the proposal block styles against `--text-meta` and `--teal-deep`, neither of which exists in `styles/tokens.css`, so those declarations were inert here; the shipped page uses `--text-micro` and `--teal`.

- [`mockups/a-shared-itinerary-is-printed-by-daypart-v2.html`](../../mockups/a-shared-itinerary-is-printed-by-daypart-v2.html) — the **superseded** print/PDF reference (superseded 2026-08-30 by `a-shared-itinerary-is-printed-as-a-story-v3.html`, which keeps its multicol fragmentation and break-safe day) (2026-08-29; supersedes v1's decorative daylight rail; [ADR-0213](../decisions/0213-a-shared-trip-changes-emphasis-and-print-is-its-own-rendering.md); [session note](../planning/2026-08-29-itinerary-sharing.md)). Web and paper now share the daypart derivation while keeping independent renderers. Summary preserves non-empty daypart sections without exact facts; Full adds times, places, and travel; Everything keeps its appendix. Generated narrative may publish automatically, but PDF creation consumes a validated result or deterministic fallback and never invokes a model. Interactive: review theme, 360/390px preview, projection, and generated/fallback narrative. All four renders have no console errors; the unscaled A4 DOM measures 47px day headers, 18px daypart headings, and 31px event rows. The generated two-page Full PDF was rendered back to images with no clipping or overlap. The checkered QR remains a mockup-only placeholder — the shipped renderer generates a real one. **Built 2026-08-30** as `backend/src/sharing/itinerary-pdf.template.ts`, which also carries the one thing this file could not show: inlining the app's fonts **without their `unicode-range` split** lets the Latin face win for every Hebrew codepoint, and the page still looks right while the PDF's Hebrew becomes unextractable.

- [`mockups/sharing-and-inviting-are-one-control-v1.html`](../../mockups/sharing-and-inviting-are-one-control-v1.html) — **the share control grants two different things, so it asks who the link is for** (2026-08-30; owner: _"The share button should be both for sharing the trip and inviting (let's mockup this)"_; [ADR-0213](../decisions/0213-a-shared-trip-changes-emphasis-and-print-is-its-own-rendering.md) 2026-08-30 amendment). `/join/<code>` (ADR-0067) adds a person to the roster with full live data and edit rights; `/s/<code>` (ADR-0213) hands a stranger a revocable projection with no account. They are two **grants**, not two formats — so the sheet gains one question above everything it already does (`למי זה הולך?`) and branches under it, rather than growing a third button beside `לינק חי` and `PDF`. Promotes: the fork as a second `ChoiceGrid` over the same `.choice-card` the levels use (no new mechanism — the whole proposed CSS block is 36 lines, and that number is the argument); the invite link drawn as a **44px tappable row** in the neutral `.share-link` idiom rather than the settings screen's `.invite-box`, which paints `--plan-tint` and a dashed violet border **outside Plan mode** and so spends rule 4's reserved hue (ADR-0028); one link-row component hosted by both the sheet and Trip Settings, since two copies of "the trip's link" is the duplication rule 8 exists to prevent; and join as the **default** audience, because it is the common case for a live trip and the one you cannot reach today without leaving the screen. Rejects, by name: three peer outcomes in one row (the two that exist are two formats of one grant, and a row of three teaches that they are interchangeable — the expensive misfire being someone who meant to send a peek and instead added their mother-in-law to the trip); one link that "lets the recipient choose", which hands the authorization decision to the party that does not hold it and inverts ADR-0067's "the code **is** the grant"; and leaving the invite in settings behind a shortcut, which keeps the long journey and adds a sign to it. A peer sees the invite branch in full — not a new rule, just what `POST /trips/:id/invite` already does (get-or-create for any member); only `rotate` is the admin's, exactly like the read-only branch's quiet manage row. Interactive: theme, 360/390px, audience, role (admin/peer), and — the feel call the file deliberately does not settle — whether the two audience cards carry a glyph, since the detail-level cards beneath them carry none. All four theme×width renders load fonts with no console errors; the audience card measures 66px and the link row 44px against ADR-0017's floor, the fork costs 66px on top of today's 346px read-only sheet, and the `.invite-box` it replaces is 67.8px.

- [`mockups/a-level-is-a-link-not-a-setting-v1.html`](../../mockups/a-level-is-a-link-not-a-setting-v1.html) — the **superseded** first pass at multi-link sharing (2026-08-31, superseded the same day by `a-link-is-a-policy-not-a-level-v2.html`). It drew one link per **detail level**, which counts the wrong thing: a level is not a policy, and Everything is a family of `2³ × 2ⁿ` of them. Kept as the dated record of the design that was corrected, and still the origin of three things v2 carries unchanged — the `--ok` live mark on the level card (rule 4: a live link is a **status**, and `--cta` resolves to `var(--ink)` in light where it reads as decoration and merges with the selected ring), the mark drawn out of flow (`.choice-card` is a flex column the grid stretches to its tallest sibling, so a mark in the icon slot would raise all three cards), and the `--miss` stop-all that renders only at two or more live links. Its own render is what caught the **manifest-order defect** described in v2's entry.

- [`mockups/a-link-is-a-policy-not-a-level-v2.html`](../../mockups/a-link-is-a-policy-not-a-level-v2.html) — **a link is a policy, so Everything holds several and Summary holds one** (2026-08-31; owner, on v1: _"The הכל category could have different levels of detail based on what you allow, so maybe for that there could be multiple different links. Have you taken that into consideration?"_; [ADR-0213](../decisions/0213-a-shared-trip-changes-emphasis-and-print-is-its-own-rendering.md) tenth amendment, **built 2026-08-31**). Promotes: `@@unique([tripId, policyHash])` over a hash of level + the three sensitive booleans + the sorted file ids — the technique `ItineraryNarrative.inputHash` already runs in the same module — which keeps `PUT` idempotent (same policy, same hash, same code, so the sheet still needs no Save) and lets every existing row keep its code, so no shipped link stops resolving; the counts 1 / 1 / `2³ × 2ⁿ` **derived** from the policy space rather than written down as three cases; the deletion of the sheet's debounced write and its `levelSaved` announcement **entirely** (under a policy key nothing in the sheet mutates a live link — a toggle selects the policy you are about to hand over), which inverts the third pass's _"moving the control moves the link"_ on the grounds that a live link is a promise to a named audience; an asymmetric body where Summary and Full keep today's loud send unit unchanged at 525.6px and Everything renders `ListRow`/`RowManageSheet` with each row titled by its **derived** policy (`קודים · פתקים · 2 קבצים`); and a list that costs no CSS, because `.share-send` is already a raised `overflow: hidden` container and `.wp-listrow` already draws its own hairline. Rejects, by name: v1's level key, which forces a choice between the sister who needs codes and the hotel that needs names; **user-typed link names**, more firmly than v1 did — the policy title says what the link _reveals_, which the app can verify, where a typed name says who it went to, which it cannot and which goes stale silently (a name stays a backlog item as an addition to the derived title, not a replacement); **one list of every link in the trip above the selector**, which lengthens the common path to serve the rare one; and **editing a live link's policy in place**, which is incoherent under a policy key since a changed policy is a different link — "let my sister also see notes" is a new link plus stopping the old, and that cost is stated rather than hidden. Interactive: theme, 360/390px, selected level, how many Everything links exist (0 · 1 · 3), and the two feel calls it does **not** settle — the mark's hue (`--ok` · `--cta`) and diameter (7 · 9px). All four theme×width renders load fonts with no console errors; Everything with three links is 664.6px against Summary's 525.6px, the create sheet 679.1px and the `⋯` sheet 384.1px, with list row 61px, kebab 44px, level card 58px (identical across all three), link row 44px, outcome 48px and switch row 52px against ADR-0017's floor. Drawing the `⋯` with invented class names measured 136.9px for five actions and is what exposed the guess; the real `.wp-row-actions`/`.wp-row-action` tree is 384.1px with the danger partition the primitive draws on its own. **Rendering v1 also found a manifest-order defect in `sharing-and-inviting-are-one-control-v1.html`**: `.modal-form` (12px) and `.share-sheet` (16px) are a one-class specificity tie decided by emission order, that file lists `screens.css` before the primitives — the opposite of the app, which reaches `form-actions.css` via `screens/Home` → `HostTasks` → `TaskSheet` → `FormActions` at `App.tsx:64` before `import './screens.css'` at :113 — and it reported 16px only because its own proposed block re-declared the rule.

- [`mockups/a-shared-itinerary-knows-what-day-it-is-v1.html`](../../mockups/a-shared-itinerary-knows-what-day-it-is-v1.html) — **which day the public reader opens on, and how the spine says where the trip is** (2026-08-31; [ADR-0213](../decisions/0213-a-shared-trip-changes-emphasis-and-print-is-its-own-rendering.md) eleventh amendment; [session note](../planning/2026-08-31-the-page-knows-what-day-it-is.md)). Owner: _"I noticed that the live sharing page opens with the first day expanded. First of all - why? … during the trip … should scroll to and expand the current day. Maybe we should also add an indication to whether it's in the future, is happening right now, or has already happened."_ Answers "why" with **nobody decided it** — `useState(0)`, and the three reader mockups all shipped `open: 1` as a demo state. Promotes, in six sections: the page opening on the day the reader is in and **only** that day (today while the trip runs, **nothing open** before it and after it — the fallback to day 1 is the same index-pick the section criticises, and a fully closed spine measures 1043px against 1510px with one card open, so the reader sees more of the trip rather than less) with `DayView`'s own **"land on now"** contract reapplied at day altitude (today only, once per link, keyed on the day and not the clock tick, a peek left above, instant under reduced motion, a URL hash winning); the page's one amber **moving off `.open`** — a disclosure state already carried by the caret, the elevation and the open body — **onto the day the trip is on**; a mark on the exception rather than a badge per card, citing `App.css`'s deleted `.chip.past` by name and measuring the rejected variant at 13px × 11 cards; the masthead's constant `מסלול חי` becoming the trip's real phase, and `עודכן עכשיו` becoming elapsed plus a refetch on tab focus; and `.nowline` inside today's card **in the same pass**, as the thing the scroll aims at — its derivation turning out to cost nothing new, since `shareTimeLabel` is the very function that built every `startLabel` and zero-padded `HH:MM` strings order lexicographically as they order chronologically. **Rejects, by name:** falling back to day 1 when there is no today, a per-card state badge, amber on both `.open` and `.is-now`, writing the open day into the URL, opening every day at once, a server-stamped `today`, and any of it on paper. Interactive: theme, ⁦360×640⁩/⁦390×844⁩, trip phase (לפני · במהלך · אחרי), detail level, the **scroll target** (כרטיס היום · קו העכשיו · קו העכשיו בכל מחיר), and the **peek** above the landed card as a control rather than a settled number. All four theme×width renders load fonts with no console errors. **What the renders settled:** the first two scroll targets are the _same scroll_ on an ordinary day — the now-line lands at 381px of a 638px viewport with today's header whole at 27px — so "scroll to the now-line" and "scroll to today's card" differ only where a day is long enough to push the line past the fold; targeting the line at any cost buys 62px and clips the date row (header at −35px), which is rejected by measurement rather than argument. **What the renders changed:** the `עכשיו` mark lost the tinted ground it was drawn with (tint-inside-a-tint measured 4.53:1 against AA's 4.5 floor; word only is 5.09:1, beside the shipped `.sh-part-head`'s 5.24:1); the now-line moved under the daypart heading rather than above the section, because at 14:05 it put an afternoon that had already begun on the future side of now; and the phase derivation moved from day ordinals to dates, after the ordinal version rendered a finished trip as an unstarted one. **Built 2026-08-31.** Three things the real page changed, none of which a static frame could show: the share's `today` had to be dawn-anchored (`shareToday`) after opening the page at 01:48 Tokyo time marked _tomorrow_ as `עכשיו`; the now-line's boundary became what has BEGUN rather than what has finished, after the seeded Tokyo day's 10:00–16:00 tour dragged an end-based boundary to the top of the day; and the landing is `landAtTop`, which was already in the app and keeps re-aiming while lazy day photos change the extent above the target.

- [`mockups/a-shared-itinerary-tells-the-trip-v4.html`](../../mockups/a-shared-itinerary-tells-the-trip-v4.html) — **the public reader page, rehauled around one rule: a row says what the thing IS, then where and when** (2026-08-30; supersedes `a-shared-itinerary-is-organized-by-the-day-v3.html`, whose day spine and daypart sections it keeps; [ADR-0213](../decisions/0213-a-shared-trip-changes-emphasis-and-print-is-its-own-rendering.md)). Owner, on their own 12-day Iceland trip: _"the live sharing when all is shared is an abomination … nothing is linked to the events!"_ and _"Sharing should tell a story, not present information in a misleading way like it does now."_ Promotes, in five sections: the masthead reading `trip.name`/`trip.destination` instead of `fallbackTripTitle`'s first-place→last-place (which on any trip you fly to names two transit airports); a bookings block (`ההזמנות`) of five derived rows above the seventy-nine, each jumping to its day; a day named from Wikidata `P131` or from where you sleep, with the stay moved into the header so it stops sorting into the afternoon by its check-in hour (and stops printing `15:00–11:00`, a range that reads backwards across midnight); one journey block over chained legs (`prev.toPlaceId === next.fromPlaceId`) with the layover named from the gap between `endsAt` and the next `startsAt` — both derivable today, neither stored; and the appendix dissolved onto the rows it belongs to behind a per-row fold. **Rejects, by name:** publishing the appendix as-is and merely restyling it (the flatness is a missing `join`, not a privacy constraint — `Note.eventId` has existed since the first migration); parsing `Place.address` for a locality (proposed in the same session and dropped for `P131`, which answers structurally on an entity read already being made, and does not break in the next country); Wikidata sitelink count as a significance metric (the provider filters sitelinks to `hewiki|enwiki` because Tokyo has hundreds, and `userRatingsTotal` gives the same tiebreak free); `rating` as a ranker (every scenic place is 4.5–4.8); and any default image for a day whose stops clear no confidence gate. **Records a privacy defect it fixes as a side effect:** the sheet's toggle reads `רק תוכן שמחובר למסלול` and `buildAppendix` queries `where: { tripId }` with no linkage filter, so every note in the trip is published. Interactive: theme, 360/390px, whether a soft stop carries a start time (owner's call: it does), and whether the day's photo cleared its gate. All four theme×width renders load fonts with no console errors; the appendix falls 410 → 209.2px, the masthead 239 → 183px, day 9 grows 1098 → 1327.7px for the photo and captions, and the journey block is 7px taller than the two rows it replaces — clarity, not compaction, and the file says so. **Built 2026-08-30** as `frontend/src/screens/SharedItinerary.tsx` plus the contract in `packages/shared/src/sharing.ts` and the derivations in `backend/src/sharing/sharing-projection.service.ts` — with two corrections the build made. The fixture's `Reykjavik → Snæfellsnes` day titles were wrong about the trip, not about the day: the owner's is a circumnavigation, so `tripShapeOf` now classifies a trip as star / rolling loop / line from its sequence of stays, and the title chain reads **region → kind → (star only) the day's significant place → route** ([ADR-0213](../decisions/0213-a-shared-trip-changes-emphasis-and-print-is-its-own-rendering.md) fourth amendment §4). And a `dir="auto"` on a value block was replaced by `autoIsolate` everywhere the mockup drew one, which is §8 of the same amendment. **Where it now differs from the shipped app (2026-08-30, same day):** the bookings block it drew above the schedule, with each row jumping to its day, moved BELOW the days and stopped jumping — the anchor threw the reader down a twelve-day document, and a reference belongs after what it refers to ([ADR-0213](../decisions/0213-a-shared-trip-changes-emphasis-and-print-is-its-own-rendering.md) fifth amendment §5). Its appendix, drawn as three per-family lists, is now one list of the same `SharedOp` rows the schedule uses — the per-family shapes were being fed by a second, unfiltered set of queries that published every note in the trip (§1). And the `autoIsolate` sweep above went one element too far: prose keeps `dir="auto"` so English descriptions read left-aligned, and only values that share a line stay isolated (§2).

- [`mockups/free-time-comes-before-the-leave-by-v1.html`](../../mockups/free-time-comes-before-the-leave-by-v1.html) — **the hole between two events reads in order, and its clock says what it is** (2026-08-31; [ADR-0206](../decisions/0206-a-travel-time-belongs-between-two-points.md) §AJ4). Two owner reports about the same two lines, which is why they are one file: _"it first shows the transit row, then the gap + שבץ button. This is the wrong order"_ and _"when there's a gap before a journey, it could show `יציאה עד X`"_. **Its proposed-CSS block is empty**, which is the honest size of both changes. §1 swaps the strip above the journey block in Trip mode; §2 gates `עד` on the leave-by being a ceiling; §3 does the same swap in Plan, where the slot is also a drop target; §4 draws the three arms that must not move. **The finding §2 turns on:** `heroLeaveBy` PULLS the leave-by forward to `departAfterMs` when the buffer lands it behind the row it leaves from (§AJ2), so on that arm the instant is the EARLIEST departure and `עד` would be false rather than redundant — which settles "always or only with a gap" on correctness, not taste. And the gate must be "was it clamped?" rather than `freeSeconds > 0`: the day's first leg out of an ambient stay has no floor at all, so its `free` is `null` rather than zero and a free-minutes test strips the word from the leg read first every morning. Interactive: theme, 360/390px, order, drag (Plan's seam only exists during one), and the three candidate rules for the wording including the one the file recommends against. All four theme×width renders load fonts with no console errors; the swap measures **0px** (299px either way), the word costs **15px** of ink (70 against 55) in a 165px box, the journey block is 40px, the strip 20px with a 48px touch area, and Plan's chip 53px. **What the render found:** `.bld-seam` is `display: none` at rest and `height: 0` during a drag with a 22px `::after` target, so on that arm the change is a claim about DOM order and `elementFromPoint`, not about pixels. **Built 2026-08-31.** **Where it is narrower than the shipped app (2026-08-31, same evening):** every frame here draws a hole BETWEEN two rows — the only hole `dayBlocks` owns — so the file said nothing about the day's two EDGES, where `PlanDay` assembles the slot and its leg by hand inside the bookend fragments and the old order therefore survived the build. The owner reported it with a screenshot ([ADR-0206](../decisions/0206-a-travel-time-belongs-between-two-points.md) §AJ5): the head read bed→leg→free, and the tail was worse at leg→bed→free, drawing the evening's free time after you had gone to sleep. The rule this file promotes is unchanged and now reaches all three sites; drawing them would need a frame carrying bookend stays, which no frame here has.

- [`mockups/a-peer-sends-what-the-admins-published-v1.html`](../../mockups/a-peer-sends-what-the-admins-published-v1.html) — **the share sheet as a non-admin sees it** (2026-08-31; [ADR-0213](../decisions/0213-a-shared-trip-changes-emphasis-and-print-is-its-own-rendering.md)'s twelfth amendment §4). Owner: _"non admins should be able to [decide what's being shared] too, just not add more links … maybe everything is made available by the admins, after they've created links for them."_ **Reading the code moved the whole problem:** the server already allows it — `assertTripAdmin` guards only create/rotate/revoke, the list has no admin check, and the PDF is on the public route — so this is not a permission change but a sheet that stopped drawing a control the API allows. The defect is a hidden control plus a pinned default: the level grid is behind `isAdmin` AND `level` stays at `FULL`, so a trip published only at `תקציר` tells its own travellers it is not shared, above a primary whose `ensureShare` throws before any request leaves (the dead control ADR-0150 §8 forbids). Promotes the admin's grid minus the cards with no link behind them, collapsing entirely at one published level, and a peer who may send an Everything link but not manage one. **Rejects by name:** showing all three with the unpublished ones disabled (advertises a level they cannot open, adds a dead control, and `ChoiceGrid` disables the whole grid rather than one option — a primitive extension for a worse answer); and merely picking the peer's single level better, which fixes the lie without giving what was asked. **Zero new CSS** — every element is already in `screens.css`. Interactive: theme, 360/390px, and which levels the admins published, the input the whole design is a function of. All four renders load fonts with no console errors; the chooser costs **+89.5px** of sheet body (292.4 → 381.9px), the level card is 58px, the link row 44px, the primary 48px and a list row 60px — identical for peer and admin, the `⋯` being the only difference. **Built 2026-08-31.**

- [`mockups/a-shared-time-says-what-it-means-v1.html`](../../mockups/a-shared-time-says-what-it-means-v1.html) — **every time range on the shared reader, and the flexible ones too** (2026-08-31; [ADR-0213](../decisions/0213-a-shared-trip-changes-emphasis-and-print-is-its-own-rendering.md)'s twelfth amendment §1–§2). Owner: _"whenever there's a time range, we should display it. That also includes flexible times like starting from.. Or until..."_ Promotes sharing asking `edgeMeaning` (ADR-0184) instead of `event.hard`, in six arms. **Four findings, three of which changed the ask:** the two renderers already disagreed and the LIVE page is the lenient one (`SharedItinerary.tsx` has no `hard` test, so a soft two-hour hike printed `10:00–12:00` on the phone and `10:00` on paper since §6 shipped); `endLabel` is already projected for every event, so the exact half needs no contract; `sharing.select.ts` selects neither window column, so the flexible half had never reached the projection; and the `hard` gate was MASKING a reversed range rather than preventing one, since a multi-day car hire is a booking, so hard, so paper printed `10:00–18:00` for a week. **§2's decision is a measurement:** the stay's clock cannot ride the stay's own line — it is `nowrap` + ellipsis and in RTL the cut falls at the logical end, exactly where the clock sits, so a real hotel name measures **275px of ink in a 206px box** and the check-in vanishes with no sign it was there; and one line holds one moment where a transfer day has two. It gets its own line, the only one in that header allowed to wrap, because a name is unbounded and a pair of clocks is not. **Rejects by name:** printing a range wherever there are two ends (the literal reading, and what produces `15:00–11:00`); and the two moments as rows inside the day (ADR-0209 §1 already refused positioning a stay's bound as a row in the app). Interactive: theme, 360/390px, and the three candidate homes for the stay's clock. All four renders load fonts with no console errors; the event row is 51px, the day header 76 → 95px with the moments, one moment 17px and two 34px (so the second is free), and the summary row 38px with no clock at all. **Its first render was 11,941px tall** — `.sh-page` carries `min-height: 100dvh`, right for a page a stranger opens and absurd inside a 360px frame — which the file now overrides and says why. **Built 2026-08-31.**

- [`mockups/a-shared-time-is-printed-as-a-range-v1.html`](../../mockups/a-shared-time-is-printed-as-a-range-v1.html) — **the A4 twin, and the one measurement only paper has** (2026-08-31; same amendment §1–§3). Two files rather than one for §7's own reason: the teal route strip was deleted from the reader page and kept printing for a week, _"the same defect one renderer behind"_. The time column is a fixed width with `nowrap`, sized once against `09:20–14:05` (52.9px of ink → 56px), and this change lets four more shapes into that cell. **The file was written to widen it and the measurement said no:** `מ-10:00` is **34.75px** and `עד 11:00` **38.38px** against the range's **55px**, because a short Hebrew word plus four digits is less ink than ten digits and a dash. So the widest shape is still the one the column was sized for, and paper's whole change is one deleted condition in `timeText` and **no CSS** — recorded because the estimate was wrong and only the render said so. **The probe is a `Range` over the cell's contents, never `scrollWidth`:** the cell is a grid item and stretches to its column, so `scrollWidth` reports the column for every shape and an overflow cannot be detected — this file's first render duly called all six 62px and the widest 6262px. Interactive: review chrome theme (the sheet itself is a fixed light palette by design, §4, so the paper never flips), preview scale with every measurement divided back out, and the column width including the 38px that predates the fix, so the claim can be pressed rather than believed. No console errors; the event row is 31px, the day header holds its existing 47px minimum with the stay's two moments on it (106px of ink in a 295.5px box, one line), and the day card is 263px either way. **Built 2026-08-31.**

- [`mockups/the-day-frame-says-three-things-v1.html`](../../mockups/the-day-frame-says-three-things-v1.html) — **what a shared day's frame says, and where the drive went** (2026-08-31; [ADR-0213](../decisions/0213-a-shared-trip-changes-emphasis-and-print-is-its-own-rendering.md)'s fourteenth amendment §1–§3). Two owner reports, one day apart: _"the day titles has gotten a little messy: too many line breaks, questionable ordering of the details"_ and _"between day parts the transit line gets omitted"_. **Neither is about the thing it names.** The mess is one missing combinator — `.sh-day-copy span` is (0,1,1) and as a _descendant_ selector caught `.sh-stay-when`'s nested `.sh-said`/`.sh-time` spans, so one clock line became six grey blocks and lost its amber; and the omitted transit line is not a daypart bug at all, because the sections are cut from the same rows the pairing walks. **The proposed-CSS block is EMPTY**, which is the argument: the fix is `>` in four rules plus a deleted field. What this file writes by hand is the _regression_, scoped to a `.mk-was` before-column at (0,2,1) so the defect can be seen beside its repair on one page. Promotes: the child combinator, a check-out that names no place (the card above already does, and the accordion never collapses headers), and a journey chain between the last **placed** row and the next. **Rejects by name:** reordering the header's lines (the symptom, and after the combinator the surviving order is the one chosen originally); appending the clocks to the stay's own line (already measured at ⁦275px⁩ of ink in a ⁦206px⁩ box, and a transfer day has two moments); raising `.sh-stay-when`'s specificity a _third_ time (the two previous repairs worked around the wide selector and left it to catch the next thing); and faking a stop for a placeless row (the public page computes no routes at all). **§4 (2026-09-01) is the same header a day later**, on a second report — _"it currently reads `Check out <time> · check in` / `<time>` … I think that it should read `Check out <time>` / `Check in <time>`"_ — and the before-column there is the shipped `·`-joined run: §2's permission to wrap put the break between `צ׳ק-אין` and its own clock, so each moment becomes its own block and the break lands where a reader can predict it. Paper takes the same break, on the owner's correction: the A4 column is wide enough that it never wraps (⁦106px⁩ of ink in a ⁦295.5px⁩ box), which is what made the first pass leave it joined and is the wrong question — the break is how the two moments read, not a wrap being repaired. Interactive: theme, 360/390px, and the day's shape (transfer with both moments · ordinary with one). All four renders load fonts with no console errors; the header goes **⁦180px⁩ → ⁦107px⁩** and 6 stacked boxes → 4, the clock goes `rgb(97,104,122)` → `rgb(145,94,30)`, the check-out moment ⁦160px⁩ → ⁦130px⁩ of ink in a ⁦248px⁩ box, the clock line is ⁦42px⁩ joined **and** split, and the placed → placeless → placed day goes 0 journey lines → 1, in the section it leads into. **Built 2026-09-01.**

- [`mockups/a-shared-itinerary-is-printed-as-a-story-v3.html`](../../mockups/a-shared-itinerary-is-printed-as-a-story-v3.html) — **the A4 renderer, same five decisions, with the one that inverts** (2026-08-30; supersedes `a-shared-itinerary-is-printed-by-daypart-v2.html`, whose multicol fragmentation and break-safe day it keeps; [ADR-0213](../decisions/0213-a-shared-trip-changes-emphasis-and-print-is-its-own-rendering.md)). Owner: _"Why נתב״ג to Frankfurt?? What does it have to do with anything? What's the teal random places on top?"_ **The inversion:** the reader page folds a booking code behind a disclosure because a reader wants the schedule and an operator wants the code; paper has no such setting, since whoever holds the printout is by that act the operator — so the same material prints inline in mono. Its twin: a 116px photo band is nothing on a page you scroll and roughly a page and a half across twelve days at two-column density, so the day's photo is a 34px square inside the header's existing 47px minimum. Also removes `.pdf-route-mini`, the capped teal stop sample beside the QR — deleted from the live page on 2026-08-30 and still printing, **the same defect one renderer behind**, which is the argument for one ADR section covering both. **Carries a shipped defect the render found and measured (§6):** `<strong dir="auto">` sets the element's BASE DIRECTION, which drives `text-align: start` as well as bidi resolution, so a Latin place name lands against the opposite edge of its column from its own caption — **229px of separation in a 288px column**, 212px on the reader page, and both renderers write it. The fix is `autoIsolate` (FSI…PDI) from `lib/bidi.ts`, whose docblock covers a _composed line joining several values_ and so left a single value alone in a block uncovered. Both mockups now render it and measure 0px. **And it does not hand-copy the renderer's CSS:** `mockups/tools/extract-pdf-css.mjs` pulls it verbatim from the template literal in `itinerary-pdf.template.ts` and **refuses** a block containing an interpolation, because the catalog's chronic failure is exactly this drift. Interactive: projection, whether a day photo prints at all (the only decision here that costs paper), and preview scale — with every measurement divided back out of the scale, so the numbers are A4's own. No console errors; day 9 falls 360 → 351px, the journey 182 → 169px, the layover line is 11px, and the day header holds the photo at the same 47px it already had. **Built 2026-08-30** as `backend/src/sharing/itinerary-pdf.template.ts`, which carries two things this file could not. The proposed CSS was first appended **above** the original block and lost silently at equal specificity — `.pdf-event` kept its 38px against 52.9px of ink, a flight's range printed over its own title, and the smoke render's overprint check passed the whole time; the block now sits last in the sheet and `itinerary-pdf.template.spec.ts` asserts that ordering. And the day photo cannot arrive by URL at all: `PdfBrowserService` aborts every request the page makes before `setContent`, so `dayPhotoDataUrls` reads each blob behind the public route's own key-prefix check and inlines it, the way the QR and the fonts already did. Verified on a real A4 render: five squares, five loaded, zero failed requests. **Where it now differs (2026-08-30, same day):** the stop description it drew at full length is capped at the source (`capCaption`, 150 characters at a sentence boundary) — a five-line Wikipedia lede costs a two-column A4 page real estate it does not have, and CSS clamping cannot help paper. Its appendix block renders the same op rows the schedule does rather than three per-family lists.

- [`mockups/a-journey-is-a-flight-plan-v1.html`](../../mockups/a-journey-is-a-flight-plan-v1.html) — **a connecting flight reads as three flights because its frame IS an event row** (2026-08-31; [ADR-0213](../decisions/0213-a-shared-trip-changes-emphasis-and-print-is-its-own-rendering.md) ninth amendment). Owner, twice in one day: _"Flights have a row for the entire journey (נתב״ג אל קפלאוויק) but also rows for each fight. Both pdf and live. This is confusing"_ and, after a fix shipped, _"still show both the full journey and the separate flights in a confusing way, also doesn't show journey leg durations (flights)"_. **The second report is why this file exists: it says the first fix was on the wrong axis.** The eighth amendment read "confusing" as duplicated NUMBERS and removed the legs' own duration and zone shift; the numbers were never the complaint, and taking a fact away made the block say less while still reading as three peers. What reading the code found: the journey renders through `article.sh-event` — the same element and type scale as a museum visit — and `.sh-legs` then indents by 30px, so the entire claim "these are inside that" rests on 30px of white space in a 360px column; and the frame's title is literally `routeTitle(first.from, last.to)`, the legs' endpoints concatenated, so the same two airports appear three times, which is why counting durations missed it — the repetition a reader trips over is the PLACES. Promotes: `.sh-trek`, a real container (teal rail + 7% tint, the grammar `.sh-day` already uses one level up, and teal because a journey is about getting between places — rule 4); a header that drops `.sh-event`'s glyph and `--sh-secondary` so it cannot be read as one of the flights beneath it; `durationMinutes` restored **per leg** at `--sh-micro`; and the same decision drawn at the print renderer's density so the two renderers cannot drift, the drift that already cost a round on `.pdf-route-mini`. **Rejects, by name:** deleting the frame row (the `ops`, `caption` and map link all hang off the parent event — deleting it deletes the flight's booking code); a bigger indent (measured 30px of 360px, and doubling it buys white space and still says nothing); restoring the per-leg **zone shift** too (the owner asked for flight durations, and three signed numbers describe one clock change — the shift a traveller acts on is origin→destination); and an accordion over the journey, since `.sh-ops` already folds and reaching a flight already costs one press. Interactive: theme, 360/390px, leg count (2/3 — three is where an indent stops being arguable), and the two feel calls it does not settle: whether the header carries `2 טיסות`, and whether the legs sit on tint+rail or rail alone. **Measured:** the whole journey goes 209.9 → 221px, +11.1px — clarity, not compaction; the header is 60px against an event row's 76px; duration phrases on the card go 2 → 4, which **is** the request. Two render findings recorded in the file: `.icon` is sized per context in this app and a new context inherits nothing (unsized, the header measured **131px** instead of 34), and `.sh-page` now carries `min-height: 100dvh` (shipped 2026-08-31 so the reader's document scrolls), so any mockup wearing that class for its type scale gets a screenful of empty ground per frame. Its §3 first shipped without the layover line and the owner caught it in review — _"The pdf should also show the wait durations"_ — so both paper columns now run through one leg renderer. **Built 2026-08-31**, the same day, as `.sh-trek` in `SharedItinerary.tsx` + `shared-itinerary.css` and `legRows` in `itinerary-pdf.template.ts`, with three corrections the build made: the header's destination is a NEW contract field (`SharedEvent.journeyTo` — `title` is `routeTitle(from, to)` and carries bidi isolates, so it cannot be parsed apart); the frame's row does **not** survive as this file drew it ambiguously — the row goes and its ATTACHMENTS are hoisted into the container, because `after()` had quietly dropped the ops fold and with it the flight's booking code (there is now a spec on `.sh-trek details.sh-ops`); and the print renderer gained an extracted `signedHours`, the journey header being the second caller of formatting that was inline in `travelFactsLine`.
