Measured live at 360px: header 56.7px against an event row's 76px, the whole journey 215.7px against this file's predicted 221px, four spans and exactly one zone pill. **Where the app had drifted from it (2026-09-01, now back in line):** this file draws the journey's whole span `14:30–23:20` on **both** its reader and paper columns, from one `JOURNEY.span` constant feeding both — and paper shipped leg one's clock instead, because the projection left `time` describing leg one and only the contract-obeying renderer showed it ([ADR-0213](../decisions/0213-a-shared-trip-changes-emphasis-and-print-is-its-own-rendering.md)'s fifteenth amendment §1). The drawing had the right answer for a week: a mockup is a drawing, not an assertion, and nothing compared the two because no fixture in the repo had a chained journey at all. What closed it was a fixture, so the fifteenth amendment adds no mockup.

- [`mockups/the-reader-hands-you-a-file-v1.html`](../../mockups/the-reader-hands-you-a-file-v1.html) — **what a file leaving the public page should look like, and the rule the app had already written** (2026-08-31; [ADR-0213](../decisions/0213-a-shared-trip-changes-emphasis-and-print-is-its-own-rendering.md) ninth amendment §4–§6). Owner: _"The download indication still bad … still the same bad looking מוריד etc instead of a spinner or something, and it still doesn't pop up the Google chrome saving"_, plus _"the live sharing page should have a button to export to pdf"_. **Almost none of this is a new design question.** `ui/Spinner.tsx`'s own docblock says _"the ONE shared spinner (ADR-0052 §4) … so every async surface has a motion cue, NOT a static word"_, and ADR-0052 §4 names the composition — a busy control shows its label **plus** a spinner, with a determinate bar "where the transport allows it". The shipped row has the bar and the word and no spinner: two thirds of a rule written four months earlier, missing the third. Three more findings: `lib/system-share.ts`'s `shareFileOrDownload` is the mechanism, and `FileOp` reimplemented **only its silent fallback branch** — the same six lines verbatim — never trying `navigator.share({ files })`, which on Android opens the system sheet and **is** the visible confirmation the owner was asking for; that shared helper carries the same-tick `revokeObjectURL` bug the one-off had already fixed with `requestAnimationFrame`, so the two implementations hold half an answer each (rule 8's exact shape, repaired by folding the rAF inward); and the owner's own PDF button one screen away renders `busy ? preparing : label` while `FormActions` two files over renders `busy ? <Spinner /> : label`, so the app contains both the right and the wrong pattern for one question. Promotes: the spinner taking the file GLYPH's slot so the row's geometry never moves; `--ok`/`--miss` for the two finished states and `--cta` for working, because "a file is arriving" is an action and not a status; reduced motion **slowing** the sweep to 2.6s rather than stopping it, matching `.spinner`'s own 1.6s (the shipped `.sh-dl-bar` killed the animation, which makes an indeterminate bar indistinguishable from a stalled download); and the reader's PDF button as `.share-outcome`, one more host of the owner sheet's control rather than a third button. **Rejects:** a spinner _instead of_ the bar (they answer two questions that are both asked — the spinner before a byte arrives, the bar only with a `Content-Length`); a determinate bar with no declared length; a toast (the public page runs no toast system, and a toast confirms far from the thing pressed); and a confirm before a reversible download. Interactive: theme, 360/390px, row state (idle/working/done/failed), whether a length was declared, and the two feel calls left open — where the PDF button lives (masthead vs after the last day) and whether the spinner replaces the glyph or sits beside the label. **Measured, and it found a shipped defect:** the live file row is **42px, under ADR-0017's 44px floor**; the proposal declares 44px, the spinner is the app's own 15px, the bar costs 3px and never grows the row, the masthead button is 36px with a `::after` reaching 44px (`ValueToken`'s technique, ADR-0177), and the foot button is `.share-outcome`'s 48px. Second render finding: a control in `.sh-public-bar` must take the `--on-dark-*` ramp (ADR-0158 §3) — drawn first with `--ink`/`--line` it rendered navy on navy, invisible on the page and entirely reasonable in the CSS. **Built 2026-08-31**, the same day, with two changes: `FileOp` and the PDF button are one `useFileHandover` hook rather than the two ladders this file drew — they ask one question, and a second ladder is how they would have drifted on the states or on which outcome says `נשלח` — and the PDF button ships in **both** placements this file left open, since measuring them showed they cost different things and answer different readers (the masthead pill is 30px on a 43px bar; the foot's `.share-outcome` is 48px), which is the same two-entry-point shape ADR-0213 already uses for the owner's control. The 42px floor violation this file measured is closed: the shipped row now measures 45.5px with `min-height: 44px`, the app's 15px spinner at its own 0.7s, and the sweep slowing to 2.6s under reduced motion instead of stopping.

- [`mockups/the-trip-card-has-room-for-one-more-control-v1.html`](../../mockups/the-trip-card-has-room-for-one-more-control-v1.html) — **what the All Trips share action costs, and who actually pays** (2026-08-30; **its recommendation is superseded by `the-trip-card-has-room-for-one-more-control-v2.html` on the owner's call — the MEASUREMENTS stand and are what v2 argues from**; owner: _"The share icon is taking much space and is causing a line overflow. Perhaps we need a long click instead?"_; [ADR-0033](../decisions/0033-all-trips-home.md) amendment). Four candidates drawn against the real card and measured off the live DOM: today, the status chip moved into the meta line, the share action taken out of its overlay into a column of its own, and a long press with no visible control. **The measurement changed the recommendation.** At 360px the shipped card leaves `.main` at 108px with the meta on three lines; the long press returns it to 150px; moving the CHIP returns it to **196px** — more than deleting the share entirely, and more than the card had before the share existed — because the chip's bordered box was always the larger tenant. So the file promotes moving the status into the meta line (§2) plus giving the action its own column (§3, which is what lets the reserved padding drop from 56px to the ordinary 14px), and rejects the long press as the default: it is the cheapest in space and the most expensive in discovery, on a surface with no other long-press affordance and where the app has already spent that gesture on drag (`lib/useHoldToDrag.ts`). Also rejects: shrinking the control (44px is ADR-0017's floor, and the saving was 6px), and letting the meta ellipsise instead of wrapping (the first thing cut is the member count, the one fact in the row that appears nowhere else on the card). Interactive: theme, 360/390/430px, and the trip's state (בקרוב · הסתיים), which changes the chip and so changes every number. A blue rule above each card paints the surviving column from the same value the table reports. **Two fidelity errors the render caught in this file itself**, both of which would have made every number pessimistic: the frame added its own 16px padding on top of `.trips-body`'s, and the fixture's meta had no destination where the reported card has one. Left open for a device pass: whether `הסתיים` belongs in the meta line at all, since the section heading above it already says the word and the dates are already in the past.

- [`mockups/the-trip-card-has-room-for-one-more-control-v2.html`](../../mockups/the-trip-card-has-room-for-one-more-control-v2.html) — **hold to share, and make the countdown loud** (2026-08-30; supersedes v1's recommendation, not its measurements; [ADR-0033](../decisions/0033-all-trips-home.md)'s 2026-08-30 amendment). The owner rejected both halves of v1: _"I don't want all rows to have the share icon. I think that a long press … Also the trip state (in X days / finished) should be more prominent, no? … the share icon is visible from inside the trip so it's not the worst thing"_. What reading the code then found is that **the app had already made this decision twice**: `lib/useHoldToOpen.ts` (ADR-0202's 2026-08-22 amendment) exists, its docblock opens with the owner's words from that session, and its stated justification is the same trade — it _"costs no pixels, which is the reason a fourth mark in the row's trailing slot was not the answer"_, and it is _"a shortcut, never the only way"_, paid for by a visible twin. The twin here is the trip header's share. So this is the third holdable thing rather than a new gesture, and the hook already owns the `selectstart` guard and the click-swallow. Promotes: the share leaving the row entirely for a hold; the countdown spending **amber**, which the colour budget files it under ("Now, countdowns…"), at the recipe `.hdr-anchor.is-back` already uses rather than a new one — noting that same rule's finding that `--amber` as TEXT on paper is 1.31:1, a fill and never ink; and **deleting `הסתיים` rather than making it louder**, since the section heading directly above it already says the word and it discriminates between no two cards in its section. Measured at 360px: today 101px of content column with the meta on three lines, hold 143px on two, and hold-with-no-chip **238px on one**, the card dropping to 74px — the largest single win in either file, and it applies to seven of the eight cards in the reported screenshot. Rejects the requested slide-out drawer, and the render is what made the case: nothing in the app opens a row sideways, so it is a new mechanism for one button needing its own dismissal and its own back-stack layer (ADR-0090) — **and with the drawer open the card is pushed far enough that the trip's flag leaves the screen**, which is the card's fastest recognition cue and the shared element the trip handoff flies (ADR-0140 §7). Interactive: theme, 360/390/430px, trip state, and the countdown's weight (שקט · נייר · מלא) — the feel call the file deliberately does not settle. **Built the same day** at the middle weight (`נייר`), on the owner's _"Do what looks best in your opinion"_ — so the file's `שקט` and `מלא` frames are now alternatives the app does not draw, and its §4 drawer never existed outside this page.

- [`mockups/tomorrow-lookahead-v1.html`](../../mockups/tomorrow-lookahead-v1.html) — **מה מחר · what Home says about tomorrow once today's plan is done** (2026-08-31) — **BUILT 2026-08-31 via v2+v3 ([ADR-0214](../decisions/0214-the-night-board-has-one-subject-and-it-is-tomorrow.md)). SUPERSEDED ON THE RECOMMENDATION by `tomorrow-lookahead-v2.html` below** (the owner, on this drawing: _"some parts are packed with too much info and everything is represented on the same level of importance visually"_) — its five-option survey, its four rejections and its measurements all stand and are the reason v2 is narrow; what v2 replaces is the recommended board, which v2's own census measures at ⁦21⁩ information runs against ⁦16⁩ for the shipped board. Read v2 first and this file for the options it chose between. Session note: [2026-08-31](../planning/2026-08-31-tomorrow-lookahead-design.md). Owner: _"after the day is over, users might want to see what's planned for tomorrow … if they opened the app and instantly got the idea at a glance of where they're headed, what's planned, it would be perfect … The natural choice is the hero, and maybe that's the direction we should go for, but we should explore more options and get creative"_, plus _"it should also look friendly and inviting"_. **Read this entry before adding anything to the night board.** The load-bearing finding is that the ask is already half-shipped and the file measures against that rather than against a blank board: `deriveNow` has no date filter, so at ⁦22:40⁩ `הבא בתור` is already tomorrow's ⁦07:12⁩ train with `מחר` beside it ([ADR-0211](../decisions/0211-a-gap-has-a-character.md) §6 / [ADR-0160](../decisions/0160-the-hero-lifts-and-shows-a-horizon.md) §M) — **the lookahead is a POINT and what is missing is a SHAPE**. Four more facts came from a throwaway `vitest` against the shipped derivations rather than a second reading (ADR-0211 §6's own method, listed in the note so a build can pin them): `gapDrawsDayRail('day-done')` is **`true`**, so the board draws a knob at ~⁦98%⁩ under `עכשיו` on a day that is over — the rail ADR-0211 §4 removed for `at-the-stay`/`empty-day` and never asked about `day-done`, and the slot the recommendation spends; `buildDayGlance(events, tomorrow, …)` already answers ⁦3⁩ segs · ⁦3⁩ anchors · `remaining ⁦5⁩` · **`nowFrac: null`** and `dayBookendStays` already answers `{woke, sleeps}`, so a lookahead is one derivation CALL and no new field; **`glance.empty` is the wrong emptiness test** — an unplanned tomorrow on a trip with a hotel probes `empty: false · segs: 0 · anchors: 2 · remaining: 2` (the stay's own edges), so the test is `segs.length === 0`; and drawing §3 found **a third slot still missing the day token** — `heroTravel` hangs off the same midnight-crossing `horizon.next`, so the lifted hero prints `t.travel.leaveAt` → `צאו ב־06:40`, a bare clock ⁦40px⁩ under a row reading `07:12 · מחר`, which is ADR-0160 §M's ambiguity a third time and a copy change. **Five candidates, each drawn on a shipped skeleton, each in a REAL viewport with chrome and nav so the fold is the frame's own edge** (body room ⁦497px⁩ at ⁦360×640⁩): **א׳ הסרט של מחר** — `.wp-board-progress`'s own tree (⁦3px⁩ track, three-slot `ends`) with tomorrow's window fractions in it, a hollow ring pointing at the block `הבא בתור` already names rather than naming it twice (ADR-0211 rejected `בדרך ל־X` partly for that, one slot down), measured **⁦53px⁩ plain / ⁦73px⁩ with the events' own emoji against the rail's ⁦23px⁩** — so the honest claim is ⁦+30/+50px⁩ and _nothing displaced_, not "free"; **ב׳ לוח הלילה** — the title slot says where you are tonight instead of `סוף היום`, ⁦+20px⁩ and **zero new CSS** (it is `at-the-stay` without its `DAY_WINDOW` bound); **ג׳ מחר במבט** — the shipped `GlanceCard` re-derived for tomorrow, the cheapest reuse in the file and rejected on a number: it lands **⁦212px⁩ below the fold** at ⁦360×640⁩ (⁦8px⁩ at ⁦390×844⁩) against an ask whose words are "instantly … at a glance", and its lead copy is `נותרו היום`, i.e. date-bound; **ד׳ הלוח מתהפך** — rejected on three collisions, none of them taste (the board's tap already belongs to the lift, a rotated box breaks the FLIP's origin measurement since `getBoundingClientRect` on a rotated element is the rotation's bounding box and that is exactly what `useLiftFlight` measures, and under `prefers-reduced-motion` a flip degrades to a face swap i.e. an overlay) — what it proves is that a full-board tomorrow already exists in the lift; **ה׳ שורה אחת** — `.stay-strip` at a second density in amber, the friendliest wording and rejected **as a band**, because [ADR-0064](../decisions/0064-day-transition-entries-and-home-band-trim.md) removed a band of exactly this shape from exactly this position; **ו׳ העומק** — the lift's foot carries the ribbon plus the one thing the collapsed board cannot ([ADR-0160](../decisions/0160-the-hero-lifts-and-shows-a-horizon.md) §4: the board is a `<button>` and a nested one made Chrome reparent everything after it, ⁦1 of 4⁩ children left inside), a `ליום של מחר` hand-off at ⁦44px⁩ rather than `.hero-act`'s ⁦34px⁩ — which is licensed for chips in a row, not for one control alone — measured ⁦617px⁩ against a ⁦622px⁩ cap, i.e. **it fits by five pixels** and any note or task on the next point tips the card onto its scroller. **Two things the render fixed about the drawing itself**: `לינה · X` in the ribbon's middle slot is drawn only when the bed **changes**, because on an ordinary tomorrow at the same hotel the render showed one ryokan printed three times on one screen (stay strip, WiFi tile, ribbon) — the duplication [ADR-0209](../decisions/0209-a-stay-is-named-once-in-the-day-it-belongs-to.md) removed by subtraction; and **this file broke the rule it cites** — ד׳'s back face was `.tl-flip-face.back`, `.back` is a GLOBAL class (`App.css:1012`, the ⁦34×34⁩ chrome back button, `display: grid`), and the face rendered at ⁦36px⁩ beside a ⁦273px⁩ front, which is ADR-0160 §C's own defect answered with ADR-0160 §C's own remedy (`data-face`, an attribute cannot collide with a class). Interactive: theme, ⁦360×640⁩/⁦390×844⁩, four states every candidate must survive (יום מעבר · יום רגיל · **מחר ריק** · **אין מחר**), and two feel calls shipped at their recommendation — the ribbon's character (נקודות ⁦53px⁩ · אימוג׳י ⁦73px⁩, emoji by default because they are content the group chose and the one channel there that is not amber) and the `מוקדם` tag, which needs a new `EARLY_START_HOUR` and is the drawing's only genuinely new fact. Renders four theme×width combinations with webfonts and no console errors. Also in the file, deliberately not proposed: weather (no pipe — ADR-0045, and ADR-0180 §4 already says it returns as its own glance card), suggestions on an empty tomorrow (ADR-0211 §8), a tappable ribbon on the collapsed board, a fifth quick-access tile (ADR-0050's tiles are deep links to data, and a day is not a datum), and Plan mode (the parallel question there is readiness — ADR-0193). The `מחר ריק` state is also where the shipped countdown ladder shows through: past ⁦24h⁩ `formatCountdown` → `dayCount` returns an **empty value** with the unit `יום`, so the tile prints one word.

- [`mockups/tomorrow-lookahead-v2.html`](../../mockups/tomorrow-lookahead-v2.html) — **מה מחר · v2 · the night board has one subject, and it is tomorrow** (2026-08-31) — **BUILT 2026-08-31 ([ADR-0214](../decisions/0214-the-night-board-has-one-subject-and-it-is-tomorrow.md) §2/§3): its hierarchy is what shipped. Its CUE is superseded** by `tomorrow-lookahead-v3.html` below, which withdrew the ⁦11px⁩ circle for a vertical halo on two grounds v2 did not have (the circle is the rail's `knob`, i.e. the **now** marker, on a day whose `nowFrac` is `null` by construction; and a ⁦3px⁩ halo was measured reaching a neighbouring block on two of six stress days). Session note: [2026-08-31](../planning/2026-08-31-tomorrow-lookahead-design.md), "Round two". The owner on v1: _"Looks very good overall. I think that some parts are packed with too much info and everything is represented on the same level of importance visually. Maybe we need to think how to make the more important stuff stand out better, lose some of the less important stuff, or maybe move to the lifted hero only."_ **Read this entry before adding a run to the board in any state.** v1 answered "what is missing" and added; v2 answers "what matters" and **subtracts** — two of its three moves are removals, and its proposed CSS block is shorter than v1's. **§1 is a census, and it is the instrument worth stealing:** it counts what the eye has to rank off the RENDERED board rather than off the source — every element carrying its own text run, bucketed by the four properties that make two runs read as one level (size · weight · colour · family) — and it reports board height, run count, the biggest bucket, head-font titles, monospace runs and filled chips for three columns (shipped · v1 · v2). At ⁦360px⁩: height ⁦273⁩ → ⁦341⁩ → **⁦195px⁩**, runs ⁦16⁩ → ⁦21⁩ → **⁦9⁩**, biggest bucket ⁦2⁩ → ⁦3⁩ → **⁦1⁩**, head titles ⁦2⁩ → ⁦2⁩ → **⁦1⁩**, mono runs ⁦5⁩ → ⁦5⁩ → **⁦2⁩**, filled chips ⁦3⁩ → ⁦4⁩ → **⁦2⁩** — so the correction is confirmed by the middle column and answered by the right one, and **v2's board is ⁦78px⁩ shorter than the one that ships today** while being the only one that says anything about tomorrow's shape. One instrument fix is recorded in the file because the first pass got it wrong in the direction of its own thesis: **a glyph is not a run**, and counting the emoji marks as four runs at one level reported the ribbon as the flatness it was measuring; the filter (`/[\p{L}\p{N}]/u`) applies to all three columns. **The fix needed no new token, and reading `board.css` is what found it:** the board already separates its two slots on three channels at once — `--amber` vs `--on-dark-faint` on the label, ⁦21px⁩ vs ⁦17px⁩ on the title, `--on-dark-strong` vs `--on-dark` in the ink — and at night that ranking is aimed at the wrong slot, because rank 1 holds `סוף היום` (a statement about what is _not_ happening) in the largest type and brightest ink on the app's loudest surface. Hence the rule the file promotes: **at night the board has ONE subject and it is tomorrow**, in three moves — (1) **the ranks swap** via `[data-rank]`, trading the two existing treatments with the countdown staying in the next-row's flex, zero new tokens and zero pixels, and the rule generalises as "rank 1 is whichever slot holds tomorrow" so an **empty** tomorrow needs no swap at all (the now-slot carries `מחר · יום פנוי`, `emptyDay`'s own words) and **no** tomorrow leaves the board exactly as it ships; (2) **four things come off** — the confirmation code, a duplication **measured at ⁦240px⁩ from its copy in the `הכרטיס הבא` quick tile** (the surface [ADR-0050](../decisions/0050-home-quick-access-deep-links-and-empty-states.md) built for it), the `קשיח` lock, v1's `מוקדם` tag (**withdrawn**), and the day token on the meta row, because the rank-1 label now says `מחר` and keeping both prints one word twice ⁦20px⁩ apart — [ADR-0211](../decisions/0211-a-gap-has-a-character.md)'s build-log rule (it refused `לילה` in the badge _and_ the label) paying out in reverse, and note the dependency: that removal is only legal because of move 1, so where the label still says `הבא בתור` the token stays; (3) **`סוף היום` goes and the ribbon loses its header** — a board whose whole subject is tomorrow has said the day is over by not talking about it, which is ADR-0211 §4's own "absence beats a pinned lie" applied to a slot instead of a rail, and with `מחר` at rank 1 the ribbon needs no caption, day number or count because it is the shape of the thing named above it, tied to it by the ring: ⁦73px⁩ → ⁦45px⁩. **§4 draws the owner's third option to the end and rejects it** — "move to the lifted hero only" is the same board with no ribbon at all, ⁦138px⁩ against ⁦195px⁩, and it is refused on the ask's own words (_"opened the app and instantly got the idea at a glance"_): a tap is not a glance. **§3 is v1's lift card unchanged**, now the only place the removed detail exists (code, lock, day number, count, each block's time, `איפה`, the leave-by), measured at ⁦587px⁩ against the ⁦622px⁩ cap with no scroller. Two smaller things the render asked for: the rank-1 label takes `.wp-board-now-label`'s own `margin-top: 14px` (probed at ⁦0px⁩ of gap, it read as falling out of the live row), and the bed line — the ribbon's only words — is drawn only when the bed moves ([ADR-0209](../decisions/0209-a-stay-is-named-once-in-the-day-it-belongs-to.md)), which is what makes the ordinary-tomorrow board the shortest of the four states at ⁦173px⁩. **v1 is not retrofitted:** its board is redrawn here from this file's own `.tl-v1-*` block for the census's before column, so no stylesheet in the manifest contains any of it and the columns differ by exactly what the proposal removes. Interactive: theme, ⁦360×640⁩/⁦390×844⁩, the four states, and the one feel call left (the ribbon's marks — emoji by default, because they are content the group chose and the only channel there that is not amber). Renders four theme×width combinations with webfonts and no console errors.

- [`mockups/tomorrow-lookahead-v3.html`](../../mockups/tomorrow-lookahead-v3.html) — **מה מחר · v3 · the ribbon on the days that break strips** (2026-08-31) — **APPROVED AND BUILT the same day** ([ADR-0214](../decisions/0214-the-night-board-has-one-subject-and-it-is-tomorrow.md); the owner's instruction was _"Looks great. Let's build this!"_). **Two visible things differ from the shipped strip, and this file is NOT retrofitted** — it is the dated record of what was decided then, and ADR-0214's build log is the record of what running the code changed: (1) **the cue's alpha** — v3 draws it at ⁦0.3⁩, which is invisible on a SOFT block (v3's own cue sits on a hard one, where a dim halo reads as a thickening); the shipped value is ⁦0.62⁩, because a halo has to out-value the fill it rings. (2) **the marks' anchor** — v3 centres each mark on its block's START instant, and the owner, on the built strip: _"the emojis are too much to the right instead of being centered to their line"_; shipped, a mark sits over its block's **middle**, computed in the derivation rather than in CSS because `thinMarks` spaces marks by the position they are drawn at. Two further build-log items have no counterpart in any drawing: the lifted hero drops its gap slot when tomorrow is the subject, and the unplanned-tomorrow arm drops the day rail as well. Session note: [2026-08-31](../planning/2026-08-31-tomorrow-lookahead-design.md), "Round three". Owner, accepting v2's hierarchy and naming the next question: _"we're not falling into the traps of edge cases like overlapping events etc. They shouldn't run over each other (visually and logically), shouldn't overpack the area again. And that applies to busy days as well, or short events that are close in time. This has to stay minimalistic and not too busy."_ **Read this entry before drawing any strip, rail or ribbon over a day's events.** **Half the ask is inherited, and a throwaway `vitest` against the real `buildDayGlance` is what established it rather than a reading:** its segments are the containment forest's **roots** (ADR-0041), so a partial overlap (⁦10:00–12:00⁩ + ⁦11:00–13:00⁩) comes back as **one** composite seg with `count: 2`, a containment as an envelope, and three events on one minute as one seg that **turns its own number off** (`showCount: false`) — six hard days, **⁦0⁩ overlapping segments**. Two events cannot be two blocks if they overlap, so "logically" costs nothing; what the probe also found is what is **not** free: a twelve-item day returns **two pairs of segments sharing a boundary exactly**, a zero-length event returns a zero-**width** seg with `point: false`, and a midnight tail clamps `endFrac` to ⁦1⁩ with `nextDay: true`. **Five rules, each drawn beside the case that forced it** (the "without" column comes from the file's own `.tl-off-*` block, so it is rendered rather than described): a **⁦4px⁩ floor**, which is `.seg.point`'s own value and is also what draws the zero-width block at all; a **⁦1px⁩ hairline of the board's own ground between adjacent blocks** — a `box-shadow`, never a `margin`, because a margin moves a block off the time it represents and the position is the datum; the **cue as a vertical halo**, replacing v2's circle; **marks that thin rather than stack**; and a **fade on a midnight tail** instead of the rail's `+1` chip (shape, not text, ⁦0px⁩ of height). **The two mistakes this file made and its own assertion caught are the most reusable part of it.** (1) The halo's first draft was a ⁦3px⁩ spread in every direction and was measured **reaching a neighbouring block on two of the six days** — the same defect as the disc it replaced, at a third the size; it is now a shadow offset on the block axis with no spread, which copies the box up and down and never sideways, so the cue cannot touch anything **by construction**, and the check survives (parametrised on `HALO_X`) so a regression to a spread goes red on the busy day exactly as it did the first time. (2) The cap and the collision were merged into one greedy step, so a cap of ⁦5⁩ became a ⁦1/5⁩ spacing **across the whole window** and a day whose five stops sit inside three hours kept **one** mark — a cap limits a count and says nothing about spacing, so it is now two passes: the collision rule (`MARK_MIN_PX = 16` over the **narrowest** track, ⁦290px⁩ ≈ ⁦55⁩ minutes, so the mark set cannot change between a ⁦360⁩ and a ⁦390⁩ phone — measured identical in all four theme×width runs) and then an even sample to the cap that always keeps the **first** (the block the title names) and the **last** (the evening, which "keep the first N" silently drops). **The precedent for thinning is shipped, and it is not ADR-0077's lanes:** a lane band buys room with height on every busy day, which is the thing the correction forbids, while `GlanceSeg.showCount` already **drops the number** on a too-narrow composite ("the exact count is one tap away"). **§3 is an assertion, not a claim** — every pair of rendered boxes compared with `getBoundingClientRect` for all six days, reported as blocks-touching · marks-touching · halo-reaching-a-foreign-block, and **⁦0⁩ · ⁦0⁩ · ⁦0⁩ in all four combinations**; the thinning row is identical in all four too. **Measured, and better than v2 could claim:** the ribbon is **⁦23px⁩** on a day the bed does not move and **the day rail it replaces measures ⁦23px⁩ live in the same file** — the shape costs nothing at all on the common day — ⁦45px⁩ only when the `לינה` line appears, and a ⁦2⁩-item day and a ⁦12⁩-item day are **the same height** because nothing here is ever laned. **Two defects in the shipped app, found by probing and deliberately left to the backlog:** the glance rail has **no separator between adjacent segments** (`.seg` carries only `min-width: 6px`), so it draws back-to-back events as one continuous bar today; and a zero-length event is **invisible** on it, because `point: false` plus zero width means `.seg.point`'s ⁦4px⁩ tick never applies. Interactive: theme, ⁦360⁩/⁦390⁩, and the marks cap (⁦4⁩ · ⁦5⁩ · ⁦6⁩ · off) — the one feel call left, shipped at ⁦5⁩, since it and `MARK_MIN_PX` are device reads rather than screenshot reads. Renders four theme×width combinations with webfonts and no console errors.

- [`mockups/glance-v2.html`](../../mockups/glance-v2.html) — **היום במבט · v2 · what the glance card says now that the board says more** (2026-08-31) — **BUILT 2026-08-31, the same day, at the owner's «let's build everything» ([ADR-0215](../decisions/0215-the-glance-card-says-what-only-it-can-say.md)).** Every fork shipped at this file's own default, so the drawing and the app agree; where they differ is written into the ADR's build log rather than retrofitted here — **the running app added one rule this file could not have**: an ambient edge whose instant falls INSIDE a block (a hotel check-in at ⁦15:00⁩ while the long-haul flight is still in the air) is dropped rather than drawn, because as a tick it landed amber-on-amber inside the flight and produced the one touching pair six drawn stress days never had. The other shipped difference is the travel line: §2d draws the ground half (`3.2 ק״מ · ~48 דק׳`) and the app ships the **air** half only, because the ground total is a roll-up over every hole in the day and Home asks its travel hook about one leg on purpose (ADR-0206 §D8's request budget). Step 3 of [the brief](../planning/2026-08-31-glance-v2-brief.md), which is amended in place with every number below. The owner asked for a glance _"upgraded, less condensed, more visually pleasing, possibly giving more information or losing information for a better look"_, and the rule the whole file derives from is what ADR-0214 did to the card's job: **the board above it now says what is now, what is next, in how long, where we are and what shape tomorrow has, so the only work left for this card is the one thing no other surface can do — the whole day at once, and what is left of it.** **§1** is the census (⁦8⁩ text runs → ⁦5⁩, ⁦7⁩ type levels → ⁦4⁩, ⁦5⁩ monospace runs → ⁦2⁩, ⁦184px⁩ → ⁦131px⁩), **§2a** takes the anchor band off the rail (a probed arrival day returns ⁦4⁩ anchors in ⁦3⁩ lanes, so `anchorsCollapsed` fires and ADR-0077's positioned band **is not drawn at all** — the day the card matters most gets the fallback; the answer is a **tick** rather than a deletion, because `buildDayGlance` gives an ambient edge no segment while ADR-0164 still counts it, so dropping the pill would drop the check-out and keep the number), **§2b** replaces the five phase fills with `spent × ahead` on one channel and `hard × soft` on the other — measured as **ink mass** (area × alpha), which is where reading the CSS only half-answered: shipped, the half you can do nothing about carries ⁦383⁩ and the half that is left carries **⁦0⁩** (a hollow ring has no fill), and the proposal reads ⁦89⁩ / ⁦657⁩ the other way with **no new hue**, at the cost of `--ok` green leaving the card. **§2c** is the subtraction measured the way ADR-0214 §3 measured the code it removed: **`19:00` is printed ⁦4⁩ times on one screen and ⁦2⁩ after**, the copies ⁦311px⁩ and ⁦214px⁩ apart, with `BoardGapSlot`'s own docblock as the witness (its `עד HH:MM` exists because _"the board left a slot empty for a fact `GlanceCard` was carrying two inches lower"_) — and on the arrival day the card alone prints `14:00` **three** times. **§2d** turns the ⁦32px⁩ mono `0 · נותרו היום` into one sentence that goes quiet at zero, and adds exactly one fact, through the existing `DayTravelTotal` component rather than a new line. **§3** is the six probed days at one height plus the collision assertion: **⁦0⁩ blocks touching · ⁦0⁩ marks touching in all four theme×width runs**, a ⁦2px⁩ mark deviation on the two days with ticks (half a ⁦4px⁩ floor, structural), and the two shipped defects visible as ⁦0px⁩ → ⁦4px⁩ (zero-length event) and ⁦2⁩ boundary-sharing pairs held apart by the ⁦1px⁩ hairline. **Three things only the render could say**, all in the file's header: a ⁦9%⁩ spent block over a ⁦5%⁩ ground **vanishes in dark** (the neutral steps are now ⁦7⁩ · ⁦18⁩ · ⁦34⁩ — a light-only pass would have shipped it); `--track-point` cannot be amber, because the probe's own short-events day has an event with no `endsAt` and amber would paint an idea as a commitment; and enlarging the mark to ⁦15px⁩ for the taller rail **made two marks touch**, caught by the assertion rather than by looking, because `DAY_TRACK.MARK_MIN_PX = 16` **is the glyph's own rendered box** (measured ⁦16.2px⁩ at ⁦13px⁩, ⁦18.7px⁩ at ⁦15px⁩) — so the glyph size and the thinning floor are one decision living in two files. **§4 answers the placement question the owner asked after seeing the file**, and the finding is that the slot was never chosen: ADR-0045 removed the fixture `מבט מהיר` row and put the derived card _"in its place"_, while the one ADR that did ask — 0188 §6, for the tasks band — drew the line exactly where this card wants to be (_"above quick-access on purpose … belongs with the board's what-now/what-next rather than beside a WiFi code"_). Two Home columns rendered at ⁦360⁩ say **⁦464px⁩ → ⁦301px⁩ from the top of the scroll column** (⁦163px⁩ less scrolling before the day's shape is on screen) and **⁦4⁩ hour labels in one column → ⁦2⁩**; on the ordinary day the recommended slot IS "directly under the board", since `ChangeFeed` collapses when empty and the tasks band is absent when nothing is due. It also opens one thing it deliberately leaves open: in that order the board's knob and the card's now-line mark the same instant on the same window ⁦30px⁩ apart, so the ⁦3px⁩ progress rail becomes a poorer copy of the track below it — and that slot already changes tenant at night (ADR-0214). **The proposed CSS block is deliberately short**: the floor, the hairline, the tick, the midnight fade and the mark row all arrive from `styles/day-track.css` by setting `--track-*`, which is what ADR-0214 §5 split them out for; what it adds is one property (`--gl2-spent`) and two lines the shared sheet should own itself (`.wp-track .track { height: var(--track-h) }`, and `.point.hard` so a tick can be a commitment — `.point` currently lands after `.hard` and swallows it). Interactive: theme, ⁦360⁩/⁦390⁩, the six days, one control per fork (inks · anchor words · lead), plus the track height (⁦14⁩ · **⁦18⁩** · ⁦22⁩), the mark size (⁦13⁩ · ⁦15⁩, coupled to the floor) and the rail's ends (**off**; when on they say the day's own bookends, never the window's ⁦07:00⁩/⁦23:00⁩). Defaults are the recommendation. Supersedes nothing yet: `glance-timeline-labels-v2.html` stays the record of ADR-0077's grammar, which this file proposes withdrawing on the Home rail only.

- [`mockups/daylight-on-the-day-v1.html`](../../mockups/daylight-on-the-day-v1.html) — **אור היום · ווידג׳ט קטן, וסימן שמגיע אליו** (2026-09-02) — **built.** One thing the shipped widget does that this file does not: **its foot's five marks are drawn, not emoji** (`ui/domain/SunGlyph.tsx`, and the design language's 2026-09-02 amendment). The mockup keeps 🌅 🌇 ✨ — which is the normal split, since in a mockup emoji do both jobs. The daylight half of _"Sunrise and sunset times could also be on the home screen and also on the day view, to indicate exactly where it catches us. It could also show golden hour times etc."_ Brief: [2026-09-02](../planning/2026-09-02-weather-and-daylight-brief.md). **Read this entry before proposing any surface for a fact the app does not currently say anywhere** — the file is the record of getting that wrong twice in opposite directions. **Pass 1** reasoned from ADR-0214/ADR-0215's subtraction discipline and reached a night-tinted GROUND behind the glance rail: ⁦+0px⁩ of height, ⁦+0⁩ text runs, ⁦+0⁩ marks, every number in its table a zero — and in dark it rendered **pixel-identical** to the rail beside it, because it mixed `--ink`, which REMAPS (⁦#16233d⁩ → ⁦#e7eaf2⁩), so "more ink" on a dark card painted night BRIGHTER than day. The owner: _"All your suggestions are soooo ugly. I want something inviting, more prominent, more friendly, not so cramped up. Also in the day view."_ **Pass 2** over-corrected to a ⁦244.8px⁩ card costing ⁦292.8px⁩ of Home. The owner: _"Too big! This should be a fun little widget, maybe have an indication on the glance and day view to easily link to where it finds us."_ **The rule the two corrections settle, and the one worth stealing: ADR-0214 and ADR-0215 subtract DUPLICATED facts and chrome that describes the drawing — they are a rule about redundancy, not a budget forbidding the app a new surface.** New information earns room; a second copy of an old fact does not. **What ships in the drawing, after a FOURTH pass removed everything else:** a **⁦105px⁩ widget** (⁦43%⁩ of pass 2) in `מבט מהיר`, **and nothing anywhere else**. An intermediate version added a pressable `.sun-mark` chip to the glance card's foot and a row to the day view's strip; the owner removed both, and the second removal forces a third — **the sky behind the glance rail went too**, because it was justified _only_ by making the mark's word and the rail's picture agree, and without the mark it is pass 1's rejected idea returning unargued. §2 and §3 are therefore both **records of removals**, each drawing the same surface in both columns, and the file asserts **⁦113px⁩ → ⁦113px⁩** on the glance card and **⁦41px⁩ → ⁦41px⁩** on the day view. The mark's own reason for going is worth keeping: once the widget sits in `מבט מהיר` on the same screen, the chip printed the same fact one scroll away — the duplication ADR-0214 measured (a code twice, ⁦240px⁩ apart) and ADR-0215 measured again (`19:00` four times on one screen). **The arc is the sun's REAL ALTITUDE**, sampled every ⁦10⁩ minutes from the same NOAA math that prints the times (proposed as `packages/shared/src/daylight.ts`, ~⁦60⁩ lines, zero dependencies — `suncalc` was rejected because `@waypoint/shared` carries **exactly one** runtime dependency today). That is what makes §4 a picture rather than an apology: at Tromsø ⁦2026-06-21⁩ the curve's minimum is **⁦+3.5°⁩** so it never touches the horizon, and at ⁦2026-12-21⁩ its maximum is **⁦−3.1°⁩** so it never reaches it. **The scale is FIXED at ⁦±90°⁩ and deliberately not fitted to the day** — a fitted scale would stretch a polar-night curve to look ordinary and §4 would say nothing. **The mark says WHAT IS NEXT and never a position, and that is a correctness decision:** `lib/glance.ts` runs the rail's window ⁦07:00⁩→⁦23:00⁩ stretched only to the earliest/latest event, sunrise is regularly earlier, and a clamped tick points at the rail's edge while its label states the true instant — **⁦43⁩ minutes off on a plain Tel Aviv September day**, ⁦154⁩ on Tokyo in June. The rejected tick is rendered beside the chosen mark rather than described. **Three things only the render could say**, each a defect this file shipped and then fixed: pass 1's invisible dark night (above); the golden band drawn in `--sun-disc` **desaturated to a dead grey over the day-blue sky**, most visibly on the day it matters most (Tromsø in June, where the sun sits below ⁦6°⁩ for hours and the band is the widest thing on the widget) — it now carries its own warm ink; and **§6's colour claim was refuted by its own first measurement** — in HSL saturation the dawn wash scored ⁦57%⁩ against teal's ⁦56%⁩, a near-tie. That is a bad metric, not a bad design: HSL inflates pale tints, the design language itself reasons in **chroma** (_"chroma, not hue angle, is what separates it from the pin hues"_), and a sky stop is a WASH while a semantic token is painted solid — so the comparison is now composited-chroma against the full token, and **the most colourful sky stop is ⁦1.7×⁩ paler than the palest semantic hue**. The sky is illustration and spends nothing from ADR-0028's budget. **Pass 2 is deliberately NOT redrawn** in §1: re-rendering its height with this pass's foot would give ⁦191px⁩ rather than the ⁦244.8px⁩ it actually measured — a straw man wearing its number — so a rejected design is recorded in prose, and mockups are not retrofitted. **§3 is a REMOVAL, not a proposal, and both its columns are the same strip.** A daylight row in the day view's `.day-ambient` was drawn and then rejected by the owner: _"these rows could become very crowded. I prefer to not have it at all if that's your best idea."_ Counted, that is right — the strip already stacks **a row per stay** (a hotel-change day is two), the day's **distance total** and Plan's **fit verdict** (`.day-fit`), so daylight would be a fifth row for a fact no truer of that day than of any other. **The day view is therefore unchanged by this feature, asserted at ⁦41px⁩ → ⁦41px⁩.** What is lost is the ask's own _"also on the day view"_, recorded rather than smoothed over; if it returns it does not return to the strip, because the day's `.sec-title` is the only slot there that costs no row and it already carries the add button. Interactive: theme, ⁦360⁩/⁦390⁩, five stress days (ordinary · long · midnight sun · polar night · arrival), and the two feel calls left — sky height (⁦48⁩ · **⁦64⁩** · ⁦80px⁩) and sky strength (⁦70⁩ · **⁦100⁩** · ⁦130%⁩), both device reads (ADR-0017). Renders four theme×width combinations with webfonts and no console errors.

- [`mockups/a-card-carries-its-own-source-v1.html`](../../mockups/a-card-carries-its-own-source-v1.html) — **כרטיס נושא את המקור שלו · איפה יושבת שורת הייחוס, ועל מה הראש מדבר** (2026-09-03) — **drawn, not built · three forks with the owner.** Session note: [`planning/2026-09-03-the-source-line-and-the-live-head.md`](../planning/2026-09-03-the-source-line-and-the-live-head.md). Manifest: `styles/tokens.css, App.css, screens.css, ui/domain/rate-card.css, ui/domain/weather-card.css` — re-run `inline-app-css.mjs` after touching any of those. Two owner reports off the shipped [ADR-0218](../decisions/0218-a-forecast-expires-and-the-widget-goes-rather-than-lies.md) build, the day it merged, plus one thing the render found that neither report mentions. **The file exists because a constraint expired.** [ADR-0180](../decisions/0180-currency-is-derived-and-a-rate-is-a-glance-card.md) §9 already drew "inside the card" and rejected it — `RateCard` is one `<button>`, an `<a>` inside a `<button>` is invalid markup, so "inside" was never a placement but a decision to drop a link the terms require, and it then wrapped to three lines on the render. **ADR-0218's build log made `WeatherCard` a plain region** (v1 has nothing to open), so an `<a>` inside _it_ is legal today and the question has genuinely changed — hence a v1 of its own rather than a re-run of §9. **§1–§2 draw three placements**: א׳ the shipped floating line · ב׳ inside for weather only · ג׳ inside for both, `RateCard` split into a `.fx-card` box plus an inner `.fx-face` button so the `<a>` is the button's **sibling**. **ב׳ is the trap rather than the cheap option** and the render is what makes it obvious: one source inside a card and another outside it, side by side, with no rule a reader could state — the split ADR-0078/0079/0094/0095 exist to undo. **A measurement changed the design mid-file:** ג׳ first came in at **⁦+9px⁩ a card** because the foot brought its own padding while the card kept its own; paying for it out of padding the card was _already holding_ (the strip's trailing ⁦12px⁩, the rate row's `--space-3`) takes it to **⁦+1px⁩ a card · ⁦+2px⁩ the section**, against a ⁦361.4px⁩ baseline. The link's 44px floor is met by the `::after` overlay `.fx-attr-link` already ships — **⁦47.2px⁩ target on a ⁦15.2px⁩ visible line**. **§3 draws the section's rhythm** with all three tenants; the daylight widget is a **labelled dashed box at its measured ⁦105px⁩ and deliberately not a drawing** — it has no source and so no attribution line, and the sibling file already paid for the alternative when an early version hand-wrote that widget and the owner read the stub as a second design. **§4 answers the second report** — _"today's weather should update based on where we are right now or where we're headed, not the start of the day"_ — and its finding is that this is **not a defect in `dayAnchorCoord`**: that function is a whole-day consensus by construction, the coordinate sibling of `dayAmbientZone`, built for daylight where one sunrise serves the day. What is missing is the coordinate twin of `liveZone`, with **one inversion** — `dayAnchorCoord` abstains on a crossing booking, and the live answer reads the **destination** (ADR-0107 §8, which is also the report's "where we're headed"). Only the live day moves; the rest of the strip keeps the day consensus. The head must then **name its place**, or a travel day shows ⁦22°⁩ over a היום tile reading ⁦31°⁩ — two true facts that read as a contradiction — so the name goes **into** the head's existing `.wx-cond` run as `place · condition` rather than adding a fifth element: measured **⁦46px⁩ → ⁦46px⁩**, free. Promotes the brief's **W6**, sharpened from "the next day's anchor" to the live one. **§5 is what the render found and neither report mentions, and it is in the shipped card**: the head and the first strip tile are the same day at the same place, so they print **the same number twice, ⁦60px⁩ apart** — ADR-0214's and ADR-0215's duplication, twice measured and twice removed. Drawn as a strip starting at **מחר**, costing ⁦+2px⁩ (a dashed beyond-tile's border) and bringing one further day on screen. Interactive: ערכת צבעים · מסך (⁦360×640⁩ · ⁦390×844⁩) · מיקום המקור (א׳ · ב׳ · ג׳) · היום ב-§4 (במקום אחד · יום נסיעה, defaulting to the travel day since that is the case the section exists for). Renders four theme×width combinations with webfonts and no console errors. **Status vs. the shipped app (revised 2026-09-03, same day): BUILT — all three forks taken** (_"let's build everything discussed here … and the weather follows you"_), as ADR-0218's 2026-09-03 amendment §A/§B/§C. Two differences worth naming, since both are cases the file did not draw until the build asked. **§6 was added during the build and is the one that changed the design**: `place · condition · amount` is the head's real worst case and it **overflows at 360px** (149px into 127px) — and with one ellipsising run the thing cut is the **amount**, last in the string and the single most actionable fact on the card (W4). So the head ships as three flex items with only the place allowed to shrink, and §6's row reads `תקין · השם מקצר, הכמות שלמה` at 360 and `שום דבר לא מקוצץ` at 390. Context gives ground; the answer does not. And the drawing's §4 pair moves the head **and** the day's own tile together, which §5 then makes moot by removing that tile altogether — the two sections were drawn in one pass and only the second's outcome ships. **One defect from the same report IS fixed** in the change that adds this file: the day strip printed `ש׳׳`, because a copy wrapper appended a geresh to `weekdayLetter`, which ICU's Hebrew `weekday: 'narrow'` already supplies — and the component's own spec asserted the bug, since both it and the component read the same wrapper.
- [`mockups/weather-as-a-glance-card-v1.html`](../../mockups/weather-as-a-glance-card-v1.html) — **מזג האוויר · ווידג׳ט קטן שהולך אחרי המסלול** (2026-09-02) — **drawn, not built.** The weather half of the same ask, and the sibling of `daylight-on-the-day-v1.html` above: it **inherits both of that file's corrections rather than repeating them**, so it opens as a small widget (⁦143px⁩) plus the **same** `.sun-mark` — one class, two features, two hosts, never two mechanisms that look alike (which is the parallel-copy failure ADR-0078/0079/0094/0095 exist to undo). The glance card's foot carries **⁦2⁩ marks for ⁦+1px⁩**. **§2 is the section that matters and the one thing a weather app cannot do:** a weather app answers "what is the weather in Tokyo"; this app knows the group is in Tokyo tonight, Hakone tomorrow and Kyoto on Thursday, so every row in the strip names its own day, its own **place**, and the forecast **there** — drawn against a generic three-day strip in the same section. **Four findings from reading the code.** (1) **The backlog is wrong about the store, and the correction belongs in the ADR:** it says _"`FxService` is the shape to copy rather than invent"_, which is right about POLICY (serve-stale-never-block, read-is-the-trigger, in-flight dedupe, `_DISABLED`, the Dexie mirror) and wrong about STORAGE — `fx.service.ts`'s own header says it needs no negative cache because _"there is exactly one document … so there is nothing to bound"_, while weather is keyed by place AND day. The accurate sentence: **weather is `PlaceEnrichment`'s store with `FxService`'s policy.** (2) **ADR-0180 §4's freshness rule must be INVERTED here**, and this is the sharpest inherited-rule divergence in the brief: that ADR decided _"absence is keyed on existence, not age"_ because a reference rate is published on a schedule and a three-day-old rate is still the rate — but a five-day-old forecast is not stale, it is **wrong**, and wrong on the surface a person checks INSTEAD of looking out of the window. §3 draws the inversion with an age control (⁦1⁩ hour · ⁦8⁩ hours · ⁦3⁩ days): past the bound the widget **goes** rather than lies. (3) **The horizon is a state, not an error** — a provider reaches ~⁦7⁩–⁦16⁩ days and a trip is often planned further out, so day ⁦12⁩ is a dashed placeholder in the SOFT grammar (`.wp-track-empty`'s own answer for "nobody has filled this in yet") and the widget is absent, per ADR-0050's derived-tile rule and `RateCard`'s `null` on an unpriceable pair. §4 draws it **beside the daylight widget, which is present on that same day** — the strongest single argument in the brief for shipping the two features separately, since building them as one makes the free half inherit the horizon of the expensive half for no reason. (4) **Nothing is spent from the colour budget:** the condition is an emoji (content), the temperature a mono run — ADR-0180 §8's treatment for a fact about the world. **§5 renders the refusal rather than asserting it**, counting text runs the way ADR-0214's census did (**⁦18⁩ proposed against ⁦24⁩ rejected**): hourly strip, humidity, pressure, wind and "feels like" are a weather tab inside a card, and that is what ADR-0004 exists to refuse. The **only** `--miss` on the page is a severe-weather alert — genuinely a status, and the brief's fork F — defaulted **off**, because spending a semantic hue on a `Could` is how a budget erodes. **§7 is the full-trip pass, and it is where the file earns its keep**: a complete Home column in `Home.tsx`'s real section order (board · היום במבט · גישה מהירה · מבט מהיר) over a **⁦12⁩-event day**, beside a complete Day column with the strip and ten `.wp-event` rows — so both widgets are measured **competing for space with `RateCard`, the four quick tiles and the board**, which is the context they will actually live in. Three assertions, two passing and one opening a question: the glance foot carrying **two** marks beside `מסתיים` stays **one line at ⁦360px⁩** (⁦16px⁩ against a ⁦15px⁩ single line); the busy rail measures **⁦0⁩ touching blocks · ⁦0⁩ touching marks** (⁦5⁩ marks after thinning, ⁦11⁩ segments after the overlap collapse), box-to-box with `getBoundingClientRect` — ADR-0214 §3's own instrument; and **`מבט מהיר` starts ⁦595px⁩ into a ⁦945px⁩ column, i.e. below the fold on a ⁦640px⁩ phone.** That last one is left open rather than fixed: it is right for what the widgets are (a fact about the world, true all day, that a person goes looking for), and it is precisely why the **marks** exist — they sit high, in the glance card's foot, carrying the half that must be immediate. Moving the widget above the fold is a section-order decision in `Home.tsx`, not a design change, and it is the same question `glance-v2.html` §4 asked once already with the same instrument. **Two process lessons are recorded in the notes panel because both cost a render.** (1) **The first collision run reported an overlapping pair, and the reflex — call it a defect — would have been wrong:** the app never draws overlapping blocks, because `buildDayGlance` returns the **roots** of the containment forest (ADR-0041) and a partial overlap comes back as ONE segment. The fidelity bug was in this file's stress data, not the design; modelling the collapse (⁦12⁩ events → ⁦11⁩ segments) returned ⁦0⁩ · ⁦0⁩. A stress case that does not model the derivation manufactures its own failures. (2) **The manifest was changed and the generator was not re-run** — `event-card.css` was added but `inline-app-css.mjs` was not, so `.wp-event-face` fell back to the browser's `inline-block` instead of the app's `grid` and every day row drew content-width. Exactly the drift the skill warns about: **re-run after any manifest change.** **§8 settles the order inside `מבט מהיר`, asked directly and answered against the first draft:** _"are we fine with the ordering? Sunrise sunset widget before the forecast?"_ — no, and it changed. The rule is **most volatile and most actionable first**: a forecast moves hourly and decides what you carry in the next thirty minutes, daylight is fixed for the whole day, a published rate moves once a day at most (ADR-0180 §4). Order: **forecast · daylight · rate**, which is also the order the ask itself wrote. The counter-argument is drawn beside it and loses on the record: daylight is the only one always present (no cold state, no horizon, never expires), so leading with it gives a stable section head — but that optimises for the **layout** rather than the **reader**, and the app already handles vanishing tenants everywhere (ADR-0050, `RateCard`'s `null`). §8's second column is the section with the forecast absent: daylight simply moves up and nothing else changes. **§7's day column also lost its daylight row this round** — see the daylight entry above; both features now leave the day view untouched. Interactive: theme, ⁦360⁩/⁦390⁩, forecast age, and the alert. Renders four theme×width combinations with webfonts and no console errors. **The stub became a defect and is now a ported component.** An earlier version drew the daylight widget here as a hand-written stub — a flat sky band, no arc, no sun — and the owner, reading the two files together, took it for a second DESIGN: _"you drew two versions. I want the daylight on the day v1 version (the parabola with the current time indicator)."_ There is only one design; the stub was a shortcut, and it is precisely the hand-copy drift the skill exists to prevent. Its renderer AND its CSS are now ported verbatim from the sibling, and the measurement table carries a **cross-file assertion** — the daylight widget must measure ⁦105px⁩ here, or the two files are drawing different things. That check earned itself immediately: the first port brought the JS across and left the CSS behind, and the widget rendered **⁦62.4px⁩**. The sibling remains the authority; if the two ever disagree, that number goes red.

- [`mockups/a-day-is-a-place-you-can-see-v1.html`](../../mockups/a-day-is-a-place-you-can-see-v1.html) — **יום הוא מקום שרואים · where a photograph goes on the two day surfaces, what the day's head says, and where the ambient strip went** (2026-09-05, three review rounds the same day) — **DECIDED, not built:** promotes [ADR-0219](../decisions/0219-a-day-is-a-place-you-can-see.md); build plan [`planning/2026-09-05-a-day-is-a-place-you-can-see-build-plan.md`](../planning/2026-09-05-a-day-is-a-place-you-can-see-build-plan.md); brief [`planning/2026-09-05-a-day-is-a-place-you-can-see.md`](../planning/2026-09-05-a-day-is-a-place-you-can-see.md). Owner, with a screenshot of the reader's photo-headed day cards: _"enhance my day view and plan day screens so that they look more beautiful, more professional and more stylish … the live sharing screen has images"_; then _"the ambient stuff (car rentals, hotel stays etc.) … I don't really like how ambient events look anyway"_; then, off the renders, _"a plus sign on the day header - what is it?"_, _"some of the text doesn't fit inside the header"_, _"several different fonts and sizes, which looks weird … maybe even lose a little information"_. **Reading the code reframed the ask:** the day rows already have a photo slot (ADR-0167 §1's `PlaceBadge` takes `photoUrl`; `badgePhoto()`, the snapshot and the Dexie cache all exist) and `EventCard` + the builder row never pass it — `Map.tsx` is the one host that does. Six sections, each tagged both-modes or one: **§1** the badge takes the photo (both; measured ⁦0px⁩: ⁦71→71px⁩, ⁦69→69px⁩); **§2** the day head as the reader's own `.sh-day-head`/`.sh-shot` drawn verbatim inside `.day-page` in both modes — three bands in one card: the 116px shot with the credit on its scrim · a 64px date tile (day of month + weekday, amber + `עכשיו` on today) beside the day's name · a footer band with at most two full-width facts (the total; Plan's verdict or past-day note) and the labelled `אירוע חדש` at the end — and the ambient strip drawn as it ships beside it, its three tenants split: fact → head line, untimed commitment → a `.transition-row` at the top of the list, a car hire's middle day → dropped; **§3** every state (today · no shot · empty day · Trip archive with the banner shortened to `לקריאה בלבד` · Plan past day with the note as a line); **§4** a passed row's photo greys (Trip only); **§5** `PlaceKnowledge` at `DECIDING` density inside `DetailSheet`'s real tree; **§6** the shelf tile with a 36px box, drawn to refuse. **Four placements the renders killed, each measured:** an icon-only `+` in the trailing cell ("what is it?"), the labelled button in that cell (clipped the name to `…ur crater ← Háifoss` at 360), the facts as `.sh-day-copy > span` lines (Plan's verdict 247px and the past-day note 269px in a 238px column), and a facts-then-button stack (a fourth band, +39px). **Typography settled at two text sizes and one display face** (numeral `--font-head`; name 15px/700; facts 12.5px/400, the total's 11px readout overridden; no mono, no emoji). Interactive: theme, width, the shot's height (88 · 116 · 140; 116 is the decision). **Two render findings that are not this file's to fix:** one credit line in two shipped treatments and two visual orders (ADR-0167 §4 now says which is which and the composition moves to shared); `.wp-maybecard-ic` is `display: block` under `.wp-placebadge`'s ring. Photographs synthetic (no Commons egress); the 40px legibility question stays ADR-0167 §18's. **Do not re-run `inline-app-css.mjs` on this file after phase 4 ships** — its "before" frames are the strip as it stood.

- [`mockups/the-app-is-seen-before-it-is-opened-v1.html`](../../mockups/the-app-is-seen-before-it-is-opened-v1.html) — **לפני שפותחים אותה · what Travelive looks like in chrome it does not own** (2026-09-05) — **PROPOSED, not built, no ADR yet:** the text forks are answered (owner, 2026-09-05), the cover fork is the recommendation this file measures and is awaiting the owner. Two reports, one subject: _"sharing links to the app appears with the logo, a title, and maybe some short description … that should work on WhatsApp etc."_ and _"the notifications show a big white rectangle instead of the app's logo (it should appear as black and white I guess)"_, the second with a screenshot of the Android status bar. **Reading the code found the second one already explained:** `src/sw.ts:165` sets `badge: '/pwa-192.png'` as well as `icon:`, Android paints the small icon from the asset's **alpha** in the status-bar colour, and the alpha of a full-bleed tile is the tile — the white rectangle is the asset working as specified, so the fix is one new monochrome asset and one line, and the large `icon` is correct today and untouched. `frontend/index.html` carries no `og:*` at all, so a pasted link is a bare URL. Five sections: **§1** the constraint that decides the rest — a PNG has no `data-theme`, so the theme toggle flips the **chat** and the cover is pinned to `tokens.css`'s light values (a `לפי הערכה` control drops the pinning to show the two-images-one-file bug); **§2** three grounds, and the failure is **symmetric** — board measures ⁦17.9:1⁩ against a light WhatsApp bubble and **⁦1.25:1⁩** against a dark one, paper ⁦1.15:1⁩ and ⁦12.48:1⁩, so no single ground clears the ⁦3:1⁩ graphic floor in both chats and the recommendation is **board plus a paper band** (max of its two regions: ⁦17.9:1⁩ / ⁦12.48:1⁩); **§3** the two covers that ship, the invitation one being `App.css:1403`'s shipped `.join-ticket` tree at ⁦1.85×⁩ rather than new art (rule 8 — the ticket already _means_ "invitation" in this app); **§4** the three approved strings in the card, clean then crowded; **§5** the badge silhouette at ⁦24px⁩ — the reported white rectangle, then solid / holed / outlined, with the outline killed on measurement (a ⁦9⁩-unit stroke on a ⁦512⁩ canvas is ⁦0.42px⁩ at ⁦24px⁩). **Five things only the render caught, four of which changed a decision:** the cover printed `כל הטיול שלכם במסך אחד`, which is `og:title` — the same sentence twice, one line apart, now the manifest's own `מרכז שליטה לטיול` (ADR-0170); the **approved** invitation wording stuttered on a long name (`הוזמנת לטיול טיול הבוגרים של כיתה יב3 ליוון`, invisible in the clean `יפן 2026` case) and is now `הוזמנת ל${name}`, which is the app's existing grammar (`הצטרפתם ל${tripName}`); the invitation cover had been drawn on a pure board, contradicting §2's own recommendation, and both covers now share the band; the ticket at ⁦2.6×⁩ was clipped by the ⁦630px⁩ canvas (perforation, avatars and `החבורה כבר בפנים` eaten by `overflow: hidden`) and the table now asserts the fit rather than trusting the arithmetic; and `11–22 בספטמבר` inside a Hebrew-leading description renders **`22–11`** (ADR-0118), proved by comparing the painted x of the two numbers — ⁦Δx = −18px⁩ raw against ⁦+18px⁩ isolated — because at ⁦12.8px⁩ in a bubble nobody orders four digits by eye. **Interactive:** chat theme, ⁦360⁩/⁦390⁩ (the bubble is a percentage, so the card width really moves: ⁦274.5⁩ → ⁦299.8px⁩), the cover's pinning, §4's bidi isolate, §5's badge inset. **Fenced as fiction:** the WhatsApp bubble, the Android status bar and the shade row are drawn from screenshots, not from anything this repo owns; every measured number is our own artwork inside them. The covers become `frontend/public/og-cover.svg` and `og-invite.svg`, cut to PNG by `scripts/gen-app-icons.mjs` (already the one cut for every raster icon, ADR-0087) — so this file's proposal block is the drawing that SVG is transcribed from and moves into no stylesheet.
